# MLA data sources — Election Atlas of India

**Reconnaissance date: 2026-09-14.** All HTTP checks in this file were run on that date from a
single host; a `curl-200` claim means the URL was actually fetched and returned content, not that
it was assumed to work.

## What this file is

The MLA page (`work/election-atlas/mla/index.html`) has three sections that need real data:

1. **Profile** — who the MLA is: name, party, constituency, photograph, term.
2. **Development fund** — the state-level analogue of MPLADS: the MLA local area development fund,
   known as *Vidhayak Nidhi*, *Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi*, *MLALAD*, *MLA LADF*, *BEUP*,
   *VANGY*, *MLACDS*, *CDF* or a dozen other names depending on the state. We need **per-MLA**
   allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed and expenditure.
3. **In the Vidhan Sabha** — the MLA's own assembly record: attendance, questions (starred and
   unstarred) and private members' bills.

This file records, for **all 36 jurisdictions** (28 states + 8 union territories):

* the exact URL of the official legislature site, and what member data it actually publishes;
* whether an official MLALAD / Vidhayak Nidhi portal exists, and whether it is per-MLA;
* the exact official URL for assembly election results;
* whether the jurisdiction runs a **shared** platform (which would let one fetcher serve many
  states) or a bespoke one;
* whether the data is machine-readable with `curl`, or needs a browser, PDF extraction or OCR.

## The fiduciary rule

**Every figure the Atlas displays must be traceable to an official government source.** This file
is therefore written to be *read as evidence*, not as a directory. Three rules were enforced
throughout:

* **No URL is invented.** Every URL below was either fetched, or seen linked from an official
  page, or seen only in a search result — and the per-jurisdiction source tables say which. Where
  nothing was found, the entry says **`not found`**. A documented gap is a finding, not a failure.
* **Only government domains count as official.** `.gov.in`, `.nic.in`, `.gov`, or a portal that is
  genuinely government-operated. Everything else — MyNeta, Wikipedia, news reports, election
  aggregators, RTI-derived press stories, private NGOs — is labelled
  **`UNOFFICIAL — needs verification`** and must never be rendered as a cited figure.
* **A structural absence is stated as such.** Five union territories have no legislature at all.
  For those, no MLA exists, no MLA fund exists, and no assembly record can exist. That is recorded
  as `no-legislature`, not papered over with a plausible-looking substitute.

### Verification legend used in the per-jurisdiction source tables

| Level | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `curl-200` | The URL was fetched during this reconnaissance and returned content. |
| `linked-from-official-page` | Not fetched directly, but the href was found in the markup of a government page. |
| `search-result-only` | Seen only in search-engine results; **not** confirmed against the government site. |
| `unverified` | Found but could not be confirmed by any means. |

### How to read the verdict column

| Verdict | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `all-official` | Every one of the three sections is covered by an official, usable source. |
| `partial` | Some sections are covered officially; others are missing or unusable. |
| `gap` | A legislature exists but essentially none of the three sections is officially sourceable. |
| `no-legislature` | No legislature exists, so no MLA record of any kind can exist. |

**No jurisdiction in India reaches `all-official`.** The best result is `partial`, and every one of
the 31 jurisdictions with a legislature falls short on at least the development fund. That is the
headline finding of this reconnaissance, and it is worth stating before the detail.

# Jurisdiction by jurisdiction

One `##` section per jurisdiction, in the Atlas's own region order (28 states, then 8 union
territories). Each covers Profile / Development fund / In the Vidhan Sabha, then the exact-URL
source list with its verification level, a format note, a gap note and a platform note.

## Andhra Pradesh (IN-AP)

**Verdict:** partial - The Andhra Pradesh Legislature runs an official Liferay DXP site that publishes a complete 175-member roster with per-MLA photographs and a 384-page born-digital "Who is Who" profile PDF (name, party, constituency, DOB, education, address), and the state CEO publishes per-constituency Form-20 results; but per-MLA development-fund data has NO official portal at all, and attendance / questions / private-members'-bills are not published per member (the questions module sits behind a login + CAPTCHA wall).

### Profile

The official site of the Andhra Pradesh Legislature is **https://aplegislature.org/** (fetched: HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=UTF-8`, 118,268 bytes; it redirects to `https://aplegislature.org/web/aplegislature`). The Legislative Assembly has its own subsite at **https://aplegislature.org/web/legislative-assembly** (HTTP 200, 95,878 bytes). The legislature is **bicameral** - a Legislative Council (Vidhan Parishad) also exists - but only the Assembly is in scope here.

The member roster is at **https://aplegislature.org/web/legislative-assembly/legislative-assembly/legislative-assembly/member-s-information** (HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=UTF-8`, 441,628 bytes). It publishes, for all **175 MLAs** (constituency numbers 1-175, all present, zero gaps, 175 distinct `mem_id` values): **name, constituency number and name, district, and party affiliation**. Tab views are offered for Term XVI / XV / XIV, District, Party Position, Party wise, Women, SC MLAs, ST MLAs and an Address List. This is not a `<table>` element - the roster is laid out in `div`/`span` markup inside a single Liferay portlet, so a scraper must parse structure, not a grid.

**Photographs ARE published.** The roster page embeds 175 JPEGs at `https://admin.aplegislature.org//preview.do?filePath=basePath&fileName=photos/16/{mem_id}.jpg` (term id 16 for the current Assembly). A sample fetch returned HTTP 200, `image/jpeg`, 24,576 bytes, 480x720 baseline JPEG. This is one of the cleanest per-MLA photo sources found in this reconnaissance.

The strongest profile artefact is the official **"Who is Who" of the Sixteenth Legislative Assembly**, linked from the site at `https://legislation.aplegislature.org/PreviewPage.do?filePath=basePath&fileName=Portlets/LatestNews/1786621611527_SixteenthAndhraPradeshLegislativeAssembly-MLA-WhoisWho.pdf` (HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, 66,723,748 bytes, **384 pages**, PDF 1.6). It is a **born-digital, fully text-extractable** document containing **175 profile pages**, each carrying Name, Date of Birth, Father's Name, Mother's Name, Educational Qualifications, Marital Status, Name of the Spouse, Children, **Positions Held (prior assembly terms)**, Permanent Address, Address for Communication, Mobile and e-mail - with the MLA's photograph on the facing page, which also repeats constituency number/name, district and party. Example extracted verbatim: `Name : SRI ASHOK BENDALAM / Date of Birth : 10-August-1982 / ... / Positions Held : 2024 onwards - Member, APLA & Govt. Whip; 2019-2024 Member, APLA; 2014-2019 Member, APLA`.

Term-wise historical member lists are published as PDFs from **https://aplegislature.org/web/aplegislature/termWiseMembersList** (HTTP 200, 54,000 bytes): Hyderabad Legislative Assembly plus 1st-16th Terms (17 PDFs). The 16th Term PDF (HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, 217,549 bytes, 7 pages) and the 15th Term PDF (223,518 bytes, 9 pages) are both Microsoft Word-produced **born-digital text PDFs** with name, constituency number, constituency name and party affiliation, and they also record mid-term disqualifications and resignations.

Per-member drill-down detail is a Liferay **portlet action URL** carrying a session-bound `p_auth` CSRF token, e.g. `...?p_p_id=AssemblyMemberInformation_APPortlet_INSTANCE_Omh6XDofLcHN&p_p_lifecycle=1&...&_AssemblyMemberInformation_APPortlet_INSTANCE_Omh6XDofLcHN_javax.portlet.action=getMemberDetails&...mem_id=3107&...term_id=16&p_auth=sIXNBXMb`. Fetching it with curl returned **HTTP 403** - the token is tied to a browser session, so the drill-down is not directly fetchable (the roster page and photos are).

The level of verification for the profile block is **strong**: every URL above was fetched and returned content.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**No official portal publishing per-MLA development-fund data was found for Andhra Pradesh, and the local official name of the fund could not be established from any government source. No official scheme-rules page was found either.**

Candidate hosts probed with curl and **all failing to resolve** (curl exit with no DNS answer, HTTP code 000): `apmlalad.ap.gov.in`, `mlalad.ap.gov.in`, `mlalad.ap.nic.in`, `apmlalad.nic.in`, `mlafund.ap.gov.in`, `mlaladfund.ap.gov.in`, `apmlaladfund.ap.gov.in`, `aplad.ap.gov.in`, `mlalad.cgg.gov.in`, `aplad.cgg.gov.in`, `apmlalad.cgg.gov.in`, `apfinance.gov.in`, `finance.ap.gov.in`, `planning.ap.gov.in`, `apbudget.ap.gov.in`. The state's own portal `https://ap.gov.in` (HTTP 200) is an **Angular SPA** (1,262-byte shell loading `runtime.js` / `polyfills.js` / `vendor.js` / `main.js`) and exposes no fund dataset; `https://dept.ap.gov.in/` is a React SPA (475-byte shell).

Two PDFs on the legislature site named `Final+Allocation+List.pdf` and `Final+Allocation.pdf` are **not** MLA funds - OCR of their first pages shows they are `GOVERNMENT OF ANDHRA PRADESH / LEGISLATURE SECRETARIAT / Notification No.336/OP.1/2014` concerning the **allocation of legislature department staff posts between Andhra Pradesh and Telangana** under the 2014 Reorganisation Act. They are a trap for any keyword-based harvester.

The national analogue confirms the gap rather than filling it: **https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/** (HTTP 200, 62,712 bytes) is scoped strictly to **Members of Parliament** - its own text refers only to Lok Sabha and Rajya Sabha members - so **AP MLAs are not covered by MPLADS** and there is no shared national per-MLA fund dataset to fall back on.

Secondary, **UNOFFICIAL** reporting indicates AP MLAs do receive a constituency-level development allocation (news reports of approximately Rs 2 crore for "special development"), but no primary government source publishing per-MLA allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure was located. Not a single number in this category can currently be traced to an official source for Andhra Pradesh.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Attendance: NOT published per member.** The legislature's Statistics page (**https://aplegislature.org/web/aplegislature/statistics**, HTTP 200, 111,453 bytes) contains a session calendar only - a table of Session / Meeting / Date for the Council and for the Assembly. Its `Statistics` and `WorkingAnalysis` columns are **empty for every row** in the Assembly table. No per-member attendance figure exists on the official site. (Secondary press reporting on AI-camera attendance is UNOFFICIAL and publishes no numbers.)

**Starred and unstarred questions: NOT published publicly.** The legislature runs a separate legacy application at **https://legislation.aplegislature.org/** (HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=ISO-8859-1`, 12,014 bytes). Its landing page lists module names including **"Member Services"** and **"Questions"**, which confirms the data exists in the legislature's own systems - but the application is gated by a login form with a **CAPTCHA** ("Your email or username / Your password / Captcha"), and every `.do` endpoint probed (`/Questions.do`, `/QuestionList.do`, `/MemberList.do`, `/login.do`, `/Home.do`) returned the **identical 12,014-byte login page**. There is no public question list.

**Private members' bills: NOT identifiable.** The Bills page (**https://aplegislature.org/web/aplegislature/bills**, HTTP 200, 129,784 bytes) publishes 86 bills for the Sixteenth Assembly as an HTML table with columns *Sl No / Title of the Bill / Legislative Assembly / Passed in Legislative Council / Date of Publication in Official Gazette / Introduced On / Passed On*, with a Term selector (XVI / XV / XIV). It carries **no private-vs-government designation and no member attribution** - no bill is linked to the MLA who introduced it. (Every occurrence of the word "private" on that page refers to *Private Universities*, a subject matter.) Per-bill full text is a genuine distinct PDF on `legislation.aplegislature.org` under `Bills/PassedBills/English/...` - verified text-extractable, 17 pages, Andhra Pradesh Gazette Extraordinary - but ranging from **4.9 MB to 142 MB per bill**.

**Daily debates exist but only as scans.** **https://aplegislature.org/web/aplegislature/archives-tree** (HTTP 200, 1,449,918 bytes) is a Session -> Day archive from 1952 onwards containing **3,497 PDF links** of the form `https://sessions.aplegislature.org/preview.do?filePath=archivesPath&fileName=A-{session}_{dd-mm-yyyy}.pdf`. The newest entry in the tree is **22-11-2024**, so the tree is stale relative to the sessions the Statistics page lists (up to Session 50, August 2026). A sample download of `22-11-2024.pdf` returned **140,373,821 bytes / 224 pages** with **zero fonts and 220 images** - a pure scanned image PDF whose thin text layer is private-use-area mangled; it requires OCR. These debates would in principle contain the day's questions, but they are not indexed per member or per question type.

In short, of the four assembly-record dimensions, **none is published as a per-member figure**, and the official site itself does not claim otherwise.

### Format note

A fetcher can get the profile block with plain curl plus PDF text extraction, and nothing more: the roster is one server-rendered HTML page (parse `div` markup, not a table), the 175 photos are plain JPEG GETs off `admin.aplegislature.org`, and the Who is Who and term-wise member lists are born-digital PDFs that extract cleanly. The blockers are elsewhere. (1) **Liferay portlet state** - the roster's per-member drill-down is a `javax.portlet.action` URL bound to a `p_auth` CSRF token that is minted per browser session, so a naive fetch returns **HTTP 403**; a fetcher must first GET the roster, harvest the fresh `p_auth` and cookie, then replay the action. (2) **A login + CAPTCHA wall** on `legislation.aplegislature.org`, which is where the Questions module lives - every `.do` endpoint returns the same 12,014-byte login page, so there is no headless-browser path either without credentials. (3) **OCR** for the session debates: the sample sitting is 224 pages / 140 MB of scanned images with no usable text layer, and the archives tree stops at 22-11-2024. (4) On the elections side, the CEO's index pages are plain HTML and curl-friendly, but the **per-constituency Form-20 PDFs are scans** (0 extractable text characters for Ichchapuram AC), so 175 OCR jobs are needed; only the Gazette "elected list" PDF extracts as text. (5) `www.eci.gov.in` and the two AP state portals are JS SPAs - for ECI the workaround is its JSON backend at `/eci-backend/public/api/get-election-data`.

### Gap note

Three of the four things this project needs cannot be sourced officially for Andhra Pradesh. **(a) Development fund:** there is no official portal, no scheme-rules page, and not even an established official local name for the fund; no per-MLA allocation, works recommended, sanctioned, completed or expenditure figure exists in any government source located. Every plausible host name fails to resolve. **(b) Attendance:** not published per member anywhere official - the Statistics page's data columns are empty. **(c) Questions (starred and unstarred) and private members' bills:** the underlying data demonstrably exists inside the legislature's own `legislation.aplegislature.org` application, which advertises a "Questions" module, but it is behind a login and CAPTCHA and publishes nothing publicly; the public Bills table attributes no bill to a member and does not distinguish private members' bills. The daily debates are published, but only as 100-200 MB scanned image PDFs with no per-member or per-question index. What IS solidly official is the profile layer (175 MLAs with photos and a 384-page Who is Who) and the election-results layer (CEO Form-20 per constituency, Gazette elected list).

### Platform

Andhra Pradesh's legislature site is **bespoke Liferay DXP deployed by a common vendor, not a shared multi-state template**. The Liferay identity is explicit in the page title (`... - Andhra Pradesh Legislature - Liferay DXP`) and in the URL surface (`/o/apla-theme/`, `p_p_id`, `p_auth`, `javax.portlet.action`, `/c/portal/login`). The footer reads *"Designed & Developed by:"* followed by the **Centre for Good Governance (CGG)** logo at `/o/apla-theme/images/cgglogo.png` (verified HTTP 200, PNG 385x61), and CGG ships custom Liferay modules under the `/o/com.cgg.constants/` namespace. The concrete common-platform evidence is that **Telangana's Legislative Assembly - the state carved out of Andhra Pradesh in 2014 - carries the same vendor and the same footer formula**: `https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/` closes with *"Designed & Developed by : <img src="img/cgg_logo.png">"* and the identical sentence *"Content owned, maintained and updated by ... State Legislature, Govt. of ..."* as AP's, and `https://aplaqlcq.cgg.gov.in/` ("AP Departments") is likewise *"Designed and Developed by ... CGG."* However Telangana's public site is static HTML + jQuery, not Liferay, and its `member-legislature.telangana.gov.in` member portal is a separate login + CAPTCHA app - so this is a **shared vendor and design language with per-state bespoke builds**, meaning one scraper does not transfer between the two. By contrast, the ECI results platform `results.eci.gov.in` IS a genuine shared template: one bucket directory serves every state with identical markup, CSS, JS and logo, keyed only by a `S<nn>` state code (`S01` = Andhra Pradesh).

---

## Arunachal Pradesh (IN-AR)

**Verdict:** partial - The Assembly runs on the shared national NeVA platform and publishes an official member roster with photos plus per-member Starred/Unstarred question lists, and ECI publishes the 2024 election results as official XLSX/JSON; but there is NO per-MLA development-fund portal (only scanned allocation letters, Rs 55.00 lakh per Assembly Constituency) and NO attendance data at all.

### Profile

Arunachal Pradesh has a unicameral legislature: the **Arunachal Pradesh Legislative Assembly** (Vidhan Sabha), currently the **Eighth** Assembly, 60 constituencies. Its official website is the state instance of the national NeVA platform: **https://arla.neva.gov.in** (fetched, HTTP 200, 68,348 bytes). The legacy domain **https://arunachalassembly.gov.in** is now a 1,199-byte stub whose entire body reads "This website is migrated to a new URL https://arla.neva.gov.in" (fetched, HTTP 200); `arunachalassembly.nic.in` does not resolve at all, and the stub's commented-out HTML still points at a dead `arunachalassembly.com`.

What it publishes, verified by fetching:

- **Member roster** - `https://arla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec?IsStateDashBoard=True` is a filter shell; the data comes from the AJAX endpoint `https://arla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=4&MSLT=0&Assemblyid=0`, which returned a 395 KB HTML fragment containing **59 members**, each with name, party, constituency, date of birth, mobile, e-mail, a photo (e.g. `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/AR/FileStructures//Member/thumb/08_29_2019_05_00_03.jpg`) and a link to `/Member/Details/{id}` (59 unique ids). Filters exist for Assembly, party, constituency, caste category (ST/GEN), female/male, first-time elected, resigned/deceased/disqualified, ex-members, plus "Download Pdf" (`/ContactDirectory/PDFforHonbleMember`) and "Download Excel" (`/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember`) - both of those download routes returned **HTTP 500** when called directly.
- **Photo** - yes, per member, at `cms.neva.gov.in` (fetched a full-size photo: HTTP 200, `image/jpeg`, 21,311 bytes).
- **Member profile page** - `https://arla.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/{id}` (e.g. `/Member/Details/9` for Pema Khandu) shows photo, name, party, "Constituency Name" with number (Mukto -[3]-), permanent address, local address, mobile, tel office and e-mail, plus tabs General / Questions / Notice / Assurances / Committee / Debates / BILLS.
- **Roster quality caveat** - the 8th-Assembly roster contains contradictions: two members against Mechuka (Kento Rina and Pasang Dorjee Sona), two against Itanagar (Nabam Tuki and Techi Kaso), two against Khonsa-East (Chakat Aboh and Wanglam Sawin), and the list still carries members of the previous (7th) Assembly with stale phone/DOB values. 59 entries against 60 seats. Party counts as displayed on the home dashboard: BJP 43, Janata Dal (JD(U)) 6, INC 3, NPP 3, Independents 3, JD(S) 1.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund in Arunachal Pradesh is NOT called "Vidhayak Nidhi". Its official name is the **Member of Legislative Assembly Local Area Development Scheme - "MLALADS" / "MLALAD"**, and in the state's own accounts it sits under Sub-Head 18 "MLA's Local Area Development Fund".

There is **no per-MLA portal** (no equivalent of mplads.mospi.gov.in). What exists officially is the Department of Planning, Investment & Finance scheme page **https://arunachalplan.gov.in/member-of-legislative-assembly-local-area-development-fund/** (fetched, HTTP 200), which lists only five items: revised Guidelines 2014; "List of ready sanctioned projects" (this row's link is literally `#` - nothing is published); allocation of fund 2020-21; allocation of fund 2019-20; and a training document. The page's own hrefs point at a dead mirror (`chockitestsite.xyz`, curl exit 52 / no connection); the same files do resolve on the government domain and were verified with curl:

- `https://www.arunachalplan.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/MLALADS_Revised_Guidelines_2014-compressed.pdf` (HTTP 200, application/pdf, 3,098,650 bytes)
- `https://www.arunachalplan.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/MLALADS_Allocation_2020_21_1st_2nd_3rd_4rh_Final-installment-compressed.pdf` (HTTP 200, 1,451,358 bytes)
- `https://www.arunachalplan.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/MLALADS_Allocation_2019_20_1st_2nd_3rd_4th_Final_Installment-compressed.pdf` (HTTP 200, 1,632,648 bytes)
- `https://www.arunachalplan.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Training_Cap_building_MLALAD_Jan2014.pdf` (HTTP 200) and `.../Monitoring-Committee-Constitution.pdf` (HTTP 200)

Format and granularity: the **2014 guidelines PDF is a pure scan - zero `/Font` objects, 13 DCTDecode images - so it needs OCR**; the allocation letters are scanned documents with an OCR text layer, and their text is **per District / per Assembly Constituency, not per MLA**. Extracted from the 2020-21 letter: Rs 3300.00 lakh was the 1st-installment release, at **Rs 55.00 lakh per Assembly Constituency**, "placed at the disposal of the respective Deputy Commissioners/Additional Deputy Commissioners", debitable under Major Head 4070, Minor Head 800 "District Planning Machinery", Sub-Head 18 "MLA's Local Area Development Fund", Object Head 53 major works, Demand 50. Per-MLA fund is therefore only partially derivable: it is a flat per-constituency allocation, not a per-MLA entitlement, and there is no published allocation limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure dataset for any individual MLA.

The scheme rules (retrieved as a text-extractable PDF from the non-government host `arunachalpwd.org`, which is the same document as the scanned official copy) confirm the design: each MLA recommends works to the **Deputy Commissioner** of his/her district, the DC implements through government agencies, the **Planning Department** is nodal, and monitoring is by physical field inspection only - there is no provision for online project reporting and no public works register. Budget-year document `https://www.arunachalplan.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Legislative-Assembly-BE-2025-26-Schematic-breakup-of-the-fund-of-Rs.250.00-lakh.pdf` exists (HTTP 200, 1,548,738 bytes) but is a scan with no extractable text and is a Legislative-Assembly-line budget schematic, not per-MLA fund data.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

- **Questions - starred and unstarred: PUBLISHED, per member.** `https://arla.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Question?MemberId={id}&AssemblyID={7|8}` returns an HTML table with columns Sr No / Question Number / **Question Type (Starred, Unstarred)** / Date / Minister / Subject / Document, where Document links point to PDFs on `cms.neva.gov.in/.../PaperLaid/...`. Verified: MemberId=53 (Wanglin Lowangdong) returned 7 questions - 2 Starred, 5 Unstarred, dated 27/08/20, with 4 PDF links; MemberId 9, 14, 30 and 46 returned "No Records Founds". The state-level `https://arla.neva.gov.in/Questions` page also exposes a search UI filtering by House (EIGHTH ARUNACHAL PRADESH LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY, AssemblyId=7), Session (only "FIFTH SESSION", 27/Aug/2020), Member (all 59 listed with member codes), Minister and Subject, backed by AJAX routes `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionBySearch` and `/Questions/FetchUnListQuestions` - several of these returned **HTTP 500** without the full form payload, so the search is not reliably callable headlessly.
- **Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED.** The string "attendance" does not appear anywhere in the home page, member directory, member profile, questions or bills pages fetched from arla.neva.gov.in; there is no attendance module, and nothing per-member.
- **Private members' bills: NOT PUBLISHED (empty module).** `https://arla.neva.gov.in/Bill/GetBillDashBoardData?UnitCode=AR` returns "No Records Founds"; the home dashboard shows "Government Bills 0" and "Committee Reports 0"; the per-member BILLS tab renders "No Records Founds" for every member checked (including the Chief Minister and a current MLA). The `Legislation`/`BILLS` navigation exists but carries no data for Arunachal Pradesh.
- Also empty: Assurances and Debates tabs (both "No Records Founds" per member). "Paper-Laid 10" is the only populated non-member counter on the dashboard.

| CAG Report No. 1 of 2021, Government of Arunachal Pradesh | https://cag.gov.in/webroot/uploads/download_audit_report/2019/Report%20No.%201%20of%202021%20Govt.%20of%20Arunachal%20Pradesh_SEGRS-0630c7d9814e2d0.41178959.pdf | yes | fund | PDF (audit commentary; aggregate, not per-MLA) | search-result-only |

### Format note

A fetcher needs three different techniques. (1) **Assembly data**: a plain `curl` GET works, but every useful endpoint is a server-side AJAX fragment, not JSON - you must first establish an `ASP.NET_SessionId` by GETting `https://arla.neva.gov.in/` and then GETting `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec?IsStateDashBoard=True` with a `Referer`, otherwise the host silently redirects to the national `neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` page (a 192,907-byte response that looks like success but contains no Arunachal data). The roster fragment then comes from `FetchMembersList`, and per-member questions from `Member_Question`; both return HTML that must be parsed. Some sibling routes (`/Questions/FetchUnListQuestions`, `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, `/ContactDirectory/PDFforHonbleMember`, `/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember`, `/ContactDirectory/GetMembersStateDashbardData`) return HTTP 500 or 404 even with a session, so the HTML "Download Excel" buttons are not a usable route. (2) **Fund data**: PDF-only - the 2014 guidelines have no text layer at all (pure DCTDecode scan, **OCR required**); the allocation letters have a noisy OCR layer that can be text-extracted but must be treated as per-district/per-constituency, and the numbers are OCR-corrupted (`R5.00`, `l:55.00`). (3) **Election results**: the cleanest path in the whole jurisdiction - ECI's own backend returns JSON to `curl` with a static header taken from its public JS bundle (`secret: ECI@MAIN825`), and the per-AC result tables are published as **XLSX** that parse directly (verified: 66 rows, winner/runner-up/party/votes/margin).

### Gap note

What cannot be sourced officially: (a) **any per-MLA development-fund figure** - no allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure exists per MLA anywhere public in Arunachal Pradesh; the only official numbers are a flat Rs 55.00 lakh per Assembly Constituency (2020-21) and Rs 3,300.00 lakh statewide, in OCR'd scanned letters, with no works register at all (the "List of ready sanctioned projects" link on the department page is `#`); (b) **attendance** - published nowhere, in any format, for any member; (c) **private members' bills** - the NeVA Legislation/BILLS module returns zero records for Arunachal Pradesh, and the per-member BILLS tab is empty even for sitting members, so bills must be sourced from Assembly debates/PDF bulletins (also not exposed through a queryable module) or not at all; (d) **debates and assurances** - empty modules per member; (e) **a reliable statewide starred/unstarred question register** - the per-member question lists work but cover only the sessions the Assembly office actually loaded (the only session offered is the "FIFTH SESSION" dated 27/Aug/2020), so a member with no 2020 questions shows "No Records Founds" even though the 8th Assembly has sat since 2024; (f) **a CEO/state election portal** - `ceoarunachal.nic.in` is down with a .NET startup error, so all election data must come from ECI.

### Platform

Arunachal Pradesh is **bespoke-free: it runs on a shared national platform**, the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA), built by the National Informatics Centre with the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs - the footer reads "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India / Content managed by: Arunachal Pradesh Legislative Assembly", with a common version badge (2.0.2). The evidence is structural and reproducible: `neva.gov.in` links ~35 legislature subdomains (`arla`, `nagaland`, `kerala`, `sikkim`, `manipur`, `gujarat`, `goa`, `mizoram`, `hpvs`, `tripura`, `tsla`, `upvs`, `loksabha`, `rajyasabha`, ...), each returning the identical template - the same `<title>Welcome to <State> Vidhan Sabha ... | National eVidhan Application`, the same nav (`/Today`, `/Business`, `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Committee`, `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, `/Debates`), the same AJAX routes (`FetchMembersList`, `Member_Question`, `FetchDataList`) and the same `cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/<STATE_CODE>/FileStructures/` document store keyed by the same `AR` unit code. A copy-paste artifact confirms the shared codebase: Arunachal pages carry the stray title suffix "| eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh". One scraper written against these routes therefore serves every NeVA state, with the state code as the only parameter.

---

## Assam (IN-AS)

**Verdict:** partial - Assam runs an official, curl-able Assembly site with a complete 126-member roster including photographs, plus a national NeVA instance and a DSpace digital library that publish starred/unstarred questions and bills as per-sitting-day PDFs, but no per-MLA MLAAD fund figures (allocation / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure) exist on any official portal, and member attendance is not published anywhere.

### Profile
The official website of the Assam Legislative Assembly (Assam Vidhan Sabha) is **https://assambidhansabha.org/** (curl 200, `text/html`, 34,139 bytes at `/home`, `<title>Assam Legislative Assembly</title>`). The old-style host `assamassembly.gov.in` does **not resolve by DNS at all** and `assamassembly.nic.in` likewise fails, so `assambidhansabha.org` is the live site; its official standing is confirmed by the Government of Assam district portal `dhemaji.assam.gov.in`, whose "About the Government" footer links "Assam Legislative Assembly" to `https://assambidhansabha.org/home`. The site is a bespoke Laravel application on nginx (`Server: nginx/1.24.0 (Ubuntu)`, `Set-Cookie: laravel_session=...`), and its footer still carries unremoved demo template content from the free BootstrapMade "Restaurantly" theme (a New York street address, `+1 5589 55488 55`, and lorem-ipsum newsletter text) alongside the real credit line "Website launched on: October 5, 2021. Published by Assam Legislative Assembly, Dispur, Guwahati 781006."

The member roster is at **https://assambidhansabha.org/members** (curl 200, 52,267 bytes). It is server-rendered HTML — name, party and constituency number+name per card, plus a contact e-mail and mobile where the Assembly holds one, and a per-member photograph. It is paginated client-side by a jQuery pagination widget: the page embeds `totalPageCount: 7` and navigates to `https://assambidhansabha.org/members?page=<n>`, i.e. **20 members per page across 7 pages for the 126-seat House** (the rendered HTML carries 20 member links, ids ranging 148–298 for the current list). A women-only variant exists at **https://assambidhansabha.org/members?gender=F**. A filter block offers birth month, gender (male/female/others), party (BJP, INC, AGP, AIUDF, BPF, UPPL, TMC, NCP, Raijor Dal, CPI(M), Independent) and free-text name search.

Each member has a **per-member detail page** at **https://assambidhansabha.org/members?id=<n>** (for example `?id=148`, curl 200) with: name, constituency (e.g. "23 - CHENGA"), political party, father's and mother's name, date of birth, present and permanent address with contact number, e-mail, marital status, spouse's name, number of children, educational qualifications, party affiliation, and — importantly for the term/assembly number — "**Date on which elected to the 16 th Assembly 2026-05-04**". So the site covers name / party / constituency / photo / term in full, queryable per member by numeric id. Verification: fetched and parsed (curl-200).

**Photographs** are direct image files at `https://assambidhansabha.org/assets/uploads/mla/profilepic/member_<id>.<ext>` where the extension varies per member (`member_149.jpg` verified curl 200, `image/jpeg`, 46,733 bytes; `member_148.jpeg` and `member_151.png` also appear in the roster markup; `member_26.jpg` returns 404 because that member's file is a different extension). The correct URL must therefore be read from the roster HTML rather than constructed.

Assam also runs the national **NeVA** (National e-Vidhan Application) instance at **https://asla.neva.gov.in/** (curl 200, 63,090 bytes, title "Welcome to Assam Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application"), headed "ষষ্ঠদশ অসম বিধান সভা" (Sixteenth Assam Legislative Assembly) and showing Governor Lakshman Prasad Acharya, Chief Minister Himanta Biswa Sarma, Speaker Ranjit Kumar Das. Its member directory is at **https://asla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec** (curl 200, 58,936 bytes) and supports per-member lookup filtered by assembly (1st through 16th), Council of Ministers, Whips, Panel of Chairpersons, birth month, gender, nominated members, first-time-elected, re-elected, political party and constituency — the constituency list returned spans all 126 seats (Abhayapuri - 16 … Dispur - 33 … Ratabari - 4). Per-member pages at **https://asla.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/<id>** (id 26 verified, curl 200, 25,948 bytes) show address, phone and e-mail plus record tabs for Questions / Notices / Assurances / Committee / Debates / Bills. **NeVA member pages carry no photograph**, and the record tabs rendered "No records found" for the members checked.

The Assembly's own **Digital Library** is at **http://aladigitallibrary.in/** (HTTP only — the `https://www` variant fails the TLS handshake, curl exit 60; the server 301-redirects plain HTTP to `https://aladigitallibrary.in/`, which works). It is **DSpace 6.3** (`<meta name="Generator" content="DSpace 6.3" />`), linked from the Assembly site header as "ALA Digital Library". Its community **"15. List of Members"** (`http://aladigitallibrary.in/handle/123456789/3344`, curl 200) holds historical member lists per Assembly (Fourth Assembly 1969 through the Fifteenth), described as "List of Members Assam Legislative Assembly (With Address & Party Wise)".

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
The fund's official name in Assam is the **Member of Legislative Assembly Area Development (MLAAD) Scheme** — its fund is called the **MLAAD Fund** — and it is also written **MLALAD** ("Member of Legislative Assembly Local Area Development") on district portals. It is *not* called "Vidhayak Nidhi" in Assam. It was introduced in **1994-95** in the line of the MP's Local Area Development (MPLAD) Scheme, and is administered by the **Transformation and Development Department** (Decentralised Planning Division), successor to the Planning and Development Department.

The official scheme page is **https://transdev.assam.gov.in/frontimpotentdata/mlaad-guidelines** (curl 200, 58,596 bytes, `<title>MLAAD Guidelines | Transformation and Development | Government Of Assam, India</title>`) and the schemes landing page is **https://transdev.assam.gov.in/schemes/mlaad-schemes** (curl 200). They state that "All schemes are implemented by the concerned Deputy Commissioner of the district on recommendation of the concerned MLA as per Guidelines of the MLAAD Scheme", that the initial 1994-95 allotment was Rs 5.00 lakh per MLA, and that the annual allocation "has been increased from the existing 100.00 Lakh ₹ per MLA to 150.00 Lakh per MLA during the financial year 2026-27 and ₹ thereafter to ₹ 200.00 Lakh per MLA per annum from the financial year 2027-28 onwards."

The **scheme rules** are separate PDF documents linked from the same department: **Revised Guidelines of MLAADS – 2013** at `https://transdev.assam.gov.in/sites/default/files/swf_utility_folder/departments/pndd_medhassu_in_oid_2/menu/schemes/revised_guidelines_on_mlaads_2013.pdf` (curl 200, `application/pdf`, 1,220,335 bytes, 28 pages, **text-extractable without OCR**, issued by the Planning and Development Department, Dispur, 2013), plus `mlaads_guidlines.pdf` (curl 200, 768,600 bytes) and document pages `https://transdev.assam.gov.in/documents-detail/mlaad-guidelines-revised` and `https://transdev.assam.gov.in/documents-detail/mlaad-guidelines-corrigendum`. The 2013 guidelines confirm "**Constituency wise fund** will be provided from the State's Plan Budget every y ear" and that the fund is "Non-Lapsable and not tied to any development sector".

**There is no per-MLA MLAAD data portal.** Candidate hosts `mlaad.assam.gov.in`, `mlalad.assam.gov.in` and `mlafund.assam.gov.in` do not resolve by DNS from this environment, and an exhaustive search of the department and district portals returns no allocation/works/expenditure query interface of any kind. What *is* officially published is:

- **Aggregate state figures only.** The Transformation and Development Department's **Annual Administrative Report 2024-25** (PDF, 6,931,529 bytes, 108 pages, text-extractable: `https://transdev.assam.gov.in/sites/default/files/swf_utility_folder/departments/pndd_medhassu_in_oid_2/menu/document/annual_administartive_report_2024-25_and_gist.pdf`, curl 200) gives a table "Amount sanctioned against different schemes under DCP Division, T & D Department during 2024-25" showing MLAADS for FY 2022-23 **allocation Rs 12,600.00 lakh / sanctioned Rs 12,600.00 lakh**, FY 2023-24 **allocation Rs 12,600.00 lakh / sanctioned Rs 11,340.00 lakh** (with Rs 10.00 lakh per LAC earmarked for Khel Maharan and Sanskritik Mahsangram), and FY 2024-25 **allocation Rs 12,600.00 lakh / sanctioned Rs 12,600.00 lakh** — in each case "against 126 nos. of LACs". The report also states the entitlement history ("At present, the amount has been increased to Rs. 1.00 Crore per LAC per annum"). Because the allocation is uniform, per-constituency *entitlement* is inferable, but no official document publishes a per-MLA figure.
- **District-level scheme-status PDFs.** At least one district publishes a works table: **South Salmara Mankachar** hosts "Year wise Status of Schemes Sanctioned for implementation under MLAADS Fund" at `https://southsalmaramankachar.assam.gov.in/sites/default/files/public_utility/Year%20wise%20Status%20of%20Schemes%20Sanctioned%20for.pdf` (curl 200, `application/pdf`, 149,357 bytes, 2 pages, **text-extractable**), with columns *Sl. No. / Name of the Schemes / Executing Agency / Amount Sanctioned (Rs. In Lakhs) / Amount Released (Rs. In Lakhs) / Balance Amount (Rs. In Lakhs) / Physical Progress / Remarks* — for example "Constn. Of Road from hatsingimari L P School to Kobad Hussain House in Fuleswar G P … 8.00 / 6.00 / 2.00 / 75% / APRs & UC yet to be received". This is **works-level but per-district, not per-MLA**, and only one district was found publishing it.
- **Prose-only scheme pages.** Most Assam district portals carry an MLAAD/MLALAD page with narrative and no figures: `https://cachar.assam.gov.in/scheme-page/mlalad` (curl 200, 130,981 bytes), `https://nagaon.assam.gov.in/index.php/scheme-page/local-area-development-about-mla-ladscheme` (curl 200, 153,360 bytes), `https://dhemaji.assam.gov.in/scheme-page/local-area-development-scheme` (curl 200), `https://hojai.assam.gov.in/index.php/suhrid-scheme-mlalad-fund` (curl 200, 23,705 bytes), `https://majuli.assam.gov.in/index.php/scheme-page/mlaads`, `https://sribhumi.assam.gov.in/scheme-page/mlaads`, `https://dimahasao.assam.gov.in/index.php/scheme-page/mla-lad`.
- **The Cachar district FY-wise MLAADS PDFs are broken links.** `cachar.assam.gov.in/scheme-page/mlalad` links `mlaads_fy_2016-17.pdf` … `mlaads_fy_2019-20.pdf` and `mlaad_guidelines_revised.pdf` on the *old* host `https://cachar.gov.in/sites/default/files/swf_utility_folder/departments/cachar_epr_amtron_in_oid_2/menu/schemes/...`; `cachar.gov.in` no longer resolves by DNS, and the same paths return HTTP 404 on `cachar.assam.gov.in`. So the only district-level works series that once existed is effectively dead.
- **SUHRID** is a separate Assam scheme drawing on MLA-LAD funds (`guidelines_and_application_form_for_suhrid.pdf`, curl 200, 600,985 bytes; `sanction_for__suhrid_scheme.pdf`, curl 200, 499,869 bytes). The sanction order PDF contains **no ToUnicode map and no extractable text — it is a scanned/image PDF needing OCR**.

In short: the fund exists as a real state scheme with official guidelines and official aggregate sanctions, but **works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure per MLA are not published for Assam on any official source**; only ₹12,600.00 lakh sanctioned across 126 LACs for the state as a whole, plus one district's partial works table.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
**Starred and unstarred questions are published** — but as per-sitting-day PDF bundles, never as a per-member index. The Assembly site's own navigation has no questions page: the menu item "**Replies and Annexure**" hrefs straight to **https://asla.neva.gov.in/Questions**, i.e. the Assembly delegates questions to NeVA. On the NeVA Assam instance the Questions page (curl 200, 18,929 bytes) organises material as **তৰাচিহ্নিত প্ৰশ্ন (Starred Question)**, **তৰাবিহীন প্ৰশ্ন (Unstarred Question)** and **চমু জাননী প্ৰশ্ন (Short Notice Question)**, with a parallel "সূচী" (index) sub-menu for Starred, Unstarred, Short Notice, **Starred Questions with Replies** and **Unstarred Questions with Replies**, and "Answers to Short Notice Questions". The menu counts on the Assam NeVA home page report **2811 questions**, 98 government bills, 295 papers laid and 126 members. The list itself is not in the initial HTML — it is fetched by JavaScript (`FetchDataList(0,0)` → `POST /Questions/FetchDataList`).

The far more usable question source is the **Assam Legislative Assembly Digital Library** (DSpace 6.3). Community **"06. Questions & Answers"** (`http://aladigitallibrary.in/handle/123456789/3219`, curl 200, 75 items) sub-divides into "2025 Questions & Answers" and "2026 Questions & Answers" (`/handle/123456789/3787`), then into sessions — e.g. "**03. Budget Session 2026**" (`/handle/123456789/4108`) — then into "**01. Starred Question**" (`/handle/123456789/4109`) and "**02. Unstarred Question**", "**03. Starred Questions with Replies**" (`/handle/123456789/4390`) and "**04. Unstarred Questions With Replies**". Items are titled by date and question-number range, e.g. "Starred Question Budget Session 2026 06th July 2026 Monday **Q 1 to 20**", "…07th July 2026 Tuesday Q No 21 to 40", up to "…15th July 2026 Wednesday Q No 141 to 160". Verification: fetched and parsed (curl-200). So starred and unstarred questions, with and without ministerial replies, are officially published for the 16th Assembly's Budget Session 2026 and for earlier sessions — one PDF per sitting day, indexed by date and Q-number range, **not by member**.

**Bills are published, but "private members' bills" are not identified as a category.** The Assembly site exposes seven HTML list pages — Bills Passed, Introduced, Pending, Assented, Lapsed, Withdrawn, Negatived (`https://assambidhansabha.org/legislation/bills/<stage>`, all curl 200) — each with filters for House (15th/14th/13th Assembly), year and session, and each row linking to a detail page such as `https://assambidhansabha.org/legislation/bills/introduced/256` which contains only the bill title plus an embedded PDF (`/assets/uploads/bills/en/En_<token>.pdf`) via a generic viewer. There is **no introducer name, no government/private classification and no party attribution** on the detail page, and a text search for the word "private" across the bills pages returns nothing. On the NeVA Assam Bills page (curl 200, 37,215 bytes) the only categories offered are **Money Bill / Financial Bill / Ordinary Bill / Constitution Amendment Bill / Ordinance Replacing Bill** with status filters Assented / Passed / Pending / Withdrawn / Negatived / Lapsed / Introduced — again no private-member category. The Digital Library likewise labels its collection "**17. Bills — Government Bills**". So an MLA's private members' bills cannot be extracted from any official source.

**Attendance is not published at all.** There is no attendance item in the Assembly site navigation, no attendance field on `https://assambidhansabha.org/members?id=<n>`, no attendance page anywhere on `asla.neva.gov.in` (the NeVA menu is Home / Today / Business / Questions / Legislation / Committee / Members / Digital Library / Debates / Contacts), and the string "attendance"/"উপস্থিতি" does not occur in the NeVA member directory markup or in the NeVA FAQ page (`https://neva.gov.in/Home/NevaFAQ?PaperCategory=1`, curl 200, 84,156 bytes). Member-level record tabs on NeVA exist for Questions / Notices / Assurances / Committee / Debates / Bills — **attendance is the one metric with no tab and no source**.

`https://assambidhansabha.org/house/reports` (curl 200) publishes committee/DRSC reports, Budget and Governor speeches, all as PDFs; `https://assambidhansabha.org/business/special-mentions` (curl 200) and `https://assambidhansabha.org/business/debates/uncorrected-proceeding` (curl 200) publish Zero Hour mentions and uncorrected debates per sitting day, again as per-date PDFs, not per member. `https://assambidhansabha.org/matters-related-to-election` (curl 200) lists members elected to Rajya Sabha / Lok Sabha with their resignation documents.

### Format note
`assambidhansabha.org` is the easiest source in this jurisdiction: plain server-rendered Laravel HTML, no JavaScript needed for the roster, with member photographs as direct image files and the roster's pagination exposed as a simple `?page=<n>` query (`totalPageCount: 7` is readable from the inline script). Bill detail pages embed a PDF via a generic `<embed>` viewer rather than a link, so the PDF URL has to be scraped out of the markup.

`asla.neva.gov.in` is the awkward one. It is ASP.NET MVC behind IIS 10, and **HEAD requests return 404**. Fetches for `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Debates`, `/Committee` and `/Today` return HTTP 302 to the *national* `/Home/NeVA` unless the client first does a GET of `https://asla.neva.gov.in/` and keeps the `ASP.NET_SessionId` cookie; with that cookie jar in place all six return 200. Even then, the question list is not in the HTML — it is fetched by jQuery `POST /Questions/FetchDataList` (with a per-language GUID in the site's own JS), and a naive GET to that endpoint returns **HTTP 500** while a naive POST returns a 200 with an empty body. A fetcher therefore needs a headless browser (or exact replication of the AJAX payload plus session) to read NeVA question lists. NEVA also emits per-notice PDFs from a shared store at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/AS/FileStructures/...`, which are directly curl-able.

The **Digital Library is HTTP-only**: `https://www.aladigitallibrary.in/` fails the TLS handshake (curl exit 60) and the site 301s plain HTTP to `https://aladigitallibrary.in/`, which works. Its HTML browse pages and item bitstreams are curl-able; **its OAI-PMH endpoint answers `Identify`, `ListSets` (100 sets, including "01. Starred Question" and "02. Unstarred Question") and `ListMetadataFormats`, but `ListRecords` and `ListIdentifiers` both return `<error code="noRecordsMatch">` even with no set filter** — so OAI harvesting is configured but not functional, and the repository must be walked by handle instead. Note the OAI `baseURL` is misconfigured to `http://localhost:8080/oai/request`.

**ECI results are the cleanest source in the whole jurisdiction**: `results.eci.gov.in` serves static `.htm` files that curl reads directly and completely, with no JS, no cookies and no framework — party-wise (`partywiseresult-S03.htm`), AC-wise (`ConstituencywiseS03<n>.htm`, n = 1…126) and per-party winning lists, all as plain HTML tables. `ceoassam.nic.in` is equally static but serves its detail (Form-21E, Form-20, gazette notifications) as PDFs.

`transdev.assam.gov.in` and the `*.assam.gov.in` district portals are Drupal; scheme and guidelines pages are HTML. The Revised MLAADS Guidelines 2013 and the Annual Administrative Report 2024-25 **do have a text layer and extract with `pypdf`** (179,622 characters from the 108-page AAR), whereas the SUHRID sanction-order PDF has **no ToUnicode map and no extractable text — it needs OCR**. The South Salmara Mankachar MLAADS status PDF is text-extractable but is 2 pages and covers one district only.

### Gap note
Three things cannot be sourced officially for Assam and should be shown as gaps rather than filled from elsewhere. **(1) Per-MLA MLAAD money.** No official source publishes allocation, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure per MLA or per constituency. The state publishes only aggregate annual figures (₹12,600.00 lakh sanctioned across 126 LACs for FY 2022-23, ₹11,340.00 lakh for 2023-24, ₹12,600.00 lakh for 2024-25) in a PDF annual report, plus the uniform per-MLA entitlement (₹100 lakh → ₹150 lakh for FY 2026-27 → ₹200 lakh from FY 2027-28) on a scheme page. The single works-level dataset located is one district's two-page PDF (South Salmara Mankachar), and the only other district works series found (Cachar's FY 2016-17…2019-20 files) is dead because the host `cachar.gov.in` no longer resolves. There is no Assam equivalent of the MPLADS portal. **(2) Attendance.** Neither the Assembly site nor NeVA publishes member attendance in any form — no page, no field, no tab, no PDF. **(3) Private members' bills.** Bills are published by stage and by category type (Money / Financial / Ordinary / Constitution Amendment / Ordinance), never flagged as government or private-member, and bill detail pages carry only a title and a PDF with no introducer. A fourth, softer gap: **questions are not indexed by member** — the official artefacts are per-sitting-day PDF bundles covering Q-number ranges (e.g. "Q 1 to 20" for 6 July 2026), so a per-MLA "questions asked" count cannot be built from official data, and the NeVA member-level Questions tab returned "No records found" for the members checked.

### Platform
Assam is a **common-platform state for legislative business and bespoke for its member-facing site**. Legislative proceedings run on the national **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, a NIC-supported product of the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs: `neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` links **38 legislature subdomains** (`asla` = Assam Legislative Assembly, alongside `bla`/`blc` Bihar, `kla`/`klc` Karnataka, `mhla`/`mhlc` Maharashtra, `tnla`, `wbla`, `goa`, `kerala`, `odisha`, `raj`, `upvs`/`uplc`, and the `loksabha`/`rajyasabha` instances), the shared content manager at `cms.neva.gov.in` is byte-identical (22,126 bytes) to Assam's `cmsasla.neva.gov.in` mirror, every instance uses the same ASP.NET MVC routes (`/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Debates`, `/Committee`, `/Member/Details/{id}`, `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`), and the Assam member-detail template even renders the title "Member Details | NeVA | **eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh**" — another state's name left in the shared view. The footer on every page reads "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India". **One NeVA scraper therefore serves all onboarded states** for questions, bills, debates, committee papers and the member directory. The Assembly's own site `assambidhansabha.org` is *not* a shared template: it is a bespoke Laravel app on nginx, built from the free BootstrapMade "Restaurantly" theme whose demo content (a New York address, a US phone number, lorem ipsum) is still sitting in its footer. Its **Digital Library is again a shared product** — DSpace 6.3, an open-source repository used across Indian institutions, with the standard `/handle/123456789/<id>` URL pattern and an `/oai/request` OAI-PMH endpoint, credited in-page to "Novo Computing in Association with BSM International". The state's district portals (`<district>.assam.gov.in`) are a third shared platform, Drupal-built, sharing path fragments such as `/scheme-page/<slug>` and `/sites/default/files/swf_utility_folder/departments/<district>_epr_amtron_in_oid_2/...` (AMTRON being the state IT agency).

---

## Bihar (IN-BR)

**Verdict:** partial - The Bihar Vidhan Sabha runs a live bespoke site publishing a complete 243-member roster with photographs (HTML table) plus question and bill archives as scanned image PDFs, while per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi fund data is nowhere officially published and member attendance is not published at all.

### Profile
The official site of the Bihar Legislative Assembly (Bihar Vidhan Sabha) is **https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/** (curl 200, `text/html`, 117,142 bytes, `<title>BIHAR VIDHAN SABHA</title>`, footer credit "Designed And Updated By Bihar Vidhan Sabha"). It is a flat, hand-maintained static site: pages are `.html` files with jQuery DataTables applied client-side, and the shared header/sidebar/footer are injected with `$.get("html2.html")`, `$.get("sidenevigation.html")`, `$.get("bottom.html")`.

The member roster is at **https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/Knowyourmla.html** (curl 200, 114,686 bytes). It is a single server-rendered HTML table with columns **Cons. No. / Photo / Cons. Name / Name / Gender / Party / Mobile / Email ID**, and I parsed **exactly 243 data rows** (constituencies 01 Valmiki Nagar … 243 Chakai) — the 18th Bihar Vidhan Sabha elected in November 2025. Every row carries a photograph as a plain JPEG at `https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/image/final%20mla%20photo%202025/<n>.jpg` (**243 unique photo files**; `1.jpg` verified curl 200, `image/jpeg`, 14,312 bytes). A Hindi version of the same roster exists at **https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/KnowyourMLA%20in%20Hindi.html** (curl 200, 129,679 bytes). Party names appear as INC / BJP / JD(U) / RJD etc., and each member also has an official email of the form `mla-<constituency>-bih@nic.in`. Former members are listed per assembly (First to Seventeenth) at **https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/former_mla.html** (curl 200). There is **no per-member detail page** on this site — the roster is a single flat table and there is no individual MLA URL. Verification level: fetched and parsed (curl-200 + table parsing).

Bihar additionally has the National eVidhan Application instance at **https://bla.neva.gov.in/** (curl 200, 88,008 bytes, title "Welcome to Bihar Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application"), showing the 18th Bihar Vidhan Sabha with menus Today / Business / Questions / Legislation / Committee / Members / Digital Library / Debates / Contacts. However its member pages render **empty**: `https://bla.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/504` and `/Member/Details/132` both return an identical 22,426-byte template ending in "Assurances **No Records Founds** / Debates **No Records Founds** / BILLS **No Records Founds**", and `/Bill`, `/Questions` and `/Bill?IsStateDashBoard=True` all redirect (location replace) to `/Home/NeVA`. No member profile data is retrievable from NeVA Bihar.

Two official-linked hosts could **not** be verified from this environment: `https://blaqrms.bihar.gov.in/` (linked from the assembly home page) fails the TLS handshake, and `https://biharvidhanparishad.gov.in/` (the Bihar Legislative Council, linked from the assembly *Related Links* page) does not resolve by DNS. Bihar is bicameral — the Legislative Council exists — but its site is unverified here.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
The fund exists in Bihar and is called the **Vidhayak Nidhi** (विधायक निधि) — the MLA local area development fund — distinct from the separate **Mukhyamantri Kshetra Vikas Yojana** (Chief Minister's Area Development Scheme) that MLAs and MLCs also recommend works under. Reported per-MLA entitlement is ₹4 crore, with the assembly's 2026 budget session debating a rise to ₹6 crore; all of those figures come from **news reports, not from an official scheme document I could reach**.

**No official portal publishing per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi data was found.** There is no Bihar equivalent of the MPLADS portal. Specifically:
- The assembly site has no fund page at all: `Vidhayak_Nidhi.html`, `vidhayak_nidhi.html`, `mla_fund.html` all return **404**, the site sitemap has no fund or nidhi entry, and the MLA roster carries no fund columns.
- `https://bvm.bihar.gov.in/` — Bihar Vikas Mission, the state mission body most often associated in reporting with MLA-fund administration — is live (curl 200, 77,783 bytes) but contains **zero** occurrences of "vidhayak", "nidhi", "MLA", or "विधायक" on its home page, and `/vidhayak-nidhi/` returns **404**.
- Candidate hostnames `vidhayaknidhi.bihar.gov.in` and `mlalad.bihar.gov.in` do not resolve at all (DNS failure) — **no such domains could be verified to exist**.
- **Caveat and limit of this search:** the departmental domains `finance.bihar.gov.in`, `planning.bihar.gov.in`, `rd.bihar.gov.in` and `panchayati.bihar.gov.in` all fail DNS resolution from this environment, so a departmental fund MIS hosted on one of them could not be tested. This is a network-resolution limit, not evidence that such a site does not exist; it remains unverified.

Nothing found publishes per-MLA allocation, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure for Bihar. The only per-MLA fund material located is third-party: the RTIwala article "Bihar MLA Fund Details: MLA-Wise Allocation & Development Works" (`https://rtiwala.com/content/bihar-mla-fund-details-mla-wise-allocation-works/`), which is **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification** and cites an RTI route rather than a government dataset. No official scheme-rules page or government resolution for the Bihar fund was located either.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
**Starred questions — published, but as scanned images.** https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/starred_question.html (curl 200, 60,569 bytes) is a DataTable listing sessions and sitting dates (e.g. "3rd Session Of 18th Bihar Vidhan Sabha", 20-07-2026, 21-07-2026 …), each date linking to one PDF such as `https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/pdf/Question/Starred_q/01-03-11.pdf`. I fetched that PDF: curl 200, `application/pdf`, 1,255,960 bytes, and its internals contain `/Image` but **no `/Font`** — it is a **scanned image with no text layer**. The index is per-session-and-date, **not per-member**; individual MLAs are not an index key anywhere.

**Unstarred questions — published, same shape.** https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/Unstared%20Question.html (curl 200, 7,746 bytes) is a collapsed session list (15th Assembly sessions onward) pointing at PDFs. https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/house_question.html (curl 200, 17,620 bytes) is the analogous DataTable for House questions. `short_notice_ques.html` (Short Notice Question) and `notputinhouse.html` ("Answer to questions not put in House") complete the set. All are scanned-PDF archives with HTML date indexes.

**Private members' bills — published, as scanned PDF lists.** https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/bill.html (curl 200) splits into **Official Bill** (https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/official_bill.html, which itself splits into "Official Bill" and "**Non Official Bill**") and **Enacted Bill** (`pdf/enacted Bill/Act List from 1937.pdf`) and **Ordinance** (`pdf/Ordinance/from 1990 to till date 2016.pdf`). The non-official (= private members') bills page is https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/non_official_bill.html, linking `pdf/non official bill/bill.pdf` (curl 200, `application/pdf`, 48,099 bytes, `/Image` present, **no `/Font`** → scanned) and `pdf/non official bill/enacted_bill.pdf`. These are bill lists, not per-member records.

**Attendance — NOT published.** The site sitemap (https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/sitemap.html, curl 200) enumerates every section and contains **no attendance section** for either current or former members; no attendance field appears on the roster, and no per-member attendance data exists anywhere on the official site or in the NeVA instance. Assembly-record reporting that exists in public (e.g. ADR-derived press coverage of "silent MLAs") is **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**.

Also present but non-member: *List Of Business*, *Daily Bulletin*, *Cut Motion*, *Call Attention*, *Zero Hour*, *Resume Of Work*, *Committee* pages, webcast at `https://webcast.gov.in//biharvs`, and rules/notification PDFs including the Bihar Legislature (Members Salary, Allowance and Pension) Act 2006 and Rule 2006 — these govern pay and facilities, **not** the MLA development fund.

### Format note
A fetcher needs four different strategies for Bihar. **(1) Plain curl + HTML table parse** works for the roster and the question/bill indexes: `Knowyourmla.html` is server-rendered (the DataTable is applied client-side but the `<tr>` rows and `<td>` values are in the HTML), the 243 photographs are direct JPEG GETs, and the indexes are static `.html` files — no cookies, no captcha, no POST. Note the shared navigation is injected by `$.get("html2.html")`, so only the body region of each page is present in the raw response, which is harmless for table parsing. **(2) OCR** is unavoidable for the assembly record: every starred/unstarred question file and both bill lists are scanned image PDFs with `/Image` present and no `/Font` (verified on two samples), so a fetcher needs a rasterise-plus-OCR pipeline. **(3) Headless browser** is required on the ECI side: `www.eci.gov.in` is a React SPA that serves a 1,279-byte shell with `<div id="root">` and `<noscript>You need JavaScript to run this app</noscript>`, with its real data behind `/eci-backend/public/api/*` endpoints (e.g. `/api/get-election-data`, `/api/assebly-election-innerpage`) that I read out of the bundle but did not exercise; `results.eci.gov.in` is static HTML but keeps only the current election, so the November-2025 assembly folder now 404s and must be captured while live or taken from the CEO instead. **(4) Scanned-PDF OCR at scale** for the state result record: CEO Bihar's Form-20 set for 2025 is 243 AC-wise PDFs, and `form_20_1.pdf` alone is 11.9 MB, produced by "HP Scan Extended Application" with no text layer — roughly 2-3 GB of scans to OCR for one election. The NeVA Bihar instance needs a browser only to discover that it has nothing: `/Bill`, `/Questions` and `?IsStateDashBoard=True` variants all client-redirect to `/Home/NeVA`.

### Gap note
Three things cannot be sourced officially. **(1) Per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi data does not exist publicly for Bihar** — no allocation limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure figure per MLA is published by any official body I could reach, and no official scheme-rules page or government resolution for the fund was located either. The fund is real and discussed constantly in the assembly (₹4 crore per MLA, with the 2026 budget session debating a rise to ₹6 crore), but the money trail is not public; the only online route found is an RTI to the district or the department. **(2) Attendance is not published** for MLAs of Bihar in any form — not per sitting, not per session, not per member — which is why every attendance claim in circulation traces to ADR rather than to the legislature. **(3) The 2025 assembly election result is only durably official as scanned images**: ECI's own Nov-2025 result pages are gone (404), leaving CEO Bihar's 243 scanned Form-20 PDFs, which are OCR-only and per-constituency rather than a queryable dataset. One further honest limit: several `bihar.gov.in` departmental hosts (finance, planning, rural development, panchayati raj) did not resolve by DNS from this environment, so a fund MIS on one of them could not be excluded, only left unverified.

### Platform
Bihar is **mixed, and the more important half of the finding is negative**: the state has a National eVidhan Application instance — a genuinely shared national platform built by the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs with NIC, whose Bihar instance is **https://bla.neva.gov.in/** with a CMS twin at **https://cmsbla.neva.gov.in/**, carrying the footer "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India" — but that instance serves **no records at all** ("Assurances No Records Founds / Debates No Records Founds / BILLS No Records Founds", and `/Bill` and `/Questions` both redirect to `/Home/NeVA`), so one NeVA scraper would fetch nothing of value for Bihar today. The site that actually carries the data, **https://vidhansabha.bihar.gov.in/**, is **bespoke**: flat hand-written `.html` files, jQuery `$.get()` includes for chrome, jQuery DataTables for listings, its own directory conventions (`Knowyourmla.html`, `Unstared%20Question.html`, `pdf/Question/Starred_q/...`, `image/final%20mla%20photo%202025/...`), and a footer credit "Designed And Updated By Bihar Vidhan Sabha" with no vendor or template signature. The CEO Bihar site (**https://ceoelection.bihar.gov.in/**) is a third, separately bespoke static site. So a single scraper does **not** cover Bihar through NeVA; the working sources are per-state bespoke markup, and the reusable asset here is the NeVA URL pattern for states that do populate it, not Bihar.

---

## Chhattisgarh (IN-CT)

**Verdict:** partial - The Assembly site publishes an official 90-member roster with photographs and the fund scheme's rules are officially published, but there is NO public per-MLA fund data (login-gated), attendance and private members' bills are not published at all, and the current-Assembly question lists exist only as scanned image PDFs.

### Profile

The official website of the Chhattisgarh Legislative Assembly is **https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/** (curl 200, 3,311 bytes). It is a legacy **frameset** site - the root URL returns only a `<frameset>` that loads three child frames - so a fetcher must address the frames directly: `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/links.htm` (navigation), `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/main.htm` (news/updates), and the content pages under `/hindi_new/`. The English entry `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/default_english.htm` (curl 200) is itself an empty frameset stub, so the site is effectively Hindi-only.

The member roster is published at **https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/mla_current.htm** (curl 200, 253,158 bytes): a single static **HTML table with 91 rows (1 header + 90 MLAs)**. It carries constituency number and name, the MLA's name, party, permanent address and phone numbers, local (Raipur) address and phone numbers, and e-mail for some members. It is titled *षष्ठम् विधानसभा के माननीय सदस्य* (Honourable Members of the Sixth Assembly), which supplies the term. Crucially it also carries **90 portrait photographs** as plain JPEGs at `satra/sixth_assembly/mla23-28/6-01.jpg` … `6-90.jpg`, each 100x124 px - directly curl-able with no authentication. Party strength is published separately in an HTML table at `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/party_position.htm` (Sixth Assembly: BJP 54, INC 35, Gondwana Ganatantra Party 1, total 90; plus the 5th, 4th, 3rd, 2nd and 1st Assemblies). The roster is **not queryable per member** - it is one static HTML table for the whole House, with no search, no filter and no JSON/API. Constituency numbers are sequential 01-90 and match the Assembly constituency numbering, so a scraper can key members by AC number.

A second, NIC-built tier exists at **https://vidhansabha.cg.nic.in/**, titled *E-Prashna Chhattisgarh, Vidhan Sabha* (curl 200, 18,373 bytes). This is an online question-management application whose footer reads *"Site Designed, Developed & Hosted by National Informatics Centre Chhattisgarh State Centre, Raipur"*. It is **fully login-gated** (MLA login at `https://vidhansabha.cg.nic.in/MLALogin.aspx` by constituency number + OTP/password + a captcha restricted to "Only 1-90"), so it publishes no public member data. A related public document repository, *e-Vidhan*, sits at `https://cgvidhansabha.gov.in/evidhan` (curl 200, 204,178 bytes) - an ASP.NET WebForms page with DataTables listing bills, budget papers and CAG reports, paginated, and also carrying a LOGIN control; it is a document store, not a member dataset. A NIC/NICSI mobile app (`https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.nic.nicsi.vs`) is advertised; NIC's own write-up states it exposes *member profiles* along with Karyasoochi, proceedings and questions, but the app is not a web-scrapable source.

Verification level: the roster, the photographs and the party-position tables were **fetched and parsed in full** (curl-200, 90 rows and 90 `<img>` tags counted).

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund is called the **विधानसभा निर्वाचन क्षेत्र विकास योजना (विधायक निधि)** - *Vidhan Sabha Nirvachan Kshetra Vikas Yojana (Vidhayak Nidhi)*, i.e. the Assembly Constituency Development Scheme. Chhattisgarh also brands it in English as **MLA-LADS** or **MLALADS**. It was introduced in the 2004-05 budget speech, replacing the earlier *Chhattisgarh Sthalīya Vikas Yojana*, and is governed by the **विधानसभा निर्वाचन क्षेत्र विकास योजना मार्गदर्शिका 2023** (MLA-LADS Guidelines 2023) as subsequently amended. It is administered by the **आर्थिक एवं सांख्यिकी संचालनालय** (Directorate of Economics & Statistics), Raipur - the same directorate that handles MPLADS for the state.

The scheme page is **https://descg.gov.in/MLALADS.aspx** (curl 200), and the equivalent MPLADS page is `https://descg.gov.in/MPLADS.aspx`. From the official scheme page: the per-constituency annual provision was raised in FY 2022-23 **from Rs 2.00 crore to Rs 4.00 crore**; an MLA may recommend capital works up to **74% i.e. Rs 296.00 lakh** of the Rs 400.00 lakh annual entitlement, and works up to **25% i.e. Rs 100.00 lakh** are sanctioned by the Collector with the approval of the district's minister-in-charge. The page reports that **in FY 2023-24 up to March 2024, 9,062 works worth Rs 34,255.55 lakh were sanctioned, of which 1,815 were completed.**

Two official portals exist:

- **https://descg.gov.in/mlaladsonline/** (curl 200) - *MLALADS Online*, the public-facing landing page of the online system, describing the scheme. The application itself is behind a **लॉगिन (Login)** control and, at the time of fetching, displayed *"Software closed for updation"*. A news report dated 18 April 2025 records the launch of this MLA LADDS online portal, described as modelled on the MPLADS online portal and piloted in Raipur district before statewide rollout.
- **https://descg.gov.in/mlalads/** (curl 200) - *विधानसभा निर्वाचन क्षेत्र विकास योजना - निगरानी प्रणाली* (the monitoring system). It has public pages **`/mlalads/Pages/Guideline.aspx`** (the scheme rules - an HTML table of 13 amendment orders dated 2017-2024, each with a PDF link), **`/mlalads/Pages/Circular.aspx`** (4 allocation/approval circulars) and **`/mlalads/Pages/Contact.aspx`** (named officers with e-mail and phone). Everything else is an authenticated **यूजर लॉगिन**.

The consolidated scheme rules document is **https://descg.gov.in/pdf/mlalad/MLALADS-2026.pdf** - *विधानसभा निर्वाचन क्षेत्र विकास योजना (MLALADS) मार्गदर्शिका-2023 (संशोधित)*, linked from the Directorate homepage. It is **22,383,276 bytes, 28 pages, with 0 font resources and 47-49 embedded images** - a pure **scanned image PDF** requiring OCR.

**This is the central gap: there is NO public per-MLA fund dataset.** The portal publishes scheme-level aggregates and the rules, but per-MLA allocated limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure are **not** published for public query. The data-entry system is genuinely per-MLA internally (the online system records each MLA's recommended works, approvals and expenditure), but access is a gated government login. I probed for public report endpoints at `/MLALADS/Report.aspx`, `/MLALADS/Pages/Report.aspx`, `/MLALADS/Pages/PublicReport.aspx` and `/MLALADS/PublicReport.aspx` - **all 404**. The public ASP.NET pages carry `__VIEWSTATE` and submit POST-only, and there is no anonymous query interface. So: **the scheme exists and is officially documented, but a public per-MLA data portal does not.**

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred questions:** the Assembly does publish daily question lists, distinguished procedurally into **तारांकित** (starred, for oral answer) and written questions - the *About Questions* page at `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/prashna.htm` sets out the rule that a member may give notice of not more than four questions per day, of which two may be starred. The question-list index at **`https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/satra/question.htm`** (curl 200, 88,959 bytes) links, for every sitting day of the Sixth Assembly, a file named `satra/sixth_assembly/<session>/qDDMMYYYY.pdf` plus consolidated "complete answers" files such as `CompAns-FebMarch2026.pdf`. The blocking finding: **these question PDFs are scanned images.** The sitting-day file `satra/sixth_assembly/Tenth/q13072026.pdf` is **56,105,807 bytes, 409 pages, with 0 font resources and 4,374 images** - image-only, no text layer, OCR required. The lists are organised **per sitting day, not per member**, so turning them into a per-MLA question count requires parsing every page.

**Unstarred questions:** the same day-wise PDFs; there is no separate unstarred-question dataset and no per-member starred/unstarred tally for the Sixth Assembly.

**Per-member question counts:** two official **per-member** question-statistics PDFs do exist but only for **earlier Assemblies** - `satra/fourth_assembly/4th assembly mem wise qtn recvdNew.pdf` (2 pages, text layer) and `satra/fifth_assembly/5thAssemblyQtn.pdf` (3 pages, text layer), both linked from `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/sadan_sambandhi.htm`. **No equivalent member-wise question document exists for the current Sixth Assembly.**

**Session-review statistics:** `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/satra/session_review.htm` (curl 200, 19,826 bytes) links a *सत्र समीक्षा (सांख्यिकी)* PDF for each of the 9 Sixth-Assembly sittings and older ones back to the First Assembly, e.g. `satra/sixth_assembly/Ninth/sankhiki-jankari-April2026.pdf` (1,398,051 bytes, 1 page). These are single-page statistical dashboards. They use CIDFont subsets with Identity-H encoding and **no usable ToUnicode CMap**, so they do not yield extractable text.

**Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED.** I enumerated the complete navigation of the site (the `main.htm` and `links.htm` frames) and there is no attendance page, no attendance statistic, and no per-member sitting record anywhere on the official site.

**Private members' bills: NOT PUBLISHED.** The site has `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/prastav.htm` - but this is merely the *text of the procedural rules* governing resolutions (Rules 130-132), not a list of bills or resolutions moved. The e-Vidhan repository at `https://cgvidhansabha.gov.in/evidhan` does carry a *विधेयक* (Bills) category, but those are government bills listed by session and category (e.g. Chhattisgarh Rent Control (Amendment) Bill 2026, Chhattisgarh Private University (Amendment) Bill 2026); there is **no private members' bills section and no bill-to-member attribution**.

Other Assembly record material that IS published: *सभा की कार्यवाही* (proceedings) at `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/satra/proceedings.htm`, where `proc13072026.pdf` (2,079,607 bytes, 40 pages) **does have a text layer** (172 font resources, 6 ToUnicode references); *पत्रक भाग-1* and *पत्रक भाग-2* (`satra/patrak-1.htm`, `satra/patrak2/patrak2.htm`); *सभा में उठाए गए महत्वपूर्ण विषय* (important subjects raised, `satra/ugv.htm`); Governor's Address and Budget Speech; and the notification of elected candidates. The *प्रकाशन* (publications) page at `https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/hindi_new/publication.htm` lists 11 publications including *सदस्य परिचय* (member introduction) and *सदस्य संदर्भ* (member reference), but the page is **plain text with no download links** (only 1 anchor on the whole page, pointing to the procedural rules), so those member publications are not reachable.

| ECI national voter services (shared platform) | https://voters.eci.gov.in/ | yes | election-results | JS web app | linked-from-official-page |
