| News: MLALADS software workshop, 24/07/2026 | https://apnachhattisgarh.com/archives/20435 | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news HTML | curl-200 |
| Chhattisgarh state portal (no MLALADS link found in nav) | https://cgstate.gov.in/ | yes | platform | HTML | curl-200 |

### Format note

A fetcher needs a **different technique per source**. The member roster and party position are plain server-rendered **HTML tables with no JavaScript** - a single `curl` of `/hindi_new/mla_current.htm` and one regex pass yields all 90 members, and the 90 portraits are ordinary static JPEGs requiring no session. But the root URL is only a frameset, so a naive crawl of the homepage sees 3 KB and nothing else; the frames must be addressed explicitly. The hard blockers are the Assembly record: the **question lists are scanned image PDFs** (one sitting day = 56 MB / 409 pages / 0 fonts), so per-member question data needs full **OCR of thousands of pages**, and the **session-review statistics PDFs use CIDFont subsets with no usable ToUnicode**, so even a text-layer route fails and they must be rendered and OCR'd. Proceedings PDFs do have a usable text layer but embed subset Devanagari fonts, so extraction quality must be checked per file. The **MLALADS portal cannot be scraped at all**: it is an ASP.NET WebForms application with `__VIEWSTATE`/`__EVENTVALIDATION`, POST-only submission, a captcha on the login, and no anonymous query or report endpoint (every public-report path I tried returned 404) - a headless browser would only reach a login wall. The **MLALADS guidelines PDF is a 28-page, 22 MB scan** requiring OCR. The **ECI main site is a React SPA** (`curl` returns a 1,279-byte shell reading *"You need to enable JavaScript to run this app"*), but ECI's backend is a genuine **JSON API** usable directly with curl at `/eci-backend/public/api/...` - one endpoint (`/api/assebly-election-innerpage`) is POST-only rather than GET. `results.eci.gov.in` is server-rendered static HTML and needs no browser. The CEO's **Form 20 PDFs have real text layers** and extract cleanly. `election.cg.gov.in` returns "Authorised Access Only".

### Gap note

The decisive gap is the **development fund**: Chhattisgarh has a real, officially documented per-MLA scheme (Vidhan Sabha Nirvachan Kshetra Vikas Yojana / MLA-LADS, Rs 4 crore per constituency per year, 74% MLA-recommended and 25% Collector-sanctioned) with an online per-MLA management system, but **no public per-MLA figures**. There is no equivalent of the MPLADS portal's per-MP allocated limit / works recommended / sanctioned / completed / expenditure for the 90 Chhattisgarh MLAs: the online system is a gated government login, the only public numbers are scheme-level aggregates on the directorate's page, and the guidelines are published as a scanned PDF. Second, **attendance is not published anywhere** - there is no attendance page on the Assembly site and no per-member sitting record. Third, **private members' bills are not published**; the e-Vidhan repository carries only government bills, with no member attribution, and the site's "प्रस्ताव" page is merely procedural rules. Fourth, **per-member question counts exist only for the 4th and 5th Assemblies**, not the current Sixth Assembly, and the Sixth Assembly's day-wise question lists are scanned images, so an exact per-MLA starred/unstarred count for sitting members cannot be sourced without OCR. Fifth, the *सदस्य परिचय* / *सदस्य संदर्भ* member publications listed on the publications page have **no download links** and are therefore unreachable.

### Platform

Chhattisgarh is **not on a single shared platform - it is a mixture, and its two most important assets are bespoke.** The public Assembly site (https://www.cgvidhansabha.gov.in/) is a hand-built legacy static site whose own markup declares `<meta name="GENERATOR" content="Microsoft FrontPage 6.0">` and `<meta name="ProgId" content="FrontPage.Editor.Document">`, organised as a frameset with a Hindi-first `hindi_new/` directory tree and an English entry that is an empty stub - no CMS, no vendor, no template. The **fund portal is bespoke to Chhattisgarh and built in-house**: https://descg.gov.in/mlalads/ carries the footer *"© Designed and Maintained by Directorate of Economics & Statistics, Raipur (C.G)"* - not NIC - and is an ASP.NET WebForms application; it follows the *concept* of the national MPLADS portal (the state directorate runs both `MLALADS.aspx` and `MPLADS.aspx`) but is a separate codebase, so **one scraper would NOT serve other states' fund portals**. The genuinely reusable element is the **NIC legislature module**: `https://vidhansabha.cg.nic.in/` (E-Prashna) and its e-Uttar companion carry the footer *"Site Designed, Developed & Hosted by National Informatics Centre Chhattisgarh State Centre, Raipur"*, use the standard ASP.NET AJAX signature (`ScriptResource.axd`, `WebResource.axd`, `__VIEWSTATE`), and share the generic NIC/NICSI *Vidhan Sabha* mobile app `com.nic.nicsi.vs` - a product whose package name and app title are not state-specific, which is the strongest indicator of a shared NIC template. I could not confirm that reuse in another state, however: the other state `vidhansabha.<state>.nic.in` hosts I tried did not resolve from this environment, so the claim rests on the NIC credit and the generic app identity rather than a cross-state diff. The CEO site is ECI-branded (falcon `eci-logo.png`, `assets/css/base.css`, `theme/css/site.css`) and plugs into ECI's national shared portals (`results.eci.gov.in`, `voters.eci.gov.in`, `affidavit.eci.gov.in`), and ECI's own `links-to-ceos` endpoint lists `https://ceochhattisgarh.nic.in/` as the official Chhattisgarh CEO site.

---

## Goa (IN-GA)

**Verdict:** partial - Goa has a real, bespoke Legislative Assembly site that publishes a full MLA roster with photos, party, constituency and term, plus a per-member bill/LAQ record and per-sitting starred/unstarred question PDFs, but ATTENDANCE is entirely unpublished, PRIVATE MEMBER BILLS have no records for the 2017 and 2022 assemblies, there is NO official per-MLA MLA-LAD fund portal anywhere, and the 2022 assembly results have no live official URL.

### Profile
**Canonical site (verified): https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/** — HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=UTF-8`, 41,305 bytes. The TLS certificate is a genuine Indian Government Entity certificate issued to `O=Legislative Assembly, Goa, ST=Goa, L=Porvorim`, with `X509v3 Subject Alternative Name: DNS:goavidhansabha.gov.in, DNS:static.goavidhansabha.gov.in, DNS:www.goavidhansabha.gov.in`. This is stronger verification than an HTTP 200 alone and confirms both the apex domain and the `static.` asset subdomain as government-operated.

A second host, **https://goavidhansabha.in/** (no `.gov.in`), serves byte-for-byte the same bespoke site (same page titles, same footer, same `mlas.php`, same asset tree) and self-identifies as "This Website belongs to Goa Legislative Assembly, Government of Goa". It is a government-operated mirror on a non-`.gov.in` domain, so it must be labelled accordingly rather than treated as canonical; prefer the `.gov.in` host for citation and for relative-link resolution.

**Member roster — https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/mlas.php** (HTTP 200, 55,466 bytes, plain HTML, curl-fetchable, no JS needed). The page is headed "MEMBERs OF Eighth Legislative Assembly 2022", i.e. the **term / assembly number is published**, and it states its provenance in-page: *"The information on this page is as per the Who's Who and subsequent Bulletins issued"*. It contains **40 roster tiles** (`div.mla-info-box`), each carrying:
- a member **PHOTO** (`https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/uploads/members/<slug>_<mem_id><timestamp>.JPG` — 37 unique legacy photos) plus 3 members whose photos are served from `https://static.goavidhansabha.gov.in/goalpub/docs/members/...`, i.e. 40 photos for 40 tiles;
- **name**, **constituency** (e.g. "Mandrem") and **party** ("Maharashtrawadi Gomantak Party") as text;
- the **party symbol** image (`/uploads/symbols/<party>_logo_*.jpg`);
- a link to the per-member page `member_detail.php?mem_id=<N>`.
The page also has a "FORMER M.L.A's" tab (`/former-mlas.php`) and an in-page "Expired" tab. There is no query API for the roster — it is one static HTML page; a site-wide `search.php?search=` exists but is a full-text site search, not a member query.

**Per-member detail — https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/member_detail.php?mem_id=94** (HTTP 200, 58,741 bytes, HTML, curl-fetchable). Verified for Shri Subhash Shirodkar and for `?dflag=CM` (Chief Minister). It publishes: `Constituency No/Name: 22 - Shiroda`; a **Personal Details** table (date of birth, email, fax, contact, office/permanent/present address); an **Election Details** table giving, per assembly term, the assembly name, constituency, party, votes polled, votes received and won-by margin — e.g. `Eighth Legislative Assembly 2022 | Shiroda | 24799 / 8307 / 2174`, plus earlier terms (Seventh 2017, Fourth 2002, Third 1999). This is a genuine per-member term record. A **"VIEW PROFILE"** button links to a PDF Who's Who sheet, e.g. `https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/uploads/members/94_promember_sshirodkar-17-UP.pdf` (HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, 3 pages, 202,348 bytes; 28 `/Font` objects and 410 `Tj`/`TJ` text operators — a real text layer, **not** a scan).

**Verification level:** roster and per-member pages were **directly fetched and parsed (curl-200)**; the member-photo and Who's Who PDF URLs were directly fetched and their internals inspected. The `.gov.in` apex domain is additionally confirmed by its government-entity TLS certificate. The `.in` mirror was fetched (curl-200) but is a mirror and is labelled non-canonical.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
**Local name: "MLA-LAD Scheme".** This is confirmed from a **primary government document**, not from the press: an official Government of Goa eProcurement tender page at `https://eprocure.goa.gov.in/nicgep/app?component=%24DirectLink&page=FrontEndViewTender&service=direct&sp=S6U1mIqRx9m32eXOFzSaPUg%3D%3D` (HTTP 200, 70,606 bytes) whose Work Item **Title and Work Description** read: *"Purchase / supply of 500 nos. plastic chairs in MLA Office for use of various Govt. related programmes **under MLA-LAD Scheme in Cortalim Constituency**"* (Tender Value ₹4,00,000, Product Category "Civil Works - Buildings"). The scheme is therefore per-constituency, tendered through the state's own GePNIC instance.

**Official portal publishing PER-MLA fund data: NO OFFICIAL PORTAL FOUND.** This is the single largest gap in this jurisdiction. What was actually tried and what each attempt returned:
- The Goa state portal search for `MLA LAD`, `MLA fund`, `Local Area Development` and `"Local Area Development Scheme"` returned **"No posts were found."** (`https://www.goa.gov.in/?s=MLA+LAD`, HTTP 200). The state's own scheme index at `https://www.goa.gov.in/government/schemes/` (HTTP 200, 618,141 bytes) lists ~200 scheme PDFs and contains **no MLA-LAD / Vidhayak Nidhi entry**.
- Candidate dedicated hostnames **do not resolve**: `mlaladgoa.gov.in`, `mlalad.goa.gov.in`, `mla.goa.gov.in`, `vidhayaknidhi.goa.gov.in`, `goa.mlalad.gov.in` — all curl `HTTP 000` (DNS failure). These are recorded as *tested-and-absent*, not as guesses.
- The Goa Government Printing Press / gazette host `https://goaprintingpress.gov.in/` returns **HTTP 503** (server unavailable) on both `http://` and `https://`; `gazette.goa.gov.in`, `goagazette.gov.in` and `egazette.goa.gov.in` do not resolve. So the scheme notification / government-resolution PDF could not be retrieved from the official gazette at the time of checking.
- The Government of India NEGD "India Stack Local" directory for Goa (`https://negd.gov.in/isl/Directory/statedata/806`, HTTP 200; 41 Goa digital solutions listed) contains **no MLA-fund or MLA-LAD solution**.
- The Assembly's own site publishes no MLA-LAD page: `mlas.php`, `member_detail.php`, `documents.php`, `tenders-and-forms.php` and the site search contain no MLA-LAD / LAD / Vidhayak content.

**What DOES exist officially, and its limits.** The only official machine-readable trace of MLA-LAD money is **tender-level**, on the NIC GePNIC instance at `https://eprocure.goa.gov.in/nicgep/app` (HTTP 200, "Government eProcurement System"). Tenders name the scheme and the constituency in free-text work titles, and carry a tender value. There is **no scheme field, no per-MLA filter, no allocation limit, no aggregation of works recommended / sanctioned / completed, and no expenditure figure**. A portal search is session-bound (ZK/JSF server-side framework) and the site cannot be queried by "MLA-LAD" as a filter. In short: **the fund exists as a state scheme with per-constituency execution, but there is NO public per-MLA data portal publishing allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure.** Per-MLA fund figures for Goa can currently only be sourced unofficially (press reports), which the project must not use as a primary source.

**Queryable per MLA?** No. **Format:** no portal; only scattered tender HTML pages. **Verification level:** the *scheme name* is verified from a fetched official tender page (curl-200); the *absence of a fund portal* is verified by direct fetches plus DNS non-resolution of candidate hosts, i.e. a documented negative finding rather than an assumption.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
The Goa Assembly site exposes a clean, curl-fetchable AJAX layer over its "The House" pages. The pattern is: a PHP shell page (`bills.php`, `questions_list.php`, `motions.php`, …) + a combobox loader `_get_combo_details.php?mode=<MODE>&a_id=<assembly_id>` returning HTML `<select>` elements joined by a `~~**~~` delimiter, + a listing endpoint `_<page>.php?<filters>` returning an HTML fragment whose fields are joined by `~*~`. **No captcha, no session cookie, no POST observed.**

- **Unstarred questions — PARTIAL / PDF.** `https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/questions_list.php` (HTTP 200, 30,817 bytes) has assembly / session / sitting filters. The listing endpoint `https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/_questions_list.php?assembly_id=14&session_id=&sitting_id=&display_id=` (HTTP 200) returns, per sitting, a card labelled "QUESTIONS OF THE DAY" with **"Starred List"** and **"UnStarred List"** links, e.g. `https://static.goavidhansabha.gov.in/goalpub/docs/docs/166_starredlist_CSTARLIST020926.pdf` and `.../166_unstarredlist_USTAR-20926.pdf`. These are **PDFs of the question lists**, not structured rows, and they are organised by sitting, not by member.
- **Starred questions — PARTIAL / PDF.** Same endpoint; the "Starred List" PDF is the starred-question set. Verified PDF internals: `166_starredlist_CSTARLIST020926.pdf` is 232,458 bytes, 35 pages, 39 `/Font` objects and 1,173 `Tj`/`TJ` text operators → **a real text layer, extraction works, no OCR required.**
- **Per-member questions — YES, and better than the list PDFs.** `member_detail.php?mem_id=<N>` renders that member's LAQs with full question text, the answering minister, the session and sitting, and the reply, e.g. for mem_id=94: *"008A - ATAL ASARA YOJANA — \*WILL the Minister for Social Welfare be pleased to state: (a) … (b) …, from Shiroda Constituency"* with a "Reply to the Starred LAQ No. 008A" attachment. So per-member question history is readable as HTML plus linked reply PDFs.
- **Assembly Bills — YES, per member, HTML.** `https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/bills.php` (HTTP 200) filters by assembly, **"Raised By" (an explicit per-member dropdown listing all 40 members with their `mem_id`s)**, session, and **Bill Type = "Government Bill" | "Private Member Bill"**. The listing endpoint `https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/_bills.php?assembly_id=14&member_id=94&session_id=&billtype_id=&display_id=` (HTTP 200) returned, per bill: *"Bill No 24 of 2022 (Government Bill) Introduced by Shri. Subhash Shirodkar"*, the bill title, "Introduced on", "Governor Assent Date", "Portfolio", "Department", and PDFs for "Bill As Introduced" and "Bill As Passed". Bills are therefore genuinely per-member and per-term.
- **Private members' bills — PARTIAL, WITH A REAL HOLE.** The filter exists (`billtype_id=2`, "Private Member Bill"), but:
  - `a_id=14` (Eighth Legislative Assembly 2022) → **"Information currently not available!!!"**, 0 records;
  - `a_id=13` (Seventh Legislative Assembly 2017) → **"Information currently not available!!!"**, 0 records;
  - `a_id=6` (Sixth Legislative Assembly 2012) → records present (*"Bill No 26 of 2013 (Privat…"*);
  - `a_id=5` (Fifth Legislative Assembly 2007) → records present (*"Bill No. 7 of 2011 (Privat…"*).
  So the site *claims* to hold private member bills and does for older assemblies, but **publishes none for the current (2022) or previous (2017) assembly**. This must be reported as a real absence, not as a scraper bug.
- **Attendance — NO. NOT PUBLISHED AT ALL.** The literal string "attendance" occurs **zero times** on `mlas.php`, on `member_detail.php?mem_id=94`, on the homepage, on `questions_list.php` and on `bills.php`. There is no attendance page, no attendance column, and no sitting-wise presence record anywhere in the navigation ("THE HOUSE" menu: Search, Session Information, LAQ, Question List, Assembly Bills, Papers Laid, Calling Attention, Zero Hours Mentions, Private Member Resolutions, Motions, List of Business and Bulletin Part I, Bulletins Part II, Reports Presented In The House, Brief Report, Valedictory Remarks of The Speaker, Rulings of The Speaker, Committees, Short Discussions, Amendments, Cut Motions). **Attendance is not published by Goa.**

Sibling record pages using the identical AJAX pattern (all curl-fetchable, all with a per-member "Raised By" combobox where relevant): `motions.php`, `calling_attention.php`, `zero_hour_mentions.php`, `papers_laid.php`, `resolutions.php`, `amendments.php`, `cut_motions.php`, `reports.php`, `brief_reports.php`, `bulletin.php`.

**Separate eAssembly application.** `https://eassemblygoa.gov.in/` (HTTP 200, 3,745 bytes) is a **Quasar/Vue single-page app** (`<div id=q-app>`, hashed `js/app.*.js` / `js/runtime.*.js` / `js/vendor.*.js` bundles, `<noscript>This is your fallback content in case JavaScript fails to load.</noscript>`). Its own route table includes `/login`, `/publicSearchArchives`, `/SearchArchives_laq`, `/SearchArchives_bill`, `/SearchArchives_callingattention`, `/reports/:tabName`, `/verbatim`. Its runtime config declares `warName:"/eAssembly3/"`, `applicantRole:"Member of Legislative Assembly"`, `MLA_ROLE_ID:21`, `downloadVideoUrl:"https://media.goavidhansabha.gov.in/"`. Note that `https://www.goavidhansabha.gov.in/questions.php` returns that **same SPA shell** (identical 3,745 bytes) rather than an HTML page. The SPA's footer reads **"An Intellis Product"** and links to `http://freethink.co.in`. This app is the paperless/archival layer; it is **not curl-readable**.

### Format note
A fetcher for Goa needs **three different modes, not one**. (1) The Assembly's own site is the easy half: plain PHP with **no captcha, no session cookie and no POST** — `mlas.php` and `member_detail.php?mem_id=<N>` are static HTML, and the House listings are **GET** AJAX calls to `_<page>.php` whose fragments are delimited by `~*~` and `[GLA_BREAK]`, with the filter dropdown contents coming from `_get_combo_details.php?mode=<MODE>&a_id=<n>` (delimiter `~~**~~`). `curl` plus an HTML parser is sufficient; a headless browser is only needed if you insist on running the site's own jQuery. (2) Linked documents are **text-layer PDFs, not scans** — verified by decompressing content streams and counting `Tj`/`TJ` operators (starred-question list: 1,173; Who's Who profile: 410; 2007 election results: 3,614) — so **PDF text extraction** is enough and **OCR is not required for any PDF checked**. (3) Two genuinely JS-only surfaces must be excluded or driven by a browser: `eassemblygoa.gov.in` (Quasar SPA, `<div id=q-app>`, hashed bundles — an **Angular/Vue-style JS-rendered SPA**) and `eci.gov.in` (React SPA, though its data comes from a **curl-able JSON API** at `/eci-backend/public/api/…`; note `api/eci-backend/public/api/assebly-election-innerpage` is **POST-only** and returns HTTP 405 to GET, and its ECI requests carry a `secret` header). Additional blockers: `eprocure.goa.gov.in` runs **NIC GePNIC on a ZK/JSF server-side framework** — direct tender deep-links can be fetched, but browsing/searching requires a `jsessionid` session; `ceogoa.nic.in` is **ASP.NET WebForms** whose useful result sheets are reached through **opaque token parameters** (e.g. `Form20n21.aspx?UOlevK=Qb25UcGilDg=`), and `results.eci.gov.in` is a set of **per-election static microsites** that are retired once the next election arrives. Finally, `goaprintingpress.gov.in` (the official gazette host, the natural home of the MLA-LAD scheme notification) was **returning HTTP 503** throughout this reconnaissance, so gazette PDFs could not be fetched at all.

### Gap note
Four things cannot be sourced officially today. **(1) The MLA-LAD fund.** Goa does run an "MLA-LAD Scheme" (verified from an official Goa eProcurement tender: *"…under MLA-LAD Scheme in Cortalim Constituency"*), but **no government URL publishes per-MLA allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure** — not the state portal, not the Assembly site, not the NEGD directory, and no dedicated host exists. The only official trace is individual tender pages naming the scheme and constituency with a tender value; there is no scheme filter and no aggregation. There is also **no official scheme-rules / government-resolution page found**, because the gazette host was down. **(2) Attendance.** Not published anywhere on the Assembly site — the word does not appear in the roster, the member pages, or the House menu. Any Goa attendance figure on the site would have to be sourced unofficially, which the project's provenance rule forbids. **(3) Private members' bills for the current and previous assemblies.** The site's own "Private Member Bill" filter returns *"Information currently not available!!!"* for both the Eighth (2022) and Seventh (2017) assemblies, while returning records for the Sixth (2012) and Fifth (2007) — so for the 2022 intake this is an empty published category, not a fetch failure. **(4) The 2022 assembly election results.** The ECI's official Goa 2022 page (`eci.gov.in/ae-2022-goa`, confirmed through the ECI backend API) contains only a press note, observer list, star-campaigner list and candidate-affidavit links — **no results**. ECI's assembly-results JSON API covers only 2023–2026, so it has no 2022 Goa data, and the 2022 results microsite on `results.eci.gov.in` now returns HTTP 404. CEO Goa's "Previous Election Results" archive stops at **2007** (its ten per-constituency PDFs are 1963, 1967, 1972, 1977, 1980, 1984, 1989, 1999, 2002, 2007), so it does not fill the gap either. A scraper can reconstruct the current house from the roster and per-member term tables, but the 2022 election-result figures currently have **no live official URL**.

### Platform
Goa is **bespoke plus a single-vendor product, and definitively NOT the NIC NeVA template** — which matters, because a NeVA scraper would silently return nothing here. The main Assembly site is a custom PHP build whose footer credit reads **"Maintained by Goa Legislature Computer Wing | Powered by Team Inertia Technologies"** (`teaminertia.com`, a private Goa web-design agency) and whose markup, URL scheme (`mlas.php`, `member_detail.php?mem_id=`, `_bills.php`, `_get_combo_details.php?mode=`) and `~*~` / `~~**~~` delimiters are unique to Goa. The paperless application is a separate proprietary product: `eassemblygoa.gov.in` renders a footer reading **"An Intellis Product"** with a link to `freethink.co.in` (freethink, Dempo Trade Center, Panaji, Goa) and carries the runtime constant `warName:"/eAssembly3/"` — a **common-vendor** product, but with a Goa-based vendor and no evidence of reuse by another state legislature. The decisive evidence is on NIC's own site: NeVA's house grid at `neva.gov.in` lists **`https://goa.neva.gov.in` with the status "Prospective"**, whereas twenty-plus other houses are marked "Onboarded" or "Digital House". Consistent with that, the Goa NeVA tenant resolves but is stale seed data — it advertises **"Second Legislative Assembly 1995"**, a "Member" count of 10, notice-board items dated August 2023, and its page contains none of the current members (Pramod Sawant, Shirodar, Arolkar and the constituency "Mandrem" are all absent), while its "Members" nav item points at the shared national `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec` page. Goa is provisioned on NeVA but has not onboarded to it, so **Goa's data must be scraped bespoke; it cannot be served by a shared NeVA scraper.**

---

## Gujarat (IN-GJ)

**Verdict:** partial - Gujarat has a functioning unicameral legislature whose entire member roster (182 MLAs of the 15th Vidhan Sabha), with names, parties, constituencies and photographs, plus per-member starred-question records and a government-bill register, is published by the NIC-built National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) and is curl-fetchable; but the MLA local-area development fund (`Vidhayak Nidhi`) has no official portal, no per-MLA allocation/expenditure data and no locatable scheme-rules page, and per-MLA attendance and private members' bills are not published at all.

### Profile

The official website of the Gujarat Legislative Assembly is **https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/** (fetched: HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=utf-8`, 95,991 bytes). The legacy government domain **http://gujaratassembly.gov.in** was fetched and 301-redirects to that same NeVA host, which is the strongest available proof that `gujarat.neva.gov.in` is the Assembly's official site rather than a third-party mirror. Note the DNS/TLS trap: `gujaratassembly.gov.in` resolves to 103.234.162.85 but its TLS certificate has no matching subject alternative name, so plain `https://gujaratassembly.gov.in/` fails with curl exit 60 - only the plain-HTTP entry point followed by a redirect works. The site identifies itself as **પંદરમી વિધાનસભા** (Fifteenth Vidhan Sabha).

**The member roster is complete and per-MLA.** It is not on the static page: `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec` returns a 66,994-byte shell that contains only the filter lists (assembly, party, constituency, gender, first-time members, by-poll members, ministers, whips), and the actual tiles are loaded by an XHR to **`https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=16&MSLT=15&Assemblyid=15`** (fetched: HTTP 200, 1,762,626 bytes). Parsing that response yields exactly **182 member tiles** (the Assembly's sanctioned strength), covering member IDs 1-327, each tile carrying:

- **name** in Gujarati, e.g. `શ્રી અભેસિંહ મોતીભાઇ તડવી` (English name is available as the `<img alt>` attribute, `Abhesinh Motibhai Tadvi`),
- **party** in Gujarati (`ભારતીય જનતા પાર્ટી`, `ભારતીય રાષ્ટ્રીય કોંગ્રેસ`, `આમ આદમી પાર્ટી`, `અપક્ષ`, `સમાજવાદી પાર્ટી`),
- **constituency**, e.g. `સંખેડા -છોટા ઉદેપુર (ગુજરાત)`,
- **date of birth**, e.g. `01 Jun 1956`,
- **photograph** - a thumbnail JPEG under `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/GJ/FileStructures//Member/thumb/...`,
- and the link `/Member/Details/{id}?AssemblyID=15`.

Party composition parsed from the roster: **BJP 162, INC 12, AAP 5, Independent 2, SP 1 = 182**, which matches the party counts the site publishes on its own homepage, so the parse is self-consistent. **All 182 tiles carry a real photograph - zero missing**, which is unusually good for an Indian state assembly.

Per-member drill-down is at **`https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/{id}?AssemblyID=15`** (example `/Member/Details/47?AssemblyID=15`: HTTP 200, 33,354 bytes). It renders party, constituency with number and district, permanent address, local/office address, mobile number and official e-mail (e.g. `mlaghatlodiya@gujarat.gov.in` for the Chief Minister's Ghatlodiya seat), plus the **full-size photograph** at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/GJ/FileStructures//Member/{file}.jpeg`. Membership is queryable per member by ID; the roster endpoint also accepts `MSLT` filters for party, gender, constituency and prior assembly (1st-15th), so lateral filtering works server-side.

**Verification level: strong.** The homepage, the roster XHR, three member profiles (IDs 144, 47, and the two presiding officers 20 and 205) and the bill register were each fetched and returned content.

One serious operational trap found and confirmed: **NeVA is multi-tenant and the Gujarat host will silently serve another state's legislature if the session is not first seeded on the Gujarat host.** Requesting `/Member/Details/144` and `/Member/Details/47` with no cookie jar, or with a stale one, returned HTTP 200 with `Member Details | NeVA | eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh` in the `<title>` and the Himachal Pradesh shell - *not* a 404, not an error. Repeating the identical request after first fetching `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/` in the same cookie jar returned the correct `Gujarat Vidhan Sabha Member Details | NeVA` page three times out of three, at 33,936 bytes instead of 22,426. A fetcher that skips the session-seeding step will harvest Himachal Pradesh data while believing it is reading Gujarat.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**No official portal publishing per-MLA development-fund data was found for Gujarat, and no official scheme-rules page (government resolution / guidelines) naming the scheme was found either. There is no `mlalad_per_mla` dataset to source.**

The fund exists - Gujarat raised it, and Gujarati media refer to it as the MLA's `ગ્રાન્ટ` (grant), `વિધાયક નિધિ` / `Vidhayak Nidhi`, or simply the MLA fund, with the annual entitlement reported at **₹2.5 crore per MLA**. But every official surface checked came up empty:

- Candidate government hosts probed for the fund and **all NXDOMAIN**: `mlalad.gujarat.gov.in`, `mlalad.gujarat.nic.in`, `vidhayaknidhi.gujarat.gov.in`, `vidhayak-nidhi.gujarat.gov.in`, `mlafund.gujarat.gov.in`, `mla.gujarat.gov.in`, `nidhi.gujarat.gov.in`, `mlaladgujarat.nic.in`. The finance/budget department hosts `finance.gujarat.gov.in`, `fin.gujarat.gov.in` and `budget.gujarat.gov.in` are likewise NXDOMAIN.
- The **Assembly's own NeVA site has no fund module**. Its pages `/Home/DigitalLibrary`, `/ImpLinks`, `/Home/MiscellaneousData?menuname=Budget` and `/Home/RulesAndDirection` were each fetched and contain **zero** occurrences of `નિધિ`, `વિધાયક`, `MLALAD`, `Vidhayak` or `Nidhi`.
- **`https://panchayat.gujarat.gov.in/gu/home`** (HTTP 200, 300,944 bytes) and its schemes page **`/gu/schemes`** (HTTP 200, 46,449 bytes) contain zero occurrences of `નિધિ`, `વિધાયક`, `ધારાસભ્ય`, `MLALAD` or `Vidhayak`, despite the rural MLA-fund works being executed through the panchayat machinery.
- **`https://gad.gujarat.gov.in/government-resolution.htm`** (HTTP 200, 47,769 bytes) hosts the General Administration Department's GR index, but its sub-indices are limited to Personnel, Planning, Administrative Reforms & Training, and NRI divisions - no scheme GR for the MLA fund.
- `https://gujaratindia.gov.in/Index` (HTTP 200, 230,368 bytes) carries no link or text matching `nidhi`, `Vidhayak`, `MLALAD` or `fund`.
- The national analogue **does not cover MLAs**: `https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/` (HTTP 200, 62,712 bytes) mentions "Lok Sabha" 14 times and "Rajya Sabha" 15 times but **"MLA" zero times and "Legislative Assembly" zero times** - MPLADS is scoped strictly to Members of Parliament, so there is no national fallback dataset for Gujarat MLAs.

Because no primary source was located, the honest reading is that Gujarat MLA-fund data is obtainable only through **RTI applications to the district collectorates / nodal agencies and through answers on the floor of the House** - not from a portal. The campaigning around this dataset is entirely unofficial: the ADR report on unspent MLA funds and the Indian Express coverage of MLA-LAD funds are press/secondary material and carry the label **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**. `https://gujaratgr.in/` is a private GR aggregator, also **UNOFFICIAL**. None of them publishes per-MLA allocation/works-recommended/works-sanctioned/works-completed/expenditure figures traceable to a government portal.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred questions ARE published per member, in an HTML table.** `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Question?MemberId={id}&AssemblyID=15` (example MemberId=47: HTTP 200, 81,709 bytes, 74 `<tr>` rows, 73 data rows) returns a server-rendered grid with the columns **ક્રમ નં** (serial), **પ્રશ્ન ક્રમાંક** (question number), **પ્રશ્નનો પ્રકાર** (question type), a date column, **મંત્રી** (minister addressed) and **વિષય** (subject). The type column is the load-bearing one: every one of MLA 47's 73 rows is typed **તારાંકિત** (starred), and MLA 1 returned 15 rows, all starred. The platform clearly models the other categories - the assembly-level questions page exposes sidebar filters for **તારાંકિત પ્રશ્ન** (starred), **અતારાંકિત પ્રશ્ન** (unstarred) and **ટૂંકી મુદ્તના પ્રશ્ન** (short-notice) - so unstarred questions are a first-class category and would appear in the same type column; however, no unstarred row was actually observed in the sampled members. Unstarred is therefore recorded as **partial**: the field exists and is populated by the same query, but it was not positively observed.

The **assembly-level** question browser is weaker than the per-member one: `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem={0|1|2}&Type={0|4|5}` returned an identical 83,847-byte body for every starred/unstarred variant tried, i.e. it does not vary with the requested type and is not a usable bulk endpoint. The per-member route is the reliable one, which makes a full-corpus harvest a 182-request loop rather than one bulk download.

`https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Notice?MemberId={id}&AssemblyID=15` (MemberId=144: HTTP 200, 1,109 bytes; MemberId=47: HTTP 200, 1,158 bytes) returns only **"No Records Founds"** for both, so per-member notices are an empty module. `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Committee?MemberId={id}` (MemberId=144: HTTP 200, 625 bytes) likewise returns "No Records Founds". `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=144&AssemblyID=15` (HTTP 200, 2,291 bytes) returns a personal-information fragment that renders largely blank.

**Bills are published, but only government bills.** `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Bill/GetBillDashBoardData?UnitCode=GJ` (HTTP 200, 15,547 bytes) returns a clean HTML `<table>` with 15 rows and the columns **Sr No, Bill Number, Title, Description, Ministry, Department(s), Date of Introduction House, Document** - e.g. `19 of 2023 | THE GUJARAT GOODS AND SERVICES TAX (AMENDMENT) BILL, 2023 | Finance | Finance | 13/09/2023`. The Assembly homepage labels this register **સરકારી વિધેયકો** = *government* bills, count 14. The per-member profile carries a **વિધેયકો** (Bills) tab, but for the sampled members it renders `કોઈ રેકોર્ડ મળ્યા નથી` ("no records found") and no AJAX endpoint backs it - only four per-member routes exist (`Member_ProfileDetail`, `Member_Question`, `Member_Notice`, `Member_Committee`), none of them for bills. **Private members' bills are therefore not published as a distinct, per-member, machine-readable category.** `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch` returned HTTP 500 on GET.

**Attendance is NOT published per member in any form.** There is no attendance column in the roster, no attendance tab on the member profile, and no attendance endpoint. The only attendance-adjacent artefact is a committee document - the homepage links `પંદરમી ગુજરાત વિધાનસભાની સભાગૃહની બેઠકોમાંથી સભ્યોની ગેરહાજરી બાબતની સમિતિનો ચોથો અહેવાલ` (Fourth Report of the Committee on Absence of Members from the Sittings of the House, dated 11/09/2026). That is a **scanned-or-born-digital PDF report on absences**, not a per-MLA attendance percentage, and committee documents sit behind a filter form at `https://gujarat.neva.gov.in/Committee` (HTTP 200) with an XHR at `/Committee/DataList` (HTTP 200, 54,854 bytes) that returns the filter controls rather than the report list. Practically, attendance would have to be reconstructed by a human from those report PDFs, if at all.

### Format note

A fetcher needs **curl + HTML parsing for the profile and assembly-record blocks, a JSON client for the ECI election pages, and nothing that requires a headless browser for the Assembly's own data** - but it must handle three specific traps. First, **session seeding is mandatory and its failure mode is silent**: the multi-tenant NeVA application returns HTTP 200 with *Himachal Pradesh* content on `gujarat.neva.gov.in` unless a cookie jar has already been established on the Gujarat host (and `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList` returns a 3,531-byte HTTP 500 error page for the same reason - it is not a broken endpoint, it is an unseeded session). Second, the roster and every per-member panel are **XHR fragments, not documents**: `FetchMembersList`, `Member_Question`, `Member_Notice`, `Member_Committee` and `Member_ProfileDetail` all need `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest` plus a `Referer` from the parent page, and only `FetchMembersList` accepts the assembly ID while the member panels need `MemberId` **and** `AssemblyID` together. Third, **all site text is Gujarati** - names, parties, constituencies, question types and bill titles - so any pipeline needs a Gujarati font/locale path and the question-type string `તારાંકિત` (starred) as a literal match key, since there is no English API mode on the Assembly host. On the election side, `www.eci.gov.in` is a **React SPA** (a 1,279-byte shell that says "You need to enable JavaScript to run this app") and cannot be scraped as HTML, but it is backed by a directly fetchable JSON API at `/eci-backend/public/api/...`; `results.eci.gov.in` is plain static HTML but **only serves the current election cycle** (all Gujarat 2022 paths tried returned 404), and the 2022 constituency-level results are a **ZIP of PDF/XLSX files** behind `https://old.eci.gov.in/files/file/14750-gujarat-general-legislative-election-2022/?do=download`, where the `?do=download` request must share a session with the file page because a bare curl of that URL returns the HTML page (94,277 bytes) instead of the 2.47 MB archive. No captcha, no login and no OCR was encountered anywhere on the Gujarat Assembly path; no PDF or scanned image is required for the profile, questions or bills blocks.

### Gap note

Three things cannot be sourced officially. **(1) The MLA development fund.** There is no Gujarat `Vidhayak Nidhi` portal, no per-MLA allocated limit, and no works-recommended / works-sanctioned / works-completed / expenditure figures - not in any format, not even as a scanned PDF. The scheme demonstrably exists and is worth roughly ₹2.5 crore per MLA per year, but its numbers are held inside district collectorates and nodal agencies and surface only through RTI and floor answers, so the Election Atlas simply cannot populate this block for Gujarat from government sources. **(2) Attendance.** The Assembly publishes no per-MLA attendance figure of any kind; the only artefact is the Committee on Absence's periodic report PDF, which is a narrative annexure on absences and not a per-member statistic. **(3) Private members' bills.** The bill register is explicitly the *government* bill register, the per-member Bills tab renders empty for every member sampled, and no endpoint backs it - so a legislative-productivity metric covering private members' bills is not derivable. A fourth, softer gap: the per-member unstarred-question series was not positively observed, so unstarred counts should be treated as unverified until a member with unstarred questions is sampled.

### Platform

Gujarat runs on a **shared national NIC template, not a bespoke site**: the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA), a Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs project delivered by the National Informatics Centre. The footer on every page reads `© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India / Content managed by: Gujarat Legislative Assembly`, and the national portal at `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` advertises itself as "Digital Legislatures - **One Nation One Application**" while enumerating **38 state/UT legislature subdomains** under a single `*.neva.gov.in` wildcard - including `gujarat`, `odisha`, `kerala`, `tnla`, `wbla`, `raj`, `upvs`, `mhla`, `goa`, `sikkim`, `tripura`, `manipur`, `mizo`, `puddu`, `tsla`, `hpvs`, `utkh`, `asla`, `bla`, `chg`, `dla`, `hrla`, `kla`, `mgla`, `mpla`, `nagaland`, `pvs`, `apla`/`apc`, `arla`, `tslc`, `uplc`, `blc`, `klc`, `mhlc`, `rajyasabha` and `loksabha`. The route vocabulary is identical across tenants - `/Member/Details/{id}`, `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`, `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Bill/GetBillDashBoardData`, `/Committee/DataList`, `/Member/Member_Question` - and file assets come from one shared CMS at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/**<UNIT>**/FileStructures/...`, where the Gujarat suffix `GJ` is the only thing that differs per state. The strongest single piece of evidence is behavioural: an unseeded session on `gujarat.neva.gov.in` returns Himachal Pradesh's assembly shell under HTTP 200, which can only happen if the Gujarat and Himachal sites are the same application instance serving multiple tenants. **Consequence for the project: one NeVA scraper serves many states at once** - the per-state work reduces to swapping the subdomain and the `UnitCode`/unit path segment, which makes NeVA states a high-leverage cluster to build against first.

---

## Haryana (IN-HR)

**Verdict:** partial - Haryana has a functioning unicameral legislature whose official site publishes a complete, curl-fetchable 90-member roster with per-MLA photographs and a per-member profile page, and it additionally runs a fully-implemented National e-Vidhan Application instance (`hrla.neva.gov.in`) on the same shared national template as Gujarat and West Bengal; attendance and question papers are published per sitting as born-digital PDFs, but per-member attendance/question/bill figures are never aggregated, private members' bills are not published as a dataset, and the state's MLA scheme (VANGY, Rs 2 crore per constituency per year) has an official scheme page but **no public per-MLA works or expenditure portal** - only a scanned guidelines PDF.

### Profile

Haryana is **unicameral** - a single Legislative Assembly (Vidhan Sabha) of **90 members**, with no Legislative Council. The official website of the Haryana Vidhan Sabha is **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/** (fetched: HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=UTF-8`, 73,856 bytes, Apache/2.4.37 on Oracle Linux with `Link: <https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/wp-json/>; rel="https://api.w.org/"`). It is a **bespoke WordPress site** with a hand-written theme directory at `/wp-content/themes/custome-theme/`, not a national template.

The current member roster is at **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/assembly-members/** (HTTP 200, 52,866 bytes). It is a plain server-rendered HTML table with the columns **Name / Sex / Constituency Name / Party**, 20 members per page, paginated across 55 pages via `/assembly-members/page/N`. The first **five pages are exactly the 90 sitting MLAs of the 15th Assembly and nothing else**: parsing pages 1-5 returned precisely 90 rows, member #90 being `Smt. Vinesh | Female | Julana | INC` (Vinesh Phogat) and member #91 flipping to the 14th Assembly (`Abhay Singh Chautala, Ch. | Male | Ellenabad | INLD`, id 53366). The party split of those 90 rows is **INC 37, BJP 47, INLD 2, Independent 3, Speaker 1** - which reconciles exactly to the official 2024 result of **BJP 48, INC 37, INLD 2, IND 3**. That reconciliation is the verification that pages 1-5 are a complete and correct sitting-member roster.

Two caveats for a scraper. First, the markup is **malformed**: `<tr>` tags are opened and never closed, so a `</tr>`-based parser returns one giant row instead of 90 - the reliable extraction anchor is the `<a href=".../mla-details?id=NNNN">NAME</a>` element followed by three `<td>` cells. Second, the page's own search form (`<form method="post" name="search-form">` with `shouse`, `s_sex`, `s_party`, `s_constituency`, `s_name`, `paged`) **does not filter**: a POST with `shouse=15&search_btn=Search` returned a byte-identical 52,866-byte page to the unfiltered GET, so the House/Party/Constituency filters are non-functional server-side and the roster must be walked page by page.

**Per-member profile pages exist and are queryable by integer id:** `https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/mla-details?id=NNNN`. Fetching `?id=59920` returned HTTP 200, 43,908 bytes - **verified twice over two independent routes (plain URL and `--resolve` pinned to 203.100.79.163), producing identical MD5 `b149a21f305de6b1f638a9372068be48`**. The page renders at least: **House Tenure (15), Constituency Name (Ambala Cantt), Name (Shri Anil Vij), Party (BJP), Date Of Birth, Birth Place, Sex, Father's Name, Marital Status, Spouse Name, No. Of Sons, No. Of Daughters, Qualifications, Place Of Education, Present Profession, Language Known, Social Activities, Hobbies, Sports And Clubs, Foreign Travels, Office If Any, No. Of Times Elected, Years Of Election, Permanent Address, Total Votes, Total Votes Polled, Winning Candidate Votes, Nearest Rival Votes**. Many of those fields are returned **empty** (the template lists every label; only a subset is populated) - the reliably populated core is name, party, constituency, house tenure and photo. An invalid id (e.g. `?id=99999`) returns the same 38,460-byte shell with all fields blank rather than a 404.

**Photographs ARE published**, as a direct JPEG on the per-member page: `https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2019/11/anil_vij.jpg` (the `<img class="img_size">` inside the profile block). Filenames are name-slugged rather than id-keyed, so they must be read from the page rather than constructed.

A historical roster covering **all earlier Assemblies** is at **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/sitting-members/** ("Who's Who", HTTP 200, 415,048 bytes). It is a **single server-rendered HTML table of 1,115 data rows** with columns S.No. / House / Name / Sex / Constituency Name / Party, spanning Houses 1-14 (House 14 = 87 rows, House 13 = 90, House 12 = 90, ... House 1 = 54). It is paginated client-side with DataTables 1.13.6. Critically, **the 15th (current) Assembly is NOT in this table** - its only row is a note: *"Members of 15th Legislative Assembly of Haryana as per Notification No. 308/HAR-LA/2024, dated 09th October, 2024 of Election Commission of India"*. So the current roster and the historical roster come from two different pages, and a harvester must not treat `/sitting-members/` as the sitting-member list despite its name.

Term dates for all 15 Assemblies are at **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/house-tenures/** (HTTP 200, 39,611 bytes) - the 15th Assembly runs **25-10-2024 to 24-10-2029**; the 14th ran 04-11-2019 to 12-09-2024. This is the citation for the "term / assembly number" field.

Verification level for the profile block is **strong**: every URL above was fetched and returned content, and the roster was cross-validated against the party arithmetic of the official result.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**Haryana has no MLALAD-style per-MLA discretionary fund, and the scheme it does run publishes no per-MLA spending data.** The fund is **not** called "Vidhayak Nidhi" in Haryana. Its official name is the **Vidhayak Adarsh Gram Yojana (VAGY)**, launched **06.07.2015** and renamed **Vidhayak Adarsh Nagar Avam Gram Yojana (VANGY)** when urban areas were added. The official scheme page is **https://haryanarural.gov.in/vidhayak-adarsh-nagar-avam-gram-yojana-vangy/** (HTTP 200, 86,316 bytes, published 23 October 2024) on the Rural Development Department site, which states its terms verbatim:

- **100% State funded scheme**; **"Rs.2.00 crore is provided to each constituency for the development works identified by the Hon'ble MLAs in their constituency as per State Govt. decision dated 26.12.2018"**;
- per-village ceiling tied to population: **Rs 2.00 Cr** for a village above 10,000; **Rs 1.00 Cr** for 5,000-10,000; **Rs 0.50 Cr** for up to 5,000;
- **5% (Rs 10.00 lacs) of the total must** be spent on rainwater harvesting/soak pits/ponds, local air-water-land pollution reduction, and solid and liquid waste management;
- MLAs may now also recommend works in municipal areas; the **Deputy Commissioner of the district is the Nodal Officer**; fund flow is **State Govt. -> DC/CEO**.

This is a **constituency-level entitlement, not a per-MLA expenditure ledger**. The page describes the policy envelope only. **No portal was found that publishes, per MLA, the allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed, or expenditure** - the exact five data points the project needs. Probes of plausible hosts all failed to resolve or do not exist: `vagy.haryana.gov.in`, `adarshgram.haryana.gov.in`, `rd.haryana.gov.in`, `pnd.haryana.gov.in`, `drdaharyana.gov.in` (all curl code 000, DNS failure), and `budget.haryana.gov.in`, `finance.haryana.gov.in` (also DNS failure). The state portal **https://www.haryana.gov.in/** contains **zero** occurrences of "Vidhayak", "Nidhi" or "MLA Fund". A keyword search of the assembly site (`https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/?s=Vidhayak+Nidhi` and `?s=MLA+fund`) returned "Nothing Found" for both.

The official **guidelines PDF is a scanned document and requires OCR**: `https://cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in/s3c3614206a443012045cfd75d2600af2d/uploads/2023/04/2023041949.pdf` (HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, 4,163,547 bytes, 8 pages, **PDF 1.3 with `/Font` count 0 and `/Image` count 32**) - image-only, no text layer. A second VANGY-related PDF on the same scheme page (`.../uploads/2024/01/20240103910903011.pdf`) is linked from the official page. A third candidate, a scanned circular on the Chief Secretary's site under the Political-and-Parliamentary-Affairs heading (`https://csharyana.gov.in/WriteReadData/Circular-&-Instructions/Political-and-Parliamentary-Affairs/14838.pdf`, HTTP 200, 1,033,253 bytes, 2 pages) was confirmed **scanned** - it was produced by a **Canon SC1011** scanner, uses `/DCTDecode` JPEG image objects and carries no extractable text - and because no OCR was available in this environment its subject matter **could not be verified**, so it must not be cited as an MLA-fund document.

The national analogue does not fill the gap either: **https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/** covers only Members of Parliament, and Haryana has no equivalent state portal. Secondary **UNOFFICIAL** reporting consistently corroborates the absence of an MLALAD scheme - the Chief Minister has twice been reported saying there is no plan for an MLA/MCLAD fund ([Indian Express, 27 Dec 2022](https://indianexpress.com/article/cities/chandigarh/opp-mla-want-local-area-development-fund-khattar-says-no-haryana-assembly-8345821/); [Times of India](https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/city/chandigarh/no-plan-for-mplad-like-fund-for-mlas-cm/articleshow/96532505.cms); [Hindustan Times, 10 Sep 2026](https://www.hindustantimes.com/cities/chandigarh-news/no-plan-for-mlalad-fund-cm-101672079791596.html)) - while separate reporting describes **ad-hoc, discretionary** releases of Rs 1 crore each to 25 MLAs ([Hindustan Times](https://www.hindustantimes.com/cities/chandigarh-news/25-mlas-get-1-crore-each-for-devp-works-101742495521376.html); [Tribune](https://www.tribuneindia.com/news/haryana/govt-allocates-rs1-crore-each-to-25-mlas-for-development-projects/); [Punjab Newsline, describing it as "VAG Yojana"](https://punjabnewsline.com/news/fund-of-rs-1cr-each-released-to-25-mlas-for-development-works-under-the-vag-yojana-cm-90591)). Those are **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification** and publish no per-MLA ledger. **Not one development-fund number for Haryana can currently be traced to an official per-MLA source.**

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Attendance: published per sitting, but NOT aggregated per member.** The page **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/members-attendance/** (HTTP 200, 83,019 bytes) is a filterable index (`House` 1-15, `Year` 1994-2026, `Session`, `Sitting`) whose result rows link to individual PDFs - e.g. `https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/Members-Attendance-April-Session-2026.pdf`, and roughly 90 further files going back to 1994 (`.../2024/11/13-November-session-2024.pdf`, `.../2021/03/18.03.2021.pdf`, and so on). **These PDFs are born-digital, not scans**: the April-2026 session file is PDF 1.7, 4 pages, with **`/Font` count 18 and `/Image` count 0** - text extraction works, OCR is not needed. But the site offers **no per-member attendance total, no percentage and no consolidated attendance report**; a consumer must parse every sitting PDF and aggregate the member rows itself. The filter form is POST-driven and the listing is server-rendered HTML.

**Starred and unstarred questions: published per sitting as PDFs, NOT per member.** The nav item at **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/questions/** (HTTP 200, 38,540 bytes) is **explanatory prose only** - it defines the three categories ("(i) Starred (ii) Unstarred (iii) Short notice") and contains **no data whatsoever**; the footer notes the page was last updated **12 February 2020**. The real lists are published as news-press-release posts on the legislative-business feed **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/newspress-releases/**, one post per sitting day, titled *"List of Starred Questions DD Month, YYYY"* and *"List of Un-Starred Questions DD Month, YYYY"*. Verified examples: `https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/news-press-release/list-of-un-starred-questions-13-03-2025/` (HTTP 200, 46,110 bytes) linking `http://haryanaassembly.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/UNSTAR-QUESTION-13-03-2025-English.pdf`, and `https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Starred-Question-18.03.2025-English.pdf` (HTTP 200, 107,973 bytes, 8 pages, `/Font` 34, `/Image` 25 - text-bearing). Signed departmental replies are also published: `https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2023/01/14_13_S_1200_V1_signed.pdf` (HTTP 200, 259,365 bytes) extracted verbatim as *"Starred Assembly Question No.60 ... Subject: Starred AQ No.60 asked by Sh. Indu Raj, M.L.A. ... From The Additional Chief Secretary to Government, Haryana, Development & Panchayats Department"*. That PDF proves **the questioner is named individually**, so per-member question attribution is derivable - but only by parsing every sitting's PDF; **the site publishes no per-member question count, no starred/unstarred tally, and no question search**.

**Private members' bills: NOT published as a dataset.** **https://haryanaassembly.gov.in/bills/** (HTTP 200, 38,450 bytes) is again **explanatory prose only** ("a bill is draft of a Legislative proposal put in the proper form..."), with no list and no data. Actual bills appear as individual posts on the same legislative-business feed (e.g. *"The Haryana Panchayati Raj (Amendment) Bill, 2026"*, *"The Haryana Private Universities (Amendment) Bill, 2026"*). Those posts are **Government Bills**, listed by title only; **nothing on the official site classifies a bill as a private member's bill or attributes a bill to the introducing MLA**, so the private-members'-bills dimension is a genuine gap.

The **NeVA instance does not close these gaps.** At `https://hrla.neva.gov.in/` the nav offers `/Questions` and `/Bill`, and the homepage advertises "Questions 2" and "Government Bills 1" - but **every one of those routes (`/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`) returns HTTP 200 with the identical 192,345-byte national NeVA landing page** (`https://hrla.neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA`), i.e. it redirects away rather than serving Haryana data. The member page `/Member/Details/32` returns a 22,426-byte JavaScript shell whose async endpoint **`https://hrla.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Question?MemberId=32&AssemblyID=15`** responds HTTP 200 with the HTML fragment *"Questions ... No Records Founds"* - the per-member questions module is **empty for Haryana**. So of the four assembly-record dimensions, **attendance and both question categories are published only in per-sitting PDF form with no per-member aggregation, and private members' bills are not published at all.**

### Format note

A fetcher needs a **mixed** toolchain. The profile layer is plain **curl + HTML parsing**: the roster and the per-member `?id=` pages are server-rendered, needing only a lenient parser because the table's `<tr>` tags are unclosed, and the roster requires walking 5 paginated URLs (the site's POST filters silently do nothing). The assembly-record layer is **PDF text extraction**, not OCR - the attendance files (`/Font` 18, `/Image` 0) and the starred-question and signed-reply files are born-digital, though the site publishes them one sitting at a time with no aggregate, so the fetcher must enumerate the legislative-business feed and every attendance PDF and aggregate member rows itself. Two specific blockers: the VANGY **guidelines PDF is a pure scan** (`/Font` 0, `/Image` 32) and needs OCR, and the NeVA instance is a **jQuery/AJAX application** whose `/Questions`, `/Bill` and contact-directory routes redirect to the national landing page, so its data (where it exists at all) is only reachable through fragment endpoints such as `/Member/Member_Question` and would in any case return "No Records Founds" for Haryana. On the election side, `results.eci.gov.in` is static HTML but keeps **only the current election** (it currently redirects to `ResultAcByeAugust2026`, and the 2024 assembly folders return 404), while `www.eci.gov.in` is a **React SPA** that needs a headless browser for its pages - its `/eci-backend/public/api/` JSON endpoints are the curl-friendly path (`get-ac-election-state` works on GET; `assebly-election-innerpage` is **POST-only** and answers JSON). There is no captcha, no session cookie and no login on any Haryana source used here.

### Gap note

Three things are missing and cannot be sourced officially. **(1) Per-MLA development-fund figures do not exist in any public Haryana source.** The state's scheme (VANGY) is documented only as a policy entitlement - Rs 2 crore per constituency per year - on a Rural Development Department page; there is **no allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure figure per MLA** anywhere on a `.gov.in` host, the guidelines are a scan, and the Chief Minister is on record (UNOFFICIAL reporting) that Haryana has no MLALAD scheme at all. The project's Development Fund panel therefore has **no official per-MLA data for Haryana** and must either show the scheme entitlement with a documented absence, or show nothing. **(2) No per-member aggregation of the assembly record.** Attendance and starred/unstarred questions exist only as dozens of separate per-sitting PDFs; the site publishes no per-member attendance percentage and no per-member question count, so those figures must be computed by parsing and summing every sitting - and the resulting number is the project's own arithmetic, not a government-published figure. Aggregate data goes back only to 1994 on the attendance index. **(3) Private members' bills are not published as such** - bills appear as titles of Government Bills on the legislative-business feed with no private-vs-government classification and no introducer attribution. Additionally, the NeVA instance that could have supplied structured per-member question data **is empty for Haryana** (`No Records Founds`), so it is not a fallback.

### Platform

Haryana is **split across a shared national template and a bespoke site**, and the shared template is the more consequential fact. The primary Vidhan Sabha website `haryanaassembly.gov.in` is **bespoke** - a hand-built WordPress theme (`/wp-content/themes/custome-theme/`, `wp-json` REST root, and a stray `Test` label in the markup) with no vendor or NIC footer credit. But Haryana **also runs a full National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) instance at `https://hrla.neva.gov.in/`**, whose title is *"Welcome to Haryana Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application"*, whose homepage shows the NeVA implementation status "पूर्णतः कार्यान्वित" (fully implemented), and whose footer credit reads **"© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India"** - the same credit carried by `gujarat.neva.gov.in` and `wbla.neva.gov.in`. Stronger still, all three state instances expose an **identical route set** (`/Today`, `/Business`, `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Committee`, `/Debates`, `/ContactDirectory`, `/Home/DigitalLibrary`) and the same `/Member/Details/{id}` and `/Member/Member_Question` endpoints, with digitised notices served from a shared `cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/{STATE}/FileStructures/...` path. **One NeVA scraper therefore serves Haryana, Gujarat and West Bengal from the same code**, and the assembly footer links to it explicitly under a heading "NeVA" beside a "Certificate of Appreciation". Two further NIC platforms recur: `haryanarural.gov.in` is a **NIC S3WaaS** site (its documents are served from `cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in`, and it credits "Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre"), and `ceoharyana.gov.in` is built on the **NIC shared CEO template** `WebsiteTemplates/ElectionCommissionHaryana/Blossom/`, the same Blossom template used across state CEO sites.

---

## Himachal Pradesh (IN-HP)

**Verdict:** partial - Himachal Pradesh has a real, functioning legislature (14th Vidhan Sabha, 68 members) whose official site is `https://hpvs.neva.gov.in` on the national NeVA platform, but the member roster/profile AJAX returns an empty template to anonymous clients, both MLA-fund monitoring portals are login-gated, and no per-member attendance / questions / bills data is published publicly.

### Profile
Himachal Pradesh has a unicameral legislature. The state's own portal, `https://himachal.gov.in/en-IN/`, links "HP Vidhan Sabha" to **https://hpvs.neva.gov.in** (verified: that href is present in the fetched HTML of the official state portal, and hpvs.neva.gov.in itself returned HTTP 200, `text/html`, 87,561 bytes, `<title>Welcome to Himachal Pradesh Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application</title>`). The site's chrome reads "Himachal Pradesh Legislative Assembly / हिमाचल प्रदेश विधान सभा / चौदहवीं विधान सभा" (Fourteenth Vidhan Sabha) and its footer credit is "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India".

A second host, `https://www.hpvidhansabha.nic.in`, is the state-branded copy of the same application: its DNS A record resolves to 164.100.88.162 (an NIC address block) with `www` a CNAME to the apex, and search-indexed routes on that host (`/Member/Details/50`, `/Notice/GetCategoryByName/369?page=24`) are the same eVidhan routes that exist on hpvs.neva.gov.in; the member page breadcrumb on hpvs literally reads "eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh". **However, hpvidhansabha.nic.in could not be fetched at all from this environment**: TCP connect to 164.100.88.162 on both :443 and :80 timed out (20s connect timeout, curl exit with `000  0`), twice, while control NIC hosts on the same session (`neva.gov.in`, `india.gov.in`, `sansad.in`) all returned 200. Treat hpvidhansabha.nic.in as real-but-currently-unreachable rather than dead.

What the assembly site actually publishes to an anonymous visitor is much thinner than the navigation implies. `/Member/Details/{id}` returns an identical 22,426-byte shell for **every** id tried (31, 50, 256, 398) and for the bare `/Member/Details`. The profile body is fetched by a jQuery `$.ajax` GET to `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=&AssemblyID=` and the page hard-codes `var Mid = '0'; var AssID = '0';`. Calling that endpoint directly - with `MemberId` of 0, 1, 2, 31, 50, 256 and 398, with and without a session cookie jar primed from the homepage - returned an identical 2,291-byte template every time, containing an empty `Name :` field and the print block heading "BLANK". The sibling endpoints `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Member/Member_Notice`, `/Member/Member_Committee` likewise return "Questions No Records Founds", "Notice No Records Founds", "Committee No Records Founds". The member card image resolves to `<img src="/Member/">`, i.e. no photo is served. The "Members" / "All Members" / "Member" navigation items all point at `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, which does not render a roster: it returns the **national** NeVA home page (192,345 bytes, byte-identical size to `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA`), and `/ContactDirectory` itself renders an empty "Contact Directory" page (15,089 bytes) with zero rows and zero `<tr>`. **Net effect: no member roster with name / party / constituency / photo is retrievable from the official site by an anonymous client.** The only per-member pages that do resolve non-thinly are the presiding-officer pages `/Member/GovDetails/43` (Governor, 16,303 bytes) and `/Member/SecDetails/1` (Secretary, 15,575 bytes), plus the homepage's four featured cards (`/Member/Details/256`, `/Member/Details/31`, `/Member/Details/398`) whose bodies are those same empty shells.

The homepage does carry house-level aggregates, but not per member: "नोटिस प्राप्त 1676" (notices received 1,676), "प्रश्न 18644" (questions 18,644), "सरकारी विधेयक 1406" (government bills 1,406), "पटल पर रखे गये साहित्य 5211", "समिति की रिपोर्ट 0", "सदस्य 68" (members 68), and a party-wise widget reading "40 भारतीय राष्ट्रीय कांग्रेस / 28 भारतीय जनता पार्टी". Formats are server-rendered HTML fragments (ASP.NET MVC on Microsoft-IIS/10.0) delivered via jQuery AJAX; there is no JSON API anywhere on the HP NeVA instance; nothing on the site is a searchable roster portal.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
The scheme in Himachal Pradesh is the **Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi (VKVN)** - "विधायक क्षेत्र विकास निधि" - variously written in state documents as **Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Yozna (VKVY)** and colloquially as **Vidhayak Nidhi**. Its official scheme-rules document is a scanned PDF at **http://planning.hp.gov.in/plg_rdp/vkvny.pdf** (curl-200, `application/pdf`, 11,588,828 bytes, 43 pages, linked from the Planning Department's SMIS navigation). Structural analysis of that file gives **0 `/Type /Font` objects, 0 `/BaseFont`, 0 `/FontFile`** against 32 `DCTDecode` (JPEG) and 4 `JBIG2Decode` image streams - it is a pure **scanned-image PDF and needs OCR**; no text layer exists.

Himachal *does* run a state-level analogue of MPLADS, and it is official: **SMIS 2.0 - Schemes Monitoring Information System**, Planning Department, Yojna Bhavan, HP Secretariat, Shimla - **https://planning.hp.gov.in/smis/default.aspx** (curl-200, 17,039 bytes, `<title>SMIS 2.0 - Schemes Monitoring Information System</title>`). Its scheme menu lists, verbatim, "Guidelines / Backward Area Sub Plan / Vikas Mein Jan Sahyog / Sectoral Decentralised Planning / **Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Yozna** / MP Local Area Development / Panchayat Wise Schemes", and its own disclaimer says "This website has been developed to monitor development schemes sanctioned by District Planning Cell. Citizens can also do social audit and submit feedback on the completed schemes. Data available on the site is entered by the Planning branches of the respective districts." That is architecturally the same thing MPLADS publishes per MP - but **the data itself is not public**: the SMIS data view sits behind "Authorised Users Login" (Login ID, Password, CAPTCHA field `VerifyCaptcha`), on ASP.NET WebForms with `__VIEWSTATE`, `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR`, `__EVENTVALIDATION` and `__RequestVerificationToken`. A second, separate official portal, **https://planning.hp.gov.in/MLA-Priority/** - "MLA Priority Scheme(s) Monitoring", "Login For:- 1. Hon'ble MLA 2. Planning Department 3. JSV 4. PWD", footer "All Rights Reserved © 2019 Department of Planning, H.P. Govt.", "Last updation 10/11/2024" - is likewise entirely login-gated (a bare `/MLA-Priority` 301-redirects to `/MLA-Priority/`).

The single **public** report in SMIS is **https://planning.hp.gov.in/smis/publicpanchayatreport.aspx** - "Himachal Public Panchayat Report", `Department Login | Public Viewing of Panchayat Works and Feedback` (curl-200, 20,036 bytes, no credentials required). It offers cascading filters `DistrictList` (12 districts), `SchemeAreaList` (Rural | Urban), `BlockList`, `PanchayatList`, `FinYearList`, `HeadList`, and two submit buttons, `Print0` = "Summary Report" and `Print` = "List of Schemes". **It is keyed by District → Block → Panchayat and has no MLA or assembly-constituency dimension at all**, so per-MLA fund figures cannot be assembled from it even though it is public. Probes for a public MLA-wise sibling (`publicmlareport.aspx`, `publicreport.aspx`, `publicmlaworkreport.aspx`, `publicschemes.aspx`, `publicworkreport.aspx`) all returned 404.

The one official document that *is* per-MLA is a works list, not a fund ledger: **https://planning.hp.gov.in/NewReleases/2025-26_MLA_Priorities.pdf** - Finance Department (foÙk foHkkx), "New Expenditure Schedule (Annexure) - priorities indicated by Hon'ble MLAs, year 2025-26". It is curl-200, 699,661 bytes, 37 pages, and unlike the VKVN guidelines it is a **text PDF** (46 `/Type /Font` objects, 38 content streams with text operators, 1 image). It tabulates, per District / Constituency, an individual work ("Widening and Maintenance of FIS Thulel…", "Improvement of LWSS Amroh…"), a scheme head (लघु सिंचाई / सड़कें एवं पुल / पेयजल योजना / पूर्ति / मरम्मत एवं रखरखाव) and a budget estimate in ₹ lakh - overwhelmingly `0.25`. It is a list of recommended works per MLA, **not** an allocated limit / sanctioned / completed / expenditure ledger, and the extracted text is legacy Kruti Dev-style non-Unicode Hindi (e.g. "fgekpy izns’k ljdkj" for "हिमाचल प्रदेश सरकार"), so it needs a font-encoding mapping rather than OCR.

Consequently: **the fund exists as a scheme, and the state monitors it in SMIS, but there is no official portal publishing per-MLA allocated limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure.** The only per-MLA rupee figures available anywhere are in news reporting (₹110 crore total and ₹1.10 crore per MLA annually, second instalment ₹52.50 lakh per MLA), which is UNOFFICIAL and must be labelled as such. Related but off-target: HP's own MPLADS guidelines PDF at http://planning.hp.gov.in/plg_RDP/mplads.pdf (MP scheme, not MLA).

### In the Vidhan Sabha
**None of the four record types is published per member on the official site.**

- **Attendance** - not published. No attendance text, link, button or endpoint exists anywhere in the fetched HP NeVA pages, and a targeted search for an official per-MLA attendance record returned only news items (e.g. a Speaker claim of "100% attendance" for a session). There is no per-member attendance figure on any official Himachal government URL that this research could verify. Format: not found.
- **Starred questions** - not published per member. `/Questions` and `/Questions?IsStateDashBoard=True` both resolve to the state home page (`/Home/NeVA`), as do `/Business?LstItem=4&IsStateDashBoard=True`, `/Bill`, `/Bill?IsStateDashBoard=True`, `/Today`, `/Debates`, `/Committee` and `/Home/DigitalLibrary`. `/Question/Index` returns a genuine 404 (1,245 bytes). The AJAX endpoint `/Member/Member_Question` returns "Questions No Records Founds". The only question figure on the site is the house-level counter "प्रश्न 18644". Format: house-level counter only.
- **Unstarred questions** - not published; the site makes no starred/unstarred distinction anywhere in its public HTML. Format: not found.
- **Private members' bills** - not published per member. The `/Bill` route redirects to home, and `/Member/Member_Notice` (the member's own notice list) returns "No Records Founds". The only bill figure is the house-level counter "सरकारी विधेयक 1406". The legacy host hpvidhansabha.nic.in is indexed with a `Notice/GetCategoryByName/{id}` route, but that host is unreachable from this environment and no per-member bill data was confirmed from any source.

What the site *does* publish publicly is notice-board and news material: `/Notice/Index/HP` (16,331 bytes), `/News` (18,060 bytes), `/Home/RulesAndDirection` (15,424 bytes), `/Home/RightToInformation` (14,593 bytes), `/Home/Demandsgrants` (19,079 bytes), `/Home/GetAllSpeechList` (15,520 bytes), `/Home/FormData` (15,306 bytes) - plus a live-webcast mirror at https://webcast.gov.in/hpvidhansabha (curl-200, 19,761 bytes), linked from the state portal as "Budget Speech Webcast". All of it is house-level, not member-level.
