### Format note
A fetcher for Himachal Pradesh needs **four different pipelines and one hard dependency**. (1) `hpvs.neva.gov.in` is an ASP.NET MVC app on Microsoft-IIS/10.0 whose chrome is plain curl-able HTML, but every piece of member data is loaded by jQuery `$.ajax` GETs (`/Member/Member_ProfileDetail`, `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Member/Member_Notice`, `/Member/Member_Committee`) that returned an **empty template for every MemberId tried, with and without a session cookie jar** - so a headless browser alone will not fix this; the data appears to require an authenticated legislator context. Curl HTML works for the notice/news/static pages only. (2) `planning.hp.gov.in/smis` is ASP.NET WebForms with `__VIEWSTATE`, `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR`, `__EVENTVALIDATION` and `__RequestVerificationToken`, cascading `__doPostBack` on every select (`DistrictList` → `BlockList` → `PanchayatList`), session cookies (`AspxAutoDetectCookieSupport=1`), and a **CAPTCHA** (`VerifyCaptcha`) plus MD5-hashed password (`md5Auth(...)`) on the login path - browser/headless-driven POST emulation required, and the gated data is unreachable without credentials regardless. (3) `vkvny.pdf` is a **scanned document** (0 font objects; 32 DCTDecode + 4 JBIG2 image XObjects over 43 pages) and needs **OCR**, not text extraction. (4) `2025-26_MLA_Priorities.pdf` is a genuine text PDF but set in a **legacy Kruti Dev-style non-Unicode Hindi font**, so `pdftotext`/pypdf yields mojibake ("fgekpy izns’k ljdkr") and a Kruti Dev→Unicode character map is required. (5) On the election side, `www.eci.gov.in` is a React SPA (1,279-byte shell) **except** for its JSON API at `https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/*`, which curl fetches cleanly as `application/json` - but that API only covers 2023-2026, and the 2022 HP assembly results directory on `results.eci.gov.in` now returns **404**. (6) `ceohimachal.hp.gov.in` is server-rendered HTML that curl can read, but every page is addressed by an **opaque encrypted `qs` token** (e.g. `ViewCMSHtmlPage?qs=LdEF%2BAuYkB5RoJeWJ8txbw%3D%3D`) with no discoverable route pattern, so the tokens must be scraped from the menu pages first. Finally, `hpvidhansabha.nic.in` is a hard blocker: DNS resolves to 164.100.88.162 but **TCP 443 and 80 both time out**, so any pipeline pinned to that host will fail from outside its permitted network.

### Gap note
Three things are genuinely missing and cannot be sourced officially today. First, **per-MLA development-fund figures**: there is no official portal in Himachal Pradesh publishing allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure per MLA. SMIS and MLA-Priority exist and are exactly the right instruments, but both are login-gated; the only public SMIS report is keyed by District/Block/Panchayat with no MLA dimension; and the one public per-MLA document (the Finance Department's 2025-26 MLA priority annexure) lists individual works at ₹0.25 lakh each rather than any allocation or expenditure total. The per-MLA rupee amounts circulating (₹1.10 crore per MLA per year; ₹52.50 lakh instalments) come only from newspapers and are UNOFFICIAL. Second, **per-member assembly record**: attendance, starred questions, unstarred questions and private members' bills are all absent as per-member data. The site's `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Debates`, `/Today`, `/Committee` and `/Home/DigitalLibrary` routes all redirect to the state home, the member-level AJAX endpoints return "No Records Founds", and the only published question and bill numbers (18,644 and 1,406) are house-level counters printed on the homepage - they cannot be decomposed by member. Attendance is not published at all, at any granularity, on any official Himachal URL found. Third, **the member roster itself**: no page on the official site returns a list of the 68 MLAs with name, party, constituency and photo. The "Members"/"All Members" navigation item serves the national NeVA home page, the Contact Directory renders empty, `/Member/Details/{id}` returns one identical empty shell for every id, and the member photo path is a broken `<img src="/Member/">`. The 68-member count and the 40 INC / 28 BJP split are visible only as homepage widgets, not as a roster. Additionally, the state-branded assembly host `hpvidhansabha.nic.in` - which is where the search index places member routes such as `/Member/Details/50` - could not be fetched at all from this environment, so its content is reported as unverified rather than absent.

### Platform
Himachal Pradesh does **not** run a bespoke assembly site: it runs the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, the NIC/MoPA single national template, on the state subdomain `hpvs.neva.gov.in`. The evidence is direct rather than inferred: the national NeVA hub at `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` lists 39 legislature subdomains in one uniform `<statecode>.neva.gov.in` scheme (hpvs, gujarat, raj, upvs, uplc, kerala, tnla, wbla, mhla, mhlc, mpla, bla, asla, arla, tsla, tslc, Jhla, Jkla, kla, klc, odisha, manipur, mizo, nagaland, sikkim, tripura, utkh, puddu, pvs, chg, hrla, dla, goa, mgla, apa, apc, loksabha, rajyasabha); the footer credit is identical on HP and Gujarat - "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India"; the **build string is byte-identical across states** - "Zone(3) Version: 2.0.2 Last Updated: 25 Mar 2026" appears on both hpvs.neva.gov.in and gujarat.neva.gov.in; the route and AJAX surface is the same across states (`/Member/Details/{id}` returns 22,426 bytes and `/Member/Index` returns 404/1,245 bytes on **both** HP and Gujarat); and the shared CMS file store carries a per-state path segment on one host, `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/HPVS/FileStructures//…` for Himachal against `…/NeVA/GJ/FileStructures//…` for Gujarat. The same template was rolled out to HP by NIC (inaugurated at Tapovan, Dharamshala) and is being extended to other states under centrally-signed MoPA agreements - so **one NeVA scraper serves many states at once**, and the HP work is a per-state configuration of a national scraper rather than a new one. Note the contrast with the fund side, which is entirely bespoke to Himachal: SMIS 2.0 and the MLA Priority Scheme portal are HP Planning Department applications on `planning.hp.gov.in`, with no counterpart in other states.

---

## Jharkhand (IN-JH)

**Verdict:** partial - Jharkhand has a live official Assembly website, a live NeVA instance and fully official election results, but the member roster is a text-only PDF with NO photographs, there is NO official per-MLA development-fund portal of any kind, and attendance / questions are not published on the public web at all.

### Profile
The official website of the Jharkhand Legislative Assembly is **https://vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in/** (curl-200, 128,508 bytes, `<title>झारखण्ड विधानसभा-झारखंड की सरकार, भारत</title>`). It is a **bespoke Drupal 7 site** (asset paths `/sites/default/files/...`, theme at `/sites/all/themes/jharkhand/`, Hindi-transliterated node aliases such as `/झारखण्ड-विधानसभा-सदस्यों-की-सूची`), with no NIC or vendor footer credit — the footer reads only "झारखंड सरकार द्वारा सर्वाधिकार सुरक्षित | आखरी अद्यतन 02-09-2026". The historical `.nic.in` domain **jharkhandvidhansabha.nic.in is NXDOMAIN (dead)**; the live site is the `.gov.in` one, yet the live site still emits outbound links to the dead domain (see In the Vidhan Sabha).

Member data is published **only as per-Assembly PDF roster files**, linked from the members index at `https://vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in/झारखण्ड-विधानसभा-सदस्यों-की-सूची`. The current (Sixth) Assembly roster is:

- **https://vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in/sites/default/files/षष्ठम-झारखण्ड-विधान-सभा-के-निर्वाचित-सदस्यों-की-सूची-अभियुक्ति-सहित.pdf** — curl-200, `application/pdf`, 224,241 bytes, 6 pages, **`/Font` 36 and `/Image` 0**.

That image count is the decisive fact: the roster is a genuine **text PDF** (so no OCR is needed) but it contains **zero images, therefore zero member photographs**. Extracting it with a real PDF parser yields the column structure: *निर्वाचन क्षेत्र संख्या* (constituency number), *विधानसभा क्षेत्र का नाम* (constituency name), *वर्ग* (category — General/ST/SC), *सदस्य का नाम* (member name), *दल का नाम* (party name) and *अभियुक्ति* (affidavit/criminal-accusation column). Worked example of the decoded text: constituency 1 राजमहल → मो0 ताजुद्दीन → झारखण्ड मुक्ति मोर्चा. Equivalent rosters exist for the first four Assemblies (`FirstMLAList.pdf`, `2ndAssemblyMember.pdf`, `3rdAssemblyMember.pdf`, `4thAssebleMember.pdf`) and the Fifth (`list2019.pdf`, plus `निर्वाचित-सदस्यों-की-सूची-300724.pdf` with affidavit). There is **no per-member HTML page and no per-member URL** anywhere on this site, so the roster is a single document per Assembly rather than a queryable per-MLA record. Photos are therefore **not available from the Assembly website**.

One genuinely per-MLA official dataset does exist, though it is assets rather than identity: the page `https://vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in/विधायको-की-सम्पति-का-विवरण` ("विधायको की सम्पति का विवरण" / declaration of property by MLAs) lists named MLAs each with a download at `https://vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in/sites/default/files/mlasampati/<name>.zip` (for example `1arjunmunda.zip`, `1hemlalmurmu.zip`, `1cpsingh.zip` — 55+ members present). This is per-MLA and official, but it is a ZIP of scanned declaration documents, not structured data, and the names present appear to belong to the Fifth Assembly rather than the sitting one.

A second, parallel official route exists on the national platform: **https://jhla.neva.gov.in/** (curl-200, 64,480 bytes, `Server: Microsoft-IIS/10.0`, `<title>Welcome to Jharkhand Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application</title>`). Its homepage does display the headline profile facts — 81 members with a party-wise breakdown (झारखण्ड मुक्ति मोर्चा 34, भारतीय जनता पार्टी 21, भारतीय राष्ट्रीय कांग्रेस 16, राष्ट्रीय जनता दल 4, CPI(ML)(L) 2, आजसू 1, झारखंड लोकतांत्रिक क्रांतिकारी मोर्चा 1, जद(यू) 1, लोजपा(रा) 1) plus the names of the Governor, Chief Minister, Speaker, Leader of Opposition and Secretary. However the **member module is an empty placeholder**: `/Member/Details/9` (Chief Minister), `/Member/Details/11` (Speaker) and `/Member/Details/87` (Leader of Opposition) all return 22,426 bytes containing **zero occurrences of the member's name, three occurrences of "No Records Founds"**, a broken `<img src="/Member/">` and a leaked default title from another state's template, *"Member Details | NeVA | eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"*. The member directory route `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec` and the `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Today`, `/Debates` and `/Committee` routes all redirect to the **national** portal at `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` (all return an identical 192,345 bytes), i.e. the Jharkhand instance's deep modules are not populated or reachable. Verification level for the NeVA instance: **curl-200**, linked from the official NeVA state list.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
In Jharkhand the fund is called **विधायक निधि (Vidhayak Nidhi)** in Hindi; the English press renders it as "MLA fund" and, in one 2026 report, "MLAD fund". The name is confirmed from an official source rather than from news: the Assembly's own search index returns official Assembly committee records naming the oversight body **विधायक निधि अनुश्रवण समिति (Vidhayak Nidhi Monitoring Committee)** inside the annual "झारखण्ड विधानसभा की समितियाँ" pages for FY 2011-12, 2013-14, 2014-15, 2015-16, 2016-17, 2017-18 and 2018-19 (entries "14. सदाचार एवं विधायक निधि अनुश्रवण समिति" and "16. विधायक निधि अनुश्रवण समिति" on `https://vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in/search/node`). So the scheme demonstrably exists and the legislature oversees it — but the committee record lists only members, with **no allocation, sanction, completion or expenditure figures**.

**No official portal publishing per-MLA fund data was found.** There is no state-level equivalent of MPLADS for Jharkhand. I probed the plausible hostnames directly and every one of them failed DNS or connection: `mlalad.jharkhand.gov.in`, `vidhayaknidhi.jharkhand.gov.in`, `nidhi.jharkhand.gov.in`, `mla.jharkhand.gov.in`, `plan.jharkhand.gov.in`, `planning.jharkhand.gov.in`, `rural.jharkhand.gov.in` and `edistrict.jharkhand.gov.in` all returned HTTP 000. The Jharkhand government hosts that *are* live carry no fund data: `https://www.jharkhand.gov.in/` (curl-200, 275,111 bytes), `https://finance.jharkhand.gov.in/` (curl-200, 93,874 bytes — its Circular, Publication, Budget and Proceedings pages contain **zero** occurrences of "विधायक", "निधि" or "MLA"), `https://jharsewa.jharkhand.gov.in/` (curl-200, 109,845 bytes — no fund references), and the Department of Planning & Development directorate page `https://www.jharkhand.gov.in/PDepartment/WebDirectory?department=32d5e36f384bebd5d3753a04a0302eec` (curl-200, 125,923 bytes, a "Who's Who" contact list only). The `jbb.jharkhand.gov.in` hit turned out to be the **Jharkhand Biodiversity Board**, an unrelated false lead.

Consequently: the fund **exists as a scheme with a legislature monitoring committee but has NO public per-MLA data portal**. The only figures in circulation are aggregates in news reporting (₹405 crore released for 81 MLAs, i.e. ₹5 crore each; a July 2022 cabinet decision raising the MLA fund to ₹5 crore), which are **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**. The documented civic route for a citizen to obtain MLA-fund usage is an RTI application, per an unofficial explainer — which is itself evidence that no proactive portal exists.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
The public web publishes **no per-member assembly record for Jharkhand**. Taking the four data points in turn:

- **Attendance** — **not published**. No attendance page, table, PDF or field exists on either the bespoke Assembly site or the NeVA instance. This is a plain gap.
- **Starred questions** — **not published as a member-level dataset.** The Assembly site routes question data to `http://172.16.20.14/questions/Default.asp`, a **private RFC1918 address on the Secretariat intranet**, which is unreachable from the public internet (the same host serves `http://172.16.20.14/oasys/Home.aspx`, labelled on the site as "OASYS (FOR Jharnet User)"). A "QMS (For Jharnet User)" entry exists beside it. The NeVA instance advertises a count of 1,130 questions and links to `/Questions?IsStateDashBoard=True`, but that route redirects to the national NeVA homepage. Only individual sitting-day question PDFs surface publicly, e.g. a home-page notice titled "तारांकित प्रश्न, मंगलवार, 10 मार्च 2026", served from `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/JH/FileStructures//Notices/...` — that is a per-sitting-day document, not a per-MLA record.
- **Unstarred questions** — **not published** on any official public page found, for the same reason: the question system is intranet-hosted and the NeVA route is empty.
- **Private members' bills** — **nominally listed but effectively unusable.** The Assembly site's विधेयक (Bills) page at `https://vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in/विधेयक` (curl-200, 51,186 bytes) lists annual bill registers for 2001 through 2026. But every one of the links is broken: the page contains **24 references to the dead domain `jharkhandvidhansabha.nic.in` and exactly 0 working local PDF links**. Sample dead targets verified as DNS failures (HTTP 000): `https://jharkhandvidhansabha.nic.in/sites/default/files/Videyak2002.pdf`. The bill lists are therefore unreachable, and in any case they are organised by year rather than by member, and cover government as well as private members' bills without distinguishing them.

Relatedly, the "विधानसभा की दलगत स्थिति" (party-wise position) page lists party strength per Assembly, but its Sixth-Assembly link also points to the dead domain (`jharkhandvidhansabha.nic.in/sites/default/files/DalgatSthiti.pdf`, HTTP 000), so the party-position page for the current Assembly is broken too.

### Format note
A fetcher needs a **mixed** toolchain, and the blockers differ per source. The Assembly site `vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in` is plain server-rendered Drupal: `curl` returns full HTML with no JS dependency, so the roster index and the per-MLA ZIP index are scraped with curl alone — but the actual data is behind **PDF text extraction in Devanagari**, and the PDFs use subset fonts with custom encodings, so a naive string/`zlib` stream scrape produces mojibake while a real parser (pypdf) decodes correctly; budget speeches for 2025-26 and 2026-27 are **scanned images (`/Font` 0)** and need OCR. The best source in the whole jurisdiction is the **ECI JSON API** (`https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/election-result?category_id=9`), which is unauthenticated, returns clean JSON, and points at `.xlsx`/`.pdf` artefacts — pure `curl`-JSON — even though the human-facing `www.eci.gov.in` is a React SPA and `results.eci.gov.in` only meta-refreshes to the current election. Conversely `affidavit.eci.gov.in` is **POST-only behind a session-bound CSRF token** (`<meta name="csrf-token">`, and the token changes between requests), so candidate affidavits require a **headless browser** or a cookie-jar that first GETs the form; posting without a session returns `{"success":true,"message":"DB details not found"}`. `jhla.neva.gov.in` serves curl-able HTML but its member/questions/bills routes redirect to the national portal, so it is effectively **browser-needed** for anything beyond the homepage counters. `finance.jharkhand.gov.in` navigates entirely through `javascript:__doPostBack(...)`, and `www.jharkhand.gov.in` emits ASP.NET session-state URLs of the form `/(X(1)S(oeakjcriesy3h4c3mdk3gpww))/PDepartment/...`, both of which mean **POST-only / session-bearing** fetching; its document store (`/Home/ViewDoc?id=...`) returns 2.5 MB single-page **scanned images** needing OCR. No captcha was encountered anywhere.

### Gap note
Three things cannot be sourced officially and must be recorded as gaps. **(1) MLA photographs.** Neither the Assembly website nor the NeVA instance publishes any member photograph. The Sixth-Assembly roster PDF is text-only with `/Image` 0, there is no per-member page to scrape a portrait from, and the NeVA member detail pages render no data at all. Official portraits are therefore unobtainable from government sources; the only official channels that *might* carry an image are the ECI candidate-affidavit documents (`affidavit.eci.gov.in`), which are behind a CSRF-protected POST form and were not retrievable in this session. **(2) MLA development-fund figures.** There is no official per-MLA — or even aggregate — publication of Vidhayak Nidhi allocation, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure. No portal exists at any plausible hostname, the Finance Department publishes nothing on the fund, and the only numbers available (₹5 crore per MLA / ₹405 crore total) come from news reporting dated to a 2026 release and are UNOFFICIAL. **(3) Assembly record.** Attendance is not published in any form; starred and unstarred question data is locked behind an **intranet-only host (`172.16.20.14`)** that is unreachable from the public internet; and private members' bills are nominally listed for 2001-2026 but every link resolves to the **dead domain `jharkhandvidhansabha.nic.in` (NXDOMAIN)**, leaving 0 working documents. Party-position data for the sitting Assembly is broken the same way.

### Platform
Jharkhand runs **two parallel legislative platforms**, and the shared one is the important finding: **Jharkhand is onboarded onto NeVA (National e-Vidhan Application)**, the NIC/MoPA common platform, at **https://jhla.neva.gov.in/** — listed from `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` under "Jharkhand झारखंड / Onboarded / Mou Signed", following the national `<state-code>la.neva.gov.in` subdomain pattern (compare `mpla.neva.gov.in` for Madhya Pradesh). The evidence of a shared template is direct and not inferred: the two instances load **byte-identical asset paths** (`/2024/css/bootstrap.min.css`, `/2024/css/fontawesome.css`, `/2024/css/style.css`), carry the same footer "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India", use the same `Zone(N)` routing construct (Jharkhand Zone 1, Madhya Pradesh Zone 3), and share the CMS origin `cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/JH/FileStructures//Notices/...`; the Jharkhand instance even leaks another state's default page title, *"eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"*. **One scraper for the NeVA markup would therefore serve many states at once.** Alongside it, the primary public Assembly website `vidhansabha.jharkhand.gov.in` is **bespoke** — a Drupal 7 build with a custom `sites/all/themes/jharkhand/` theme, Hindi-transliterated node aliases and a self-hosted PDF repository, carrying no NIC or vendor credit — so the two cannot be scraped with the same code.

---

## Karnataka (IN-KA)

**Verdict:** partial - The Karnataka Legislative Assembly publishes a complete, curl-fetchable member roster and an official full-text JSON API covering starred/unstarred questions per member, but there is no official portal publishing PER-MLA development-fund figures and no official publication of MLA attendance.

### Profile

Karnataka has a bicameral legislature; the lower house is the Karnataka Legislative Assembly (ಕರ್ನಾಟಕ ವಿಧಾನ ಸಭೆ). The official site is the Karnataka Legislature at **https://kla.kar.nic.in/** (the Assembly section index is https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/assembly.htm). Both were fetched and returned HTTP 200 with `text/html`.

The member roster of the current (16th) Assembly is published at **https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/member/16assemblymemberslist.htm** - verified curl-200, 149,144 bytes of plain static HTML. It is a single HTML table with exactly four columns: **Sl No. / Constituency / Member Name / Party**, each row carrying both an English and a Kannada rendering. All 224 constituencies are present. There is **no photo, no term/assembly start-end date, and no individual member profile page** in this roster - it is a flat list, not a queryable portal. Party is given as a full name plus abbreviation (e.g. "BHARATIYA JANATA PARTY (BJP)", "INDIAN NATIONAL CONGRESS (INC)"). A separate https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/member/partyposition.htm gives the party-wise seat position, and https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/member/members.htm is the section shell whose left-hand menu uses jQuery `.load()` to inject the roster into a `#contents` div (the roster is therefore reachable directly as the static fragment above).

**Photos are published, but only inside a PDF.** The "Who's Who Book (ಸದಸ್ಯರ ಪರಿಚಯ ಪುಸ್ತಕ)" at **https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/member/16thWhosWho.pdf** was downloaded: HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, **30,663,754 bytes**, containing **3,191 `/Image` entries and 597 `/Font` entries**. The presence of thousands of image XObjects against ~224 members indicates one portrait photograph per member plus scanned annexures; the substantial `/Font` count means a real text layer also exists. This is the only official per-member photographic source. Members' contact addresses are published separately as bilingual PDFs (`16membersaddress_eng.pdf`, `16membersaddress_kan.pdf`, dated 01-09-2026, 200 OK) but these are address lists, not profiles.

A second official surface exists: **https://klaexplorer.karnataka.gov.in/** ("Explorer Console"), linked from the Assembly site under the label "Document Management System". It is a Create React App single-page application, but it is backed by a working **JSON API** (see below).

Verification level for the profile data: roster and address PDFs are **curl-200**; the Who's Who PDF is **curl-200**.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

Karnataka's scheme is called **KLADS - the Karnataka Legislators' Area Development Scheme** (rendered in the official Karnataka Economic Survey as "Karnataka Legislators' Local Area Development Scheme"). The Kannada colloquial and press form is **ಶಾಸಕರ ನಿಧಿ ("Shasakara Nidhi" - Legislators' Fund)**; it is also referred to in state press as the MLA LAD fund or MLA fund. There is no "Vidhayak Nidhi" branding in Karnataka.

**No official per-MLA data portal was found.** This is the central finding for this subsection. Concrete negative evidence: `klads.karnataka.gov.in`, `mlalad.karnataka.gov.in` and `lad.karnataka.gov.in` do **not resolve** (curl exit 6, no DNS); the Government of Karnataka portal https://karnataka.gov.in/ contains **zero** occurrences of "KLADS", "MLALAD", "Vidhayak" or "Nidhi" in its 314,531-byte homepage; and repeated web searches for a per-MLA works/expenditure portal surface only news articles and NGO reports. There is no state-level analogue of the MPLADS portal (https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/) for Karnataka MLAs.

The **scheme rules do exist officially** but the host is now dead. The document "PROCEEDINGS OF THE GOVERNMENT OF KARNATAKA - Guidelines KLADS (English)" is a **47-page** government order. Its original URL, `http://www.hkrdb.kar.nic.in/documents/Downloads/Government Rules and Guidelines/Guidelines KLADS (English).pdf`, **no longer resolves** (`hkrdb.kar.nic.in` has no DNS record). An archived copy is retrievable: **https://web.archive.org/web/20240604163707/https://hkrdb.kar.nic.in/documents/Downloads/Government%20Rules%20and%20Guidelines/Guidelines%20KLADS%20(English).pdf** - verified curl-200, 273,210 bytes, `application/pdf`, with **74 `/Font` and 0 `/Image` entries**, i.e. a genuine text PDF, not a scan. It uses embedded TrueType subsets with ToUnicode maps (4 `/ToUnicode`, 4 `/FontFile2`), so text extraction works with a proper PDF parser but a naive regex over raw streams yields garbage.

The official **Economic Survey of Karnataka** (Directorate of Economics and Statistics, `des.kar.nic.in`) carries a section numbered "14.3 Karnataka Legislators' Local Area Development Scheme" in the 2015-16 edition. That host **also no longer resolves**; the volume is reachable only through the Internet Archive. Aggregate-only scheme figures (state-wide allocation, release and utilisation) have historically appeared there - never per-MLA.

Per-MLA fund figures that do circulate publicly come from non-government bodies: B.PAC (Bangalore Political Action Committee) MLA fund utilisation reports and the OpenCity/CKAN "Bengaluru MLA - Local Area Development Funds" datasets. These are **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification** and are recorded in the sources table only as leads, never as provenance.

Verification level: scheme name and the existence of official guidelines are confirmed from an archived official PDF; **the per-MLA portal is 'not found'**.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred and unstarred questions: published, and queryable per member - but through two different surfaces with different quality.**

1. *Bulk PDF surface.* Questions are listed by sitting date at **https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/questions/presentqns.htm** (current session) and **https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/questions/previousqns.htm** (all past sessions; verified curl-200, 254,964 bytes). Each sitting day links to three PDFs following a strict filename convention: `lasq<DDMMYYYY>.pdf` = **Starred Questions**, `lausq<DDMMYYYY>.pdf` = **Un-Starred Questions**, and `lar351<DDMMYYYY>.pdf` = the Rule 351 answers. A sampled starred-question PDF (`lasq17082026.pdf`) returned HTTP 200, 150,860 bytes, with **77 `/Font` against only 3 `/Image` entries** - it is a **text PDF, not a scan**, so text extraction is viable. These daily PDFs are organised **by date, not by member**; `lausq17082026.pdf` returned **404** on direct fetch, so the unstarred filename convention is present in the HTML but that particular day's file was not retrievable - a fetcher must read the index rather than construct filenames.

2. *JSON API surface (the valuable one).* **https://klaexplorer.karnataka.gov.in/api/sd/sh?qp=<query>** was fetched and returns **HTTP 200 `application/json`** - roughly 400 KB for a common surname. It is an Elasticsearch-backed search over the entire legislative record (the sample hit was a 1959 debate), returning `{"timeTaken", "numberResults", "debateResults": [...], "analysis": {...}}` with `_source` fields including: `questioner_name_eng` / `questioner_name_kan` (**per-member**), `minister_name_eng`, `minister_portfolio_eng`, `debate_title_subject_eng` / `debate_title_subject_kan`, `debate_subject_kan`, `debate_participants_eng` / `debate_participants_kan`, `debate_section_date`, `assemblyNumber`, `sessionNumber`, `bookId`, `startPage`, `endPage`, `yearBook`, `placeSession`. Critically, **`debate_title_subject_eng` carries the literal values "Starred Questions" and "Unstarred Questions"** - verified by a query on "Siddaramaiah" returning 23 Starred and 4 Unstarred among the top 50 rows, so the starred/unstarred distinction is a queryable field and not merely a document title. The API also exposes `sectionType` and `issues_section_eng`. A companion endpoint **/api/sd/df** returns aggregate `analysis` buckets including `debateParticiapantsKan_bucket`, a per-member activity count (e.g. 3,558 documents for one member), which is the closest thing to an official per-member activity index. **/api/sd/whoswho/all** returns HTTP 200 JSON, and **/api/sd/ot** serves the bills/review index. The JS bundle reveals the full filter vocabulary: `srt, qt, qp, dtf, anf, snf, dsubfEng, dsubfKan, dpfEng, dpfKan, dbf, ytf, sectionDateFrm, sectionDateTo, issfEng, issfKan, tagfKan, tagfEng, asn, ssn, bookId` - i.e. debate-participant, debate-subject, year, assembly-number and session-number filters all exist, and the bundle ships a preset with `dsubfEng: ["Legislation ( Introduction of Bills)"]`, confirming **bills are a filterable debate subject**. Attempts to drive these array-valued filters directly (as `dsubfEng[]=`, JSON-encoded, or `dpfEng=`) returned **HTTP 400 or 500** - the simple `qp=` full-text query works from curl, the structured filters need the SPA's exact axios serialisation to be reverse-engineered.

**Private members' bills: published as lists, NOT per member.** **https://kla.kar.nic.in/assembly/bills/allbills.htm** (curl-200, 23,018 bytes) indexes session-wise PDFs named `bills_list<NNNNN>.pdf` ("i) Bills Introduced and Passed List", "ii) Bills"). The 15th-Assembly example is `bills_list15100.pdf`. These are per-session consolidated lists of bills introduced/passed/withdrawn. They are **not labelled by member sponsorship** and are not split into government vs. private members' bills; the only per-member bill linkage is indirectly through the Explorer API's "Legislation ( Introduction of Bills)" subject filter.

**Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED.** This is an explicit gap. "attendance", "Attendance" and the Kannada "ಹಾಜರಾತಿ" return **zero occurrences** across the Assembly index page, the Members section, Party Position, Review, Legislators' Home, Secretary's Report and Legislation pages, all of which were fetched and scanned. No attendance register, no per-member sitting-day count, and no absence record exists on the official site. The only per-MLA attendance figures in circulation come from **ADR (Association for Democratic Reforms)** - a non-government body - which publishes "Karnataka MLAs Performance (2018 to 2023) - Included Attendance Data". That is **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification** and cannot serve as provenance. Note that "Leave of Absence by Members" does appear as a debate subject in the Explorer API, but that records grants of leave on particular days, not cumulative attendance.

Verification level: roster, question index and bills index are **curl-200**; the Explorer API is **curl-200**; attendance is **not found**.

### Format note

A fetcher needs a **mixed** strategy, and only one source is genuinely easy. The KLA roster, question index and bills index are plain static HTML tables over HTTP - `curl` plus an HTML parser is sufficient, no browser. The payload behind them is PDFs, and the good news is that those PDFs are **text PDFs, not scans** (the sampled starred-questions PDF carried 77 `/Font` objects against 3 `/Image`), so PDF text extraction is enough - OCR is not required for the question record. The single OCR/imaging caveat is the **Who's Who PDF: 30.6 MB with 3,191 embedded images**, where member portraits must be extracted as images (confirming 224 portraits is a per-page mapping exercise, not a text problem). The richest source is the **Explorer JSON API**, which `curl` reaches directly with a simple `qp=` full-text query and which returns per-member, per-subject records including the literal strings "Starred Questions"/"Unstarred Questions" - but its structured filters (`dsubfEng`, `dpfEng`, `ytf`, `asn`) are array-typed and returned 400/500 when sent as plain or `[]`-suffixed parameters, so either the SPA's axios serialisation must be reverse-engineered or the fetcher must pull broadly by `qp=` and filter client-side. Two blockers are worth naming: the Explorer backend is a **custom Node/Express service** (an error response leaked the path `/home/klasuser/project/explorer/kla/explorer/server/node_modules`), so its behaviour is undocumented and could change without notice; and the KLA site is served over **plain HTTP-era static files** with jQuery 1.5.1 and no TLS issue but no API contract. For elections, **eci.gov.in is a JavaScript-rendered SPA** - every path returns the same 1,279-byte shell, so a headless browser is mandatory there and HTTP status codes cannot be used to test whether a page exists - whereas **results.eci.gov.in is static HTML** and curl-fetchable. The state CEO site is plain HTML, but several of its CMS routes (`/213/elections/kn`, `/285/form-20`, `/360/acts-and-rules/kn`) return **404**, so a fetcher must not construct those URLs.

### Gap note

Three things are missing and cannot be sourced officially. **First and most important: there is no official portal publishing PER-MLA development-fund figures for Karnataka.** No allocation, works-recommended, works-sanctioned, works-completed or expenditure figure can be traced to a government URL for an individual MLA. The scheme (KLADS) and its 47-page government guidelines exist, but the guidelines host `hkrdb.kar.nic.in` no longer resolves, and the only retrievable copy is an Internet Archive capture - official in content, not in hosting. Karnataka therefore has no state-level equivalent of the MPLADS portal. Any per-MLA fund number this project displays would have to come from B.PAC, OpenCity/CKAN or news reporting, all **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**; the honest outcome is to show the fund field as unavailable with provenance to that effect. **Second: MLA attendance is not published at all** - not as a register, not as a per-member sitting count, and not inside the Explorer API's per-member data. The only attendance numbers available are ADR's compilation, which is a non-government source. **Third: private members' bills are not published per member.** The Assembly publishes only a per-session consolidated list of bills introduced and passed; it does not break bills down by sponsoring member, and it does not separate government bills from private members' bills. A related confirmed dead end is that the Economic Survey and DES Karnataka hosts (`des.kar.nic.in`) have also stopped resolving, removing what was historically the only official aggregate scheme reporting.

### Platform

Karnataka is **bespoke, not a shared NIC/NeVA template** - and unusually, it runs **two different bespoke platforms of its own**. The Assembly site `kla.kar.nic.in` credits **"national informatics centre, bengaluru"** in its footer (linked to `http://www.kar.nic.in`) and is hosted on NIC infrastructure at 164.100.201.90, but it carries no NeVA / e-Vidhan branding, no NeVA JS, and no cross-state shared markup - it is hand-rolled static HTML with jQuery 1.5.1 and `.load()`-injected fragments, whereas NeVA deployments expose a recognisable national template. News reporting in 2026 indicates Karnataka is only *moving* toward e-Vidhan, which is consistent with it not yet being on the national platform. Its sibling, the **Explorer Console** at `klaexplorer.karnataka.gov.in`, is a separately built Create React App with a custom Node/Express + Elasticsearch backend (index `klaprod`; a leaked error stack showed `/home/klasuser/project/explorer/kla/explorer/server/node_modules`), again with no vendor or national template markers. The **CEO Karnataka** site is a third distinct build: its footer reads **"Designed, developed and hosted by: Centre for e-Governance, Government of Karnataka"** - a state agency, **not NIC** - and it uses a WordPress-style CMS with `menu-item menu-item-has-children level_0` / `cd-primary-nav` classes and numeric routes of the form `/<node-id>/<slug>/kn`. Practical consequence for this project: **no single scraper spans Karnataka's own sources, and none of them will transfer to another state.**

---

## Kerala (IN-KL)

**Verdict:** partial - Kerala has a sitting legislature and an excellent official per-member PROFILE source (all 140 MLAs with name, party, constituency and photo in one HTML table) and a curl-fetchable JSON API for starred/unstarred questions, but there is NO official portal publishing per-MLA development-fund figures, and no attendance data is published at all.

### Profile

Kerala has a unicameral legislature, the **Kerala Legislative Assembly (Kerala Niyamasabha)**, currently the **16th KLA** (elected May 2026; the site's own KLA dropdown on `/index.php/questions/starred` runs to "16th Kla" and the current session is `session_id=39`, "Session 1"). Two official domains are live and both are government-run: the modern site at **https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/** and a legacy static site at **https://www.niyamasabha.org/** (e.g. `https://www.niyamasabha.org/codes/members.htm`, which still serves the 16th-KLA member list with `nic.in` email-address images). The `.nic.in` host is the canonical one and is the target of all internal navigation.

**Member roster — yes, complete and per-member.** `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/index.php/content/sitting_member` returns a single plain `<table>` with the exact columns `Sl. No | Name of member | Party Name | Constituency | Photo`. I parsed it: **140 data rows** (Kerala has 140 assembly constituencies), and **all 140 rows carry an `<img>` in the Photo cell** - 100% photo coverage, no placeholder rows. Party distribution parsed from that page: Indian National Congress 63, CPI(M) 27, IUML 22, Kerala Congress 7, CPI 7, Independent 4, BJP 3, RSP 3. Photos are plain JPGs at a predictable path, e.g. `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/images/member/abin-varkey-kodiyattu_member_16_36.jpg`, where the filename embeds the assembly number and a member id (`_member_16_36` = 16th KLA, id 36). Note the roster's party tally differs slightly from the ECI result (ECI gives CPI(M) 26 / CPI 8); the assembly page appears to lag on party attribution for one or two members.

**Two further per-member endpoints exist.** `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/index.php/content/member_search` is the full historical roster: an HTML table of **1088 rows** with columns `Sl. No | Name of member | Member in KLAs | Photo`, where the "Member in KLAs" cell links each member's per-assembly profile. Individual profiles live at `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/index.php/profile/index/<id>` and `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/index.php/content/member_homepage/<id>`; both return the member's party, elected date, `@niyamasabha.nic.in` email, father/spouse name, marital status, and present/permanent address. Data-quality caveat observed on `/index.php/profile/index/1209`: the "Elected Date" field rendered as `1111-01-01`, i.e. a placeholder, so that field should not be trusted without cross-checking against the ECI result.

**Verification level:** `curl-200` for every profile URL above - each was fetched by curl with a browser User-Agent and returned the content described.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**The local name is not "Vidhayak Nidhi".** In Kerala the scheme is the **"Special Development Fund for MLAs" (SDF)**, also abbreviated **MLA-SDF** in tender notices and district documents. It is administered by the **Finance (Nodal Centre) Department**, not by a planning board, and funds are released to each **District Collector** through a separate PD Account for onward payment to implementing officers. This is established by a *text-extractable* official Government Order I fetched and parsed: `https://lsgd.kerala.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/lsgd_orders_pdf/go20020118_6941.pdf` — "Guidelines Modified on Special Development Fund for MLAs", **G.O.(P) No. 60/2002/Fin, dated 18-1-2002**, which reads in the earlier G.O. `1234/2001/Fin dt. 29.10.2001` that introduced "the newly introduced Special Development Fund for MLAs", and orders that administrative sanction be issued by the District Collector, that funds be released in three instalments (first up to 50%), and that each Collector open a separate PD Account. A second official guidelines index at `https://www.celsgd.kerala.gov.in/celsgd/gen/schemes_guidelines.php` (Kerala LSG Department engineering wing) lists two MLA-specific orders in its `Guidelines/` folder: `G.O.P.112-07-MLA.pdf` and `G.O.P.236-05-MLA.pdf`.

**No official portal publishing per-MLA fund data was found.** I probed and rejected a series of plausible-but-nonexistent hosts (all returned HTTP 000 / no DNS or no service): `mlafund.kerala.gov.in`, `mlasdf.kerala.gov.in`, `sdffund.kerala.gov.in`, `mla.kerala.gov.in`, `planning.kerala.gov.in`, `mlafund.lsgkerala.gov.in`, `sdf.lsgkerala.gov.in`. These are recorded here as **negative** results only — none of them is a real source and none should appear in the site. Kerala therefore has **no state-level equivalent of the MPLADS portal**: there is no per-MLA table of allocated limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure. What exists instead is fragmented and non-aggregated: (a) the scheme *rules* as GOs; (b) individual SDF works surfacing as **tenders** on the official LSG Department tender portal `https://tender.lsgkerala.gov.in/pages/displayTender.php` (searchable by district, local-body type, local body and free text; SDF works appear in tender descriptions as, e.g., "MLA-SDF-Tirurangadi LAC-2023-24"), which yields work-level records but no per-MLA totals; and (c) Malayalam press releases such as `https://prd.kerala.gov.in/ml/node/332206` announcing an MLA fund sanction, which are announcements, not data.

**Broken official link worth recording.** The Kerala Finance Department's own document repository (`www.finance.kerala.gov.in`, a Joomla `com_docman` site) publishes "Guidelines on Special Development fund for MLAs" at `http://www.finance.kerala.gov.in/index.php?option=com_docman&task=doc_download&gid=191&Itemid=34`. That URL returns **HTTP 200 with a 383-byte JavaScript stub** whose only content is `window.location = "http://www.infofinance.kerala.gov.in" + "/index.php?" + urlParams`. The redirect target **does not respond** (`http://infofinance.kerala.gov.in/` and `https://www.infofinance.kerala.gov.in/` both curl to `000`). The hostname does resolve in DNS (103.251.43.95) but the web service is unreachable. Every `finance.kerala.gov.in` docman GO link therefore dead-ends; the only mirror of the SDF GO that actually serves bytes is the LSGD copy cited above.

**Verification level:** `curl-200` for the LSGD GO PDF, the CELSGD guidelines index and its two MLA PDFs (but see the OCR caveat below), and the tender portal; `search-result-only` for the `prd.kerala.gov.in` release; the dead `infofinance` redirect verified by direct curl.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred and unstarred questions: YES, published and machine-readable, but per-QUESTION, not per-member.** The landing pages `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/index.php/questions/starred` and `.../questions/unstarred` are *not* where the data is — they render only three empty dropdowns (KLA name, Session, Department) and an AJAX handler. The real data sits behind a POST-only JSON API with a CodeIgniter CSRF token, which I reverse-engineered from `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/js/common.js` and successfully drove with plain curl:

1. GET the landing page, scrape the token out of `onchange="selectkla_session('<token>', ...)"`, and keep the cookie jar.
2. POST `index.php/bills/selectkla` with `klaid=<1..16>&csrf_test_name=<token>` → JSON session list, e.g. `[{"session_id":"39","session_name":"Session 1"}]` for the 16th KLA; for the 15th KLA it returns sessions 23–31.
3. POST `index.php/questions/get_all_starred_questions_date` (or `.../get_all_unstarred_questions_date`) with `kla=<n>&session=<id>&department=<n|empty>&date=&csrf_test_name=<token>` → JSON array of `{"id","date"}`, e.g. for KLA 15 / session 23: `[{"id":"2044","date":"10-06-2021"}, ...]`.
4. POST `index.php/questions/get_all_search_starred_question` with `id=<qid>` (no CSRF field needed) → a JSON object with keys `id, kla_id, session_id, depaertment, date, file, html_content, is_stared`. Verified for `id=2044` (`is_stared: "Yes"`, 75k of HTML) and `id=2056` (`is_stared: "No"`, 123k of HTML, 286 KB response). The same detail endpoint serves both starred and unstarred records; `is_stared` distinguishes them.

The payload is rich: `html_content` carries the **full question and answer text in Malayalam including the questioners' names as printed** (for id 2044: "ശ്രീ. ഇ. ടി. ടൈസൺ മാസ്റ്റർ … ശ്രീ. സി.സി. മുകുന്ദൻ", i.e. Sri E.T. Tyson Master and Sri C.C. Mukundan), and the HTML embeds per-question PDF links such as `http://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/images/s00091-100621-01-15.pdf`. The `file` field on unstarred records names a bulk PDF, e.g. `business_question__15__23__2021-06-10_60b8ce6b64b6a.pdf`. **The critical limitation for this project:** the member attribution is *prose inside the question text*, in Malayalam, with no structured member id or name field. There is no per-MLA question COUNT anywhere on the official site. Any per-MLA starred/unstarred tally would have to be derived by name-matching Malayalam honorific strings against the roster — a heuristic, not a sourced figure. One further handling note: passing the literal string `Select` as `department` to the unstarred endpoint triggers a raw PostgreSQL error (`syntax error at or near "Select"`, `models/Business_model.php` line 1437); passing an empty `department` works. The department must be a numeric id.

**Attendance: NOT published.** This is a clean negative. I grepped the sitting-member roster, `/index.php/content/member_analysis`, `/index.php/content/party_position`, the party-position page and the women-members page for the string "attendance" — **zero hits on every page**. No attendance figure, percentage or sitting-days record exists per member on the official site, in any format. Kerala does not publish MLA attendance.

**Private members' bills: effectively absent, with one genuine substitute.** The bills search UI at `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/index.php/bills/billview` does contain a private-member dimension — the page carries a hidden `<div id='private_bill_members'>` with a label "Introduced by Member" and a `<select id="member">` populated with member names (Abdul Hameed Master, Parakkal Abdulla, …), and `common.js` gates it behind `type == 2`. But the backing endpoint `POST index.php/bills/getdetails/<id>` with `kla`, `type`, `stat`, `member`, `category` and `csrf_test_name` returned **HTTP 200 with a 0-byte body for every combination I tried** (type 1 and 2 × KLA 15 and 16 × id 0 and 2). The static bill category pages `billview/1` … `billview/11` (Published, Introduced, Referred To, Passed, Assented, Withdrawn, Reconsidered, Withheld, Lapsed, Removed) each render a header row followed by **"No details"**. So the private-member-bills search is a UI shell with no data behind it. What *does* exist is **Private Member Resolutions**: `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/index.php/business/index/private_member_resolutions` returns a real HTML table (345 rows) with columns `Sl. No. | Date on which the resolution was adopted | Name of Member who moved the resolution | Text of Resolution (as adopted)`, grouped by assembly in Malayalam headings (e.g. "ഒന്നാം കേരള നിയമസഭ" for the 1st KLA). That is a per-member named record, but of resolutions, not bills, and the resolution text is Malayalam.

### Format note

A fetcher would need **four different techniques for four different sources**, and it is not a browser-only job: (1) the **profile roster is plain curl HTML** — one `<table>`, five columns, 140 rows, photos as ordinary JPGs at stable paths, no JS required; (2) the **questions data is a curl JSON API but POST-only with a CSRF handshake** — the fetcher must first GET the landing page, scrape the `csrf_test_name` token from an inline `onchange` attribute, retain a cookie jar, POST to `/index.php/bills/selectkla` for the session id, then POST again for the date list and again for each question's detail. A headless browser is not required, but a naive `curl <url>` is useless. Watch two traps: the unstarred endpoint returns a raw PostgreSQL 500 error page if `department` is the string `Select` (pass it empty or numeric), and the questions API returns `Content-Type: text/html` even though the body is JSON, so a content-type sniffing fetcher will mis-parse it; (3) the **election results are curl HTML tables** on `results.eci.gov.in` plus **text-extractable PDFs** on the CEO site (fonts present, no OCR needed) — the only wrinkle is that the ECI result URL is campaign-scoped and guessed carefully (`ResultAcGenMay2026/...`, state code `S11`), and `results.eci.gov.in/` itself auto-refreshes to whichever election is current, so the Kerala 2026 path must be hard-coded; (4) the **fund GOs split into two kinds** — the LSGD copy of G.O.(P) 60/2002/Fin is a normal text PDF extractable with any PDF library, while both CELSGD MLA GOs are **scanned images with zero embedded fonts** and need **OCR**. There is no fund data portal to fetch at all. No captcha, no login and no session cookie was needed for any source; the assembly site is a CodeIgniter app with CSRF protection whose backend is PostgreSQL (revealed by an error page), and its bills endpoint is POST-only and returns empty.

### Gap note

Three things are missing and **cannot be sourced officially**. First and most important, **there is no per-MLA development-fund data of any kind** — no allocated limit, no works recommended, no works sanctioned, no works completed, no expenditure, for any MLA, in any format, on any Kerala government domain. Kerala has the scheme (Special Development Fund for MLAs) and publishes its *rules* as GOs, and individual SDF works appear as tenders on the LSG portal, but the fund is administered by District Collectors through PD Accounts with no public reporting layer, so a per-MLA fund figure for Kerala cannot be honestly assembled from official sources. Second, **attendance is not published in any form** — not per member, not per sitting, not aggregate. Third, **private members' bills have no retrievable data**: the search UI advertises an "Introduced by Member" filter but its endpoint returns an empty body, and all eleven bill-status pages show "No details", so only private member *resolutions* (a different instrument) are available. Two secondary caveats: the starred/unstarred question data attributes questioners only as Malayalam honorific strings inside prose with no structured member id, so any per-MLA question tally would be a heuristic derivation rather than a sourced figure; and the Finance Department's own GO downloads are broken (JS redirect to an unreachable `infofinance.kerala.gov.in`), so the accessible official copy of the SDF GO depends on a single LSGD mirror.

### Platform

**Bespoke, not a shared template — and definitively not NeVA.** Kerala's site is its own application, evidenced by the footer on `https://www.niyamasabha.nic.in/`: *"This site is designed and hosted by [NIC logo] Kerala and maintained by IT Section Kerala Legislative Assembly"*, linking to `kerala.nic.in` — i.e. NIC provides hosting infrastructure, but the application is built and maintained by the Assembly's own IT Section. The markup is distinctive rather than template-shaped: a CodeIgniter app (`index.php/<controller>/<method>` routing), Bootstrap plus Highcharts/CanvasJS, ASP-era legacy components and `htm/main.asp` on the LSG tender portal, and hand-written `common.js` exposing bespoke endpoints (`bills/selectkla`, `questions/get_all_starred_questions_date`, `business/selectklamembers`) whose misspelling of `depaertment` in the SQL and JSON is a signature peculiar to this codebase. The decisive negative evidence is on NeVA's own portal: `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` renders a per-state status list, and its entry reads **"Kerala കേരള — Prospective — Yet to sign MoU"**, while many other states read "Onboarded", "Digital House" or "Live on NeVA Platform". Kerala also ships its own mobile app (`com.kla.eniyamasabha`, "eNiyamasabha - Kerala Legislative Business and information at your fingertips") distinct from the national `evmember.sbl.nationalevidhan` app. Practical consequence for this project: **there is no shared-platform multiplier here** — Kerala's assembly scraper is a one-state scraper, and no template reuse with other states can be assumed.

---

## Madhya Pradesh (IN-MP)

**Verdict:** partial - the Vidhan Sabha publishes an official, curl-fetchable member roster with names, parties, constituencies and photographs as bulk PDFs, but publishes NO per-member assembly record (no attendance at all), and there is NO official public portal for per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD fund figures.

### Profile
The official website of the Madhya Pradesh Legislative Assembly is **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/** (verified curl-200, `text/html`, 41,616 bytes, Hindi-language, server-rendered static HTML). Its footer states: *"Contents provided, updated and maintained by MP Vidhan Sabha Secretariat. Website designed and hosted by National Informatics Centre, Madhya Pradesh State Centre."* So the site is NIC-built but bespoke-coded, not a shared template.

Member data lives under the **सदस्यगण (Members)** menu, whose "वर्तमान" (current / 16th Vidhan Sabha, 2023–2028) entry is **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/16mlaprofile.htm** (curl-200). That index links to a set of bulk **PDF** downloads, not to per-member pages:

- **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/sadasyasuchi16vs.pdf** — the member list (734 KB; PDF 1.4, Producer `Skia/PDF m150` i.e. Chrome print-to-PDF; 61 `/Font`, 4 `/Type0` + `/Identity-H` with 4 `/ToUnicode`, 99 images). Text-layer PDF, so names/parties/constituencies are extractable; the embedded images are member photos.
- **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/AllMember16vs.pdf** — all members (1.3 MB; 28 `/Font`, 315 `/Image`) — text plus photographs.
- **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/mlaprofilevsxvi030826.pdf** — titled *"सोलहवीं विधान सभा के मान. सदस्यों के जीवन वृत्त"* (biographies of 16th Assembly members) — 2.97 MB, 416 `/Font`, **1,433 images** (the richest single photo source), Producer `Foxit PhantomPDF Creator 7.0.6.1107`. Partial `/ToUnicode` (4 Type0 fonts with Identity-H); Devanagari extraction is partial and may need OCR.
- **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/member_all.pdf** — 686 KB, 111 `/Font`, **0 images**, 5 `/Type0` with `/ToUnicode` (Producer `Foxit PhantomPDF Printer 7.0.6`) — pure text roster, no photos.
- Party-wise rosters, each also PDF: **BJP16vs.pdf**, **INC16vs.pdf**, **BAP16vs.pdf** (Bharat Adivasi Party); category rosters **SC16vs.pdf**, **ST16vs.pdf**, **Women16vs.pdf**, **firsttimer16vs.pdf**; plus **sadasyasuchi16vs.pdf**, **16thvs_bi-election.pdf** (by-election / deceased / resigned members) and **vsxvianalysis.pdf** (member analysis). Party position is also published as **JPG maps** at **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/16map.htm** (image-only, no text layer).
- Separate single-post PDFs exist for presiding officers and leaders: **speaker.pdf**, **opp_leader.pdf**, **minister.pdf**, **nominated.pdf**; the 16th Assembly cabinet is at **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/16mantrimandal.htm**.

**Is it queryable per member? No.** There is no per-MLA HTML page, no search box, no member-detail route and no JSON/XHR API on mpvidhansabha.nic.in. Every member artifact is a whole-Assembly bulk PDF (or JPG) that must be downloaded and parsed. Verification level for the roster/photo source: **curl-200** (fetched and inspected directly).

A second, NeVA-hosted assembly site exists at **https://mpla.neva.gov.in/** (curl-200). It advertises per-member routes — `/Member/Details/{id}`, and AJAX fragments `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail`, `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Member/Member_Notice` — but **the MP instance is onboarded and unpopulated for member records**: `/Member/Details/218` (200, 22,426 bytes) renders empty panels reading *"No Records Founds"* for Assurances, Debates and BILLS; `GET /Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=218` (200, 2,291 bytes) returns a shell whose `Name:` field is blank; `/Member/Member_Question` and `/Member/Member_Notice` both return *"No Records Founds"*. So NeVA currently adds **no** usable MP member data.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
The fund in Madhya Pradesh is officially the **विधायक स्थानीय क्षेत्र विकास निधि** — *Vidhayak Sthaniya Kshetra Vikas Nidhi*, commonly written **विधायक निधि** (Vidhayak Nidhi) in official and press usage. The Government of India records the scheme under the English name **MLALAD** (*Member of Legislative Assembly Local Area Development Scheme*).

**No official public per-MLA fund portal was found. This is a confirmed negative, not an unsearched gap.** Every plausible government hostname was tested and returns **NXDOMAIN**: `mlalad.mp.gov.in`, `mlaladapp.mp.gov.in`, `vidhayaknidhi.mp.gov.in`, `vidhayak.mp.gov.in` (all Status 3 at the `.gov.in` zone via Google and Cloudflare DoH on 2026-09-14). `mlalad.mp.gov.in` in particular does not exist.

The **only official government document that describes an MP MLA-fund system** is on the National e-Governance Division's India Stack Local directory (MeitY, Government of India): **https://negd.gov.in/isl/Directory/statedata/244** (curl-200, 103,345 bytes). It carries an entry titled **"MLALAD Application — MADHYA PRADESH"**, categorised *"Solution for Government Internal Functioning / Workflow automation"*, *Published By: SeMT*. It states the application "is a digital platform designed to facilitate the management and allocation of funds provided under the Member of Legislative Assembly Local Area Development Scheme (MLALAD) in Madhya Pradesh… enables legislators to propose, track, and monitor local development projects within their constituencies… from fund allocation and project approval to monitoring and reporting," with modules for proposal submission, approval, phased fund disbursement, project execution/monitoring and completion reporting. Implementing agency is listed as **MPSEDC** (Madhya Pradesh State Electronics Development Corporation); front-end Java, back-end SQL, hosted **on-premise**; contact *Vinay Pandey, Deputy Director, Vidhan Sabha, vinay.pandey@mp.gov.in*. Critically, the entry's **"Website Link" field is empty** — the NEGD record documents that such a system exists but publishes **no public URL**, and the module/use-case tables on the page are blank.

What the Assembly's own site publishes in this area is **not the Vidhayak Nidhi**: **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/vsgrant.htm** leads to **vsgrant16vs.htm** (16th Assembly) and **vsgrant15vs.htm** (15th Assembly), covering **स्वेच्छानुदान** (*swechchhanudan* — discretionary grant) at the disposal of the Speaker, Deputy Speaker and Leader of Opposition. That page is a UTF-16-encoded HTML table with only three columns — क्रमांक (serial), दिनांक (date), टिप्पणी (remark) — for 36 (Speaker) and ~60+ (Leader of Opposition) sanction dates between 2024 and 2026. It carries **no amounts, no MLA names, no constituency breakdown, no works recommended/sanctioned/completed and no expenditure**, and it is not the MLA local area development fund at all. It is aggregate-by-officeholder, not per-MLA.

No official scheme-rules page, government resolution or guidelines PDF for the MP Vidhayak Nidhi was found on any `.gov.in` / `.nic.in` domain. The homepage does carry a rules PDF, **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/876-V%20S%20Karya%20Sanchalan-2023%20(E).pdf**, but it is the *Madhya Pradesh Vidhan Sabha Procedure and Conduct of Business Rules 2023* (labelled on the homepage as "मध्यप्रदेश विधान सभा प्रक्रिया तथा कार्य संचालन संबंधी नियम"), **not** the MLA fund scheme. (PDF detail: 170 pages, PDF 1.7, 4 `/ObjStm`, Producer `Acrobat Distiller 7.0`, Creator `PageMaker 5.0`; inside the object streams there are 102–130 `/Font` entries and `Subtype/Type1` fonts with **no `/FontFile`** — non-embedded legacy typefaces and no `/ToUnicode`, so text extraction yields mojibake and OCR or a legacy font CMap is required. It is **not** a scan; only 2 `CCITTFaxDecode` images exist.)

The only description of the fund's current data situation is **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**: Dainik Jagran (https://www.jagran.com/madhya-pradesh/bhopal-mp-mla-fund-monitoring-new-software-for-online-tracking-40136411.html) reports that the Planning, Economics and Statistics Department is having MPSEDC build monitoring software for the *विधायक स्थानीय क्षेत्र विकास निधि*, that the scheme has been extended to FY 2030-31 at roughly ₹2,875 crore, that the annual entitlement is **₹2.5 crore per MLA** (with MLAs demanding ₹5 crore), and — the decisive statement — that *in the present arrangement there is no concrete system to determine how much of the fund an MLA has used*, because works sanctioned on MLA recommendation are cleared at district level and their reporting never reaches the state. That is consistent with the confirmed absence of any portal.

**Summary for the fund: the scheme exists and is named; a government-internal MLALAD application exists and is documented on an official Government of India page; but there is NO public, official, per-MLA (or even aggregate) fund figure source in Madhya Pradesh.**

### In the Vidhan Sabha
The Assembly publishes **sitting-date-wise** records, never member-wise ones. The hub is **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/house%20proceedings/hp.htm** (curl-200), which for the 16th Assembly (and 11th–15th) lists, per sitting day, four artifact families:

- **कार्यवाही (proceedings)** — `hpDDMMYY.htm` **and** `hpDDMMYY.pdf` (HTML plus PDF).
- **प्रश्नोत्तरी (questions & answers)** — `quesDDMMYY.htm` and `quesDDMMYY.pdf`. This is the **unstarred-question** answer set: verified `ques220726.pdf` is 7.6 MB, Producer `Microsoft Word 2013`, 372 `/Font`, 879 `/Image` — a text-layer PDF with heavy embedded imagery, extractable without OCR.
- **तारांकित प्रश्न (starred questions)** — `starDDMMYY.pdf`, **published**. Verified `star220726.pdf` (91,057 bytes; Producer `Skia/PDF m150` i.e. Chrome print-to-PDF; 4 `/Type0` + `/Identity-H` fonts with 4 `/ToUnicode`, 0 images) — a clean text-layer PDF, directly extractable.
- **शुद्धि पत्र (corrigenda)** — `spDDMMYY.pdf` (verified `sp220726.pdf`, 84,151 bytes, 46 `/Font`).

So **starred questions: yes** and **unstarred questions: yes**, both as PDFs, both **per sitting date and per question — with the name of the asking member inside the question text, but with no member index and no per-member aggregate**. You cannot ask the site "how many questions did MLA X ask"; you must download every `ques*` / `star*` PDF and attribute them yourself.

**Attendance: NOT published — nothing at all.** There is no attendance page, table, PDF or portal anywhere on mpvidhansabha.nic.in, and no equivalent on mpla.neva.gov.in (`/Member/Member_ProfileDetail` has no attendance field and returns blank). This is a hard, absolute gap. Indian media has reported the same for years (e.g. Navbharat Times, "मध्य प्रदेश के विधायकों की अटेंडेंस का कोई रेकॉर्ड नहीं") — **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**, cited only as corroboration of the absence found on the official site.

**Private members' bills: NOT published as such.** **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/legislation.htm** → **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/bill.htm**, which fans out to year-wise pages **bill_2014.htm … bill_2026.htm**. Those pages are titled *"The Bills Introduced and Passed by the Madhya Pradesh Legislative Assembly and Assented by Hon'ble Governor of M.P. / Hon'ble President of India"* and their columns are: bill number, bill name, **भारसाधक सदस्य (Minister-in-charge)**, department, date of introduction, date of discussion and passing, Governor's assent date, President's assent date, and Act number/year. Every introducer listed is a **Minister** — i.e. these are **government bills that became Acts**; each row links a `Bill No N.pdf` and an `ACT No N of YYYY.pdf`. There is **no private members' bills list, no defeat/withdrawal register, and no member-wise bill index**. (Note: strings containing "निजी" on these pages mean *private universities / private schools* in bill titles, **not** private members' bills.) As of the 16th Assembly, `bill_2026.htm` lists only 3 bills and `bill_2025.htm` 30 — the pattern is government legislative business only.

Adjacent record material that *is* published: **session statistics** at **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/stats/stat.htm** → **stat16vs.htm** … **stat11vs.htm** (a session-number/session-name index; per-session aggregate counts — Assembly-level, never member-level); **संक्षिप्त कार्य विवरण** (concise daily business) at **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/sanshipt/sankv.htm** → `s16vs.htm` … `s12vs.htm` with per-day `sDDMMYY.pdf`; and **pending assurances** at **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/ashwasan/pending_assurances.htm** (curl-200, 262 KB) with per-date `aswDDMMYY.pdf` files plus some "पूर्ण उत्तर" (complete answer) PDFs — again by date, not by member.
