### Format note
A fetcher can do a lot with plain `curl` here, but nothing is a JSON API. **mpvidhansabha.nic.in** is static server-rendered HTML with no login, captcha or JS requirement — all index pages (`16mlaprofile.htm`, `hp.htm`, `bill.htm`, `stat*.htm`, `sanshipt/*.htm`, `vsgrant*.htm`) are directly curl-able; watch for **UTF-16-encoded** pages (`vsgrant16vs.htm` decodes as UTF-16, not UTF-8) and for filenames containing **literal spaces** in URLs (`house proceedings/hp.htm`, `876-V S Karya Sanchalan-2023 (E).pdf`, `Election2023/2-List of Successful Candidates (2).pdf`), which must be percent-encoded. The payload is **PDF**: roster, biography, question and bill files are all PDFs. Most are text-layer PDFs and extract cleanly — `sadasyasuchi16vs.pdf`, `member_all.pdf`, `star220726.pdf` and `sp220726.pdf` were produced by **Chrome/Skia print-to-PDF** and carry `/Type0` + `/Identity-H` with `/ToUnicode`; `ques220726.pdf` is **Microsoft Word 2013** output; `mlaprofilevsxvi030826.pdf` is Foxit. Two exceptions need OCR rather than extraction: **`876-V S Karya Sanchalan-2023 (E).pdf`** (PageMaker 5 → Distiller 7, 170 pages, non-embedded legacy Type1 Devanagari fonts, **no `/ToUnicode`** → mojibake) and the party maps on `16map.htm` (bare JPGs, no text layer). Some older roster PDFs (`AllMember16vs.pdf`) show `/ToUnicode` count 0 despite being text PDFs, so a per-file probe is required rather than a blanket assumption.

**mpla.neva.gov.in** is ASP.NET MVC with jQuery: the shell is curl-able HTML and the per-member fragments are **plain GET XHRs** (`/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=N`) that work with curl, but they return empty for MP. **neva.gov.in** and **negd.gov.in/isl** are ordinary curl-able HTML. **ceoelection.mp.gov.in** is ASP.NET **WebForms**: the results pages are HTML with `ViewState`, several controls are postback-driven dropdowns, and some pages return `000`/empty on odd requests — but the result **PDFs** are direct GETs and are the reliable extraction target. Two real blockers: **https://www.eci.gov.in/ is a React SPA** (`<noscript>You need to enable JavaScript to run this app.</noscript>`, curl returns a 1,279-byte shell; data comes from relative `/api/*` endpoints discovered in `main.e468b53e.js`, and `old.eci.gov.in` is the static fallback), and **https://ceomadhyapradesh.nic.in/ does not resolve at all**, so any fetcher hitting the URL still published by ECI must fall back to `ceoelection.mp.gov.in`. **https://results.eci.gov.in/** is only useful during a live election: it currently redirects to the August 2026 by-election index and every Dec-2023 path returns 404.

### Gap note
Three things cannot be sourced officially and must be treated as documented gaps rather than data to be found elsewhere. **(1) Attendance does not exist in any form.** The Vidhan Sabha publishes no attendance figure, table or PDF for any member or any sitting, and the NeVA member profile has no attendance field. No amount of scraping will produce it; only an RTI to the Vidhan Sabha Secretariat (or the unofficial ADR analyses) could, and ADR publishes **question counts**, not attendance. **(2) There is no official per-MLA fund source.** The Vidhayak Sthaniya Kshetra Vikas Nidhi / MLALAD scheme is real, a government-internal **MLALAD Application** built by MPSEDC is documented on MeitY's own NEGD India Stack Local portal — and that page's *Website Link* field is **empty**; no `mlalad.mp.gov.in`-style host exists in DNS. No allocated limit, no works recommended / sanctioned / completed, and no expenditure figure is published per MLA (or even in aggregate) by the state. The Assembly's `vsgrant*.htm` pages are a different instrument (स्वेच्छानुदान for the Speaker, Deputy Speaker and Leader of Opposition) and publish **dates only, no amounts**. **(3) Per-member assembly activity cannot be aggregated officially.** Starred and unstarred questions are published as per-sitting-date PDFs and private members' bills are not published as a category at all — only **government bills that became Acts** appear in `bill_YYYY.htm`, keyed to the Minister-in-charge. So the site's own record cannot answer "how many questions did MLA X ask" or "which private members' bills did MLA X introduce". Finally, the ECI's own CEO-links page still points at a hostname that no longer exists in DNS, and the Dec-2023 assembly result sub-site on `results.eci.gov.in` has been retired (404) — the authoritative 2023 figures now live only on the state CEO's site as PDFs.

### Platform
Madhya Pradesh is a **common-platform** state for its *legislature* and **bespoke** only in its legacy website. The assembly is live on the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, NIC's "One Nation One Application" legislative workflow system, at **https://mpla.neva.gov.in/** — NeVA's own home page lists Madhya Pradesh with status **"Onboarded"** and links exactly that host, and the page carries the footer **"© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India"**. The evidence of a shared template is concrete and machine-checkable: NeVA allocates one hostname per house on the pattern `*.neva.gov.in` (`mpla`, `mhla`, `hpvs`, `gujarat`, `bla`, `arla`, `apa`, `chg`, …); MP's instance and Maharashtra's instance (**https://mhla.neva.gov.in/**) both load **`/2024/css/style.css`**, expose the **identical AJAX endpoint set** (`/Home/CalenderSession`, `/Home/EventVideos`, `/Home/GetNevaImplemantationStatus`, `/Home/ChangeInEngCulture`, `/Home/ChangeInLocalLanguage`) and carry the identical copyright line, differing only in a state-prefixed CMS host (`cmsmpla.neva.gov.in` vs `cmsmhla.neva.gov.in`) over the shared `cms.neva.gov.in`. MP's own Assembly published the procurement on its site — **https://mpvidhansabha.nic.in/mpvseoineva080825.pdf**, *"MODEL SPECIFICATIONS for Selection of Managed Service Provider (MSP) (System Integrator)… at Madhya Pradesh Vidhan Sabha, Bhopal for NeVA Implementation"* — and NIC's NeVA template even leaks across states in markup (the MP member page's sidebar rendered a hardcoded *"eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"* label). One scraper written against the NeVA member/questions/bills routes therefore generalises to every onboarded house — **but not usefully for MP today**, because MP's per-member NeVA content is empty (`No Records Founds`) and the real, populated profile data still sits on the bespoke, NIC-MP-hosted **mpvidhansabha.nic.in**, which shares its template with no other state.

---

## Maharashtra (IN-MH)

**Verdict:** partial - The Maharashtra Legislature runs its own official site at https://mls.org.in/ publishing a text-extractable Assembly roster PDF (name / party / constituency, no photo) and PDF-only starred/unstarred question lists, but there is NO photo roster, NO private-members'-bills publication, attendance only for 2014-2017 as a scanned PDF, and NO official public per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi fund portal.

### Profile

The official website of the Maharashtra Legislature (Vidhan Sabha + Vidhan Parishad) is **https://mls.org.in/** (verified curl-200, `text/html; charset=utf-8`, 56,468 bytes; page title "Maharashtra Legislature"; footer "Copyright © 2025 Vidhan bhavan | legal disclaimer / Designed and maintained by MAHARASHTRA LEGISLATURE SECRETARIAT"). It is a single bespoke ASP.NET WebForms site (`.aspx` URLs, `aspnetForm`, `__VIEWSTATE`, `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR`, `zone1` content placeholder) serving both Houses.

There is **no HTML member table, no searchable member portal and no JSON/XHR API**. The member roster exists only as two PDF downloads, reached from the "विधिमंडळ सदस्य" → "विधानसभा" navigation item, whose landing page `https://mls.org.in/list-of-mla.aspx` (curl-200) contains nothing but two links:

- Current (15th) Assembly: **https://mls.org.in/PDF2025/15-Assembly-24-11-2024.pdf** - verified curl-200, `application/pdf`, 613,125 bytes, PDF-1.7, **234 `/Font` entries and 0 `/Image` entries across 19-20 pages → a genuinely text-extractable PDF**. It is the Election Commission of India gazette notification No. 308/MH-LA/2024 dated 24 November 2024 (Election Commission of India, Nirvachan Sadan) republished as Maharashtra Government Gazette असाधारण भाग चार-क. Extracted text shows a clean three-column schedule: `AC No. | AC NAME | NAME OF ELECTED CANDIDATE | PARTY AFFILIATION (IF ANY)`, running the full 288-seat list (page 2 sample: "1 Akkalkuwa (ST) Amshya Fulji Padvi Shiv Sena", "2 Shahada (ST) Rajesh Udesing Padvi Bharatiya Janata Party"). Note the schedule's numeric AC number and English names extract cleanly, but the Marathi-language portions of the gazette extract with doubled/garbled matras (e.g. "महाारााष्ट्रट शाासन" for "महाराष्ट्र शासन") because the PDF embeds a legacy non-Unicode Devanagari font - a real extraction blocker for Marathi strings.
- Previous (14th) Assembly: **http://mls.org.in/PDF2024/budget/list-of-mla.pdf** - verified curl-200, `application/pdf`, 43,524,593 bytes, PDF-1.7, with **187 `/Font` and 5,645 `/Image` entries → image-heavy / partially scanned**, consistent with a photo-annotated roster but not machine-parseable without considerable image handling.

Party-strength breakdown is likewise a **scanned** PDF: **https://mls.org.in/PDF2023/MONSOON/pakshnihay-yadi.pdf** (curl-200, 1,534,402 bytes, **0 `/Font`, 2 `/Image`** - i.e. pure bitmap, zero extractable text).

**Term / assembly number** is stated in the document titles ("१५ वी विधानसभा" = 15th Assembly). **PHOTO: not published.** No official Maharashtra government or legislature page was found that carries per-MLA photographs. The Chief Electoral Officer's own "Legislative Assembly Members" navigation item does not host a roster at all - it simply links back out to `http://mls.org.in/PDF2025/15-Assembly-24-11-2024.pdf` (extracted from `https://ceoelection.maharashtra.gov.in/ceo/`, curl-200), confirming the CEO has no independent member dataset. Verification level for the roster: **curl-200, fetched and content-confirmed**.

There is also an internal, non-public legislative system: **http://103.209.38.132/els/login.htm** (also mirrored at `http://115.242.60.172/els/login.htm`) returns a "Vidhan Mandal | लॉगिन" username/password login page (curl-200, `text/html;charset=utf-8`, 14,120 bytes) with the footer "Copyright © 2021 Maharashtra Knowledge Corporation Ltd. All Rights Reserved", plus a notice to use Mozilla Firefox and Unicode fonts. This is the members-only "ELS" system through which Maharashtra MLAs file questions and notices online (Maharashtra was reportedly the first state to accept Assembly questions online). It is **not a public data source**.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund in Maharashtra is officially the **आमदार स्थानिक विकास कार्यक्रम** ("MLA Local Development Programme", commonly abbreviated **स्थाविका**), popularly called **आमदार निधी / Vidhayak Nidhi**. It is administered by the **नियोजन विभाग (Planning Department)** under budget head **4515 0012**. This is confirmed from the primary GR text itself (GR क्रमांक स्थानिका-0422/प्र.क्र. 53/का-1482, dated 21 April 2022), which reads: "आमदार स्थानिक विकास कार्यक्रमांतर्गत सन 2022-2023 या आर्थिक वर्षाकरिता रु. 1468.00 कोटी एवढी तरतूद करण्यात आली आहे" and cites the accounting head "(01) (00) (01) आमदार स्थानिक विकास कार्यक्रम (45150012)". A Zilla Parishad page independently names it "स्थानिक विकास (आमदार निधी) कार्यक्रम".

**There is no official public per-MLA fund portal. This is the single largest gap in this jurisdiction.**

- The state's MLA/MLC fund tracking system is **e-Samarth**, launched around late August 2025 and piloted in Pune district. Per contemporaneous reporting it is an internal departmental workflow: MLAs upload proposals, scrutiny is digitised, and funds move by direct transfer. **No official e-Samarth URL could be found.** Direct probing of plausible hosts (`esamarth.maharashtra.gov.in`, `samarth.maharashtra.gov.in`, `mla.maharashtra.gov.in`, `nidhi.maharashtra.gov.in`) produced DNS/connection failures (`000`), and **`https://esamarth.in/` resolves to a parked domain** returning a 114-byte stub that redirects to `/lander` - it is **NOT an official government resource** and must not be cited. The Maharashtra IT Corporation site `https://mahait.maharashtra.gov.in/` (curl-200) contains no e-Samarth link.
- What **is** published officially is the **scheme rulebook and allocation orders**, not per-MLA outcomes:
  - Guidelines: **https://gr.maharashtra.gov.in/Site/Upload/Government%20Resolutions/Marathi/201607051539392716.pdf** - "आमदार स्थानिक विकास कार्यक्रम - सर्व समावेशक मार्गदर्शक तत्वे" (comprehensive guidelines), नियोजन विभाग, Mantralaya. Verified curl-200, `application/pdf`, 3,402,861 bytes, **36 pages, 164 `/Font`, 28 `/Image` → text PDF**, but the Marathi text extracts with the same legacy-font corruption (विकास → "निकास").
  - Annual allocation: **https://gr.maharashtra.gov.in/Site/Upload/Government%20Resolutions/Marathi/202204211327394516.pdf** - GR क्रमांक स्थानिका-0422/प्र.क्र. 53/का-1482 dated 21 April 2022, sanctioning and distributing the 2022-23 provision. Verified curl-200, `application/pdf`, 150,240 bytes, 3 pages, 66 `/Font`, 12 `/Image` → text PDF with the same encoding caveat.
- The Government Resolution portal itself is **https://gr.maharashtra.gov.in/1145/Government-Resolutions** (curl-200). It is an ASP.NET WebForms index (`frmMOLSite`, `__VIEWSTATE`/`__EVENTVALIDATION`, `WebForm_OnSubmit`) with a department dropdown (`ctl00$SitePH$ddlDepartmentType`, option `16` = नियोजन विभाग) and a **mandatory CAPTCHA field** (`ctl00$SitePH$txtimgcode`). Its own site-search endpoint (`/Site/Search?searchText=...`) returns only "Error !!!". The portal footer credits **"महाआयटी द्वारा विकसित"** (developed by MahaIT).

**Aggregate-only** fund context exists in Planning Department publications (`https://plan.maharashtra.gov.in/`, curl-200, whose Annual Plan documents state "MLA/MLC Local Area Development Programme is being implemented in the State since 1984-85"), and in the state treasury/budget system, but neither is per-MLA and neither reports works recommended / sanctioned / completed / expenditure per legislator. Reporting has noted the state itself lacks consolidated records of LAD money given to MLAs and MLCs, which is consistent with the absence of any publication.

Conclusion: the scheme exists and is well documented as a **scheme**, with per-MLA *allocations* implied by GR; but **no official portal publishes per-MLA allocated limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure**. The national MPLADS analogue (https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/) has **no Maharashtra state-level equivalent**.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Attendance - published, but only for one defunct period, as a scan.** The navigation link "सदस्यांची सभागृहातील उपस्थिती (२०१४ ते २०१७)" points to **https://mls.org.in/attendance.aspx**, which was fetched (curl-200, 31,130 bytes) and found to be **an empty template** - the page body contains no attendance data at all, only the site chrome and footer; the sole substantive outbound link is the PDF **https://mls.org.in/pdf%202018/monsoon%202018/assembly%202014-17%20attendance.pdf**. That PDF is **27,316,995 bytes with 0 `/Font` and 78 `/Image` entries → a scanned image PDF requiring OCR**, and it covers **only 2014-2017** (14th-Assembly-era, partly). A parallel Council file exists at `pdf 2018/monsoon 2018/council 2014-17 attendance.pdf`. **There is no attendance data for the 2019-2024 or 2024-2029 Assemblies, and nothing per-member in any machine-readable form.**

**Starred questions - published as PDFs, per sitting, not indexed per member.** Index page **https://mls.org.in/Starred_list_Assembly.aspx** (curl-200, 18,761 bytes) lists session-wise sub-pages running from "सन २०१४ चे हिवाळी अधिवेशन" through "सन २०२६ चे अर्थसंकल्पीय अधिवेशन". It links onward to per-session pages, not directly to PDFs.

**Unstarred questions - published as PDFs, per list number.** Index page **https://mls.org.in/atarankit-yadi-assembly.aspx** (curl-200, 25,172 bytes) enumerates sessions back to 2015; each session page enumerates numbered lists. Example session page **https://mls.org.in/atarankit-yadi-assembly-monsoon-2026.aspx** (curl-200) exposes 10 PDFs at paths such as `unstarred list/monsoon-session-2026/unstarred_question_yaadi_37 - final.pdf`, `..._42.pdf`, `..._46.pdf`. A sample was downloaded (list 37, monsoon 2026: curl-200, `application/pdf`, 734,359 bytes, PDF-1.5, 43 pages, **102 `/Font` and 129 `/Image` → mixed text/image, text extractable**). Extracted content confirms the structure: each entry carries a question number, the **questioning MLA's name with constituency in parentheses**, the date, the minister addressed, and the full question and answer - e.g. "श्री.चेतन तुपे (हडपसर)", "डॉ.नितिन राऊत (नागपूर उत्तर)", "श्री. प्रकाश आबिटकर (०७-०४-२०२६)". So the data is **member-identifiable but organised by question, not by member**, and there is no member index; building a per-MLA question count means parsing every list PDF across every session. Marathi text extraction from these PDFs is partly corrupted by the same font/ToUnicode issue (e.g. "प्रश्नोत्तराांची" for "प्रश्नोत्तरांची"), so the member name strings will need normalisation or OCR.

**Private members' bills - NOT published.** The legislature publishes legislation only under "विधेयके (विधानसभा)", i.e. **शासकीय विधेयके (government bills)**, at **https://mls.org.in/Vidheyake_Assembly-2026.aspx** (curl-200) with year pages 2021-2026 split into "पुरः स्थापित केलेली विधेयके" (introduced) and "संमत केलेली विधेयके" (passed). A direct string search for **अशासकीय** ("non-official" = private members') across the Assembly page, the Legislature-Members page and the bills page returned **zero occurrences**. No list of private members' bills exists on the official site.

**Proceedings.** Day-wise verbatim proceedings (पत्रक भाग दोन) are at **https://mls.org.in/PatrakBhag_Sabha_W.aspx** (curl-200, 202,802 bytes) listing **396 PDF links** with paths like `PDF2024/budget/PATRAKBHAG2/01-ASSEMBLY.pdf`. A sample (`01-ASSEMBLY.pdf`) was downloaded: 23,542 bytes, `/Font 2`, `/Image 4`, 1 page, and text extraction produced **unusable garbage** ("atlsqa,t0 r|:p,gnl4|E ...") - i.e. effectively image-based, **OCR required**. The older proceedings search/archive linked from the site (`http://103.204.167.130/` for "कार्यवृत्त १९३७-२००३" and `http://183.87.89.162/dissearch.php` for "चर्चा") **both timed out and returned no content** - they are offline or unreachable.

**Explicitly NOT published:** per-member attendance for any current term; per-member question counts or a member-question index; any private members' bills data; MLA photographs; and any live/queryable roll of members (the only machine-readable roster is a point-in-time gazette PDF).

### Format note

A fetcher needs **four different pipelines and cannot rely on any single one**. (1) **Curl + HTML**: the mls.org.in index/navigation pages are plain server-rendered ASP.NET HTML and fetch cleanly, but they hold almost no data - they are link directories. (2) **Curl + PDF text extraction** for the highest-value artefacts: the 15th-Assembly roster (`PDF2025/15-Assembly-24-11-2024.pdf`) and the CEO gazette elected list are true text PDFs with hundreds of font objects and zero images, and the numbered AC / English name / party columns parse cleanly; the unstarred-question list PDFs are mixed text+image and also extract. The universal blocker in this class is **legacy non-Unicode Devanagari font encoding**: extracted Marathi comes out with doubled matras and consonant substitutions (`विकास` → `निकास`, `महाराष्ट्र` → `महाराष्ट्रट`), so Marathi strings need either a ToUnicode remap, a font-aware extractor (e.g. pdftotext with a proper CMap) or a Unicode normalisation pass - while ASCII member names and AC numbers are reliable as-is. (3) **Headless browser** for `https://www.eci.gov.in/`, which is a React SPA serving a 1,279-byte `<div id="root">` shell with a `<noscript>You need to enable JavaScript</noscript>` notice; `https://results.eci.gov.in/` is lighter (a meta-refresh redirect) but its Nov-2024 assembly folders now return **HTTP 404**, so the 2024 assembly result must be taken from the CEO Maharashtra pages instead. (4) **OCR** for the scanned class: assembly attendance 2014-2017 (27.3 MB, **0 fonts, 78 images**), party-wise strength (**0 fonts, 2 images**), the 14th-Assembly list (43.5 MB, 5,645 images) and the day-wise Patrak Bhag proceedings (236 KB sample with unusable extracted text). Finally, the **GR portal** at `gr.maharashtra.gov.in` is a genuine hard blocker: ASP.NET WebForms with `__VIEWSTATE` / `__EVENTVALIDATION`, a **POST-only** search submit (`ctl00$SitePH$btnSearch`) and a **mandatory CAPTCHA** (`ctl00$SitePH$txtimgcode`) - it cannot be scraped by curl without session + CAPTCHA handling, so individual GR PDFs (whose URLs are guessable only from their numeric IDs, e.g. `201607051539392716.pdf`) are the practical route. Two further endpoints linked from the official site - `http://103.204.167.130/` and `http://183.87.89.162/dissearch.php` - **did not respond at all**, so the historical proceedings search is currently unavailable rather than merely awkward.

### Gap note

**No official public per-MLA fund data exists for Maharashtra.** The Vidhayak Nidhi / आमदार स्थानिक विकास कार्यक्रम scheme is real, well-funded (Rs 1,468 crore for 2022-23) and documented through GRs and guidelines, but nothing official publishes per-MLA allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure. The state's tracking system, **e-Samarth**, is an internal departmental workflow with **no discoverable public URL** (and `esamarth.in` is an unrelated parked domain), so the Maharashtra MPLADS-equivalent simply does not exist publicly. Alongside that: **MLA photographs are not published** on any official page found; **private members' bills are not published at all** (only government bills); **attendance exists only for 2014-2017 as a scanned PDF** and every more recent term is missing entirely; **questions are published only as per-list PDFs** organised by question rather than by member, so no per-member starred/unstarred count can be read off directly; and there is **no queryable member roster or API** - the current roster is a single point-in-time gazette PDF, which will silently go stale after any by-election (e.g. the bye-elections to Maharashtra Assembly constituencies running in 2026) unless re-downloaded. The 14th-Assembly roster and the party-strength table require OCR. Finally, note a **provenance trap**: the "Legislative Assembly Members" link on the Chief Electoral Officer's own site points back at the legislature's PDF rather than to any CEO-hosted dataset, so those two sources are not independent corroboration of one another.

### Platform

Maharashtra's legislature site is **bespoke, not a shared multi-state template**. The decisive evidence is a Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs / PIB release dated **17 July 2026** stating that the Maharashtra Legislature only "expressed its commitment to adopt the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)" and that a meeting at Vidhan Bhawan concluded with "a consensus to expedite the signing of a Memorandum of Understanding ... paving the way for the early implementation of the project" - i.e. as of September 2026 **NeVA is not implemented in Maharashtra**, so `mls.org.in` is not an NeVA instance and no scraper can be shared with NeVA states. Corroborating signals: the site is hand-rolled ASP.NET WebForms on a bare domain (`mls.org.in`, not a `*.nic.in`/NeVA host) with `zone1`-style content placeholders, hand-written Marathi nav markup and hard-coded PDF year folders (`PDF2023/`, `PDF2024/`, `PDF2025/`, `PDF2026/`); its footer credits **"Designed and maintained by MAHARASHTRA LEGISLATURE SECRETARIAT"** with **no NIC, no CDAC and no vendor credit**; and its internal legislative workflow system (ELS) is a **state-PSU product, "Maharashtra Knowledge Corporation Ltd" (MKCL)**, not a national platform. By contrast, the state's *executive* surfaces do sit on common platforms - `plan.maharashtra.gov.in` and `zppalghar.gov.in` carry the footer **"Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre"** and run NIC's S3WAAS/WordPress template (shared `wp-content`, `document-category` taxonomy, `cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in` asset host), while `gr.maharashtra.gov.in` is credited **"महाआयटी द्वारा विकसित"** (built by MahaIT). **Net effect for the atlas: legislative scraping is state-specific; only the executive/department layer is template-shareable.**

---

## Manipur (IN-MN)

**Verdict:** partial - Manipur has a functioning unicameral legislature whose official site publishes a complete 60-member roster with per-MLA profile pages and photos, Starred and Unstarred question listings, and a bills register, but it publishes NO attendance data anywhere, NO per-MLA development-fund data on any government domain (the MLADF fund exists but has no public portal), and its "Unstarred Questions" page is a 99.99%-text-identical duplicate of the Starred Questions page.

### Profile

Manipur is a unicameral legislature: the **Manipur Legislative Assembly** (Manipur Vidhan Sabha), currently the **Twelfth** Assembly, 60 Assembly Constituencies. Its official website is **https://assembly.mn.gov.in** - curl-fetched with a browser User-Agent, HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=UTF-8`, 57,781 bytes, `<title>Home | Manipur Legislative Assembly</title>`. The domain `assembly.mn.gov.in` is a genuine state-government host (not a `.nic.in` subdomain but a government-operated `.gov.in` portal).

What the site publishes for members, all verified by fetching:

- **Member roster - `/members`** (HTTP 200, 89,186 bytes, `<title>Members of 12th Manipur Legislative Assembly</title>`). It yields **60 distinct per-member profile URLs** of the form `/members/profile/{slug}` (e.g. `/members/profile/okram-ibobi-singh`, `/members/profile/nemcha-kipgen`). The same 60 are mirrored at `/members/list-of-members`. Companion pages: `/members/former-members` (HTTP 200), `/members/party-position` (HTTP 200, "Party Position of the 12th Manipur Legislative Assembly"), `/members/panel-of-chairmen` (HTTP 200), `/members/women-members` (HTTP 200, **5** member profiles). The homepage additionally carries per-party seat counts as `<title>` fragments for BJP, Naga People's Front, Janata Dal (United), Independent, National People's Party, Indian National Congress, Kuki People's Alliance and "Vacant".
- **Per-member profile fields** - fetched `/members/profile/okram-ibobi-singh` (HTTP 200, 39,509 bytes). The page renders: breadcrumb "Member of Twelfth Manipur Legislative Assembly"; **Name**; **Constituency** (e.g. `31-Thoubal`); **Political Party** (e.g. `Indian National Congress`) plus the party logo image; **Date of Birth**; then "Additional Details" holding **Father's Name**, **Place of Birth**, **Permanent Address**, **Present Address**, **Educational Qualification**, **Email**, **Contact** (mobile), and a long **Position Held** career chronology (for Ibobi Singh: 1984 election as Independent, ministerial posts, three terms as Chief Minister, 2017 Leader of the Opposition, 2022 re-election to the 12th Assembly). Term/assembly number is therefore present in the page and in the roster title ("12th"), not as a structured year field.
- **Photo - YES, per member.** The profile renders `<img src="/user/pages/images/members/31_O Ibobi.webp">` (the filename is prefixed with the constituency number). Fetched `https://assembly.mn.gov.in/user/pages/images/members/31_O%20Ibobi.webp` -> HTTP 200, `image/webp`, **45,882 bytes**. Party logos live at `/user/pages/images/parties/{inc|bjp|...}.webp`.
- **Format** - plain server-rendered **HTML**, one static page per member, plus WebP images. There is no JSON/XHR API and no search endpoint; the roster is a static list. A machine-readable `sitemap.xml` is served (HTTP 200, 19,265 bytes, **106** URLs) and enumerates every member profile slug, which is the cleanest way to discover the roster.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund in Manipur is **not** called "Vidhayak Nidhi". The name used in Manipur is **MLADF - "MLA Local Area Development Fund"** (occasionally written "MLA LAD"), which is the state's analogue of the central MPLADS scheme.

**No official portal found. There is no state-level equivalent of https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/ for Manipur**, and no government page publishing per-MLA allocation, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure. This was tested negatively against three official targets rather than simply not searched:

- **assembly.mn.gov.in** - the string "local area development", "MLADF", "MLALAD", "vidhayak" and "nidhi" appears **nowhere**. Every navigation page was fetched and grepped (`/`, `/members/list-of-members`, `/secretariat`, `/rules-and-directions/handbook-for-members`, `/rules-and-directions/resume-of-business`, `/committee`, `/contact-us`, `/budget-documents`, `/resolutions`, and a notification page) with zero hits, and `sitemap.xml` (106 URLs) contains **no** URL matching `attend|fund|lad|nidhi`.
- **manipur.gov.in** (the state portal) - the built-in site search returns **no matching articles** for `?s=MLADF`, `?s=MLA+fund`, or `?s=MLA+Local+Area+Development` (all HTTP 200, empty result sets). The only fund-related page surfaced by that search is the unrelated "Manipur state illness assistance fund" (`/?page_id=1772`).
- **planningmanipur.nic.in** - does not resolve at all (curl exit 0 / no connection), so the Planning Department, which is the usual nodal department for MLALAD-type schemes in other states, has no reachable website for Manipur.

The fund's existence is attested only by **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification** press: PTI via Business Standard ("Manipur MLAs to get Rs 25 lakh under MLADF to fight COVID-19"), a manipur-update.com report that the Governor disapproved a road improvement work under the MLA Local Area Development Fund, and e-pao.net commentary on MLADF allocation. None of these is a government source, none publishes per-MLA figures, and no scheme-rules page, government resolution or guidelines PDF for Manipur's MLADF could be located on any government domain. Accordingly `mlalad_per_mla` is **none** and the portal URL is **not found** - which is itself the finding: the scheme exists as a state disbursement but is entirely opaque at the per-MLA level.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

- **Starred questions: PUBLISHED, per member, machine-readable.** `/business/questions-and-answers/starred-questions` (HTTP 200, 133,001 bytes, `<title>Starred Questions</title>`). The table columns are **Starred Question No. | MLA concerned | Date of answer in the House | Minister & Department | Subject**, and each record additionally carries the verbatim **"Question:"** text, the **"Answer:"** text, and a **Download** link with a file size. The MLA is identified by name **and constituency number** (e.g. "Surjakumar Okram / MLA, 35-Khangabok"), so the data is genuinely per-member. Counting across all pages: **99 question records**, **98 unique question PDFs**, spanning **32 distinct sitting dates** from 2022 to 2026. PDFs sit at `/user/pages/files/business/questions-answers/starred/Q{n}.pdf` (Q2-Q100).
- **Pagination is a URL path segment, not a query string**: `/business/questions-and-answers/starred-questions/page:{n}`. Pages **1-4** carry real data (25, 23, 19, 25 rows); pages **5 and above return HTTP 200 but are empty stubs** (49 KB of chrome with no question rows) rather than 404, so a scraper that pages until 404 will loop forever - it must stop on empty.
- **Unstarred questions: the page exists but is a DUPLICATE of the starred listing.** `/business/questions-and-answers/unstarred-questions` returns HTTP 200 with `<title>Unstarred Questions</title>`, four pages of data, and its own PDF directory `/business/questions-answers/unstarred/Q{n}.pdf`. However, a character-level diff of the rendered text of all four starred pages against all four unstarred pages gives a **similarity ratio of 0.9999** with only **4 differing blocks, none longer than 25 characters** - and every one of those blocks is a file-size label. The question numbers, MLA names, constituencies, sitting dates, ministers, departments, subjects, question wording and answer wording are **byte-identical**. The unstarred page even retains the column header **"Starred Question No."**. The two pages are therefore not two distinct datasets: the HTML listing is one dataset rendered twice, and only the downloaded PDF differs by directory. Treating them as separate starred/unstarred registers would double-count. Coverage of the roster is also thin - the question corpus names only **11 distinct MLAs/constituencies** out of 60.
- **Short notices: systematically absent.** `/business/questions-and-answers/short-notices` (HTTP 200) renders the single word **"Coming Soon"** with no data.
- **Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED.** There is no attendance page, no attendance module, and no per-member attendance figure. The string "attendance" does not occur in any page fetched from the assembly site, and `sitemap.xml` contains no attendance URL. This is a hard gap, not a format problem.
- **Private members' bills: PARTIAL.** `/bills` (HTTP 200, 1,083,497 bytes, `<title>Bills</title>`) is a large HTML table - **610 `<tr>` rows** - with columns **Sr. No. | Name of the bill(s) | Introduced on | Passed on | Assented on | Remarks | Download**, and it is a register of **bills passed by the Assembly** going back to 1972, with per-bill PDFs. It does capture private members' legislation at least once - row text includes "The Manipur Official Language (Amendment) Bill, 2006 (**Private Members' Bill** introduced by Shri O. Joy Singh, MLA)" - and it records non-passage outcomes such as "Withdrawn by the Mover" and "Referred to the Select Committee on 17th January 1974". But there is **no per-member private members' bills register** and no filter by mover; a member's private-member bill record can only be reconstructed by text-searching the mover's name in this one table.
- Also published (not member-attributed): `/proceedings` (per-sitting PDFs offered in three scripts - **Manipuri Meiteilon**, **English Translate**, **Meitei script** - indexed by date from 24 March 2022 onward), `/business/list-of-business` (per-sitting List of Business PDFs), `/business/papers-laid`, `/bulletin` (Bulletin Part I PDFs, `/user/pages/files/bulletin/part-i/No {n}.pdf.pdf`), `/acts`, `/ordinances`, `/resolutions`, `/budget-documents`, `/administrative-reports`, `/committee`.

### Format note

A fetcher needs **three different techniques**, and the roster is the easy part. (1) **Assembly profile and question data: plain `curl` GET works, no browser, no session, no cookie, no captcha.** `assembly.mn.gov.in` is server-rendered HTML behind a Grav flat-file CMS; a single GET with an ordinary browser User-Agent returns the full markup. Discover the roster from `sitemap.xml` (106 `<loc>` entries, member slugs included) rather than screenscraping `/members`. Paginate questions with path segments `/page:{n}` and **stop on an empty result, not on a 404** - pages 5+ return HTTP 200 with no data rows. The real blocker in the assembly data is not fetching but *semantics*: the Unstarred page duplicates the Starred page text at 0.9999 similarity, so any pipeline must dedupe on (question number, sitting date, MLA) or it will silently double-count, and the QA PDFs are **mixed-format** - of six sampled, four (`starred/Q2`, `Q3`, `Q5`, `Q10`) have **zero `/Font` objects** and are pure image scans needing **OCR**, while two (`unstarred/Q1`, `unstarred/Q2`) carry text layers, and file sizes range from 168 KB to 14.8 MB. (2) **Election results**: the CEO Manipur per-AC Form 20 PDFs are text-extractable (`01.pdf`: 15 `/Font`, 3 pages) so `pdftotext` suffices, but the same office's flagship **Statistical Report 2022 is an 82 MB scan with 0 `/Font` and 193 `/Image` objects - full OCR over 82 MB**. Cross-check against ECI: `old.eci.gov.in` is plain curl-able HTML, but the modern `www.eci.gov.in` is a **React SPA requiring a headless browser** (`<div id="root">`, "You need to enable JavaScript to run this app"), and although `www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/election-result?category_id=N` is a curl-able JSON API needing **no auth header**, its categories 1-12 cover only 2024-2025 elections - `get-ac-election-details?iYear=2022&st_code=S14` returns `"total_count":0`, so **ECI's JSON API cannot serve Manipur 2022 at all**. (3) **NeVA routes**: the Manipur NeVA instance is unreliable - `/Members` 404s, `/Bill` `/Business` `/Debates` `/Committee` silently redirect to the central national page (a 192,345-byte response that looks like success but contains no Manipur data), `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList` returns **HTTP 500**, and `/Questions` **timed out past 60 seconds**. Net: `mixed` - mostly curl HTML plus PDF text extraction, with OCR required for the Q&A PDFs and the 2022 statistical report, and one headless-browser need if ECI's SPA is used.

### Gap note

What cannot be sourced officially, stated plainly: (a) **Any per-MLA development-fund figure whatsoever.** There is no allocated limit, no works recommended, no works sanctioned, no works completed and no expenditure figure for any individual Manipur MLA on any government domain. The state's fund is called MLADF but has no portal, no scheme-rules page, no government resolution and no published allocation register; the state portal's own search returns nothing for "MLADF", "MLA fund" or "MLA Local Area Development", the Assembly site never mentions the fund, and the Planning Department domain does not resolve. Manipur MLAs' fund activity is knowable only from journalism, which fails the provenance standard. (b) **Attendance - in any form, for any member.** No attendance page exists, the word does not appear on the site, and the sitemap has no attendance URL. (c) **A trustworthy unstarred-questions register.** A page exists and serves distinct PDFs, but its listing text is a 99.99% duplicate of the starred listing, so starred/unstarred cannot be distinguished from the HTML - the distinction is only recoverable (if at all) by opening and OCR'ing ~98 PDFs per set. (d) **Question coverage of the full roster.** The question corpus names only 11 of 60 members, so most MLAs have no documented question record; and `short-notices`, an entire published questions sub-module, is empty ("Coming Soon"). (e) **A per-member private members' bills register.** Private members' bills are present only as rows inside a 610-row bills-passed table; there is no filter, index or per-member view, so a member's PMB record is not officially queryable. (f) **A working NeVA instance.** Manipur is nominally a NeVA state with a live subdomain, but that instance serves a 404 for members, HTTP 500 for the shared member AJAX route, and a >60 s timeout for questions - so no cross-state NeVA scraper can extract Manipur through it. (g) **An official 2022 result set on ECI's modern infrastructure.** ECI's JSON API and results portal do not carry Manipur 2022; the 2022 official record is the CEO Manipur per-AC Form 20 PDFs and an 82 MB scanned statistical report.

### Platform

Manipur's operational assembly website is **BESPOKE, not a shared template** - and this is the notable finding, because Manipur is on the official list of NeVA states. The real data lives at `assembly.mn.gov.in`, which is a **Grav flat-file CMS** with a hand-built theme: assets are served from the Grav content tree (`/user/themes/mla/css/app.css`, `/user/pages/images/members/...`, `/user/pages/files/business/...`), the markup is Tailwind-style with arbitrary-value selectors leaking into class attributes (`[&.active>a]:decoration-3 [&.active>a]:text-slate-800`), URLs are semantic slugs (`/members/profile/okram-ibobi-singh`, `/business/questions-and-answers/starred-questions/page:3`), and the footer carries **no NIC or vendor credit at all** ("Copyright © 2026 Manipur Legislative Assembly. All Rights Reserved.", contact e-mail a `gmail.com` address). By contrast, Manipur *does* have a NeVA subdomain, `https://manipur.neva.gov.in/` (HTTP 200, 52,194 bytes), titled "Welcome to **Manipur** Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application", with the standard footer "National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India" - and the shared-platform evidence is concrete and reproducible: the **`/2024/css/style.css` file is byte-identical across states** (MD5 `5681e25a3a6114cc92501eace9e97aff` on manipur, nagaland, tripura and sikkim), the title pattern is the same modulo the state name, and each instance carries the same 20 `/2024/` asset references and the same 9 top-level nav routes. **But for data extraction the NeVA route is a dead end for Manipur**: `/Members` returns 404, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Debates` and `/Committee` all redirect to the central `neva.gov.in` page, `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=4&MSLT=0&Assemblyid=0` - the shared AJAX route that works for Arunachal - returns **HTTP 500**, and `/Questions` timed out. Only `/ContactDirectory` (15,538 bytes) and `/Home/RulesAndDirection` (15,424 bytes) return Manipur-specific content. The assembly site's own footer lists "NeVa Manipur" under Related Links but with `href="#"`, so it does not even link to its instance. **Conclusion: one NeVA scraper will NOT serve Manipur; Manipur needs its own scraper** against a bespoke CMS whose platform is shared with no other state observed.

---

## Meghalaya (IN-ML)

**Verdict:** partial - Meghalaya has a fully official MLA profile source (both a bespoke assembly site and an NIC-hosted NeVA instance, both publishing name/party/constituency/photo and per-member question records), but the MLA-area development fund has NO public per-MLA data portal, and attendance is not published anywhere.

### Profile
Meghalaya has a unicameral legislature, the Meghalaya Legislative Assembly (60 seats, currently the Eleventh Assembly). Two distinct OFFICIAL websites publish member data, and they are not the same system.

(1) **`http://megassembly.gov.in/`** - the Assembly's own bespoke site (Laravel + Vue). Note it is reachable over **HTTP only**: the TLS handshake on port 443 fails outright (`curl: (35)`), so `https://megassembly.gov.in/` cannot be fetched at all. Its member roster is a plain JSON file, `http://megassembly.gov.in/storage/members/members.json` - verified curl-200, `application/json`, 27,111 bytes, **60 records**, one per sitting MLA. Each record carries: `name`, `party_name.party` + `party_name.short` (party split verified: NPP 33, UDP 12, AITC 5, VPP 4, HSPD 2, BJP 2, IND 2 = 60), `constituency` (e.g. "1- Nartiang (ST)"), `district`, `email` (filled for 47 of 60), and `image` (a photo path - **all 60 records have an image**, e.g. `/storage/Shri Sniawbhalang Dhar.jpg`; verified curl-200 `image/jpeg` 19,554 bytes, 512x700 JPEG). Two fields are empty in every record: `biodata` is `"-"` for all 60 and `phone` is `"-"` for all 60. The file is queryable per member only by filtering the whole 60-record array client-side; there is no per-member URL. Term/assembly number is **not** a field in this file - it must be inferred, because the companion file `http://megassembly.gov.in/storage/eLibrary/ex_member.json` (curl-200, 58,269 bytes) is keyed by house number `"01"` through `"10"` and holds **614 ex-members** (name, party, constituency; no photo, no district). So `members.json` = the Eleventh Assembly, and 1-10 are the historical houses. The file's own `updated_at` timestamps are current (2026-02-13), so the roster is maintained.

(2) **`https://mgla.neva.gov.in/`** - the Meghalaya instance of the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA), hosted by NIC. Its member directory lives at `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, with party-wise filtered views (`?MLT=4&MSLT=53` = NPP, etc.) and a data endpoint `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`. The returned fragment is HTML cards, each with the MLA's **photo** (served from the shared NeVA CMS, e.g. `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/ML/FileStructures//Member/thumb/03_23_2023_06_40_42.jpg`), name, party, constituency + district, and a link to a per-member page `/Member/Details/{id}?AssemblyID=11`. That per-member page is **queryable per MLA** and adds father's name, email, mobile, party and constituency via `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId={id}&AssemblyID=11` (verified for MemberId=104: name, father's name, email, mobile, party, constituency all returned). NeVA's member count agrees with the bespoke site: 60 members, same party split (NPP 33, UDP 12, AITC 5, VPP 4, HSPDP 2, BJP 2, IND 2). The per-member NeVA page exposes tabs for General / Questions / Notice / Assurances / Committee / Debates / BILLS, but only General, Questions, Notice and Committee have working handlers - the Assurances, Debates and BILLS tabs have no `onclick` and render a static "No Records Founds" placeholder, so they are non-functional.

Verification level: both profiles sources are **curl-200, fetched and parsed**. `megassembly.gov.in` is official (Government of Meghalaya); `mgla.neva.gov.in` is official (NIC / Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs NeVA instance, linked from the official `https://neva.gov.in/` portal).

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
**No official per-MLA fund data portal exists. Not found.** Meghalaya does not call this fund "Vidhayak Nidhi", "Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi" or "MLALAD". There is no Meghalaya equivalent of the MPLADS portal.

What does exist is scheme documentation showing that two state schemes are **MLA-discretionary**, but publishing nothing whatsoever about money per MLA:

- **Special Rural Works Programme (SRWP)** - official scheme page at `https://meghalaya.gov.in/schemes/content/37331` (curl-200, fetched). The description states the schemes "are of general in nature and are **selected by the members of the Legislative Assembly (MLA)** and implemented through the Local Dorbars of villages." Scheme Beneficiaries: Individual, Community, Others. Sponsor: State. The district-level mirror of the same text at `https://easternwestkhasihills.meghalaya.gov.in/scheme/srwp/` (curl-200, fetched) adds "Beneficiary: Identified by Local MLAs", "Benefits: Local Area Development", "How To Apply: Village communities may seek the help of local MLAs to avail this scheme."
- **Chief Minister's Special Rural Development Fund (CMSRDF)** - official scheme page at `https://meghalaya.gov.in/schemes/content/37322` (curl-200, fetched). Description: "The schemes undertaken in the programme are varied in nature and are **selected by the Members of the Legislative Assembly (MLA)** and NGOs and are implemented through local durbars and beneficiaries organization under the supervision of respective Deputy Commissioners of the District."

Neither page publishes any figure - no allocation, no works recommended, no works sanctioned, no works completed, no expenditure, not per MLA and not even in aggregate. Both pages carry a "Click here to View/Download" link, but **no downloadable document exists on either page** (zero `.pdf` / `.doc` hrefs found in the fetched HTML). The nominal scheme-rules source, the Community & Rural Development Department site `http://www.megcnrd.gov.in/`, is broken: its homepage returns a bare directory listing ("manual", "public_html"), it has no usable navigation, and the one SRWP guidelines PDF that search engines index, `http://www.megcnrd.gov.in/forms/srwp.pdf`, now returns **404**. Money figures for the Meghalaya MLA scheme appear only in press reporting (e.g. disputes about MLA scheme implementation covered by the Shillong Times and India Today NE), which is UNOFFICIAL and is not a usable provenance source.

Conclusion: the scheme exists and is explicitly MLA-directed, but **the fund is not published per MLA in any form** - no portal, no PDF, no aggregate table. This is a genuine documented gap, not a data-format problem.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
**Starred and unstarred questions: YES, per-member, official.** On the NeVA instance, `/Member/Member_Question?MemberId={id}&AssemblyID=11` returns an HTML table with the exact columns `Sr No | Question Number | Question Type | Date | Minister | Subject | Document`, where **Question Type is literally "Starred" or "Unstarred"** (both values confirmed present in the returned markup) and `Document` links to the question PDF on the shared NeVA CMS (e.g. `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/ML/FileStructures///PaperLaid/11/9/11_9_US_4241_V1.pdf`). Verified: MemberId=50 (Dr. Mukul Sangma, Leader of Opposition) returned **285 table rows** (232,954 bytes) covering the current sessions; MemberIds 34, 36, 102 and 104 returned "No Records Founds". So the per-member question record is real and curl-fetchable, but **coverage is partial** - only members whose questions have been ingested into NeVA have rows. Separately, the Assembly's bespoke site publishes whole-house session PDFs, not per-member records: `http://megassembly.gov.in/storage/eLibrary/question.json` (curl-200, 60,259 bytes) is a JSON index keyed by session code (e.g. `112026BS`), each entry a sitting date plus a PDF path, and for recent sessions the **filename itself carries the classification** - `2026-02-17_question_(Starred Question).pdf` vs `2026-02-26_question_(Unstarred Question).pdf` - with a numeric `question_type_id` where **1 = Starred and 2 = Unstarred** (confirmed by cross-checking filename against id across 63 session keys). Older sessions have `question_type_id: null`. Companion session indexes on the same site: `bulletin.json`, `lob.json` (List of Business), `calendar.json`, `budget_speech.json`, `governor_address.json`, and `proceeding.json` - but note `proceeding.json` (debates) is **stale**: its latest entries are from 2014, so Assembly debates are not published for the current house. The NeVA instance also publishes session documents (Starred/Unstarred Question lists and answers, Advanced List of Starred/Unstarred Questions, List of Business, Bulletin Part-I, notifications) as PDFs under `cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/ML/FileStructures//Notices/`.

**Private members' bills: NOT published.** The NeVA bills module at `/Bill` offers a Bill Type filter whose options are exactly `Government Bill` and `Private Members Bills`, and a status filter of `Assented / Passed / Pending / Withdrawn / Negatived / Lapsed / Introduced`, across all 11 assemblies. However querying the search endpoint `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch` with `BillTypeId=2` (Private Members Bills) returned **"No Records Founds"** - and so did `BillTypeId=1` (Government Bill) for both AssemblyID=11 and all assemblies, on repeated attempts with a warm session. No private members' bill record is retrievable. The per-member BILLS tab is likewise a non-functional placeholder. Assembly-level counts do appear on the NeVA landing page ("Government Bills 189"), but the underlying listing endpoint returned empty to direct requests.

**Attendance: NOT published. This is a hard gap.** The string "Attendance" appears **zero times** across the NeVA Meghalaya questions page, bills page, member directory and member detail page, and NeVA has no attendance module. Neither site publishes member-wise attendance, and there is no NPAT/PRS-style substitute from an official source.

### Format note
A fetcher needs **different handling per source, and one of them is actively hostile to simple HTTP**. The Assembly's bespoke site is the easiest: `members.json`, `ex_member.json` and the `eLibrary/*.json` session indexes are static JSON files with no auth, no cookies and no JavaScript - plain `curl` returns them directly. Its one serious trap is **that HTTPS does not work at all** (port 443 handshake fails); a fetcher must use `http://`, which also means the roster is fetched in cleartext. Its question/bulletin/LOB PDFs are **scanned images**: the sampled starred-question PDF (451,781 bytes, 18 pages) is built from **JBIG2** bitonal page images with an **ABBYY FineReader 12** OCR text layer on top, and the text is stored as subset TrueType glyphs requiring `/ToUnicode` mapping (48 CMap references) - so plain grep on decompressed streams yields mojibake, and extraction needs a real PDF library, not naive `zlib` + regex. The NeVA instance is the opposite: it is a plain ASP.NET MVC app returning **HTML fragments, not JSON**, and every deep URL **302-redirects to `https://mgla.neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` unless a session is warmed first** - the fetcher must GET the homepage to obtain `ASP.NET_SessionId` and set the `UnitCode`, then request the deep page with a cookie jar and a Referer, otherwise every endpoint silently returns the generic 192,345-byte NeVA landing page. Its per-member question table is then ordinary HTML and parses cleanly. The CEO Meghalaya sources are straightforward HTML plus genuine **text** PDFs (the AC Result Report is Microsoft Excel 2016-generated with 25 `/Font` objects; the per-AC Form 20 sheets likewise carry real fonts), so normal PDF text extraction works. `old.eci.gov.in` is static HTML. Only the modern **`eci.gov.in` needs a headless browser** - it is a React single-page app (`<div id="root">`, `/static/js/main.e468b53e.js`, "You need to enable JavaScript to run this app"), and its data comes from `/api/*` JSON endpoints (e.g. `/api/get-ac-election-state`, `/api/assebly-election-innerpage`) which would need to be reverse-engineered. No captcha, no POST-only form, and no ZK/JSF framework were encountered anywhere in Meghalaya. The one historical-archive limitation: `results.eci.gov.in` does **not** serve the 2023 assembly results any more - all probed `ResultAcGenMar2023/` paths return 404, as it only hosts the current election.

### Gap note
Three things are missing and cannot be sourced officially. (1) **The development fund is the biggest hole**: Meghalaya's MLA-directed schemes (SRWP and CMSRDF) have official scheme-description pages but **no per-MLA allocation, works-recommended, works-sanctioned, works-completed or expenditure figures anywhere** - no portal, no PDF, no aggregate table, and the department's own site is broken with its guidelines PDF returning 404. Fund amounts surface only in press reporting, which cannot satisfy a provenance requirement. (2) **Attendance is not published at all** - NeVA has no attendance module, the string appears nowhere across either assembly site, and there is no official per-MLA attendance source for Meghalaya. (3) **Private members' bills are empty**: the NeVA bills module defines a "Private Members Bills" bill type and a full status taxonomy, but querying it returns "No Records Founds" for every assembly, and the per-member BILLS tab is a dead placeholder - so no private member's bill record is retrievable. Two smaller caveats: current-assembly **debates are not published** (the bespoke site's `proceeding.json` stops in 2014, and NeVA's per-member Debates tab is non-functional), and the bespoke roster's `biodata` and `phone` fields are empty for all 60 MLAs, with no date-of-birth available on NeVA either (the DOB row renders blank). Finally, **consolidated official assembly results for 2023 are no longer on the ECI results portal** (404 on all probed archive paths), so the citable official record is the CEO Meghalaya PDFs, not a live ECI results URL.

### Platform
Meghalaya runs a **hybrid: an NIC common platform plus a separate bespoke site**. The common platform is unambiguous and is the important reusable finding - Meghalaya is a live instance of the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, built by NIC with the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs, at `https://mgla.neva.gov.in`, listed on the official NeVA portal `https://neva.gov.in/` as "Digital House Meghalaya - Digital House Live on NeVA Platform" among 38 `<state-code>.neva.gov.in` sibling instances (apa, arla, asla, bla, chg, dla, goa, gujarat, hpvs, hrla, kerala, kla, klc, manipur, mgla, mhla, mhlc, mizo, mpla, nagaland, odisha, puddu, pvs, raj, sikkim, tnla, tripura, tsla, tslc, uplc, upvs, utkh, wbla, ...). The strongest concrete evidence that this is one product and not a coincidental resemblance: Meghalaya's instance and **Nagaland's instance share byte-identical asset paths** (`/2024/css/style.css`, `/2024/css/bootstrap.min.css`, `/2024/js/jquery.min.js`, `/2024/js/bootstrap.bundle.min.js`) and **identical route names** (`/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Debates`, `/Home/NeVA`), and both serve member photos and question PDFs from the single shared CMS host `cms.neva.gov.in` under `NeVA/{STATE}/FileStructures/`. The footer of every Meghalaya NeVA page reads verbatim "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India" with "Content managed by: Meghalaya Legislative Assembly", and the page even carries a cross-state link to "eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh". **One scraper written against the NeVA template therefore serves Meghalaya and all the other onboarded Houses**, with only the state path segment and the `<state-code>` subdomain changing. By contrast, `megassembly.gov.in` is **bespoke** - a Laravel application (`csrf-token` meta tag, `/js/app.js`, `/js/vue.js`) with hand-written Vue components (`<vip desgn_code="HSP">`, `<house-detail>`, `<table-of-content>`, `<footer-vue>`) and its own private JSON storage convention under `/storage/`, sharing nothing with NeVA.

---

## Mizoram (IN-MZ)

**Verdict:** partial - Mizoram has a functioning unicameral legislature (9th Mizoram Legislative Assembly, 40 seats) whose official site publishes a complete 40-member roster with per-MLA profile pages, photos and party affiliation, and whose legislative record is carried on the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) instance at mizo.neva.gov.in which exposes PER-MLA Starred and Unstarred question lists; but there is NO attendance data anywhere, NO statewide per-MLA development-fund portal (MLALADS works data is scattered across individual district websites with no consolidated allocation or expenditure figure), and the 2023 assembly election result link on the CEO site is broken.

### Profile

Mizoram is a unicameral legislature: the **Mizoram Legislative Assembly** (Mizoram Vidhan Sabha), currently the **Ninth** Assembly, 40 Assembly Constituencies. Its official website is **https://www.mizoramassembly.in** - curl-fetched with a browser User-Agent, HTTP 200, `text/html; charset=utf-8`, 52,702 bytes, `<title>Mizoram Legislative Assembly</title>`. Note the domain is a plain `.in` (not `.gov.in` or `.nic.in`); it is nonetheless the genuine official assembly site, evidenced by (a) the NeVA Mizoram portal `mizo.neva.gov.in` footer reading "Content managed by: Mizoram Legislative Assembly", (b) the site's own footer linking to NeVA, the National Portal of India, the GOI Web Directory and the State Portal of Mizoram, and (c) the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs listing Mizoram among the NeVA "Digital Houses" (PIB release 2294959, 5 Aug 2026).

What the site publishes for members, all verified by fetching:

- **Member roster - `/members`** (HTTP 200, 94,099 bytes). It yields exactly **40 distinct per-member profile URLs** of the form `/members/details/{id}` (ids 15, 16, 19, 20, 24, 30, 43, 51, 56-87) - i.e. the full 9th Assembly, one card per sitting member, each with a portrait thumbnail and the constituency rendered as `18-Aizawl South I`. There is **no ex-members page** (the string "ex-members" does not occur), so the roster is current-members-only.
- **Per-member profile fields** - fetched `/members/details/79` (HTTP 200, 45,077 bytes). The page renders the member's name, **constituency** (`18-Aizawl South I`), **Father's Name**, **Date of Birth**, **Place of Birth**, **Present Address**, **Permanent Address**, **Educational Qualification**, **Marital Status**, **Name of Spouse**, **Number of Children**, **Political Party Affiliation** (`Zoram People's Movement`), **Hobbies**, **Special Interests**, **Social Activities/Experience**, **Countries Visited**, **Date of Election** (`2023-12-03`) and **Election Period** (`5 Years`). The **term / assembly number** is present as page chrome ("9th Assembly") rather than as a structured per-member field.
- **Photo - YES, per member.** Portraits are served from `https://mizoramassembly.in/storage/members/January2024/{hash}.jpg` (e.g. `/storage/members/January2024/bxQnKSrK5E5a3Zff8oLQ.jpg` for C. Lalsawivunga). Some roster cards instead use `/storage/images/{uuid}.jpeg`. Filenames are opaque hashes, so photos are discoverable only by parsing the roster HTML.
- **Format** - plain server-rendered **HTML**, one page per member, no JavaScript required. There is **no JSON/XHR API and no search endpoint** for members; the roster is a single static list and the only per-member key is the numeric id in the URL.
- **A second, machine-friendlier roster exists on NeVA.** `https://mizo.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList` (HTTP 200, 79,812 bytes, requires a session cookie) returns the whole roster in server-rendered HTML: name, **party**, **constituency**, date of birth and a "View more" link. `https://mizo.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=77` returns a compact HTML fragment with Name / Party Name / Constituency Name / State Name; `https://mizo.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/54` renders the same plus a **photo hosted at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/MZ/FileStructures//Member/{file}.jpg`**.
- The NeVA Mizoram **MemberList** control lists exactly **40 members with ids** (49, 51-88), confirming the 40-seat house and giving a stable per-MLA key for the questions API.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund in Mizoram is **not** called "Vidhayak Nidhi". Its official name is **MLALADS - "Member of Legislative Assembly Local Area Development Scheme"**, written **MLA-LADS** on the district web pages. The scheme has been operational since **2005-06** and its governing document is **"GUIDELINES OF MLALADS (Revised on 2023)"**, issued by the **Government of Mizoram, Planning & Programme Implementation Department, Aizawl**, consolidating notifications No.H.12020/1/05-PLG (3 June 2013), No.H.12020/1/05-PLG (21 November 2019), No.H.12020/1/05-PLG (30 March 2022), No.A.60011/8/2018-GAD/Pt (17 January 2023), No.H.12020/2/2022-PLG (18 January 2023) and No.B.16012/2/2023-LESDE/5 (21 February 2023). That PDF is at **https://planning.mizoram.gov.in/uploads/attachments/2024/02/bcadb653693a36e21c4b0c0c315cb788/pages-113-mla-lads.pdf** (HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, 430,389 bytes, 17 pages) and is a **text PDF** - 70 `/Font` objects against 3 `/Image`, so it extracts (with kerning artefacts).

**There is NO statewide per-MLA MLALADS portal. There is no Mizoram state-level equivalent of https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/.** No consolidated page publishes per-MLA allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure for all 40 constituencies. Instead the works data is **decentralised across individual district websites**, each organised by assembly constituency and financial year:

- **Mamit district** - the richest instance. `https://mamit.nic.in/1-hachhek-st-ac/` (HTTP 200, 441,704 bytes) holds **seven** year-sections for 1-Hachhek (ST) A/C covering **2019-20, 2020-21, 2021-22, 2022-23, 2023-24, 2024-25 and 2025-26**, each a works table with columns **Sl/No | Name of Works | Executing Agency | Amount Sanction | Remarks** (later years relabel the money column `Total Amount(Rs)`), e.g. `1 | Clearing of Khawthar place at Luimawi | VC Luimawi | 20,000 | Complete`. `https://mamit.nic.in/2-dampa-st-ac/` (HTTP 200, 140,311 bytes) does the same for 2-Dampa. `https://mamit.nic.in/mlalad-fund-utilization/` is the index listing the constituency pages 1-Hachhek, 2-Dampa and 3-Mamit, and nests them beside **MPLADS** ("WORKS TAKEN UP UNDER MLALADS / MPLADS").
- **Kolasib district** - `https://kolasib.nic.in/mla-lads-fund/` (HTTP 200, 116,294 bytes), titled "Detail of Works under MLA-LADS fund in the District". A three-column table **NAME OF ASSEMBLY CONSTITUENCY | NAME OF MLA | DETAIL OF WORKS**, grouped by financial year **2023-2024** and **2024-2025**, listing `4 - TUIRIAL / K LALDAWNGLIANA`, `5 - KOLASIB / LALFAMKIMA`, `6 - SERLUI / LALRINSANGA RALTE` each with a "Get Details" link.
- **Saitual district** - `https://dcsaitual.mizoram.gov.in/page/works-under-mla-lads` (HTTP 200, 1,415,247 bytes) is the same **NAME OF ASSEMBLY CONSTITUENCY | NAME OF MLA | DETAIL OF WORKS** table for financial year **2024-2025**, covering `8 - CHALFILH / LALBIAKZAMA` (cell reads in Mizo: "Aizawl District khawih a nih avangin details a awm lo" - *no details, because it is executed by Aizawl District*), `9 - TAWI / Prof. LALNILAWMA` (links to a detail page) and `21 - LENGTENG / F.RODINGLIANA` (empty cell). The linked page **https://dcsaitual.mizoram.gov.in/page/mlalads-9-tawi-2024-2025** (HTTP 200, 1,542,686 bytes) is a genuine per-MLA works table titled "MLALADS, 9-TAWI (2024-2025)" with columns **Sl. No | Implementing Agency | Name of Works/GIA | Total Amount**, e.g. `3 | President, Mualpheng Branch YMA | Construction of YMA Office Building at Mualpheng | 500000`.
- **Lawngtlai district** - `https://lawngtlai.nic.in/mz/detail-of-works-under-mla-lads-fund-in-the-district-mz-2/` (HTTP 200, 156,138 bytes) publishes per-constituency sections headed with **both the constituency and the MLA's name**, e.g. `36- Tuichawng (Name of MLA- Rashik Mohan Chakma)`, with columns **S.No. | Name of Works | Implementing Agency | Amount | Remarks**. The **Remarks** column is the most informative found anywhere in the state: values include **Completed**, **On-going**, **Not Complete**, and explanatory notes such as `Final payment (half of the total amount`. A second section covers Lawngtlai West Development Committee works.
- **Serchhip district** - `https://dcserchhip.mizoram.gov.in/page/mlalads` (HTTP 200, 1,174,876 bytes) indexes `MLALADS 26-Serchhip`, `MLALADS 27- Tuikum`, `MLALADS 28-Hrangturzo`, plus a historical page `/page/list-of-recommended-works-under-mla-lad-scheme-26-serchhip-st-ac-from-2015-2016`, and nests them beside MPLADS (Rajya Sabha and Lok Sabha).

**What is published is per-work, not per-MLA-total.** Every district page gives a list of individual works with an amount sanctioned/approved and a completion status. **No government page anywhere publishes the MLA's annual allocated limit**, nor a single consolidated "expenditure" figure per MLA, nor "works recommended" as distinct from "works sanctioned". The guidelines prove those figures are *administratively maintained*: the mandated reporting format at **Annexure III** of the guidelines is a per-constituency utilisation return reading *"Fund received from the constituency (Rs. in lakh): A. Balance of Fund carried forward from the previous year; B. Fund received during the year; C. Amount of interest Accrued; D. Total (A+B+C); E. Total cost of work sanctioned; F. Total cost of work unsanctioned (D-E); G. Actual expenditure; H. Total fund available with the District Administration (D-E)"*, signed by the Deputy Commissioner. Section 3.4 of the guidelines further requires release of the second instalment "on the basis of information furnished regarding progress of utilization of fund and physical achievements in the format placed at Annexure III", and section 3.5 requires an implementing agency to display a **pink sign board bearing name of MLA, year of construction, name of work, sanctioned amount and name of Implementing Agency**. So the data exists as a routine return to the **Nodal Department (Planning & Programme Implementation Department)** but is **not published**. The per-constituency release mechanism is described in the same guidelines: "The total fund earmarked for the financial year for each constituency shall be released to the concerned Deputy Commissioner immediately after the vote on Accounts/Budget is passed or shall be released in two equal installments"; an advance of 75% may be sanctioned as first instalment, with the balance on production of a Utilization Certificate. The guidelines set no fixed rupee entitlement per MLA. A **Mizoram State Project Monitoring Dashboard (MPLAN)** and an **SDG Dashboard** were inaugurated on 28 October 2024 (DIPR release 299/2024-2025), and the SDG dashboard is reachable at https://sdg.mizoram.gov.in/ (HTTP 200) - but neither is an MLALADS per-MLA publication and neither was reachable at a mplan/dashboard hostname.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

- **Starred questions: PUBLISHED, PER MEMBER, machine-readable on NeVA.** `https://mizo.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Question?MemberId={id}` returns an AJAX HTML fragment with columns **Sr No | Question Number | Question Type | Date | Minister | Subject | Document**. Fetched `?MemberId=77` (Jeje Lalpekhlua) -> HTTP 200, **66 table rows (65 questions + header)**, containing both types, e.g. `1 | 145 | Unstarred | 28/08/25 | Vanlalhlana | Artahkawn - Biate kawng chungchang` and `3 | 4 | Starred | 27/08/25 | Vanlalhlana | East Lungdar - Leng kawng chungchang`. The list is genuinely member-attributed and current to the 7th Session of the 9th Assembly. Coverage is uneven: `?MemberId=54`, `?MemberId=60` and `?MemberId=58` returned **"No Records Founds"**, so only members who actually filed questions have rows.
- **Unstarred questions: PUBLISHED, PER MEMBER** via the same endpoint - the `Question Type` column distinguishes `Starred` from `Unstarred`, and the NeVA `/Questions` page exposes separate left-nav entries for **Starred Question**, **UnStarred Question**, **Short Notice Question** and **Inter Session Questions**. The underlying fragment endpoint is `https://mizo.neva.gov.in/Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem={n}&Type={n}` (HTTP 200, 60,227 bytes), whose Advanced Search offers **Select House** (9th / 8th Assembly), **Select Session** (Seventh Session down to 1st Session), **Session Date**, **MemberList**, **DepartmentList** and a free-text **Subject** field. The **MemberList** select contains all **40 MLAs with ids**, so questions are directly queryable per MLA. The related search endpoints are `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionBySearch`, `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionAdvBySearch`, `/Questions/FetchUnListQuestions` and `/AssemblyDocs/QuestionSubjectSearch`.
- **The assembly's own site publishes questions only as session bundles, not per member.** `https://www.mizoramassembly.in/question` (HTTP 200, 73,149 bytes) is an HTML table with columns **# | Date | Period | Assembly | Session | Remarks | Action**, filtered by Period = State / Union Territory / District Council, e.g. `1 | 27-08-2026 | State | 9th Assembly | 7th Session | With answer | Download`. Each row's Download points to a session question PDF such as `/storage/questions/August2026/QSTN-01M0T7EWNXTX352YXYKDHPG4R2.pdf`. **There is no member column and no member filter on this page** - the MLA's identity appears only inside the PDF body (the fetched 7th Session starred-question PDF names the asker, e.g. "Pu JEJE LALPEKHLUA ... 10-Aizawl West III"). That question PDF is **1,970,837 bytes** and, despite raw `0 /Font` markers, inflates to **658 `/Font` objects** with 41 text-bearing content streams (31,742 extractable characters) - so it is a **digital text PDF, not a scan**, though the Mizo-language portions are CID-encoded (`<0030002C003D0032...>`) and need the embedded `ToUnicode` CMap to resolve.
- **Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED.** The string "attendance" occurs **zero times** on every page I fetched from the assembly site (`/members`, `/proceeding`, `/bulletin`, `/government-bill`, `/list-of-business`) and **zero times** on the NeVA Mizoram member pages, including the member detail page, whose tabs are only General | Questions | Notice | Assurances | Committee | Debates | BILLS. There is no attendance page, no attendance module and no per-member attendance figure on any Mizoram government domain. This is a hard gap, not a format problem.
- **Private members' bills: PARTIAL.** `https://www.mizoramassembly.in/private-bill` (HTTP 200, 55,085 bytes) is an HTML register with field rows **Title of the bill | Department Concern | Submitted by | Date of Introduction | Passing Date | Date of Sending to Law Deptt for obtaining assent | Bill No & Year | Date of Assent by Lt. Governor/Governor/President**, and it **is** member-attributed - the record reads `Submitted by: Dr. VANLALTHLANA` for "The Mizoram (Land Revenue) (Amendment) Bill, 2023". It is filterable by Period (State / Union Territory / District Council). However the register is **very thin: only 2 private-bill PDFs appear** (`/storage/private-bills/February2023/7aiSrjkCqkllWfEmw0Rj.pdf` and `/storage/private-bills/October2022/429wbtKF6DwEIoSNvqkX.pdf`), so it is not a usable per-member private-members'-bill record. The fetched bill PDF is a **SCAN** - 0 `/Font`, 2 `/Image`, `/DCTDecode`, 145,529 bytes, one page. On NeVA, `/Bill` (HTTP 200, 35,008 bytes) offers **Select House**, **Select Session**, **Bill Category** and **Select Year** (2011-2026), but the Bill Category list is **Money Bill | Financial Bill | Ordinary Bill | Constitution Amendment Bill | Ordinance Replacing Bill** - there is **no "Private Member Bill" category**, and the string "private member" occurs zero times.
- **Also published (not member-attributed):** `/proceeding` (per-sitting proceedings), `/bulletin` (Bulletin Part II/III, e.g. "Bulletin Part - II (No. 149) - Prorogue the Seventh Session of the Ninth Mizoram Legislative Assembly - 14/09/2026"), `/government-bill` (HTTP 200, 110,567 bytes), `/list-of-business`, `/resolution`, `/committee-reports`, `/gazette`, `/governor-speech`, `/budgetspeech`, `/calendar`. The assembly's `/election` page is about **biennial elections** (notice of nominations, Form 7B contesting candidates, counting supervisors) - **not** general assembly election results.
