### Format note

A fetcher needs a **mixed** toolchain, and the single hard blocker is the assembly site's own two-page split. **curl + HTML parsing** covers almost everything valuable: the 40-member roster and every per-MLA profile (`www.mizoramassembly.in/members`, `/members/details/{id}`), the private-bills register (`/private-bill`), all five district MLALADS works pages (Mamit, Kolasib, Saitual, Lawngtlai, Serchhip), the MLALADS guidelines PDF (**text PDF**, `pdftotext`-extractable), and the CEO results index pages. Two extra requirements attach to the NeVA instance: `mizo.neva.gov.in` sub-pages return the **national** NeVA shell unless a session cookie is carried, so a fetcher must first GET `/` and keep the `ASP.NET_SessionId` cookie; after that the per-MLA payload arrives as an HTML fragment from `GET /Member/Member_Question?MemberId={id}` and `GET /Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem={n}&Type={n}` (both plain GETs, no POST, no captcha, no token). **OCR is required** for the statutory election results: the CEO Form 20 district PDFs are 29-page, 300dpi 2476x3508-pixel `/DCTDecode` scans with no meaningful text layer, so 2013/2018/2021 assembly results cannot be read without OCR. **PDF text extraction works for the assembly question PDFs** (658 fonts, real text streams) but the Mizo-language spans are CID-encoded and need the embedded `ToUnicode` CMap to decode, while the private-bill PDFs are pure scans. **A headless browser is required only for `www.eci.gov.in` and `mizoram.gov.in`**, which are JavaScript SPAs that return an identical ~1 KB shell for every path; `results.eci.gov.in` itself is plain curl-able HTML. Finally, the district MLA-LADS pages under `*.nic.in` are 100-440 KB because the NIC template inlines its full CSS - large but still one curl per page.

### Gap note

The central gap is the **development fund**. Mizoram publishes MLALADS works data, but there is **no statewide per-MLA MLALADS portal and no state-level analogue of mplads.mospi.gov.in**. What exists is a set of *district* pages, each covering only the constituencies lying in that district, each with its own URL slug and its own column names, some with the works behind a further click and some with empty cells, and **none publishing the MLA's annual allocated limit**. No government source publishes, for any Mizoram MLA, the four figures the project wants as a set - allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed, expenditure. The guidelines' **Annexure III** utilisation return proves the state *does* compute "Total cost of work sanctioned", "Total cost of work unsanctioned (D-E)" and "Actual expenditure" per constituency, and the guidelines require the second instalment to be released against it - but that return goes to the Planning & Programme Implementation Department and is **not published**. A per-MLA expenditure total therefore **cannot be sourced officially** and must not be estimated by summing district works tables, which are incomplete (Saitual's own table shows 8-Chalfilh with no details at all, and 21-Lengteng's cell is empty). Two further hard gaps: **attendance is not published anywhere** in any format (the word does not appear on any assembly or NeVA page fetched), and **private members' bills** are a two-row register, not a per-member record. On the election side, the **2023 assembly result link on the CEO site is broken** (`/form_twenty` renders "Not Found") and the ECI 2023 assembly-results sub-site is not reachable at its predictable paths (all 404), so the most recent general election result is currently obtainable only from the scanned predecessor archives or by re-finding ECI's live result URL each cycle.

### Platform

Mizoram sits on **two shared national/state platforms plus one bespoke site**, which is unusually useful for scraper reuse. **(1) Legislative business runs on NeVA - the National e-Vidhan Application - the strongest common-platform finding here.** `mizo.neva.gov.in` is one instance of a single national application built by the **Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs with NIC**: its footer reads "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India | Content managed by: Mizoram Legislative Assembly"; the underlying pages use identical ASP.NET MVC routes (`/Member/Details/{id}`, `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`) and the same `ASP.NET_SessionId` cookie shape as the generic NeVA build, and the state member-detail page even leaks the shared menu entry "eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh". The Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs confirms Mizoram is one of 21 NeVA "Digital Houses" (PIB 2294959, 5 Aug 2026) and neva.gov.in lists "Digital House Mizoram ... Live on NeVA Platform". One scraper for `/Member/Member_Question` and `/Questions/FetchDataList` therefore serves every NeVA state. **(2) District websites are split across two further templates.** The `*.nic.in` district sites (mamit, kolasib, lawngtlai, aizawl, lunglei, champhai, serchhip, saiha) all carry the identical footer "**Powered by Swass - Secure, Scalable and Sugamya Website as a Service**" plus "National Informatics Centre", and all expose the same `<meta name="generator" content="Powered by WPBakery Page Builder ...">` on WordPress - **this is NIC's S3WaaS district template, reused nationwide**; the MLA-LADS works tables live inside it, so their markup (a `table.table` with constituency/MLA/works columns) is reusable across districts. The `*.mizoram.gov.in` sites (dcsaitual, dcserchhip, planning, dipr, ceo) instead carry "**Powered by SENHRI. A Content Management System provided by Department of Information & Communication Technology, Government of Mizoram**" - Mizoram's own **state-level CMS**, shared across Mizoram departments but not across states. **(3) The assembly's public website `www.mizoramassembly.in` is BESPOKE** - and notably *not* the NeVA front end: `nginx` with Laravel `XSRF-TOKEN` and `assembly-session` cookies, a custom Bootstrap theme under `/theme/css/`, and it merely links to NeVA under "Related Links". Within the SENHRI family, `www.eci.gov.in` and `mizoram.gov.in` are additionally separate JS SPAs.

---

## Nagaland (IN-NL)

**Verdict:** partial - Nagaland has a fully official, curl-fetchable MLA profile source with photos and per-member questions on its National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) instance, plus official assembly election Form 20 result sheets from the state CEO, but there is NO public per-MLA development-fund portal and attendance is published nowhere.

### Profile
Nagaland has a unicameral legislature, the **Nagaland Legislative Assembly** (60 seats). Two distinct OFFICIAL systems exist and they do complementary, non-overlapping jobs.

(1) **`https://nla.nagaland.gov.in/`** - the Assembly Secretariat's own site, fetched curl-200 (text/html, 233,197 bytes). It is a **WordPress** site running the Divi v4.24.3 theme (`<meta content="Divi v.4.24.3" name="generator"/>`), which means it exposes the standard WP REST API. Enumerating `/wp-json/wp/v2/pages?per_page=100` returns exactly **23 pages** (curl-200, JSON, 2,963 bytes): home, history, speaker, deputy-speaker, chief-minister, governor, committees, about/functions/organization of the Assembly Secretariate, notifications, RTI, contact and the legal boilerplate pages. **There is no member roster.** The site publishes no list of MLAs - the only individual office-holders with pages are the Speaker (Shri. Sharingain Longkumer), Deputy Speaker, Chief Minister and Governor. There is no photo gallery of members, no party list, no constituency list, and no per-member page. It also does **not link to NeVA anywhere** (zero `neva` hrefs, zero `e-vidhan` / `evidhan` strings in the homepage markup). This site is effectively a news/notifications CMS for the Secretariat.

(2) **`https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/`** - Nagaland's instance of the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, hosted by NIC, and this is where every piece of member data lives. It is officially linked from the national portal: `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` contains `<a href="https://nagaland.neva.gov.in" title="Assembly">` beside the Nagaland flag asset `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/NeVA/FileStructure/HouseImage/NL.png`. Homepage curl-200 (text/html, 95,477 bytes), title "Welcome to Nagaland Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application".

The member roster is a single curl-fetchable endpoint: **`https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`** (curl-200, text/html, ~390 KB, no query parameters required). It returns **exactly 60 member cards**, one per sitting MLA, on `AssemblyID=14` (the Fourteenth Nagaland Legislative Assembly, constituted after the March 2023 election). Each card carries: **name** (60/60 populated), **party** (60/60 - one is the literal string `"."`), **constituency in the form `Name -District (Nagaland)`** (60/60), **date of birth** (59/60 populated), a Legislators' Home room or local address, a mobile number, and a **thumbnail photo** (60/60 - every member has one). Photos are served from the shared NeVA asset CMS, e.g. `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/NL/FileStructures//Member/thumb/03_25_2023_11_39_59.jpg`; the full-size photo for the same member (`.../Member/03_25_2023_12_19_51.jpg`) was verified curl-200, `image/jpeg`, 47,827 bytes.

The roster **is queryable per member**. Each card links to `/Member/Details/{MemberId}?AssemblyID=14` (curl-200 HTML, ~28 KB), which renders tabs wired to `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail`, `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Member/Member_Notice` and `/Member/Member_Committee`. The profile fragment `https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId={id}&AssemblyID=14` was verified for MemberIds 30, 121 and 126 and returns: father's name, date and place of birth, mobile, party, constituency, state, caste category, marital status, spouse name, number of sons and daughters, local address, permanent address, highest qualification, interests, travels and social participation. Term/assembly number is available only as the shared `AssemblyID=14` parameter (and the `/Bill` house selector lists all fourteen houses by name) - it is not a field on the member record itself.

**Verification level: curl-200, fetched and parsed** for the roster, the 60 photos, and three per-member profiles. Party labels from NeVA need a caveat, see below.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
**No official portal publishing per-MLA fund data exists. Not found.** Nagaland's fund is not called "Vidhayak Nidhi" or "MLALAD" or "MLALADF". In this state it is the **Local Area Development Programme (LADP)**, also written **LADF** in current Assembly debate coverage, and the earliest official instrument names it the **"MP & MLA Local Area Development Programme Fund (MP & MLA LADPF)"**.

Three official documents establish that the scheme is real, and all three stop short of publishing money:

- The **Government of Nagaland Finance Department (Revenue Branch) Notification F. No. FIN/REV-34/VAT/2001, dated Kohima, 4 November 2011**, hosted at `https://nagalandtax.nic.in//docs/Notification/Act/Issued%20by%20Finance%20Department/VAT/2011/Notification%20regarding%20MP%20and%20MLA%20LADPF.pdf` (curl-200, application/pdf, 188,742 bytes, 4 pages). Its text names the fund explicitly: "Whereas the Deputy Commissioner of the Districts release fund from Member of Parliament and Member Legislative Assembly Local Area Development Programme Fund (MP & MLA LADPF) to individuals, groups or bodies etc. for executing various schemes under MP & MLA LADPF". The instrument only grants a VAT composition rate of one paisa per rupee on LADPF works and orders Deputy Commissioners to deduct and remit it - it contains **no allocation, no works, no expenditure and no per-MLA figure**.
- The DIPR Nagaland publication **"Basic Facts 2019"**, `https://ipr.nagaland.gov.in/sites/default/files/2021-11/Basic%20Facts%20Inside%202019_0.pdf` (curl-200, application/pdf, 743,631 bytes, 252 pages), carries the official scheme description of the Local Area Development Programme. Its opening lines are indexed as "LOCAL AREA DEVELOPMENT PROGRAMME (LADP) - Came into being during the annual Plan 2003-04 by renaming the erstwhile District Pla[nning]...". **Honest limitation:** I could not extract the body text myself - the PDF uses Identity-H CID fonts with `ToUnicode` CMaps across 373 font objects, and my extractor recovered only the Latin-1 subset (~28 KB of 252 pages). The scheme description is therefore *seen in a search index pointing at an official PDF*, not read directly. Treat the wording as a lead, and the PDF's existence and its Identity-H encoding as verified.
- Official DIPR press releases confirm the delivery mechanism, and in doing so confirm why there is no per-MLA data: LADP is approved by the **District Planning and Development Board (DPDB)** under the chairmanship of the local MLA. `https://ipr.nagaland.gov.in/mokokchung-dpdb-approves-ladp-2022-2023-ro-mokokchung-district` (curl-200) records that the monthly DPDB meeting for Mokokchung was held "under the Chairmanship of MLA & Chairman DPDB Mokokchung, Imkong L Imchen" and that the house approved LADP for the district. Approval is thus **district-and-year granular in narrative press releases**, never a per-MLA money table.

Exhaustive negative checks for a portal (all performed this session): the NeVA instance has **no fund module** - the strings `ladp`, `ladf`, `mlalad`, `local area` and `fund` appear zero times in the NeVA Nagaland navigation; the state portal search `https://nagaland.gov.in/search?q=LADP` returns no LADP result; `https://nagaland.gov.in/act-rules` (curl-200, 464 KB) contains zero occurrences of `LADP`, `LADF`, `Local Area` or `Vidhayak`; the Nagaland Open Government Data page `https://nagaland.gov.in/ogd` (curl-200) only links out to `https://data.gov.in/search/site?query=nagaland`; and the administering department's own site, `https://nagalandplanning.in/` (Department of Planning & Transformation, Government of Nagaland), has **zero occurrences of `LADP` and `Local Area`** across its homepage, `/downloads`, `/notifications` and `/archive` pages. Candidate fund subdomains (`ladp.nagaland.gov.in`, `mlalad.nagaland.gov.in`, `mla.nagaland.gov.in`) do not resolve.

Conclusion: LADP is a real, decades-old, MLA-directed state scheme, but **the fund is published at district/narrative level only and never per MLA**. There is no allocation limit, no works-recommended, works-sanctioned, works-completed or expenditure figure attributable to an individual MLA from any official source. This is a genuine documented gap, not a data-format problem.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
**Starred questions: YES, per-member, official - but thin and partly test data.** `https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Question?MemberId={id}&AssemblyID=14` (curl-200) returns an HTML table with the exact columns `Sr No | Question Number | Question Type | Date | Minister | Subject | Document`, where the **Question Type column literally reads "Starred"** and the `Document` cell links to the question PDF on the shared NeVA CMS (e.g. `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/NL/FileStructures///PaperLaid/14/4/14_4_S_446_V1.pdf`, in the `PaperLaid/14/4/` house-and-session folder). MemberId 126 returned 9 Starred rows with real subjects ("E.E DIVISION OFFICE AT TOBU", "BUDGET ALLOCATION UNDER DUDA"), dated 29/02/24. **However MemberId 30 (Chief Minister Dr. Neiphiu Rio) returns only two rows whose subjects are the literal strings "Test question 5" and "Test Question III" dated 17/09/22, and MemberId 121 returns "No Records Founds".** So the per-member question record exists and is fetchable, but Nagaland's NeVA question corpus is substantially unpopulated and contains placeholder data. **Coverage must be treated as partial, not as a complete question record.**

**Unstarred questions: NOT observed.** Across every per-member question response fetched, the only `Question Type` value returned was `Starred`. Zero rows classified "Unstarred" were found for any member queried. The classification field exists in the schema, so the mechanism is present, but no unstarred row was retrievable for Nagaland.

**Private members' bills: classification exists, per-member data does not.** The NeVA `https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/Bill` page (curl-200) carries a `BillTypeList` select whose options are exactly `<option value="1">Government Bill</option>` and **`<option value="2">Private Members Bills</option>`**, so the category is a first-class field. The working list endpoint is `https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/Bill/GetBillDashBoardData?UnitCode=NL` (curl-200, text/html, 48,234 bytes; the identically-returning `/Bill/GetBillStateDashBoardData?UnitCode=NL` also works). It returns **52 bill rows** with columns `Sr No | Bill Number | Title | Description | Ministry | Department(s) | Date of Introduction | House | Document`, ranging from "01 of 2021" to "THE NAGALAND TOWN AND COUNTRY PLANNING (FOURTH AMENDMENT) BILL, 2025", with 34 links to introduction PDFs on the NeVA CMS. But this listing carries **no member attribution and no private/government flag** - the word "Private" does not appear in it at all - and 7 of the 52 rows are placeholder entries ("test bill", "Bill Demo", "Demo Bill for Nagaland Assembly"). The filtered search endpoint `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch` returned **HTTP 500** on GET and **HTTP 200 with a 0-byte body** on POST (both for `BillTypeId=2` Private Members Bills and `BillTypeId=1` Government Bill). **No private members' bill is retrievable for any Nagaland MLA.**

**Attendance: NOT published. This is a hard gap.** The string "Attendance" appears **zero times** in every NeVA Nagaland page fetched (member directory, per-member profile, member detail, bill page, questions page, homepage), `https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/Attendance` and `https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/Member/Attendance` both return **404**, and NeVA has no attendance module. The Assembly Secretariat's WordPress site publishes no attendance either. No official member-wise attendance record exists for Nagaland.

Two further NeVA modules exist but return no data to direct requests: `/Member/Member_Committee?MemberId=30&AssemblyID=14` returned 4 rows (the only working tab besides General and Questions), while `/Member/Member_Notice?MemberId=30&AssemblyID=14` returned "No Records Founds". The whole-house endpoints `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Committee/DataList` and `/Debates/FetchDataList` all return **HTTP 500** on GET and **HTTP 200 with a 0-byte body** on POST - they appear to need parameters I could not derive from the page markup, so I cannot state that house-level questions, committee or debates data is retrievable.

**Data-quality caveat on party.** NeVA's party field contains **no occurrence of the string "NDPP"** (0 matches across the 390 KB roster) and labels **33 of 60 members "Naga Peoples's Front (NPF)"**, including Dr. Neiphiu Rio, who is the NDPP leader. This does not reconcile with the NDPP-BJP coalition that formed the government after March 2023, so NeVA's party labels for Nagaland appear to have collapsed several parties into NPF. **Party names from NeVA must be cross-checked against the CEO Nagaland Form 20 result sheets before publication.** The constituency and name fields show no such problem - the NeVA roster's `Shri H. Tovihoto Ayemi / Bharatiya Janata Party / Dimapur-I` matches the winner and party printed in the official Form 20 for AC 1 (Dimapur-I).

### Format note
A fetcher needs a **mixed** toolkit, and the good news is that the highest-value target is the easiest.

- **The MLA roster is plain curl HTML and needs no browser.** `https://nagaland.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList` returns ~390 KB of HTML with no parameters, no token and no cookie, from which all 60 members (name, party, constituency, DOB, photo URL) parse with a single regex over `<a class="card" href="/Member/Details/{id}?AssemblyID=14">` blocks. Photos are then plain JPEG GETs on `cms.neva.gov.in`. Per-member detail and per-member questions are likewise HTML and curl-able, but some NeVA endpoints behave better after a homepage warm-up that sets an ASP.NET session cookie, so a fetcher should prime a cookie jar first.
- **Nothing on NeVA is JSON.** Every data endpoint returns HTML fragments (or nothing), and the whole-house `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Committee/DataList` and `/Debates/FetchDataList` endpoints answered **HTTP 500 on GET and a 0-byte HTTP 200 on POST** - the parameter contract is not discoverable from the served markup. Treat those three as unusable without reverse-engineering the site's AJAX payloads. The only genuinely JSON endpoint in Nagaland's legislature estate is the unrelated WordPress REST API on `nla.nagaland.gov.in`, which contains no member data.
- **CEO Nagaland (Django) is POST-only and CSRF-gated.** Results cannot be fetched with a GET: the archive requires a `csrfmiddlewaretoken` scraped from the HTML plus a session cookie, a first POST (`election_category=NLA&election_type=G&get_year=`) that returns JSON `{"yearlist": ...}`, a second POST (`election_archive` + `year=2023`) that returns the HTML archive listing, and a **third POST per document** (`id={n}&download=`) that streams the PDF. Form 20 PDFs are digitally generated, not scans - AC 1 (Dimapur-I) extracted cleanly once the `BT...ET` hex-string blocks were decoded, but AC 2 (Dimapur-II) yielded zero text under the same extractor, so the PDFs mix encodings and a robust pipeline needs `ToUnicode` CMap handling for the CID-encoded ones.
- **The ECI main site is a React SPA** (`<noscript>You need to enable JavaScript to run this app.</noscript>`), so `https://www.eci.gov.in/` itself needs a headless browser - but its backing API, `https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/`, is plain curl-able JSON and is the better entry point. `results.eci.gov.in` is the opposite: plain static HTML, no JS needed.
- **One official fund document is a genuine scan.** The 2011 LADPF notification is a **CCITTFaxDecode bitmap PDF** (fax-compressed bilevel images across 4 pages) carrying a hidden OCR text layer written in PDF invisible render mode (`3 Tr`), so it is machine-readable only if you decode the text operators; a naive `pdftotext`-style extraction returns nothing. The DIPR "Basic Facts 2019" PDF is the harder case: 252 pages of Identity-H CID text needing `ToUnicode` CMaps, which defeated a straightforward extractor.
- **No captcha, login or payment wall was encountered anywhere.** The only real blockers are the POST/CSRF contract on CEO Nagaland, the three dead NeVA XHR endpoints, and PDF encoding variance.

### Gap note
What is MISSING and cannot be sourced officially is substantial, and it is concentrated in exactly the area the Atlas most wants.

**There is no per-MLA development fund data at all.** Nagaland's LADP exists, is decades old, and is explicitly MLA-directed (the MLA chairs the DPDB that approves it), but no official source publishes an allocation limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure figure for any individual MLA - not on a portal, not in a PDF, not in a table. The only official artefacts are a 2011 tax-circular naming the fund and a stream of district-level DIPR press releases saying a DPDB approved LADP for a given district and year. The administering department's own website does not mention LADP a single time. **This is a permanent, documented gap, not a scraping problem.**

**Attendance does not exist anywhere.** There is no attendance module, no attendance URL, and zero occurrences of the word "Attendance" in any Nagaland legislature page. A member's "record in the Vidhan Sabha" can therefore never include attendance from an official source.

**The assembly record is thin and partly fake.** Starred questions are per-member and real for some members, but the Chief Minister's own tab returns literal placeholder rows ("Test question 5") and other members return nothing, so question counts must not be presented as complete. Unstarred questions were not observed at all. Private members' bills are selectable as a category but yield no retrievable record through any working endpoint, and the one working bills list is house-level with no member attribution - and a seventh of it is demo data. Attendance, unstarred questions and private members' bills are three of the four things the Atlas wants from the assembly record, and all three are effectively absent.

**Party labels from NeVA are unreliable for Nagaland** and contain no "NDPP" string at all despite NDPP leading the government - a fetcher that trusts the roster's party field will publish wrong party data for over half the house. Election results are recoverable, but only from CEO Nagaland's POST-gated Form 20 per-AC PDFs, and the set is incomplete: the 2023 archive lists **59 of the 60 assembly constituencies, with AC 31 missing**. The ECI's own JSON API does not cover Nagaland's February 2023 election at all (its 2023 slice contains only Telangana, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Mizoram and Chhattisgarh), and the old `results.eci.gov.in` folder for that election returns 404.

### Platform
**Common platform, not bespoke - Nagaland's legislative data runs on the NIC National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) template, so one scraper serves many states.** The evidence is concrete and reproducible. The national portal `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` links Nagaland's instance directly with `<a href="https://nagaland.neva.gov.in" title="Assembly">` under the pattern `{state}.neva.gov.in`, alongside siblings including `gujarat.neva.gov.in`, `goa.neva.gov.in`, `asla.neva.gov.in` (Assam), `bla.neva.gov.in` (Bihar), `mgla.neva.gov.in` (Meghalaya), `arla.neva.gov.in`, `jkla.neva.gov.in`, `jhla.neva.gov.in`, `chg.neva.gov.in`, `apa.neva.gov.in`, `apc.neva.gov.in`, `hpvs.neva.gov.in` and `dla.neva.gov.in`. Fetching three instances confirms a single shared codebase: Nagaland, Gujarat and Assam all return the **byte-identical CSS asset list** `/2024/css/bootstrap.min.css`, `/2024/css/fontawesome.css`, `/2024/css/style.css` (identical fingerprint `31826352fea3`), the **identical page-title template** "Welcome to {State} Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application", the **identical endpoint set** (`/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, `/Member/Details/{id}`, `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Committee/DataList`, `/Debates/FetchDataList`), and the identical bundled stack (CanvasJS, jQuery Flot, Bhashini translation plugin). Documents and member photos are served from one shared asset host, `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/{UNIT}/FileStructures/`, where each state has a unit folder - `NL` for Nagaland, `ML` for Meghalaya, `GJ` for Gujarat.

Nagaland nonetheless also runs a **separate bespoke assembly website**, `https://nla.nagaland.gov.in/` (WordPress + Divi), which is content-only: it carries the Secretariat's news, notifications, office-holder pages and RTI material, holds no member roster, and does not link to its own NeVA instance. So the accurate picture is a bespoke CMS for assembly *news* sitting beside a shared national platform for assembly *data* - and it is the shared platform, not the bespoke site, that any scraper should target.

---

## Odisha (IN-OD)

**Verdict:** partial - Odisha has a live unicameral legislature with an official roster (name/party/constituency/photo, 147 members) and a genuinely public per-MLA MLALAD works JSON API, but MLA attendance and private members' bills are published nowhere officially, the per-MLA fund allocation limit is login-gated, and the richest election documents (Form 20, gazette) are scanned images requiring OCR.

### Profile

Odisha has a legislature: the **Odisha Legislative Assembly (Odisha Vidhan Sabha)**, unicameral, 147 elected members, currently the **17th Assembly (05/06/2024 - continuing)**. The official site is **https://assembly.odisha.gov.in/** (verified curl-200). The legacy domain **https://odishaassembly.nic.in/** still resolves but 301-redirects to the new domain; the state portal https://odisha.gov.in/ still links to the legacy `odishaassembly.nic.in` address, so the redirect is the only path between them. The site was relaunched in August 2025.

The roster lives at **https://assembly.odisha.gov.in/MemberofAssembly.aspx** (verified curl-200, 99 KB). It publishes, per member: **name** (with honorific), **party** (full name, e.g. "Bharatiya Janata Party"), **constituency name with district and state** (e.g. "Anandapur - ( Keonjhar , Odisha )"), **date of birth**, **postal address**, **phone number**, **email** (obfuscated as `name[at]gov[dot]in`), and a **photograph** (`images/MemberofAssembly/<Name>.jpg`) plus a party-logo image. This is a complete profile source: all five fields the Atlas needs are present, photo included. The page self-reports a filter of "By Assembly: 17th Assembly(05/06/2024-Continuing) (147)" and renders a doughnut chart of the house composition (BJP 53.06%, BJD 34.69%, INC 9.52%, Independent 2.05%, CPI(M) 0.68% - i.e. 78/51/14/3/1 = 147).

The roster is **paginated 16 members per page across roughly 10 pages**, using ASP.NET WebForms `btnPrev`/`btnNext` POST-back buttons with a hidden `hfPageIndex` field. I verified the pagination is drivable by curl: a POST carrying `__VIEWSTATE`, `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR`, `__EVENTVALIDATION` and `btnNext=Next` advanced the list from page 1 (Shri Abhimanyu Sethi …) to page 2 (Shri Badri Narayan Patra, Smt. Barsha Singh Bariha, …) and returned `lblPageIndex = 2`. So the full 147-member roster is harvestable, but only by chaining POST-backs, not by GETting a URL per page. There is no JSON/XHR roster API and no per-member profile URL - the card is the only representation, and it has no detail page behind it.

A separate **"Who is Who" PDF** is published at https://assembly.odisha.gov.in/DocumentDetails/WhoANDWho/Who%20is%20Who_01_11_2025(2).pdf (17 MB, 17th Assembly). It is a **scanned image PDF** (`/Font` = 0, `/Image` = 170) and needs OCR.

A second, nominally richer source exists but is **non-functional**. Odisha's National e-Vidhan Application instance at **https://odisha.neva.gov.in/** has member pages at `/Member/Details/{id}` whose HTML defines AJAX tabs General | Questions | Notice | Assurances | Committee | Debates | BILLS, hitting `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail`, `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Member/Member_Notice`, `/Member/Member_Committee`. In the shipped markup the member id is **hardcoded** (`var Mid = '0'; var AssID = '0';`) and never bound from the route. I verified the consequence directly: `Member_ProfileDetail`, `Member_Question` and `Member_Notice` return **byte-identical empty shells** for `MemberId=1`, `2`, `100`, `1383`, and for no MemberId at all - each rendering "No Records Founds". `/Member` and `/Member/Index` are 404 and `/Member/AllMembers` is HTTP 500. NeVA Odisha therefore contributes **no usable member data** in its current state.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

In Odisha the fund is called **MLA LAD** (MLA's Local Area Development fund, abbreviated **MLALADS** in government usage); it is not called "Vidhayak Nidhi" in state documents. It is administered by the **Planning & Convergence Department, Government of Odisha** (Lok Seva Bhawan, Bhubaneswar), and the scheme has run since **1997-98**. In August 2025 the Chief Minister launched a dedicated portal together with revised scheme guidelines.

The portal is **https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/** (verified curl-200, 151 KB). **Caution: `mlalad.odisha.gov.in` (without the "s") does not resolve at all (curl exit 6); only the plural hostname exists.** Nor is plain HTTP served.

What it publishes publicly:

1. **Per-MLA recommended works** - page **https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/IPMS/Report** ("MLA Wise Recommended Work Detail"), filterable by Financial Year, District, Constituency and MLA name.
2. A **machine-readable JSON API** behind that page - **`GET https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/MIS/MISReport/GetMLAWiseRecommendedWorkDetailsReport`**. I fetched this successfully with curl and it returned real per-MLA rows. It is a DataTables server-side endpoint and has three sharp requirements: (a) a session cookie plus the page's `__RequestVerificationToken` echoed in a `RequestVerificationToken` header, (b) the DataTables `order[0][column]` and `order[0][dir]` parameters - **omit them and the endpoint silently returns zero rows**, and (c) a **non-zero `DistrictId`**; `DistrictId=0` also returns zero rows. District ids are **344-373** (30 districts). `FinancialYear` is an integer: `2025` = 2025-26, `2026` = 2026-27.

Each JSON row is genuinely per-MLA and carries: `fullName` (the MLA), `constituencyName`, `districtName`, `financialYear`, `letterNo`, `strRecommendationDate`, `recommendationAmount` (₹ lakh), `projectId`, `projectCode` (pattern `MLALADS/OD/<districtId>/<...>/<n>` - note the `OD` state code), `workDescription`, `workCategory`, `workSubCategory`, `locationType`, `blockULBName`, `wordVillageName`, `workStatus` (e.g. "Pending with Collector", "Pending with EA", "Completed", "Not Started"), `sanctionAmount`, `sanctionAmountInLakhsMWD`, `implementionAgencyName`, `strWorkCompletionDate`. This is **per-MLA, per-work**: allocation is not given, but works recommended, sanctioned, completed and in-progress are all derivable, and the recommended amount is explicit per work.

Two caveats on that API. First, `grandRecommendationAmount` is **not** the MLA's total - it repeats the whole filtered set's total (₹4,342.65 lakh for every Cuttack MLA in FY 2025-26), so per-MLA totals must be summed from `recommendationAmount` by `fullName`. Second, `iTotalRecords` and `recordsFiltered` are **unreliable** (both returned 0 while 100 rows were delivered), so pagination must be driven by `start`/`length` rather than by trusting the counters.

3. **Aggregate "since inception" data** - page **https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/IPMS/LegacyReport**, covering 1997-98 to 2024-25, with one row per financial year giving Recommended Amount, Sanctioned Amount, Expenditure Incurred, No. of Projects Sanctioned, Completed and on-going. It reports **396,801 projects sanctioned** and **₹4,54,965 lakh** recommended in total. This page is **aggregate only - there is no per-MLA breakdown and no MLA selector on it**.
4. **List of MLAs PDF** - https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/Utility/DownloadFileV1/List%20of%20MLAs.pdf (3 pages, serial no / member name / constituency with SC-ST reservation). This is a **text PDF** (`/Font` = 24) and I extracted names from it successfully.

**What is NOT public.** The per-MLA **allocation limit** has no public machine-readable figure. The portal's own navigation lists "MLALAD Limit ( Road & Education )" and "MLALAD Admin Expenditure", but their PDFs are served through `OpenLPDocument()` and are **scanned images** - I fetched `e586891c-…pdf` (57 KB), `bab0534b-…pdf` (9.4 MB, guidelines English), `48cf670a-…pdf` (177 KB, operationalisation) and `09fedebb-…pdf` (319 KB, admin expenditure) via `/Utility/DownloadFileV1/` and **every one has `/Font` = 0** - pure raster, OCR required. The `GetMLAWiseFundLimitDetailsReport` endpoint referenced in the portal's own client-side report controller **redirects to the application login page**, confirming the per-MLA fund-limit report is authenticated-only. The scheme rules pages are **https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/IPMS/Guidelines** and **https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/IPMS/SOP**, with the main guidelines also at https://mlalads.odisha.gov.in/Utility/DownloadFileV1/guideline.pdf (37 pages, 135 `/Font` - a genuine text PDF, unlike the guideline copy on the homepage). The portal carries no government-resolution (GO) number on any page I fetched; the standalone guidelines PDF is the closest artefact to scheme rules.

An incidental but telling defect: the portal's "Feasibility Report" menu item points at **`C:\Users\Guruprasad\OneDrive\Desktop`** - a developer's local Windows path shipped into production HTML.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred and unstarred questions ARE published**, but **per sitting day, not per member**. The page **https://assembly.odisha.gov.in/ViewQuestion.aspx** is an ASP.NET post-back form with Assembly (17th-14th), Session (First-Sixth) and a From/To date range. I POSTed it (17th Assembly, Sixth Session, 2024-07-01 to 2026-09-14) and it returned a table with columns **Sl. No | Date | Assembly Type | Session | STARRED Question | UNSTARRED Question**, each row linking to a per-day PDF:

- starred: `https://assembly.odisha.gov.in/Uploads/st{DDMMYY}.pdf`
- unstarred: `https://assembly.odisha.gov.in/Uploads/ud{DDMMYY}.pdf`

(e.g. `st190226.pdf`, `ud190226.pdf`, `ud200226.pdf`, all curl-200). These are **text PDFs** - 85-215 `/Font` entries, Type0/Identity-Subtype fonts with `/ToUnicode` CMaps and hex-string text operators - so they are extractable with a real PDF library, **not** OCR. Attributing a question to an individual MLA therefore requires downloading and parsing each sitting day's PDF and matching names; there is no per-member question view, no question-level filter by member, and no JSON.

**Attendance is NOT published.** I searched every navigable assembly page (`Home`, `AboutUs`, `RTI`, `ViewChapters`, `Rules`, `Who`, `ViewAssemblyBulletin`, `ViewNotifications`, `ViewEvents`, `ViewAmenities`) for the string "attendance" and got **zero hits**. NeVA Odisha would be the natural home for it, but its member tabs are the broken `MemberId = 0` shells described above. There is no official source for Odisha MLA attendance.

**Private members' bills are NOT published.** The assembly navigation has "Legislative Business" (`ViewListOfBusiness.aspx`) and "Assembly Bulletin" (`ViewAssemblyBulletin.aspx`), but both are dropdown-driven document viewers whose option lists are empty/placeholder ("Please select a document from the dropdown to view it here", "Select -- Select --"). The phrase "private member" appears on the site **only in the text of `Rules.aspx`** (the procedural rules), never as an actual bill list or bill document. NeVA Odisha's `/Bill` route **302-redirects to the national `/Home/NeVA` landing page** (returning the 192 KB national NeVA page, not Odisha content) - as do `/Business`, `/Debates` and `/Committee`.

So of the four record types: questions **yes** (per-day PDF), attendance **no**, private members' bills **no**, and no member-level assembly-record view of any kind.

### Format note

A fetcher needs **four different strategies**. (1) `assembly.odisha.gov.in` is classic ASP.NET WebForms: curl works, but both the roster and the question search are **POST-only** (`__VIEWSTATE` / `__EVENTVALIDATION` must be carried and re-submitted per page, and the roster pages 16 at a time), and question PDFs need a real PDF text extractor (Type0/Identity fonts with ToUnicode CMaps - not OCR). (2) `mlalads.odisha.gov.in` is the friendliest source: a **curl-able JSON API**, but it demands a session cookie plus a `RequestVerificationToken` header harvested from `/IPMS/Report`, plus `order[0][column]` and `order[0][dir]` and a non-zero `DistrictId` - without any of the three it silently returns an empty result set rather than an error. (3) `ceoodisha.nic.in` serves plain HTML over a WordPress stack, but its substantive results documents (all 147 Form 20 sheets, the elected-members gazette) are **scanned raster PDFs with zero embedded fonts - OCR is mandatory**, at 1.8-4.2 MB per constituency. (4) `eci.gov.in` is a **JS-rendered React SPA**, so the main commission site requires a headless browser, while `results.eci.gov.in` is static HTML but **only hosts the current election** - the 2024 assembly archive URLs return 404. `odisha.neva.gov.in` is JS-rendered and, for member data, empty. No captchas, no Cloudflare interstitials, no session walls apart from the MLALAD login for the fund-limit report; the principal cost is **OCR volume**, not anti-bot defence.

### Gap note

Three things cannot be sourced officially in machine-readable form. First, **MLA attendance**: it is published nowhere - not on the assembly site (zero occurrences of the word), not on NeVA Odisha (member tabs return empty shells for every id), and not from the CEO. The only attendance-adjacent artefact found was an **ADR (Association for Democratic Reforms) NGO report**, which is UNOFFICIAL and must be labelled as such; a provenance-first site should show attendance as unavailable rather than import it. Second, **private members' bills**: no official bill list, bill document, or bill status exists for Odisha - the assembly's own Bill-adjacent menus are empty placeholders and NeVA Odisha's `/Bill` route redirects to the national landing page. Third, the **per-MLA fund allocation limit**: the portal names the document ("MLALAD Limit (Road & Education)") but ships it as a scan, and the structured `GetMLAWiseFundLimitDetailsReport` endpoint is behind a login, so the allocated ceiling per MLA is not publicly retrievable; only *recommended* and *sanctioned* amounts per work are. Two further soft gaps: the per-MLA works API exposes only **FY 2025-26 and 2026-27** (all earlier years exist only as the portal's state-wide aggregate table), and **expenditure incurred is aggregate-only** - the per-MLA JSON carries `sanctionAmount` and completion status per work but no expenditure field. Finally, per-constituency completed-vote detail (Form 20) is available officially but **only as scanned images**, so any election-result ingestion for Odisha is an OCR project rather than a parsing one.

### Platform

Odisha runs **two unrelated platforms plus one commercial CMS**, and the split matters for scraper reuse. The legislature's own site, `assembly.odisha.gov.in`, is **bespoke** - a custom ASP.NET WebForms build with odds-and-ends markup (`memeber-item`, `brand-mark-memeber`), hand-rolled POST-back paging and a CanvasJS chart inlined into the page; it shares no template, no URL pattern and no vendor with any other state. In parallel, Odisha operates an instance of the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)** at **https://odisha.neva.gov.in/**, which **is** a genuine shared national template: its footer reads "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved **Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India**", and the national host `neva.gov.in` carries subdomains (`odisha`, `kerala`, `gujarat`, `tnla`, `mhla`, `wbla`, `raj`, `sikkim`, `manipur`, `nagaland`, `tripura`, `puddu`, `utkh`, `mizo`, `goa`, `asla`, `bla` and more - roughly 38 legislature instances) that share the same `/Member/Details/{id}`, `/Member/Member_*` and `/Home/NeVA` routes. The reflow is visible in Odisha's own HTML: the Odisha member page embeds the string **"eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"**, and Odisha's empty `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Debates` and `/Committee` routes redirect to the *national* landing page, which serves notices from **Gujarat Vidhan Sabha and Himachal Pradesh Vidhan Sabha**. The Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs confirmed in a PIB release (05 Aug 2026) that **Odisha Legislative Assembly is one of 21 Legislative Houses transitioned to a Digital House under NeVA** (33 of 37 legislatures have signed MoUs). **Caveat for scraper reuse: the shared template is real but, for Odisha, currently hollow** - member routes return empty for every id - so NeVA yields one scraper for many states only where a state has actually populated its instance. Separately, the election authority `ceoodisha.nic.in` runs **WordPress with the commercially licensed "Bridge" theme** (`wp-content/themes/bridge-child`) - a common vendor product, but a CMS rather than a legislature template, and not shared with the assembly's own systems.

---

## Punjab (IN-PB)

**Verdict:** partial - The legislature site is a fully official, curl-fetchable NeVA 2.0 instance with a complete 117-MLA roster including photos and a JSON API, but attendance is published as an empty dataset and no official per-MLA development-fund portal exists for Punjab at all.

### Profile

Punjab has a unicameral legislature, the **Punjab Legislative Assembly (Punjab Vidhan Sabha)**, currently in its **16th Assembly** (constituted 2022; 117 seats, 34 reserved for SC). Its official website is **https://punjabassembly.gov.in/** - fetched with curl, HTTP 200, 569,381 bytes of server-rendered HTML, `<title>Legislative Assembly, Punjab</title>`. The `www.` and bare-HTTP forms return the identical 569,381-byte document, so both `https://punjabassembly.gov.in/` and `https://www.punjabassembly.gov.in/` are valid. Note that **`punjabassembly.nic.in` does NOT resolve** (curl exit 6, DNS NXDOMAIN) despite appearing in search-engine results; only the `.gov.in` host is live.

Member data is genuinely complete and per-member queryable. The roster index at **https://punjabassembly.gov.in/Members** is a server-rendered HTML page paginated into 5 pages via `?Page_No=2..5` (24+24+24+24+21 = **117 member links**, verified by taking the union of `MembersProfile?MemberID=` across all five pages). Each roster card carries an `<img>` whose path follows the pattern **`/NevaMemberPic/16/<filename>.jpg`** - exactly **117 distinct photo paths** were counted across the five pages, so the photo is published for every sitting member, not a subset. The roster page also exposes party affiliation in clear text (Aam Aadmi Party, Indian National Congress, Shiromani Akali Dal, Bharatiya Janata Party) and constituency names.

The per-member profile is at **https://punjabassembly.gov.in/Members/MembersProfile?MemberID={id}&AssemblyID=16** (fetched 200, 110,467 bytes of server-rendered HTML). It publishes: member name, party name, constituency name with number (e.g. "Dhuri -[107]- Sangrur"), permanent address, local address, mobile number, email, **profile photo**, and a Personal Details block with father's name, place and date of birth, caste category, marital status, spouse, children, educational qualification, sports activity, interests, foreign travel and "Positions Held" narrative. Tabs for Questions, Notices, Assurances, Committee Members, Debates, Bills and Attendance exist on the same page.

There is a **second, parallel official surface** on the national NeVA instance at **https://pvs.neva.gov.in/** (curl 200, 84,083 bytes) with per-member pages at **https://pvs.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/{id}**. It is thinner than the `.gov.in` site - for Bhagwant Mann it renders only a General tab plus "No Records Founds" for Assurances, Debates and BILLS. The two sites are separate deployments of the same NeVA template; the `.gov.in` site is the richer and the one to scrape for profiles.

Verification level: **curl-200** for every profile-side URL above. Nothing on the profile side is behind a login, captcha or session cookie - the `(S(...))` session-token form seen in older indexed URLs is not required, and all pages fetched cleanly on a cold curl with no cookie jar.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**No official portal publishing per-MLA development-fund figures was found for Punjab, and there is good evidence that no such per-MLA fund is currently operative.** This is a real and load-bearing gap, not a search failure.

On the name: Punjab does **not** use "Vidhayak Nidhi" or "Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi". Those are **Himachal Pradesh** terms - searching them returns Himachal Pradesh CAG audit reports and HP government notifications, which is a trap worth stating explicitly so a later researcher does not import an HP URL into the Punjab record. Punjab's own assembly and state portals contain **zero** occurrences of "Vidhayak", "Nidhi" or "MLALAD" (verified by grepping the fetched bytes of `punjabassembly.gov.in` and `punjab.gov.in`).

What Punjab appears to have instead is the **"Rangla Punjab Vikas Scheme"**, approved by the Cabinet and reported at **Rs 585 crore for district-level development, allocated at Rs 5 crore per assembly segment across all 117 segments**, with a first instalment of Rs 213 crore reported released. Critically, this is a **per-assembly-segment** allocation administered by the state government (the Finance Minister is quoted setting phase-completion deadlines), **not a per-MLA discretionary fund**. All of this is sourced only from news reporting (Indian Express, Times of India, Hindustan Times, Babushahi) and is therefore **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**; I could not locate a government order, scheme guideline PDF or portal for it on `punjab.gov.in`. The state's own Orders/Notifications index at `https://punjab.gov.in/impnotifications/` was fetched (200, 190,929 bytes) and contains **zero** occurrences of "Rangla", "Vidhayak", "MLA" or "Nidhi".

Several candidate official hosts were probed directly and **do not exist** (DNS NXDOMAIN, curl exit 6): `mlafund.punjab.gov.in`, `mlalad.punjab.gov.in`, `vidhayak.punjab.gov.in`, `mla.punjab.gov.in` and `lgd.punjab.gov.in`. Only `finance.punjab.gov.in` (200, 54,873 bytes) and `rangla.punjab.gov.in` (200, 59,434 bytes) resolve - and **`rangla.punjab.gov.in` is a trap**: despite the "rangla" name it is the *Chief Minister's Rangla Punjab Fund*, a **WordPress-based public donation portal** for flood relief (`wp-json`, `xmlrpc.php` present; SBI donation form, 80(G) receipts). It is a distinct thing from the *Rangla Punjab Vikas Scheme* (development) and publishes no fund-utilisation data of any kind.

Contextual reporting corroborates that per-MLA funds have been absent: Indian Express ("Bereft of MLALAD funds, Punjab legislators feel helpless", Sep 2024), Times of India ("No Plan For Mplad-like Fund For Mlas: Cm") and Hindustan Times (CM "promises" discretionary funds on MPLADS lines) - all **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**, but mutually consistent. There is no Punjab analogue to the national MPLADS portal at mplads.mospi.gov.in.

Verification level: `not-found` for a portal. The absence is positively established by (a) direct DNS failure of six candidate subdomains, (b) zero keyword hits on both the assembly and state portals, and (c) the absence of any such link from the assembly site's own navigation.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED.** This is the clearest negative finding of the survey. The member profile page renders an Attendance tab, and the underlying XHR endpoint is **`/Members/GetAllMemberAttendanceDates_Neva_Latest?AssemblyID=16&SessionID={session}&MemberID={id}`**. I called it for **27 different members** (MemberIDs 4, 13, 14, 20, 21, 25, 41, 46, 52, 59, 60, 65, 75, 90, 99, 104, 109, 110, 119, 124, 128, 129, 130, 131, 146, 204, 210) across **five separate sessions** (397, 389, 388, 386, 385) of the 16th Assembly. **Every single call returned the empty JSON array `[]`.** The rendered page agrees, showing "Attendance - No Record Found" under the tab. The endpoint is live and correctly shaped (`{sessionDates, Attendancestatus}` per the page's own JavaScript) - it is simply not populated. So Punjab *per-MLA attendance percentages cannot be sourced officially*, and any site that shows them for Punjab is deriving them from something else.

**Questions: published, but session-wise documents, NOT per-member.** Two server-rendered shells exist, **https://punjabassembly.gov.in/Questions/StarredQuestion** (200, 74,075 bytes) and **https://punjabassembly.gov.in/Questions/UnStarredQuestion** (200, 74,178 bytes), each with Assembly/Session dropdowns populated by AJAX. The backing JSON endpoints are **`/Questions/StarredQuestionSession`** and **`/Questions/UnStarredQuestionSession`**, both returning 200 with `application/json`. The payload carries `RecordId, Heading, FilePath, FilePathLocal, CreatedDate` - i.e. **links to consolidated session PDFs in English and Punjabi**, e.g. `.../16/185/20260810/Starred/Approved Starred Question 10 August English.pdf`. So the assembly publishes *the questions asked in a session* as documents, **not** a per-member count of starred or unstarred questions. Separately, the per-member **`/Members/GetQuestions?MemberCode={id}`** endpoint exists and returns valid JSON, but it returned `[]` for every member tested (146, 59, 52, 204, 210) - the per-member question index is also unpopulated. Starred and unstarred questions are therefore best recorded as **"published only as session-level PDFs, not attributable per MLA."**

**Private members' bills: published as documents, NOT per-member.** **https://punjabassembly.gov.in/Legislations/IntroducedBills** (200, 380,559 bytes) and **https://punjabassembly.gov.in/Legislations/PassedBills** (200, 364,205 bytes) are server-rendered HTML tables with roughly 343 "Bill No" rows and **373 PDF links** on the Passed Bills page alone. There is no bill-type or member column in the markup, and no per-member endpoint. Bill documents are **scanned images** - see the format note.

What is **NOT** published: per-MLA attendance (endpoint empty), per-MLA question counts (endpoint empty; only session PDFs), Assurances and Notices (both render "No Record Found" on the member page), and any per-MLA bill sponsorship index.

| Department of Finance, Punjab | https://finance.punjab.gov.in/ | yes | fund | HTML | curl-200 |
| punjabassembly.nic.in (referenced in search results) | https://punjabassembly.nic.in/ | yes | profile | DOES NOT RESOLVE - DNS NXDOMAIN | unverified |
| ceopunjab.nic.in (referenced in search results) | https://ceopunjab.nic.in/ | yes | election-results | DOES NOT RESOLVE - DNS NXDOMAIN | unverified |
| Candidate MLA-fund subdomains (mlafund / mlalad / vidhayak / mla / lgd .punjab.gov.in) | https://mlafund.punjab.gov.in/ (and 4 siblings) | yes | fund | DO NOT EXIST - DNS NXDOMAIN | not-found |
| Indian Express - bereft of MLALAD funds | https://indianexpress.com/article/cities/chandigarh/bereft-of-mlalad-funds-punjab-legislators-feel-helpless-we-are-for-attending-bhogs-weddings-only-9588664/ | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| Indian Express - Rs 5-cr development fund per segment | https://indianexpress.com/article/cities/chandigarh/punjab-cabinet-meet-117-assembly-receive-rs-5-crore-development-fund-9964631/ | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| Times of India - Rangla Punjab Vikas guidelines | https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/city/chandigarh/rangla-punjab-vikas-guidelines-get-punjab-cabinets-nod-to-boost-grassroots-devpt/articleshow/120594087.cms | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| Times of India - no plan for MPLADS-like fund | https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/city/chandigarh/no-plan-for-mplad-like-fund-for-mlas-cm/articleshow/96532505.cms | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| Hindustan Times - CM promises discretionary funds | https://www.hindustantimes.com/cities/chandigarh-news/punjab-cm-promises-discretionary-funds-to-mlas-on-lines-of-mplads-101656358185544.html | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| Hindustan Times - Rs 213 cr released under Rangla Punjab | https://www.hindustantimes.com/cities/chandigarh-news/213-cr-released-for-rangla-punjab-scheme-101764268755175-amp.html | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| National MPLADS portal (national analogue, for comparison) | https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/ | yes | fund | searchable portal | search-result-only |

### Format note

A fetcher needs a **mixed strategy with one OCR branch**, and the good news is that the profile pillar is fully curl-able. The assembly site is classic server-rendered HTML - no ZK/JSF, no `__VIEWSTATE`, no anti-forgery token, no session cookie requirement - so `curl` with a browser User-Agent returns complete markup: `/Members?Page_No=1..5` yields all 117 members and 117 photo URLs in plain HTML, and `/Members/MembersProfile?MemberID=&AssemblyID=` yields the full profile. On top of that, the site exposes a **genuine JSON API surface** (ASP.NET Core, evidenced by RFC 9110 `application/problem+json` validation errors): `GetAssembly`, `GetSession`, `GetSessionByAssemblyId`, `GetQuestions`, `GetAllMemberAttendanceDates_Neva_Latest`, `StarredQuestionSession`, `UnStarredQuestionSession`, and a separate full-text engine at `/DigitizationApllication/api/VSSearch` (`AssemblyData` needs no parameters; the search root requires `q` and returns 616 KB of JSON for `q=education`). Two caveats: the *user-facing* search at `/vidhan-search/search` is a **Vite/React SPA** (`<div id="root">`, hashed bundle, bundles PDF.js) that must be driven headlessly if you want its UI, though its backend is directly curl-able; and `https://www.eci.gov.in/` is a **React SPA requiring JavaScript** ("You need to enable JavaScript to run this app", `<div id="root">`), so ECI election-result pages need a headless browser. PDF handling is **split and must not be treated uniformly**: starred/unstarred question PDFs contain a real text layer (one sampled file: 33 `/Font` vs 23 `/Image`) so ordinary PDF text extraction works, whereas **bill PDFs are scans with no text layer at all** (two sampled passed-bill PDFs: `/Font: 0`, `/Image: 8`, 12.5 MB and 12.4 MB) and therefore require **OCR** - and at that size, roughly 12 MB per bill, a full historical bill harvest is a heavy storage commitment. There is no captcha anywhere on the assembly site.

### Gap note

Three gaps are documented and none of them is a search failure. **First and largest: no official per-MLA development-fund data exists for Punjab.** There is no state equivalent of the MPLADS portal, no `mlalad`/`mlafund`/`vidhayak` host resolves, and neither the assembly site nor the state portal contains the words "MLA", "Vidhayak", "Nidhi" or "MLALAD". The "Rangla Punjab Vikas Scheme" figures (Rs 585 crore, Rs 5 crore per segment, Rs 213 crore first instalment) are sourced only from news reporting and are allocated **per assembly segment rather than per MLA**, so even if an official scheme page is later found it would likely be **aggregate/segment-level, not per-MLA** - I could not locate a government order or guideline PDF for it. Consequently the Election Atlas **cannot show allocated / recommended / sanctioned / completed / expenditure figures per Punjab MLA from any official source**, and must either omit that pillar for Punjab or publish it explicitly as unavailable. **Second: attendance is published as an empty dataset.** The endpoint is live, correctly shaped and reachable, but returned `[]` for all 27 members across 5 sessions tested. **Third: questions and bills are not attributable per MLA** - they exist only as session-level consolidated PDFs, and the per-member `GetQuestions` endpoint is likewise empty. Also worth recording as a trap: `rangla.punjab.gov.in` is the *Chief Minister's Rangla Punjab Fund* flood-donation site (WordPress), which is **not** the *Rangla Punjab Vikas Scheme* and publishes no utilisation data.

### Platform

Punjab runs on a **shared national template, not a bespoke site: NeVA 2.0 (National e-Vidhan Application), hosted by the National Informatics Centre**, and this is one of the strongest common-platform findings available. The evidence is unusually explicit. The site's own footer reads verbatim **"Copyright © 2022 NeVA 2.0: All Rights Reserved National Informatics Centre & Punjab Vidhan Sabha Secretariat"**. The `*.neva.gov.in` family is enumerated from the national portal at `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA`, which links dozens of state instances (including `pvs`, `gujarat`, `kerala`, `hpvs`, `goa`, `asla`, `bla`, `hrla`, `chg`), and the central asset host `cms.neva.gov.in` namespaces uploads by state code - **`cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/PB/`** for Punjab alongside **`/NeVA/GJ/`** (Gujarat), **`/NeVA/HPVS/`** (Himachal) and **`/NeVA/OR/`** (Odisha). Retrieving `pvs.neva.gov.in`, `gujarat.neva.gov.in` and `kerala.neva.gov.in` returns **the same `<title>` string with only the state name substituted** ("Welcome to *Punjab/Gujarat/Kerala* Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application") and **identical internal route names** (`/Member/Details`, `/Member/GovDetails`, `/Member/SecDetails`). Punjab's photo assets are served from a path literally named **`/NevaMemberPic/`**. As a final piece of template-sharing evidence, the Punjab member page at `pvs.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/146` leaks the **hardcoded string "eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"** - another state's name, left in the shared markup. A NeVA-branded member mobile app also exists (`evmember.sbl.nationalevidhan`). Practically: **one scraper written against the NeVA template and its `/Member/...` routes should serve many states**, with the Rajasthan/Himachal-style state-specific sites being the ones needing bespoke handlers.

---

## Rajasthan (IN-RJ)

**Verdict:** partial - Rajasthan has a working legislature with an excellent official per-MLA profile roster (200 members with photos), a genuinely per-MLA fund JSON API, and per-member starred/unstarred question data, but MLA attendance is advertised yet its query endpoints return a server error, and the private-members'-bills pages publish nothing.

### Profile

The official assembly site is **https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/** (curl-200, `text/html`). It is an ASP.NET WebForms application (leaked `__VIEWSTATE` / `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR` / `__EVENTVALIDATION` fields and `__doPostBack` handlers); the footer of every page credits **"Designed & Hosted by National Informatics Centre"**, and the pages carry an `Images/NICLogo.jpg` asset.

Member data is published in full on the static page **https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/Containers/Members/ConstituencyWise.aspx** (curl-200, 563 KB). I fetched and parsed it: it contains **202 `<tr>` rows, of which 200 are MLA rows** carrying exactly the fields the project needs — serial number, a per-MLA portrait image, member name with a bracketed numeric member ID (e.g. `Sh. Vasudev Devnani (147)`), district, constituency, party, and the list of Vidhan Sabha terms served (e.g. `12, 13, 14, 15, 16`). The header row is literally `S.No. | Name of Member | District | Constituency | Party | Vidhan Sabha Terms`.

**Photos are real and per-member.** The page references **200 distinct `<img>` tags** under the path pattern `https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/HamareVidhayak/MLAPic/<member_id>.<ext>` (extensions vary: `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.JPG`), and the filename stem matches the bracketed member ID in the name cell (e.g. `.../MLAPic/1410.jpg` for member 1410). This is a clean, scrapeable, one-URL-per-MLA photo source.

There is also a dynamic search form at **https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/HamareVidhayak/RptMemberSearch.aspx** (curl-200) exposing dropdowns for district (499 options, Hindi names), constituency, party, constituency category (SC/ST/general), member status and Vidhan Sabha term, plus a name text box and a Hindi/English/Urdu radio set. **This form's postback is broken** — a correctly-formed ASP.NET POST returns `HTTP 302` to `/404?aspxerrorpath=/HamareVidhayak/RptMemberSearch.aspx`. The static ConstituencyWise page is therefore the working profile source, and it is sufficient.

Additional official member breakdowns, all curl-200 on the same host: `PartyPosition.aspx` (667 KB), `WomenWise.aspx`, `ReservedSeats.aspx`, `ConstituencyWise.aspx`, `Contacts.aspx`. The current house is the **16th Vidhan Sabha**; a roster PDF of the 16th House is published at `https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/Attachments/PDF/16Results/Election_Final_04Dec2023.pdf` (curl-200, `application/pdf`, 1,010,825 bytes, 17 pages, `/Font`=191 and `/FontFile`=17 versus `/Image`=51 — a real text PDF, not a scan).

Rajasthan **also** runs a full **National e-Vidhan Application** instance at **https://raj.neva.gov.in/** (curl-200), titled "Welcome to Rajasthan Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application". Its per-member route is `/Member/Details/<id>`, and member portraits are served from the shared national CMS at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/RJ/FileStructures//Member/<id>.jpg` (curl-200, `image/jpeg`). The NeVA member-detail template currently renders "No Records Founds" for the Debates/Bills/Assurances tabs on the sample member I opened, so NeVA is a secondary profile source; the legacy NIC roster is the working one.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund's official name in Rajasthan is **विधायक स्थानीय क्षेत्र विकास योजना** ("Vidhayak Sthaniya Kshetra Vikas Yojana" — MLA Local Area Development Scheme). This is not a guess: the RDIWMS scheme master lists it verbatim as **scheme code 9**, alongside its parliamentary twin सांसद स्थानीय क्षेत्र विकास योजना and the tab labels `MLALAD` / `MPLAD`. Colloquially the state press calls it "विधायक निधि" (Vidhayak Nidhi); no state portal uses that as its domain — `vidhayaknidhi.rajasthan.gov.in`, `mlalad.rajasthan.gov.in` and `lad.rajasthan.gov.in` all return NXDOMAIN.

**There IS a public per-MLA portal, and it is machine-readable.** The Rural Development & Panchayati Raj Department's works-management system — branded **RDIWMS**, "ग्रामीण विकास एवं पंचायती राज विभाग / Rural Development and Panchayati Raj Department (version 3.0.11.21)", footer credit **"Website designed and developed by National Informatics Center, Rajasthan"** — is served on two hostnames, **https://ework.rajasthan.gov.in/** and **https://rdprwms.rajasthan.gov.in/**. The public MLA-fund dashboard is:

**https://ework.rajasthan.gov.in/iwmsweb/Pdmn/Dashboard_graph_amchart_new.aspx** (curl-200, 191 KB)

Its "MLA" tab renders a per-MLA table whose column headers I extracted exactly: `क्र. सं.` (S.No.) | `माननीय विधायक का नाम` (Hon'ble MLA name) | `निर्वाचन क्षेत्र` (constituency) | `अनुमोदित कार्यो की संख्या` (no. of approved works) | `वित्तीय स्वीकृत कार्यो की संख्या` (no. of financially sanctioned works) | `पूर्ण कार्यो की संख्या` (no. of completed works) | `उपलब्ध राशि(लाखों में) = प्रारंभिक राशि + अन्य रसीदें + आवंटित राशि` (available amount in lakhs = initial amount + other receipts + **allocated amount**) | `अनुशंसित राशि (लाखों में)` (recommended amount) | `वित्तीय स्वीकृत राशि(लाखों में)` (financially sanctioned amount) | `व्यय राशि` (expenditure).

The dashboard itself is amCharts-driven, but it is backed by an ASP.NET `[WebMethod]` JSON endpoint that I called successfully:

**POST https://ework.rajasthan.gov.in/iwmsweb/Pdmn/Dashboard_graph_amchart_new.aspx/get_mla_data**
Content-Type `application/json`, body `{"a_no":16,"fin_yr":0,"dist_code":0}` → **HTTP 200, 95,964 bytes of JSON, 200 records**. Parameters are `a_no` (assembly number; `16` = 16th Vidhan Sabha), `fin_yr` (`0` = all years) and `dist_code` (`0` = all districts) and can be filtered per district and per financial year. Each record is `{s_no, HMLAName, const_name, no_proposed, recommended_amt, no_fs, no_cc, fs_amt, avail_amt, remain_amt, district_name}`, wrapped in a `{"d":[...]}` envelope with a `__type` discriminator. Names arrive HTML-entity-encoded (e.g. `&#2325;&#2369;...`) and need `html.unescape`.

I read the dashboard's own JavaScript to confirm the field-to-column mapping rather than guessing: position 1 `s_no`, 2 `HMLAName`, 3 `const_name`, 4 `no_proposed`, 5 `no_fs`, 6 `no_cc`, 7 `avail_amt`, 8 `recommended_amt`, 9 `fs_amt`, 10 `remain_amt` — i.e. the tenth JSON key `remain_amt` renders under the column **headed `व्यय राशि` (expenditure)**. The key name and the column semantics disagree, so a consumer should treat `remain_amt` as the expenditure slot as displayed, and be cautious.

Totals I computed from the live response (200 MLAs, all financial years combined) give a sense of scale: **58,791 works approved, 35,027 financially sanctioned, 9,959 completed; ₹1,150.06 crore recommended, ₹1,947.15 crore financially sanctioned, ₹3,116.00 crore available, ₹939.15 crore in the expenditure column.** Sample row: `कुमारी रीटा चौधरी | मण्डावा | proposed 423 | recommended ₹773.49 lakh | sanctioned 236 | sanctioned ₹1,359.81 lakh | completed 51 | available ₹1,517.78 lakh`.

Beyond the per-MLA summary, RDIWMS also publishes **per-work** report tables at **https://rdprwms.rajasthan.gov.in/iwmsweb/Pdmn/rpt_schemewise_other_scheme_district.aspx** (curl-200; a `scheme=yes&scode=9` MLALAD variant and a `scode=10` MPLAD variant exist). One such report I fetched returned 1.45 MB with 191 rows and columns `S.No. | Work Id | Work Name | MP Name | MLA Name | District Name | Block Name | Panchayat Name | Work Recipt Date | AS No. | AS Amount | AS Date | TS No. | TS Amount | TS Date | FS No. | FS Amount | FS Date | Expected Completion Time | Expected Date` — i.e. **per-MLA, per-work sanction-stage detail with amounts and dates**, including rows naming individual MLAs and their constituencies. Some parameter combinations return only the report shell with no rows, so these report URLs are fragile and must be driven through the dashboard's own filters.

Note precisely what is **not** published as a standalone number: there is no public per-MLA **sanctioned allocation limit** (the "₹5 crore/year" or "₹10 crore" figures that circulate in the press are quoted only in news reports and in the `आवंटित राशि` *component* of the available-amount formula, never as a labelled per-MLA entitlement). The official scheme-rules side exists only as departmental PDFs — e.g. **https://finance.rajasthan.gov.in/PDFDOCS/NEWSINFO/AppropriationReply12Mar2025_14347.pdf** (curl-200, `application/pdf`, 966,827 bytes, 40 pages, `/Font`=74, `/Image`=100 — text PDF) which discusses MLA LAD scheme rules in a legislative appropriation reply. A Planning Department MLALAD evaluation study is cited at `https://plan.rajasthan.gov.in/content/dam/planning-portal/DEO/Reports/Evaluation%20Studies/2007-2008/Full%20StudyNo-415.pdf`, but that path returned the portal's HTML shell (12,288 bytes `text/html`), not a PDF, on 2026-09-14 — so the Planning Department guidelines page is **not directly fetchable** in my testing and no live government-resolution HTML page for the scheme was found.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Questions (starred / unstarred): PUBLISHED, per-member, queryable, and machine-readable.** The house's business system lives on a separate host, **https://rlaoasys.rajasthan.gov.in/QuestionsWeb/**, whose pages carry "Site designed & hosted by National Informatics Centre / Contents provided by Rajasthan Legislative Assembly Secretariat, Jaipur". The member-wise page is **https://rlaoasys.rajasthan.gov.in/QuestionsWeb/MemberWise.aspx** (curl-200). It offers `विधान सभा सदन` (house: 16, 15, 14, 13, 12, 11), `विधान सभा सत्र` (session) and `सदस्य का नाम` (member name — 209 options). The **question types are exactly the ones the project needs**, taken from the site's own `QuestionWise.aspx` type dropdown: **`तारांकित` (starred), `अतारांकित` (unstarred), `अन्तः सत्र` (intra-session)**.

I POSTed this WebForms form successfully and got real per-member rows. For house 16, session 2, member `श्री अंशुमान सिंह भाटी` the result table had **79 data rows — 57 unstarred and 22 starred**; for `श्री अनिल कुमार शर्मा`, **10 rows — 7 unstarred and 3 starred**. Columns are `क्र.सं. | प्रश्न सं. | प्रश्न का प्रकार | आइटम नं. | प्रश्न का विषय | प्रश्न प्राप्ति तिथि | सूचीबद्ध तिथि | विभाग का नाम`. Session 3 (the sitting session at the time of research) returned no rows — the current session's questions are instead redirected to NeVA: the page links out to **https://raj.neva.gov.in** with the note "(16वीं विधान सभा के तृतीय सत्र एवं आगे के सत्रों के प्रश्नों हेतु यहां Click करें)" — "for questions of the 16th Assembly's third session and onward, click here". So questions are split: **sessions up to 2 live in the legacy NIC portal; session 3 onward live in NeVA.** Related pages on the same host: `QuestionWise.aspx` (by type), `DateWise.aspx`, `DepartmentWise.aspx`, `SessionWise.aspx`, `AdvanceSearch.aspx`, and Motions at `https://rlaoasys.rajasthan.gov.in/QuestionsWeb/Motions_Informations.aspx`.

**Attendance: ADVERTISED BUT NOT RETRIEVABLE.** The assembly hosts a "Members Attendance Information System" section with four sub-reports — `Datewise Attendance Status`, `Datewise Total Attendence`, `Session Wise Attendence`, and **`Member Wise Attendance`** — at **https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/MemberAttendance/DatewiseAttendanceStatus.aspx** (curl-200, 14,402 bytes) and **https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/MemberAttendance/MemberWiseAttendance.aspx** (curl-200, 15,363 bytes). Both are stub pages whose only control is a `सदन संख्या` (house number: 16, 15, 14, 13) dropdown wired to an ASP.NET `__doPostBack`. **Every postback I attempted returned `HTTP 302` to `/404?aspxerrorpath=/MemberAttendance/...`** — a server-side error, reproduced with a full browser-equivalent form body (all hidden fields plus the selected dropdown value) and with a warm session cookie. A control POST of a non-postback body to the same pages returns `HTTP 200`, so the host is not blocking POST wholesale; the postback handler itself is failing. The attendance section therefore renders as a navigation shell and **no attendance figure is obtainable** from the official site.

**Private members' bills: PAGES EXIST BUT PUBLISH NOTHING.** **https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/Containers/Legislation/PrivateBillsIntroduced.aspx** (curl-200, 102,313 bytes) and **https://assembly.rajasthan.gov.in/Containers/Legislation/PrivateBillPassed.aspx** (curl-200, 102,814 bytes) both render with a full site chrome but **zero data rows** (one `<tr>` total, the layout row) and no PDF links. The sibling pages `GovernmentBills.aspx` and `Ordinance.aspx` exist on the same path. So "private members' bills introduced / passed" is, on the official record, empty for the current house — indistinguishable from "not yet populated".

**Proceedings and debates are separately published** at **https://rlaaprise.rajasthan.gov.in/** (curl-200): `MemberWiseList.aspx` (curl-200, 90 KB), `SubjectWiseList.aspx`, `DateWiseMemberList.aspx`, `DepartmentWiseList.aspx`, `HouseProceedingView.aspx` and `MemberVideoView.aspx`. These are member-wise proceeding indexes, not attendance.
