### Format note

A fetcher needs a **mixed** toolkit. The single best source is a **JSON API over POST**: `get_mla_data` takes `{"a_no":16,"fin_yr":0,"dist_code":0}` with `Content-Type: application/json;charset=utf-8` and a `Referer` of the dashboard page, and returns all 200 MLAs with fund figures in one 96 KB response. Profile data is a **plain HTML table** parseable with curl + any HTML parser (`ConstituencyWise.aspx`), with photos as 200 directly addressable JPEGs — no session, no captcha. Questions require an **ASP.NET WebForms POST**: you must first GET the page, harvest `__VIEWSTATE`, `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR` and `__EVENTVALIDATION`, then POST every form control including the dropdowns with their selected values and set `__EVENTTARGET` to the control id; I verified this works and returns per-member rows with the starred/unstarred type. Two things will bite: **the assembly host's own postbacks are broken** (`302 → /404?aspxerrorpath=...`) for both the attendance reports and the member search, so any scraper must not depend on them; and **`rlaoasys.rajasthan.gov.in` presents a TLS chain Python's default trust store rejects** (`CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED: self signed certificate in certificate chain`) while macOS `curl` succeeds — plan for a `--cacert` / relaxed-verify path. ECI (`www.eci.gov.in`) is a **JS-rendered React SPA** whose `/api/*` paths return the SPA shell to a plain GET and to a JSON POST alike, so it needs a headless browser or endpoint reverse-engineering; the stable official alternative is the CEO Rajasthan **text PDFs**, which need PDF text extraction (they carry real fonts, so no OCR), with the caveat that the 2023 statistical report is ~111 MB. **No OCR is required anywhere in Rajasthan.**

### Gap note

Three things cannot be sourced officially. **First and most important, MLA attendance**: the assembly advertises a four-report "Members Attendance Information System" including `Member Wise Attendance`, but every query endpoint returns `302 → /404`, so no attendance percentage or sitting count exists in any retrievable official form — and NeVA's member page also renders "No Records Founds" for its attendance-adjacent tabs. **Second, private members' bills**: the official `PrivateBillsIntroduced` and `PrivateBillPassed` pages render empty, so a per-MLA private-bill record is simply not published (whether because none were introduced or because the page was never populated is not determinable from the site). **Third, a published per-MLA sanctioned allocation limit** ("entitlement" / ceiling): RDIWMS exposes the allocated amount only as one unnamed component inside `उपलब्ध राशि` (available amount = initial + other receipts + allocated), so the total available figure is official but the annual entitlement behind it is not separately published. Two further caveats: the fund dashboard's JSON key `remain_amt` renders under a column headed `व्यय राशि` (expenditure), so the API naming and the rendered semantics disagree and the field must be handled deliberately; and the ECI December-2023 assembly results archive for Rajasthan (`AcResultGenDecNew2023/statewiseS20.htm`) now returns 404, so **the durable official election-results source is the CEO Rajasthan statistical-report PDF, not the ECI results subdomain**.

### Platform

**Rajasthan uses a shared national platform — this is the headline finding.** The house's current-facing portal is a **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)** instance at **https://raj.neva.gov.in/**, built by the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs and NIC ("© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved | Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India"). The national NeVA page at **https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA** enumerates **40 `*.neva.gov.in` subdomains** — including `raj`, `upvs`, `upcl`, `mhla`, `mhlc`, `kerala`, `gujarat`, `odisha`, `tnla`, `wbla`, `mpla`, `hpvs`, `tsla`, `loksabha`, `rajyasabha` and `cms` — so **one scraper written against the NeVA member-detail route (`/<state>/Member/Details/<id>`) and the shared asset CMS (`https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/<STATE>/FileStructures/Member/<id>.jpg`) serves every adopting state**. Reinforcing the shared-template conclusion: the Rajasthan member page's breadcrumb literally still reads "eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh", a leftover from the common template. Rajasthan's older host set (`assembly.rajasthan.gov.in`, `rlaoasys.rajasthan.gov.in`, `rlaaprise.rajasthan.gov.in`) is a **bespoke NIC-built ASP.NET WebForms stack** — all three credit "National Informatics Centre" and all three share the same postback idioms and the same broken-postback defect — and the fund portal (`ework.rajasthan.gov.in` / `rdprwms.rajasthan.gov.in`, RDIWMS v3.0.11.21, "designed and developed by National Informatics Center, Rajasthan") is a separate NIC-built state system. So Rajasthan is mid-migration: **NeVA for the current session onward, legacy bespoke NIC WebForms for everything before it — and the fund data lives in neither, but in the RDPR department's own RDIWMS.**

---

## Sikkim (IN-SK)

**Verdict:** partial - Sikkim has a legislature whose official website is the NIC/Ministry-of-Parliamentary-Affairs National e-Vidhan Application instance at https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/, which does publish a full 32-member roster with party, constituency and photograph, but it publishes no attendance data, no retrievable question records, no private-members'-bills records, and Sikkim operates no MLA local area development fund at all.

### Profile
**Official legislature site: `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/`** (curl-200, HTTP 200, `text/html`, 70,050 bytes). The page title is `Welcome to Sikkim Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application`; the masthead reads **"Sikkim Legislative Assembly / सिक्किम विधान सभा / ELEVENTH ASSEMBLY"**. The footer credits *"© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India — Content managed by: Sikkim Legislative Assembly"*, Version `2.0.2`, Last Updated 25 Mar 2026. There is no separate bespoke Sikkim assembly domain: `www.sikkimassembly.nic.in`, `sikkimlegislativeassembly.nic.in`, `sikkimvidhansabha.nic.in`, `sikkimassembly.gov.in`, `sikkimlegislativeassembly.gov.in`, `assembly.sikkim.gov.in` and `slasikkim.nic.in` **all fail DNS resolution** (curl exit 6), and the Wayback Machine CDX API returns **zero captures** for `sikkimassembly.nic.in` and `sikkimlegislativeassembly.nic.in` (`archive.org/wayback/available` → `"archived_snapshots": {}`), so no legacy bespoke site ever existed under those names.

**Member data — what is published.** The Members module (`https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`) is a JS-driven page. Its data endpoint is a **GET returning an HTML fragment**:

```
https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=0&MSLT=0&Assemblyid=11
```

With an ASP.NET session cookie this returned **HTTP 200, 401,993 bytes** and a card list of **32 members**. Each card carries: **photograph**, **name**, **party**, **constituency name**, **date of birth**, **residential address**, **mobile phone number** and **email** (obfuscated as `aditya[dot]golay[at]sikkim[dot]gov[dot]in`). Example raw card:

```html
<a class="card" href="/Member/Details/60?AssemblyID=11">
  <div class="card-header">
    <img src="https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/SK/FileStructures//Member/thumb/01_18_2025_12_00_44.png" alt="ADITYA GOLAY">
    <h3><span>SHRI ADITYA GOLAY </span></h3>
    <h6>SIKKIM KRANTIKARI MORCHA </h6>
  </div>
  <div class="card-body">
    <table><tr><td class="value-lable">Constituency Name :</td><td> SORENG-CHAKUNG -SORENG (SIKKIM) </td></tr>
           <tr><td class="value-lable">Date of Birth :</td><td>15 Aug 1990</td></tr></table>
    <ul class="icon-info"><li>Lumsey, Tadong, Gangtok</li><li>9832629091</li>
        <li>aditya[dot]golay[at]sikkim[dot]gov[dot]in</li></ul>
```

So **name = yes, party = yes, constituency = yes, photo = yes, term/assembly = yes** ("ELEVENTH ASSEMBLY" is a hard-coded masthead; the endpoint takes `Assemblyid=11` for the 11th, and the sidebar exposes `Assemblyid` values 4,5,6,7,8,9,10,11 for the Fourth–Eleventh Assemblies). Party representation on the homepage is returned as a doughnut-chart payload `[{"y":32,"name":"SKM"}]` — i.e. all 32 seats SKM in the Eleventh Assembly.

**Per-member photos** are direct image URLs under `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/SK/FileStructures//Member/thumb/…` (curl-200, `content_type: image/png`; the sampled file `01_18_2025_12_00_44.png` is actually JPEG data, 85×83 px). Per-member detail pages exist at `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/{id}?AssemblyID=11` (curl-200, 35,227 bytes for id 60). **Important gotcha:** `/Member/Details/{id}` *without* `?AssemblyID=…` renders an empty stub that hard-codes `MemberId='0'`, and `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=…&AssemblyID=…` returns a `BLANK` placeholder — the working route is the card-list endpoint above.

**Secondary official roster.** The Government of Sikkim state portal publishes a plain, cookie-free HTML table at **`https://www.sikkim.gov.in/mygovernment/whos-who/mlas`** (curl-200, 72,107 bytes, "Last Update : 11-09-2026"). It has exactly three columns — `Name | Position | Constituency` — for all 32 members (e.g. `SHRI. SONAM LAMA | Hon'ble MLA | Sangha`). It publishes **no party** and **no photo**, and the rows are text only (no per-member links, no images in the table).

**Format summary for the profile layer:** NeVA Sikkim member data = **HTML fragment from a GET endpoint, requires a session cookie, embeds direct image URLs** (not JSON, not PDF). State-portal roster = **plain static HTML table**.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
**No MLA local area development fund exists in Sikkim, and therefore no per-MLA fund portal exists.**

This is a negative finding built on primary budget documents rather than on absence of search results. I downloaded and text-extracted **all 51 department "Demands for Grants" PDFs for 2026-27** from the Government of Sikkim Finance Department (`http://sikkimfred.gov.in/Budget_2026-27/Demands_for_grants2026-27.aspx`, PDFs at `.../Documents/DemandForGrants2026-27/pdf/N. DemX.pdf`, all curl-200, text PDFs, `/Font` objects present, `/Image` count ~2 — not scans). A word-boundary full-text search across the entire corpus returned:

- `\bMLA\b` — **0 matches**
- `\bMLAs\b` — **0 matches**
- `MLALAD` — **0 matches**
- `Vidhayak Nidhi` / `विधायक निधि` — **0 matches**
- `Constituency Development` — **0 matches**
- `Local Area Development` — **1 match only**, in `45. Dem43.pdf`, and it is not for MLAs.

The single match is in Demand No. 43 (Rural Management & Development / Panchayat), and reads in full:

```
63 Local Area Development Fund for Adhakshya and Upadhakshya
63.00.49 Other Revenue Expenditure  1800 1800 1800 3600
Total 63 Local Area Development Fund for Adhakshya and Upadhakshya  1800 1800 1800 3600
```

That is a **panchayat-head** (Adhakshya / Upadhakshya) local area development fund of ₹18.00 lakh (2025-26 RE) rising to ₹36.00 lakh (2026-27 BE) — it is explicitly *not* an MLA fund, and it is not per-MLA.

**Demand No. 24 "LEGISLATURE"** (the assembly's own demand, `25. Dem24.pdf`) was read in full. Its complete revenue section is: Speaker & Deputy Speaker salaries/allowances/travel/medical; **Discretionary Grants** by the Speaker (₹50.00 lakh), by the Deputy Speaker (₹45.00 lakh) and by the Chairman, Financial Committee (₹10–20 lakh); **Members** salaries ₹1.98 crore, allowances ₹1.45 crore, domestic travel, foreign travel, medical, other revenue expenditure ₹56.47 lakh; Legislative Secretariat establishment; Legislator's Hostel; a contribution to the Regional Institute of Parliamentary Studies and Training for the North-East; and pensions to ex-members. **There is no allocated limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure line for any per-MLA development fund, and no such head anywhere in the state budget.**

The **budget speeches** corroborate this: `CM Speech 2026.pdf` (813 KB, 114,061 chars extracted) contains **0** occurrences of `\bMLA\b`, `\bMLAs\b`, `Local Area Development`, or `Vidhayak`; `HCM Speech(Budget) English 2025.pdf` (234 KB, 98,781 chars) contains **0** occurrences of `\bMLA\b` and **0** of `Local Area Development` (one rhetorical mention of "MLAs" in a historical passage).

The **statutory position** agrees. The Law & Parliamentary Affairs Department's official list of rules touching MLAs (`https://www.sikkim.gov.in/department/departmentmenudetails?url=Menu%3dlaw-parliamentary-affairs-department%2flist-of-rules-and-regulations`, curl-200) contains only: *The Sikkim Ministers, Speaker, Deputy Speaker and Members of the Sikkim Legislative Assembly (Travelling Allowance) Rules, 1977*; *The Sikkim Legislative Assembly Members (Residential Accommodation) Rules, 1981*; and *The Sikkim Legislative Assembly Members (Payment of Pension) Rules, 1984*. **No area-development-fund rules, no constituency-development-fund scheme rules.**

**Fund name in Sikkim: none — there is no such fund.** The nearest state-level analogue that actually exists is the **MPLADS** scheme for Sikkim's single Member of Parliament, which is a Government of India national scheme with the national portal at `https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/` (curl-200) — that covers the MP, not MLAs, and must not be presented as an MLA figure.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
The NeVA Sikkim instance has the **module scaffolding** for all four record types, but in every case the publicly queryable record set came back empty.

- **Starred questions — module present, no data retrievable.** `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/Questions` (curl-200 with session cookie, 19,394 bytes) exposes the categories **Starred Question, UnStarred Question, Short Notice Question, Inter Session Questions**, plus a `List` group (**Starred, Unstarred, Short Notice**) and a **Reply** group (**Reply Starred, Reply UnStarred, Reply Short Notice**). The endpoints are `/Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem=…&Type=…` (curl-200, 64,900-byte filter form) and `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionBySearch?AssId=&SessId=&SessDateId=`. I resolved the real IDs from the page's own `<option>` values (AssId **11** = ELEVENTH ASSEMBLY; SessId **1–10**; SessDateId **273–276**) and queried `AssId=11&SessId=10&SessDateId=276`, `AssId=11&SessId=9&SessDateId=0`, and `/Questions/FetchUnListQuestions?AssId=11&SessId=10` — **every one returned `No Records Founds`** (HTTP 200, ~600 bytes). Per-member question lookup `/Member/Member_Question?MemberId=10&AssemblyID=11` and `MemberId=53` likewise returned `No Records Founds`. The homepage counter claims **Questions = 28** portal-wide, but that count is not backed by a retrievable public record set through these endpoints.
- **Unstarred questions — same status: module present, `No Records Founds`.**
- **Private members' bills — filter present, no records.** `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/Bill` (curl-200, 35,377 bytes) exposes `Government Bill / Private Members Bills`, bill categories (Money, Financial, Ordinary, Constitution Amendment, Ordinance Replacing), statuses (Assented, Passed, Pending, Withdrawn, Negatived, Lapsed, Introduced) and assembly/session/year selectors. The search endpoint `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch` returned **`No Records Founds`** (HTTP 200, 580 bytes) for the default query, for `AssemblyID=11&FromYear=2024&ToYear=2026`, and for the default with `AssemblyID=0`; two other filter combinations returned HTTP 500. The homepage counter claims **Government Bills = 12**. `Private Members Bills` (BillTypeId=2) returned HTTP 500 rather than data.
- **Attendance — NOT PUBLISHED AT ALL.** There is no attendance module anywhere on NeVA Sikkim: the Members module sidebar exposes Member by Assembly, Panel of Chairpersons, Council of Ministers, Whip, Birthday, Male/Female Member, Nominated, First Time Elected Members, Members Elected in Bye-Election, Political Party, Constituency Name, Resigned/Deceased/Disqualified Members, Caste Category wise, Ex-Members, Amenities, Members Booklet and Who's Who — **and no attendance or voting-record section**. No per-member attendance figure is published by the assembly, by the state portal, or anywhere else official.
- **Debates** are published: `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/Debates` (curl-200) offers **Corrected Debates** and **Uncorrected Debates**, with the data endpoint `/Debates/FetchDataList?LstItem=&Type=` returning a House/Session filter form (7,875 bytes). These are session documents, **not per-member records**.
- **Committee records:** `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/Committee` (curl-200) offers Committee Formation / Committee Meetings / Committee Reports; `/Committee/DataList?ItemId=2` (curl-200, 25,063 bytes) exposes category (Annual Activities Report, Standing Committee Report, C&AG Reports, Other Committees Report) and type (Financial, Legislative, Committee of Privileges…) filters, but `/Committee/FetchCommitteeReport?ItemId=2&Year=2025-2026&CommitteeTypeId=0&CommitteeCategoryId=0` returned **`No Records Founds`**. Homepage counter: **Committee Reports = 40**.
- **Digital Library** exists at `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/Home/DigitalLibrary` (curl-200, 19,647 bytes).

Session documents (List of Business, notices, notifications) *are* published as PDFs on the NeVA CMS at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/SK/FileStructures//…` — but these are aggregate House documents, not per-member records.

### Format note
A fetcher needs **no browser and no OCR, but it does need a cookie jar**. NeVA Sikkim issues `Set-Cookie: ASP.NET_SessionId=…` and any module page requested cold returns **HTTP 302 → `/Home/NeVA`** (which is the national NeVA landing page, 192,345 bytes, titled "National e-Vidhan Application - Digital Legislators"). The working sequence verified here is: `GET https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/` then `GET https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec` with `-c/-b cookiejar.txt`, after which `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=0&MSLT=0&Assemblyid=11`, `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Debates`, `/Committee`, `/Today` and `/Business` all return HTTP 200 with real content. All data endpoints are **GET-returning-HTML-fragments** — there is no JSON/XHR API on the NeVA Sikkim instance (the only JSON seen is an inline `CanvasJS` doughnut payload for party representation, `[{"y":32,"name":"SKM"}]`, embedded in the homepage HTML). Member photographs are plain image URLs on the separate `cms.neva.gov.in` host and need no cookie. Specific blockers: (1) the session-cookie requirement with a 302-to-national-portal failure mode, which silently looks like "no data"; (2) `/Member/Details/{id}` renders an empty stub unless the `?AssemblyID=` query parameter is present — the server-side route ignores the path id; (3) `/ContactDirectory/PDFforHonbleMember` and `/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember` (the roster "Download Pdf" / "Download Excel" links) return **HTTP 500** even with a valid session, so the advertised bulk exports are broken; (4) `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch` returns HTTP 500 for several filter combinations instead of an empty set; (5) some selector endpoints (`/Questions/SessionList`, `/Bill/SessionList`, `/Bill/GetAllAssembliesList`) return HTTP 500 on GET and appear to require POST. Elsewhere: the Sikkim state-portal Who's-Who MLAs table is plain static HTML (`curl` straight through, no cookie). CEO Sikkim is plain ASP.NET HTML plus **text-based, pypdf-extractable PDFs** (the 2024 elected-candidates PDF yielded all 32 constituency / candidate / party rows with `pypdf`, `/Font` objects present, not a scan). The Finance Department budget corpus is also text PDFs (no OCR). The **only** browser-only source is ECI: `https://www.eci.gov.in/` is a React SPA that serves a 1,279-byte "enable JavaScript" shell for every path, and the ECI results host now redirects to the current election index with the 2024 assembly archive paths returning 404.

### Gap note
Three real gaps, one of them total:

1. **No MLA development fund exists to source.** Sikkim operates **no** MLA local area development fund, Vidhayak Nidhi, Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi or equivalent. There is therefore **no per-MLA allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed, or expenditure figure** anywhere in official Sikkim data — not because a portal is missing, but because the scheme is absent from the state budget, from the budget speeches, and from the Law Department's register of MLA-related rules. The Election Atlas must render this section as an explicit, cited "no such scheme in this state" rather than as missing data, and must not substitute MPLADS figures (which cover Sikkim's Member of Parliament, a different office). The nearest genuine state fund is the panchayat-level *Local Area Development Fund for Adhakshya and Upadhakshya* (₹18 lakh RE 2025-26 / ₹36 lakh BE 2026-27), which is **not** per-MLA and not usable for this section.
2. **No attendance data of any kind.** NeVA Sikkim has no attendance module, no division/voting record, and no per-member sitting record. Sikkim MLAs' attendance in the Vidhan Sabha cannot be sourced officially — the only published attendance-style statistics for Sikkim exist for the **Member of Parliament**, produced by third parties (e.g. PRS India), and those are both off-target and unofficial.
3. **Question and bill records are advertised but not served.** The portal's own homepage counters state Questions = 28, Government Bills = 12, Committee Reports = 40, Paper-Laid = 316, Members = 32 — yet every public query endpoint for questions (`FetchDataListQuestionBySearch`, `FetchUnListQuestions`, per-member `Member_Question`) and for bills (`FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, including the `Private Members Bills` filter) returned `No Records Founds` or HTTP 500. So per-member starred/unstarred question counts and the existence or content of any private member's bill **cannot currently be established from official sources**. The module structure proves Sikkim *intends* to publish these records; the record set is not retrievable today. Session-level documents (List of Business, notices, notifications, debates) *are* published on the NeVA CMS at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/SK/FileStructures/…` and could be parsed to reconstruct member activity indirectly, but that is a document-mining exercise, not a published per-member record.

A minor fourth gap: the roster's advertised bulk exports (`/ContactDirectory/PDFforHonbleMember` and `/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember`) are broken with HTTP 500, so the 32-member roster must be scraped from the `FetchMembersList` HTML fragment.

### Platform
**Common platform, not bespoke — national NIC template.** Sikkim's assembly runs on the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, a Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs + National Informatics Centre product, evidenced by (a) the page footer on `sikkim.neva.gov.in`: *"© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India — Content managed by: Sikkim Legislative Assembly"*, version `2.0.2`; (b) the `neva.gov.in` homepage listing **40+ sibling instances sharing identical markup and URL structure** — `sikkim.neva.gov.in`, `gujarat.neva.gov.in`, `kerala.neva.gov.in`, `manipur.neva.gov.in`, `mizoram`, `nagaland`, `odisha`, `raj`, `tripura`, `hpvs`, `upvs`, `wbla`, `mhla`, `tnla`, `tsla`, `goa`, `puddu`, `loksabha`, `rajyasabha`, `pvs`, `jkla`, `jhla`, `asla`, `arla`, `apa`, `bla`, `klc` and more; (c) identical route names across instances — `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Debates`, `/Committee`, `/Member/Details/{id}`, `/Today`, `/Business` — and the same AJAX endpoint names (`/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`, `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`) on the Sikkim instance as on other state instances; (d) the NeVA implementation-status page on `neva.gov.in` marking **Sikkim as "Digital House — Live on NeVA Platform"**; and (e) a shared backend host, `cms.neva.gov.in`, holding every state's files under a two-letter unit prefix (`/NeVA/SK/FileStructures/…` for Sikkim, `/NeVA/GJ/…` for Gujarat). Practically all NeVA houses use the same unit-code substitution (`currentUnit = 'SK'`, `UnitCode = 'SK'`) hard-coded in otherwise identical JavaScript — meaning **one scraper, parameterised on the unit code, serves every NeVA state**. Sikkim has no bespoke assembly site and never had one: `sikkimassembly.nic.in` has zero Wayback captures. Sikkim's *election* infrastructure is separate from this: `ceo.sikkim.gov.in` is a **bespoke state CEO site** (its own ASP.NET application with `/DownloadsPublic/ResultList`, `/DownloadsPublic/ReportList`, `/Election/ElectionDetails`, not an ECI common template), while the ECI national layer (`eci.gov.in`, a JS SPA) sits above it.

---

## Tamil Nadu (IN-TN)

**Verdict:** partial - the profile roster (name / party / constituency / PHOTO, all 234 members) and the assembly election results are fully official and curl-fetchable as plain HTML, but the assembly record exists only as per-sitting-day debate/question PDFs with no per-member attendance, questions or private-members'-bill data, and the MLACDS development fund has NO public per-MLA portal (scheme rules and aggregate sanctions only).

### Profile

Tamil Nadu is unicameral (the Legislative Council was abolished in 1986); the 17th Tamil Nadu Legislative Assembly was constituted after the May-2026 general election. The official site is **https://assembly.tn.gov.in/** (curl-200, `text/html`, 39,469 bytes). The member roster lives at **https://assembly.tn.gov.in/17thassembly/members.php** (curl-200, 141,080 bytes) and is a **single plain HTML table of all 234 members in one page** - I parsed 234 data rows and 228 `<img>` entries pointing at `members/NNN.jpg`. Columns are: Serial No. | NAME (with party abbreviation AND constituency in parentheses) | ADDRESS | TEL NO.(R) | Email Address | PHOTO. So name, party and constituency are all present in one text cell (e.g. `1 Aadhav Arjuna Hon. Thiru. (TVK) (Minister) (Villivakkam)`), and the term/assembly number is implied by the URL path of the page. Photos are served at the fully predictable pattern **https://assembly.tn.gov.in/17thassembly/members/NNN.jpg** - I fetched `014.jpg` and got a real baseline JPEG, 91x130 px, 1,834 bytes, curl-200. There is **no per-member detail page**: the roster is one alphabetical list, and no `member.php?id=` style links exist anywhere in the markup. A PDF rendering of the same roster exists at **https://assembly.tn.gov.in/17thassembly_members.pdf** (curl-200, 83,614 bytes, 8 pages) whose text extracts cleanly in modern PDF tooling and yields `SL | CONSTITUENCY | NAME | PARTY | EMAIL` - I extracted 14,013 characters, e.g. `30 PALLAVARAM J.KAMATCHI TVK mlapallavaram@tn.gov.in`. Party strength is also published at https://assembly.tn.gov.in/17thassembly/partyposition.php. Note that a ready cross-check of the profile data exists on the Chief Electoral Officer's own site (see Election results below). Verification level: curl-200, fetched and parsed. The one caveat is freshness - the site is hand-maintained PHP (`members.php` is a static includes-based page), so it reflects the position as at its last upload rather than a live database.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund in Tamil Nadu is **NOT** called Vidhayak Nidhi or MLALAD. Its official name is the **Member of Legislative Assembly Constituency Development Scheme (MLACDS)**, administered by the **Rural Development and Panchayat Raj Department** (RDPR). The scheme description page is **https://www.tnrd.tn.gov.in/schemes/st_mlacds.html** (curl-200; also reachable as https://tnrd.tn.gov.in/schemes/st_mlacds.php). It states the scheme is fully state-funded, that "each MLA identifies and proposes the works to be executed in his Constituency", and that the allocation rose from Rs.1.75 crore to **Rs.2.00 crore per Constituency per annum from 2011-2012**, split into a Tied Component of Rs.1.125 crore (earmarked by Government) and Rs.0.875 crore of MLA's choice. The current rules are in **G.O.(Ms.) No.145, Rural Development and Panchayat Raj (SGS.1) Department, dated 10.12.2021**, published as a PDF at **https://tnrd.tn.gov.in/project/go_files/3_99999_2021_145.pdf** (curl-200, 25,237,288 bytes, 34 pages). I downloaded and text-extracted it: it is an **OCR'd (searchable) PDF**, not a raw scan - 52,709 characters extracted across all 34 pages. It documents the allocation history - Rs.2.00 crore (2019-20) → **Rs.2.50 crore** → **Rs.3.00 crore** per constituency per annum, reduced back to Rs.2 crore for 2020-21 (Rs.1 crore Tied + Rs.1 crore Untied) by way of appropriation reduction, with Rs.235 crore diverted to the Health Department for COVID-19 detection through TNMSC. It contains the scheme guidelines, the eligible-works lists, and the aggregate administrative sanction for the year - **but no per-constituency line items**. I searched for a dedicated per-MLA portal and found none: I probed `mlacds.tn.gov.in` and `tnmlacds.tn.gov.in` (both DNS-fail / HTTP 000, i.e. non-existent), `data.tn.gov.in` (HTTP 000, unreachable), and the national OGD platform data.gov.in (curl-200, no MLACDS dataset surfaced). The RDPR site publishes annual **Policy Notes** which discuss MLACDS only in aggregate (https://www.tnrd.tn.gov.in/policynotes.html lists them; e.g. https://www.tnrd.tn.gov.in/policynotes/rd1_e_pn_2024_25.pdf, curl-200, 251 pages, from which I extracted 145,580 characters), and the department publishes MLACDS Government Orders per year under the `tnrd.tn.gov.in/project/go_files/` path, but that directory returns **HTTP 403** on listing so the GOs can only be found by direct URL. The RTI proactive-disclosure handbook at https://www.tn.gov.in/rti/proactive/rural/handbook_RD_PR.pdf (curl-200, 5,397,543 bytes) also describes the scheme. **Bottom line: the MLACDS exists as a fully documented state scheme with published rules, eligibility and per-constituency entitlement (Rs.2-3 crore/year), but the Government of Tamil Nadu publishes NO per-MLA data - no works recommended, no works sanctioned, no works completed and no expenditure by member or by constituency.** It is aggregate-only.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Attendance: not published.** **Starred and unstarred questions: not published as per-member data.** **Private members' bills: not published.** I probed the assembly site directly for these - `questions.php`, `attendance.php`, `17thassembly/questions.php`, `17thassembly/bills.php`, `16thassembly/questions.php`, `questions/questions.php` and `mla_questions.php` all returned **HTTP 404** with a 16-byte body. The assembly's Documents menu (https://assembly.tn.gov.in/documents/menu.php) publishes only handbooks, rules, salary Acts, facilities forms, ex-MLA pension forms and committee brochures - I enumerated every PDF link on it and there is not one question list, attendance register or bill list among them. What the Assembly DOES publish is the **full verbatim record per sitting day**: the Debates index at https://assembly.tn.gov.in/debates/debates_menu.php (curl-200, 121,006 bytes) links several hundred PDFs under `./pdfdocs/NNN-DDMMYY.pdf`, running from 2011 to May 2026. I downloaded the 19-02-2026 sitting (https://assembly.tn.gov.in/debates/pdfdocs/160-19022026.pdf, curl-200, 1,714,246 bytes, **345 pages**) and it is a text PDF, not a scan - 603,560 characters extract cleanly, with only 5 of 345 pages near-empty, and its first page confirms "TAMIL NADU LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY DEBATES / OFFICIAL REPORT". Crucially, **the Tamil text in these PDFs is legacy-encoded (TSCII/TAB-style pre-Unicode Tamil fonts) and does not round-trip**: the same file yields `வியாாழக்கிழமை` where the correct text is `வியாழக்கிழமை`, i.e. vowel signs and some consonants are silently lost. The English portions extract correctly, but Tamil losses are silent, which matters because starred questions are tabled in Tamil. A parallel and more current channel exists on the **NeVA national platform**: the Tamil Nadu NeVA homepage (https://tnla.neva.gov.in/) carries a Notice Board whose items link to per-day starred-question PDFs on the national NeVA CMS, e.g. **https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/TN/FileStructures//Notices/17_1_S_03-09-2026.pdf** ("Starred Questions, Thursday, 03 September 2026") and `.../e586d3ae-fc3b-43bc-8db6-1d8a23baca61.pdf` ("Starred Question - 01/09/2026"), alongside "List of Business" and "Brief" PDFs. I downloaded the 03-09-2026 starred-question PDF (curl-200, 251,224 bytes, 2 pages, text layer present, 1,285 characters extractable) and it exhibits exactly the same legacy-Tamil mangling - it does identify each question by member and question number (`*21 தி. மா. NM. ...`) but the text is not reliably machine-readable as Tamil. Critically, the TN NeVA portal's own menu is **not wired up**: `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Debates`, `/Today`, `/Business`, `/Committee` and `/Home/DigitalLibrary` all **HTTP 302 → /Home/NeVA** (the generic national landing page) even with the `?IsStateDashBoard=True` parameter present in the markup, `/Members` returns **404**, and only `/` (59,359 bytes), `/ContactDirectory` (15,538 bytes) and `/News` (18,060 bytes) serve state-specific content. So Tamil Nadu has no searchable question or bill database - only notice-board PDFs and the 345-page daily debate volumes. Historians' note: the assembly also maintains a separate DSpace digital repository at https://tnlasdigital.tn.gov.in/jspui/ (curl-200) and an archive of historical member lists and five-yearly "Review" PDFs at https://assembly.tn.gov.in/archive/archive_menu.php (curl-200) covering the 1st (1952) to 15th (2016) Assemblies.

| RDPR Policy Note 2025-26 (MLACDS aggregate) | https://www.tnrd.tn.gov.in/policynotes/policy_notes_eng_2025-2026.pdf | yes | fund | PDF (download timed out at 60 s; linked from official index) | linked-from-official-page |
| RDPR site map | https://www.tnrd.tn.gov.in/sitemap1.html | yes | fund / platform | HTML | curl-200 |
| TN RTI proactive disclosure - RDPR handbook | https://www.tn.gov.in/rti/proactive/rural/handbook_RD_PR.pdf | yes | fund | PDF, 5.4 MB | curl-200 |
| **Chief Electoral Officer, Tamil Nadu** | https://elections.tn.gov.in/ | yes | election-results | HTML (ASP.NET WebForms) | curl-200 |
| **CEO TN - MLAs, TN Legislative Assembly 2026** | https://elections.tn.gov.in/TNLA2026_MLA.aspx | yes | election-results / profile | HTML table, 237 rows (AC no. + name + member + party) | curl-200 |
| CEO TN - MLAs, TN Legislative Assembly 2021 | https://elections.tn.gov.in/TNLA2021_MLA.aspx | yes | election-results | HTML table | curl-200 |
| ECI - Party-wise results, Tamil Nadu (S22) | https://results.eci.gov.in/ResultAcGenMay2026/partywiseresult-S22.htm | yes | election-results | HTML table, 234 ACs | curl-200 |
| ECI - Constituency-wise candidate results (sample AC 63) | https://results.eci.gov.in/ResultAcGenMay2026/candidateswise-S2263.htm | yes | election-results | HTML (all candidates + votes) | curl-200 |
| ECI - Results landing, May-2026 | https://results.eci.gov.in/ResultAcGenMay2026/index.htm | yes | election-results | HTML (static) | curl-200 |
| Election Commission of India (main site) | https://www.eci.gov.in/ | yes | election-results | JS-rendered SPA (`<div id="root">`) | curl-200 (shell only) |
| Open Government Data Platform India | https://www.data.gov.in/ | yes | fund | searchable portal - no MLACDS dataset found | curl-200 |
| PRS India - MLA Track (Tamil Nadu) | https://prsindia.org/mlatrack/anitha-r-radhakrishnan-1 | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | assembly-record | HTML | search-result-only |

### Format note

A fetcher needs a **mixed** toolkit, but the highest-value sources are the easiest. The profile layer is pure `curl` + HTML table parse: `17thassembly/members.php` is one page containing all 234 members, and photos are at a fully predictable `members/NNN.jpg` path, so the entire roster plus images can be harvested with no browser at all. The election-results layer is likewise plain `curl` HTML - `elections.tn.gov.in/TNLA2026_MLA.aspx` (ASP.NET WebForms, but the table is server-rendered, so no `__doPostBack` interaction is needed) and `results.eci.gov.in` (static HTML, no JS). Blockers appear in three places. First, **`www.eci.gov.in` itself is a React SPA** (`<div id="root">`, `main.e468b53e.js`) and returns only a 1,279-byte shell to curl - use `results.eci.gov.in` instead, which is server-rendered. Second, the **assembly record is PDF-only and the Tamil is legacy-encoded**: the debate volumes are large (345 pages for one sitting) and although a text layer exists, Tamil vowel signs and some consonants are silently dropped, so extracting Tamil questions needs either a Tamil-legacy-to-Unicode transcoder or OCR of a re-rendered page; the English matter in the same files extracts fine. Third, **the TN NeVA portal is a shell** - its `Questions`, `Bill`, `Debates` and `Business` routes all 302 to the national landing page, so any scraper written against the shared NeVA URL pattern will silently harvest the wrong (national) page rather than erroring. The MLACDS GOs at `tnrd.tn.gov.in/project/go_files/` are OCR'd searchable PDFs, but that directory itself returns **403** on listing, so files must be addressed by exact URL. No captcha, session cookie, login or POST-only endpoint was encountered on any source used here.

### Gap note

Three things cannot be sourced officially and should be declared absent rather than approximated. (1) **MLACDS per-MLA fund data does not exist publicly.** There is no portal publishing allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure per MLA or per constituency - not on tnrd.tn.gov.in, not on tn.gov.in, not on data.gov.in. What exists is the scheme's rules and its per-constituency entitlement (Rs.2-3 crore/year, G.O.(Ms.) No.145 of 10.12.2021) plus annual aggregate allocations in Policy Notes and Budget documents. DO NOT substitute the uniform Rs.2-3 crore entitlement for a real "allocated / spent" figure - that would be inventing per-MLA numbers. The works-level data is held by District Collectors, who accord administrative sanction, and is only reachable by RTI per district. (2) **Attendance is not published at all** - not per member, not per sitting, not in aggregate. Any attendance figure for a Tamil Nadu MLA comes from a non-government source. (3) **Per-member questions and private members' bills are not published.** Starred questions are visible only as per-day PDFs (on the NeVA notice board and inside the debate volumes), so question counts per member could in principle be derived from those PDFs, but no official per-member tally exists, unstarred questions are not separately indexed, and private members' bills have no published list on any government site. Also note that **the Tamil text of every assembly PDF is legacy-encoded and lossy**, so even a derived per-member question count would rest on unreliable Tamil extraction. A further caution: the profile roster on `assembly.tn.gov.in` is manually maintained static PHP and carries no "last updated" stamp, so term/party changes may lag - the CEO's `TNLA2026_MLA.aspx` page is the better temporal anchor for the 17th Assembly.

### Platform

Tamil Nadu runs **two** platforms at once, and the distinction matters. The main data-rich site, **assembly.tn.gov.in**, is **bespoke** - a hand-built PHP template with its own asset tree (`theme/css/site.css`, `assets/css/megamenu.css`) and no counterpart anywhere else - but it is government-built by the **National Informatics Centre**, as the footer states plainly: *"Designed, Developed and Hosted by National Informatics Centre (NIC)"*. Separately, Tamil Nadu is onboarded to the **national NeVA (National e-Vidhan Application)** template at **https://tnla.neva.gov.in/**, and neva.gov.in lists it with the status **"Digital House"** and records a "Token of Appreciation" to the Tamil Nadu Assembly for successful NeVA implementation. The shared-platform evidence is conclusive: I fetched both the Tamil Nadu and Karnataka NeVA instances and their markup is identical - the same asset paths (`/2024/css/style.css`, `/2024/js/jquery.min.js`, `/2024/css/bootstrap.min.css`, `/2024/css/fontawesome.css`, `/2024/images/favicon.png`, `/2024/images/BharatWhite.svg`, `/2022/images/g20-logo.png`, plus the shared Bhashini translation script) and the same navigation routes (`/Today`, `/Business`, `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Committee`, `/Debates`, `/ContactDirectory`, `/Home/DigitalLibrary`, `/Questions?IsStateDashBoard=True`), with the **only** difference being the state background image `/2024/images/background/TN.jpg` versus `KA.jpg`. NeVA is a Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs + NIC product, and the national portal exposes the same URL convention for ~30 legislatures (`<code>.neva.gov.in`: tnla, kla, gujarat, odisha, raj, upvs, wbla, asla, bla, …). So **one scraper written against the NeVA pattern would address many states at once** - but for Tamil Nadu that scraper would currently return nothing useful, because TN's NeVA deployment is a shell: only the homepage, `/News` and `/ContactDirectory` serve state content, while `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Debates` and `/Business` 302 to the generic national page and `/Members` is a 404. The productive Tamil Nadu sources - the roster with photos, the CEO's MLA list and the ECI results pages - are on bespoke or ECI-hosted HTML and each needs its own parser.

---

## Telangana (IN-TG)

**Verdict:** partial - the official Telangana Legislature site publishes a complete, machine-readable 119-MLA roster with party, constituency and per-MLA photographs plus full personal profiles, and election results are official from both ECI and the State CEO, but there is NO official per-MLA development-fund (CDF) portal of any kind and no attendance or private-members'-bill data per member.

### Profile

The official website of the Telangana Legislature is **https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/** (verified: curl-200, `text/html`, 462,102 bytes, `<title>Legislative Assembly - Legislative Assembly - Telangana-Legislature</title>`). Telangana is bicameral; this single site hosts both the Legislative Assembly (Vidhan Sabha) and the Legislative Council (Vidhan Parishad), with the Council on parallel routes (`/council`, `/councilmemprofile`, `/councilSessions`). The site is a bespoke server-rendered Java application (Tomcat-style 404 pages, Struts-style `.do` endpoints on sub-hosts, base64-encoded `?q=` query parameters).

**No HTML tables are required to scrape the roster.** The members page **https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/assemblymemprofile** (curl-200, 274,438 bytes, 135 `<img>` tags) renders **all 119 MLAs of the Third Legislative Assembly (2023-)** as repeated server-rendered tile blocks in plain HTML, one per member:

```
<div class="ce-mip-mp-tile-container " id="mp-tile-person-id-89156">
  <a class="ce-mip-mp-tile" href="getmemberdetailsnew?q=bWVtaWQ9MzU3NiZjb25zdGlkPTEmdGVybWlkPTE2Jm9yZGVyYnk9MQ==">
    <img class="ce-mip-mp-picture" src="https://admin-legislature.telangana.gov.in//preview.tsl?filePath=photoPath&fileName=16/3576.jpg">
      <div class="ce-mip-mp-name textdec">DR. PALVAI HARISH BABU</div>
      <div class="party_color" style="background-color:#F67800"></div>
      <div class="ce-mip-mp-constituency">BJP</div>
      <div class="ce-mip-mp-province">SIRPUR</div>
```

Parsed counts from that single page: **119 member tiles, 119 with a photograph, 119 with a per-MLA detail link, 100% with name + party + constituency.** Party is carried in the `.ce-mip-mp-constituency` div and constituency in the `.ce-mip-mp-province` div (the vendor's class names are swapped relative to their meaning - note this when writing a selector). Party is also encoded as a hex colour in `.party_color`. The detail link's `q` parameter is standard base64 and decodes cleanly, e.g. `bWVtaWQ9MzU3NiZjb25zdGlkPTEmdGVybWlkPTE2Jm9yZGVyYnk9MQ==` -> `memid=3576&constid=1&termid=16&orderby=1`, giving a stable machine-readable member id, constituency id and term id per MLA.

**PHOTO: yes, and it is a real curl-able image.** Photographs are served by a separate host from `https://admin-legislature.telangana.gov.in//preview.tsl?filePath=photoPath&fileName=16/<memid>.jpg`. Verified: `.../16/3576.jpg` returned curl-200, `application/octet-stream`, 458,752 bytes, confirmed by `file` as `JPEG image data, JFIF standard 1.02, 2784x3551, baseline` - a high-resolution press-grade portrait, roughly 450 KB each, so ~54 MB for the full set. The `16/` path segment is the term id.

**Term / assembly number: yes, selectable.** The roster page carries a term dropdown with three values - `termid=16` = III (2023- ), `termid=15` = II (2018-2023), `termid=14` = I (2014-2018). Fetching `/assemblymemprofile?termid=15` returns HTTP 200 with a different 274,438-byte page (different MD5 from the term-16 page), i.e. the historical rosters are reachable and Term I-III are all published this way.

**Per-MLA detail page: yes.** `https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/getmemberdetailsnew?q=<base64>` (curl-200, 58,473 bytes) publishes a "MLA Details / MLA Personal Profile" with **Personal Details, Legislature Experience and Contact Details**: full name, party, constituency, father's name, mother's name, date and place of birth, spouse's name, number of children, education qualifications, special interests, profession, legislature experience and positions, countries visited, permanent address, contact numbers and **email id**. For `memid=3576` (Sirpur) it returns "DR. PALVAI HARISH BABU (Bharatiya Janata Party) SIRPUR" with DOB 05/07/1979, M.B.B.S./M.S.(Ortho)/D.N.B.(Ortho), two mobile numbers and `harishpalvai@gmail.com`. Note this personal-contact data is genuinely published by the legislature and is therefore technically official, but any republishing decision should treat home address and personal mobile numbers as sensitive.

**Cross-check roster (official, lower fidelity):** the Telangana State Portal publishes a plain HTML table of the same 119 members at **https://www.telangana.gov.in/legislature/members-of-legislative-assembly/** (curl-200, 167,146 bytes, exactly 1 `<table>` with 120 `<tr>` = 1 header + 119 members) with columns `S. No | Candidate Name | Party Name | Constituency`. It carries **no photograph, no term number and no member id**, so it is a usable fallback/freshness check but not a substitute.

**Negative finding - the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) instance for Telangana is an empty shell.** The assembly is on NeVA at **https://tsla.neva.gov.in/** (curl-200, 41,691 bytes, `<title>Welcome to Telangana Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application</title>`, labelled "Third Legislative Assembly", dashboard reads "House Not In Session", "Questions 0", "Government Bills 1", "Member 2"). Its Members nav item points at `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, which **302-redirects off the state instance to the national portal** `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` (both return the identical 192,345-byte page). Probing the member detail route directly, `https://tsla.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/{1,2,3,5,10,50,100,119}` **all eight return the same 688-character empty page** containing `No Records Founds` three times (for General Questions / Debates / BILLS) and - tellingly - the hard-coded template label **"eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"**. NeVA therefore holds **zero Telangana member records**; the country-level template renders a blank, wrong-state page. The same redirect applies to `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Debates` and `/Committee` on the Telangana instance, which all resolve to `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA`. Do **not** treat tsla.neva.gov.in as a usable data source for this jurisdiction.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**The fund is not called "Vidhayak Nidhi" in Telangana. It is called the Constituency Development Fund (CDF).** Telangana press, budget coverage and legislative usage are consistent on this term; the state does not use "Vidhayak Nidhi", "Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi", "MLALAD", "MLA LADF" or "MPLADS" for MLAs. The reported annual entitlement is **Rs 5 crore per MLA** (enhanced from Rs 3 crore in the 2021-22 Telangana budget), with the state budget allocating a lump sum (e.g. Rs 800 crore reported as allocated for CDF) rather than per-MLA scheme outputs. These figures are press-reported and are **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**; no official Telangana document stating them was fetched in this reconnaissance.

**No official portal publishing per-MLA CDF data exists as far as this reconnaissance could establish.** No official page was found that publishes, per MLA, an allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure. Approximately 25 candidate hostnames were probed with curl and **every one failed to resolve (HTTP 000)**: `mlacdf.telangana.gov.in`, `cdf.telangana.gov.in`, `mlafund.telangana.gov.in`, `mlalad.telangana.gov.in`, `cdfonline.telangana.gov.in`, `cdfportal.telangana.gov.in`, `mlas.telangana.gov.in`, `mla.telangana.gov.in`, `services.telangana.gov.in`, `tsonline.telangana.gov.in`, `legislativeaffairs.telangana.gov.in`, `vidhansabha.telangana.gov.in`, `planning.telangana.gov.in`, `tsgo.telangana.gov.in`, `go.telangana.gov.in`, `cdf.nic.in`, `mlacdf.nic.in`, `mlacdf.in`, `tgcdf.nic.in`, `cdf.telangana.nic.in`, `mlas.telangana.nic.in`, `mlalad.telangana.nic.in`, `telangana.nic.in`, `tgplanning.nic.in`, `tgfinance.nic.in`. One host that does resolve, `https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/`, was searched for the strings `constituency development`, `CDF` and `Vidhayak` across its assembly, members and statistics pages: **zero hits** - the fund is not a legislature-site subject at all. `https://finance.telangana.gov.in/FirstPage.do` (the Telangana State Finance Department Portal, curl-200, 61,424 bytes) carries Budget, HODs list, Search HOA, Posts & Employee Details, Photo Gallery and File Management System, but **no CDF/MLA-fund module**.

**Scheme rules: an official portal exists for Government Orders but was not machine-searchable.** The official Telangana Government Order Issue Register is **https://goir.telangana.gov.in/** (curl-200, 70,852 bytes, `<title>Welcome to Telangana Government Order Issue Register</title>`), the correct place to look for the CDF scheme GO. It is a classic ASP.NET WebForms application: the only form POSTs to `./` with `__VIEWSTATE` (2,348 chars), `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR` and `__EVENTVALIDATION` (1,688 chars), and search fields `DDLDeptname`, `sectddl`, `DdlGo_cat`, `txtGoNo`, `DDLGoType`, `txtfrmdate`, `txttodate`, `ddlyear`, `txtSearchText`. A scripted POST replaying the ViewState with `txtSearchText=constituency development fund` returned **HTTP 302 -> `/Errors.aspx`** ("Unable to reach server. Please try again"), i.e. the search needs a real browser session. **The CDF scheme rules GO was therefore NOT retrieved**, and its document number remains unknown.

**How the data actually surfaces today:** only through RTI. Telangana reporting on MLA CDF utilisation ("33% MLA, 44% MLC funds unused since 2014-15", "Telangana MLAs barely spent 40% of development funds", "MLAs, MLCs fail to utilise funds for development: RTI", "High Court issues notices as Seethakka rues sanction of paltry funds under CDF") all derives from RTI replies, not from any published dataset. That is corroborated by third-party how-to content explaining that MLA fund usage in Telangana must be obtained "through online RTI". **All of these are UNOFFICIAL - needs verification.** The precise statement of the gap is: the CDF exists as a state scheme with per-MLA entitlements, but there is no public per-MLA portal, no published works-sanctioned list, no completion or expenditure figures, and no scheme-rules page reachable without a browser-driven GOIR session.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Attendance: NOT published.** No attendance page, module or dataset exists anywhere on `legislature.telangana.gov.in`, and the NeVA Telangana instance returns "No Records Founds". No official Telangana source publishes per-MLA sitting attendance. Press coverage discussing MLA attendance exists ("Revanth instructs Congress MLAs to attend Assembly", "Revanth Reddy warns Congress MLAs over Assembly absence") and the third-party PRS India page for the 2nd Telangana Assembly (2018-2023) presents legislature-level statistics - **both UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**, and neither yields a per-MLA attendance figure.

**Answers / questions: a searchable form exists, but it is NOT per-member.** The site publishes **https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/answersList** ("ANSWERS LIST"). It is a **search form**, not a browsable per-MLA list, with exactly five filters - `termId` (III 2023- / II 2018-2023 / I 2014-2018), `sessionId`, `sessionDate`, `department`, and `ans_type` = **Oral Answers (1) / Written Answers (3) / Placed (2)**. The site does **not** use the words "starred" and "unstarred"; the Oral/Written split is the functional analogue (oral answers follow starred questions, written answers follow unstarred ones), so **starred and unstarred questions are represented as a question-type dimension but only at the aggregate department x session level, never attributed to an individual member.** Critically, the form is `<form id="answersList" action="postanswerList" method="post">` - a **POST-only endpoint**. A curl POST to `https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/postanswerList` with a seeded session cookie and `termId=16&ans_type=1` returned **HTTP 403 (96 bytes)**, so the result set could not be retrieved during reconnaissance. The `sessionId`, `sessionDate` and `department` selects are empty on first load, i.e. they are populated by a dependent AJAX call after `termId` is chosen, which is another browser-session requirement.

**Private members' bills: NOT published per member.** **https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/billsActs** ("Bills and Acts") renders a real HTML table of 63 bills for the Third Assembly with columns `Sl No. | Title of the Bill | Legislative Assembly (Introduced On / Considered and Passed on) | Considered and Passed in Legislative Council | Assented to by the Governor/President | Date of Publication in Official Gazette` (e.g. "THE CORE URBAN REGION (INTEGRATED GOVERNANCE) BILL, 2026" introduced 11/09/2026, passed 12/09/2026). It has `termId` and `sessionId` selectors (sessions I-VIII listed for the current term). **There is no member-sponsor column and no private-member-bill category** - every bill is listed by title and date only. So the Assembly's own bill record is published, but **which MLA introduced which bill, and specifically which private members' bills were introduced, is not derivable from this page.**

**What else the official site does publish** (all curl-200 on `legislature.telangana.gov.in`, all effectively aggregate or by-sitting rather than per-member): `papersLaid` - a 305-row HTML table with `Sl. No. | Subject | Laid On | Download` (e.g. "The Audit Report of the Telangana Samagra Shiksha (TSS)", laid 07/09/2026); `bulletins` - 53 rows with `Bulletin No. | Bulletin Date | Subject`; `statistics` - a session index (Session I-VIII, dates, download); `assemblyDebates` - a very large page carrying **2,866 PDF references** (per-sitting debates); `committeeReport?q=<base64>` - committee reports; plus `ordinances`, `resolutions`, `shortDiscussions`, `statements`, `papersLaid`, `appropriation`, `budget`, `governoraddress`, `gazettes`, `archives`, `review`, `rulesandprocedures`, `formsDownloads`, `socioEconomic`. Committee-bill PDFs are served from a second host, `https://committees-legislature.telangana.gov.in//PreviewPage.do?q=<base64 of "filePath=basepath&fileName=UploadFiles/Pdfs/committee_01.08.2026.pdf">`.

**Access quirk worth recording:** several of these routes are **session-gated**. `/answersList`, `/billsActs` and `/resolutions` return HTTP 404 or the boilerplate message "Invalid URL, Kindly enter correct URL." on a cold curl. Seeding a cookie jar with a single GET to `https://legislature.telangana.gov.in/assembly` first, then requesting the same URL with that jar, returns HTTP 200 with the real content (`/answersList` 57,589 bytes; `/billsActs?q=` 209,361 bytes). A Referer header alone does not work - the session cookie does.

### Format note

A fetcher needs **several different mechanisms, and the official site is not a single clean scrape**. (1) `legislature.telangana.gov.in/assemblymemprofile` is **plain curl-able HTML** - no browser needed - and yields all 119 members with name, party, constituency, member id and photo URL in one 274 KB response; the historical terms are the same page with `?termid=15|14`. (2) MLA photographs are **plain curl-able JPEG binaries** from the separate host `admin-legislature.telangana.gov.in//preview.tsl?...`, with no auth and no referer requirement, ~450 KB each. (3) Per-MLA detail pages are curl-able HTML but the navigation parameter is **base64-encoded** (`?q=<base64>`) - the payload is trivially reversible, but the fetcher must decode/encode it rather than construct a readable querystring. (4) Several modules - `/answersList`, `/billsActs`, `/resolutions` - are **session-gated**: a cold curl gets HTTP 404 or "Invalid URL, Kindly enter correct URL.", and the fix is a cookie jar seeded by one GET to `/assembly`; a Referer header alone is not sufficient. (5) The `/answersList` **results are POST-only** (`action="postanswerList"`) and an otherwise-well-formed POST with a seeded session returned **HTTP 403**, while `sessionId` / `sessionDate` / `department` are loaded by a dependent AJAX call after `termId` is selected - so **the question/answer result set realistically requires a headless browser**, not curl. (6) Election results split by format: the **ECI statistical report PDF is text-extractable** (`/Font` 15, `/Image` 3, 120 pages) and needs only PDF text extraction, but **every CEO Telangana Form-20 constituency PDF is a scanned image** - six sampled files each had **`/Font` 0** with 18-34 embedded images - so per-constituency candidate-level results from the CEO need **OCR**. (7) `https://www.eci.gov.in/` itself is a **JS-rendered React SPA** (a 1,279-byte `<div id="root">` shell with `main.e468b53e.js`, plus a `<noscript>` demanding JavaScript), so the ECI site navigation needs a headless browser even though its PDF assets are plain curl-able files; `results.eci.gov.in` now serves only the current live cycle and had no Telangana-2023 archive. (8) `goir.telangana.gov.in` is **ASP.NET WebForms** requiring a full `__VIEWSTATE` / `__EVENTVALIDATION` round-trip; a scripted POST was rejected to `/Errors.aspx`, so a real browser is required. There is **no captcha anywhere** on the sources that worked, and no login was needed for the roster or photos. Broader risks: the site is HTTP-200-friendly to a browser User-Agent but its 404s are Tomcat-style HTML, and one former official domain (`telanganalegislature.org.in`) has been **abandoned and squatted**, so any hard-coded link to it silently returns a domain-sale page rather than an error - a fetcher that does not check content type will ingest parking-lot HTML as if it were government data.

### Gap note

Four things cannot be sourced officially today. **(1) There is no official per-MLA Constituency Development Fund data of any kind** - no allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure per MLA, no aggregate-by-MLA table, and no scheme-rules page reachable without a browser-driven GOIR session. The CDF demonstrably exists (it is a budget line and a recurring subject of Assembly and High Court proceedings) and the reported per-MLA entitlement is Rs 5 crore, but every per-MLA figure in circulation comes from **RTI replies reported in the press**, which are UNOFFICIAL - needs verification. This is the single largest gap and it means the site's fund column cannot be built for Telangana from official sources. **(2) Attendance is not published** in any form, per-MLA or otherwise. **(3) Private members' bills are not published per member** - the Bills and Acts table records each bill's title, introduction date, passing date, assent and gazette date but carries no sponsor column and no private-member category, so it is impossible to attribute any bill to an MLA from the official record. **(4) Questions are published only as a filterable answer-set, not as per-member counts** - there is no per-MLA starred/unstarred tally, and the Oral/Written/Placed breakdown is queryable only by term, session, session date and department, and only through a POST endpoint that returned HTTP 403 to curl; note also that the official site never uses the words "starred" and "unstarred", so a mapping decision is required before this data can be labelled as such. A secondary caution: the National e-Vidhan Application instance at `tsla.neva.gov.in` *looks* like it should carry all four assembly-record dimensions and carries **none** - it is an onboarded-but-empty shell whose member pages render a blank template labelled "Himachal Pradesh". Any pipeline that discovers NeVA state hosts by URL pattern will mis-classify Telangana as instrumented when it is not.

### Platform

**Telangana is on a common platform in name but its working data sits on a bespoke, single-vendor site - so the honest answer is both, and the reusable layer is the vendor, not the national template.** The Telangana Legislature has signed an MoU and is **onboarded onto the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, the NIC/MoPA national template: `https://tsla.neva.gov.in/` (Assembly) and `https://tslc.neva.gov.in/` (Council) follow the `<statecode>la.neva.gov.in` / `<statecode>lc.neva.gov.in` pattern shared with `apa.neva.gov.in`, `asla.neva.gov.in`, `bla.neva.gov.in`, `arla.neva.gov.in` and `jhla.neva.gov.in`; every instance carries near-identical titles ("Welcome to *<State>* Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application"), shared asset paths (`/2024/images/...`, `/2022/images/...`), a shared CMS at `cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/<STATE>/FileStructures//...`, and the footer credit "&#169; 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved | Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India", with version string "Version: 2.0.2". The decisive caveat is that the Telangana NeVA instance is **content-empty**: no member records, no questions, no debates, no bills - so one scraper targeting NeVA would collect nothing for Telangana. The data actually lives on **`legislature.telangana.gov.in`, a bespoke Java application whose footer reads "Designed & Developed by : `<img src="img/cgg_logo.png">`"** - i.e. the **Centre for Good Governance (CGG)**, the Hyderabad-based government society, whose logo asset returns curl-200 (`image/png`, 15,023 bytes). CGG is the genuinely reusable thread across jurisdictions: the **Andhra Pradesh Legislature** at `aplegislature.org` serves its theme CSS from the vendor's own Java package path, `/o/com.cgg.constants/css/main.css?themeId=aplatheme_WAR_aplatheme` (verified curl-200), proving the same vendor and naming convention on a second state's legislature; and the Telangana Finance Department portal's own meta keywords include the string "finance department cgg". Within the Telangana site, the member module uses a recognisable vendor template - `ce-mip-mp-tile-container`, `ce-mip-mp-picture`, `ce-mip-mp-name`, `ce-mip-mp-constituency`, `ce-mip-mp-province` ("mip" = member information profile) - and the same `admin-legislature.telangana.gov.in//preview.tsl?filePath=...&fileName=...` and `<host>//PreviewPage.do?q=<base64>` asset patterns appear across the legislature and committee hosts. **Practical conclusion for the scraper: write one CGG-pattern adapter (`ce-mip-mp-*` tiles + `preview.tsl` photos + base64 `?q=` detail links) and expect it to reach Telangana and Andhra Pradesh, while treating the NeVA `*.neva.gov.in` family as a separate, larger-scale template that is currently unpopulated for Telangana.**

---

## Tripura (IN-TR)

**Verdict:** partial - the 13th Tripura Legislative Assembly is fully live on the NIC-hosted National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) with a curl-fetchable 60-member roster including photographs, per-member profiles and per-member starred/unstarred questions, but there is NO official portal of any kind publishing per-MLA development-fund figures, and member attendance is not published.

### Profile

The Tripura Legislative Assembly's official website is **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/** (HTTP 200, `text/html`, 90,856 bytes, curled successfully; page title "Welcome to Tripura Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application"). It is not bespoke: it is Tri­pura's tenant instance of the central **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, hosted by the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs and the National Informatics Centre, and the footer reads "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India / Content managed by: Tripura Legislative Assembly". The legacy domain **https://tripuraassembly.nic.in/** (and `www.`) still resolves in DNS to 164.100.178.251 but **refuses TCP on both port 80 and port 443** - it is dead. Its last Wayback capture (2024-12-29) shows a static HTML+PDF site with no searchable roster; treat that host as retired.

The member roster is published and is machine-fetchable. The human-facing page is **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec** ("Reach out to us - Members Contact Directory | Tripura Vidhan Sabha"), which is a filter shell; the actual rows arrive from an AJAX GET to **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=0&MSLT=0&Assemblyid=13** returning a **466,993-byte HTML partial containing exactly 60 member cards**. Each card carries: a **photograph** (`<img src="https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/TR/FileStructures//Member/thumb/06_15_2023_11_26_47.jpg">`, thumbnail; the full-size file is the same path without `/thumb/`, curled at HTTP 200 `image/jpeg`), the member's **name** (Bengali by default, English after the culture switch), the **party**, the **constituency name with district and state** (e.g. "Matarbari -Gomati (Tripura)"), the **date of birth**, and the local postal address, plus a link to `/Member/Details/{id}?AssemblyID=13`. This is a genuine **per-member queryable** roster: the filter UI drives it by `MLT` (party family / category), `MSLT` (party or sub-category) and `Assemblyid` (1st through 13th Assembly), so historical Assemblies back to the 1st are addressable with the same endpoint. Party breakdown for the 13th Assembly is embedded in the homepage chart data as `[{"y":33,"name":"BJP"},{"y":13,"name":"TMP"},{"y":10,"name":"CPI(M)"},{"y":3,"name":"INC"},{"y":1,"name":"IPFT"}]` = 60 seats.

Two bulk exports are advertised and both work: **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/PDFforHonbleMember** (HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, ≈3.28–3.61 MB; `%PDF-1.4`, 16 `/Font` objects and 4 `/Image` objects, so it is a **text PDF, not a scan**) and **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember** (HTTP 200, `content-type: application/excel`, 46,457 bytes - although the body is an Excel-flavoured HTML export with `MsoNormal` styles rather than a real .xlsx, so it parses as HTML). A per-member profile page exists at **/Member/Details/{id}?AssemblyID=13** (HTML, 31,466 bytes) with an AJAX detail endpoint **/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=78&AssemblyID=13** returning father's name, date and place of birth, e-mail, mobile and alternate mobile, party, constituency, state, caste category (General/SC/ST/OBC), marital status, spouse, number of sons/daughters, local address, highest qualification and hobbies.

Verification level: **curl-200** for every URL above, from this session, with the session-cookie + Referer requirement described in the format note. There is no captcha, no login, and no POST-only gate on the public pages, though the site is an ASP.NET MVC application that returns the *national* NeVA homepage (192,345 bytes) instead of content when the request carries neither the `ASP.NET_SessionId` cookie seeded by a first visit to `/` nor a `Referer`.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The scheme exists in Tripura and its local name is the **"MLA Area Development Fund"**, also written **"MLA Area Development Scheme"** in Government of Tripura communications - see the Directorate of Information & Cultural Affairs press release *"Passenger shed in Charilam under MLA Area Development Scheme"* at https://ica.tripura.gov.in/sites/default/files/1206-22%20Eng%20PR%20-%20Passenger%20shed%20in%20Charilam%20under%20MLA%20Area%20Development%20Scheme.pdf (curl-200, `application/pdf`, 102,176 bytes). Tripura does **not** use the names "Vidhayak Nidhi", "Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi" or "MLALAD" for this scheme.

**No official portal publishing per-MLA fund data was found.** This is the central gap for Tripura. Exhaustively checked and negative: the Tripura State Portal site search at https://tripura.gov.in/search/node?keys=MLA+fund (and the same search for `Vidhayak`, `area+development+fund`, `MLA+Area+Development`) returns **"Your search yielded no results."**; https://tripura.gov.in/schemes lists no MLA-fund entry; the Finance (http://finance.tripura.gov.in/), Rural Development (http://rural.tripura.gov.in/) and Planning (http://planning.tripura.gov.in/) department sites carry no MLA-fund link; the assembly's own NeVA portal carries no fund section; and the NEGD India Stack Local directory page for Tripura (https://negd.gov.in/isl/Directory/statedata/244) lists 10 Tripura digital solutions, none of them a fund-monitoring system. Host existence probes for `mlaadf.tripura.gov.in`, `mlafund.tripura.gov.in`, `rdd.tripura.gov.in` and `assembly.tripura.gov.in` returned **no DNS record at all**.

Consequently the fund is known publicly only at **aggregate announcement level, from press reports** - the Chief Minister announced in January 2024 a hike of Rs 25 lakh per year, taking the annual MLA area development fund to Rs 75 lakh per MLA (reported by ANI, India Today NE, Sentinel Assam and Prantidintime; all UNOFFICIAL). There is **no official source for allocated limit per MLA, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure for any individual MLA in Tripura**, and no official published scheme-guidelines / government-resolution page was found either. Per-MLA status: **none**. There is no national fallback either - MPLADS at https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/ covers Members of Parliament only, not MLAs.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred and unstarred questions: PUBLISHED, per-member and per-session, as curl-fetchable HTML.** The assembly serves a per-member question register via AJAX GET **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Question?MemberId=78&AssemblyID=13** (HTTP 200, `text/html`, 13,122 bytes). The returned table has exactly the columns Sr no | Question No | Question Type | Date | Minister | Subject, and the Question Type field explicitly distinguishes **তারকাচিহ্নিত (starred)** from **তারকাচিহ্নহীন (unstarred)** - for member 78 the response contained rows of both types (e.g. starred question 174 of 27/03/25, unstarred question 232 of 10/07/23). A site-wide question browser exists at **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/Questions** with the filter IDs mapped as: `LstItem=0` starred questions, `LstItem=1` unstarred questions, `LstItem=3` short-notice questions, and under `LstItem=2` the types 4/5/24 = starred/unstarred/short-notice answers and 18/19/25 = starred/unstarred/short-notice replies. Supporting lookup endpoints return real JSON (`content-type: application/json`): **/Questions/SessionList?AssemblyId=13** (session codes, e.g. the 10th session of the 13th Assembly = SessionId 175 / SessionCode 10), **/Home/AssemblyChange?AssemblyId=13** (session metadata), and **/Business/MemberListForQuestion1?FromAssemblyId=13&ToAssemblyId=13** (a JSON array of members with `MemberCode` and `MemberName`). Historical sessions reach back to the 1st Assembly, and the homepage counter reports 3,547 questions in total.

**Private members' bills: NOT clearly published as a distinct category.** The NeVA bill module at **https://tripura.neva.gov.in/Bill** exposes `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, `/Bill/GetAllAssembliesList`, `/Bill/SessionList` and `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, and the assembly homepage counts **"Government Bills: 131"** for the 13th Assembly. Each member's detail page carries a bills tab (বিল), but for member 78 that tab rendered "No Records Found". No per-member bill list was successfully retrieved, and no separate "private members' bills" category was observed on the public portal - only the government-bill counter. Treat per-member bill data as **partial/unresolved** rather than confirmed.

**Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED - a definite, verified gap.** The NeVA FAQ at https://tripura.neva.gov.in/Home/FAQ answers item 17, "How Members mark the attendance in the House?", with "By touching attendance button in e-book and then place registered finger on biometric device to mark attendance." That confirms attendance data **is captured internally** (biometric, through the member e-book app) but the public portal exposes **no per-member attendance figure or report of any kind**. Grepping the rendered pages of /Business, /Debates, /Committee, /Home/FormData and /Home/DigitalLibrary for "attend" returned **zero matches**. The member detail page carries tabs for General / Questions / Notice / Assurances / Committee / Debates / Bills - there is **no attendance tab**. Anyone claiming a per-MLA attendance percentage for Tripura is sourcing it from a non-government aggregate (e.g. the ADR assembly-performance report for 2008-2012, which is UNOFFICIAL).

Also published and curl-fetchable on the same portal, for context: **Debates** (https://tripura.neva.gov.in/Debates plus `/Debates/FetchDataList`), questions by date via `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionRJ`, `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionBySearch` and the advanced search `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionAdvBySearch` (parameters FromAssemblyID, ToAssemblyID, fromSessionCode, toSessionCode, DepartmentID, Subject, MemberID, MinsitryMinistersID), the paper-laid/Business register (`/Business`, counter 522), committee reports (`/Committee`, counter 29), notices and bulletins, list-of-business and starred/unstarred question booklets for each sitting day as PDFs on `cms.neva.gov.in`, demands for grants, budget, economic survey, rules and directions, and live TV. Total notices received counter: 33.
