### Format note

A fetcher for `tripura.neva.gov.in` needs **curl with a cookie jar and a Referer, not a headless browser**. The site is ASP.NET MVC (headers show `X-AspNet-Version: 4.0.30319`, `Set-Cookie: ASP.NET_SessionId=...`, `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, and a permissive CSP that allows inline script). Without seeding a session by first GETting `/`, and without sending `-e https://tripura.neva.gov.in/` (or the relevant in-site page) on the follow-up request, every content route silently returns the **national** NeVA homepage (192,345 bytes) with a `200` status instead of an error - a trap that yields a wrong-but-plausible page rather than a failure. With the cookie jar and Referer, all of the roster, profile, question and bill routes return their real HTML. Only a handful of routes are POST-gated (e.g. `/Home/_FooterContent`), and none of the data-bearing ones are. There is **no captcha, no login and no JSF/ViewState/ZK** in the way - the AJAX endpoints are plain GET query-string calls returning HTML fragments or JSON, so a scraper is a small number of `curl`-equivalent requests per member. The one genuinely unpleasant detail is **language state**: the portal defaults to `lang=bn-IN` and member names, parties and constituency names come back in Bengali, with English only after calling `/Home/ChangeInEngCulture?lang=7C4F28F6-02FC-4627-993D-E255037531AD&langCulCode=en-US` inside the same session; a robust pipeline must pin the culture or it will store Bengali strings. The bulk roster PDF needs ordinary PDF text extraction (it has real fonts, not scanned images). By contrast **https://www.eci.gov.in/** is a React single-page app (`<div id="root">`, `main.e468b53e.js`, "You need to enable JavaScript to run this app") returning a 1,279-byte shell - ECI itself is **browser-needed**, and `results.eci.gov.in` is only a meta-refresh redirector pointing at whichever election is current, so it cannot be used for the 2023 Tripura result by URL. For that, the CEO Tripura Form 20 PDF is the reliable machine-readable route. Finally, the state portal and the CEO site are ordinary server-rendered Drupal/HTML pages (`curl-html`) with no obstacles.

### Gap note

Two things cannot be sourced officially for Tripura and must not be fabricated. First, **per-MLA development-fund data does not exist in public official form**: there is no portal, no dashboard, no PDF and no disclosure page anywhere on a `.gov.in`/`.nic.in` host giving any MLA's allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure. The scheme is real - it is called the **MLA Area Development Fund / MLA Area Development Scheme** and the annual entitlement was raised to Rs 75 lakh per MLA in January 2024 - but every figure in circulation traces to press coverage of ministerial announcements, not to a government data release, and even the scheme's guidelines/government-resolution page could not be located. If the Election Atlas needs a per-MLA fund section for Tripura, that column has to be rendered as an explicit, documented "not published by the Government of Tripura" rather than filled from news. Second, **attendance is not published**. NeVA captures attendance biometrically through the members' e-book application (documented in the assembly's own FAQ), but no per-member attendance figure, percentage or report is exposed on the public portal, and the member detail page has no attendance tab at all - so any attendance number for a Tripura MLA is necessarily from a non-government aggregator. A third, smaller gap: private members' bills are not visible as a distinct category - the portal publishes a government-bill counter (131) and a per-member bills tab, but that tab returned no records for the member sampled and no per-member bill list could be retrieved.

### Platform

Tripura is on a **national shared NIC template, not a bespoke site**: the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, built by the National Informatics Centre under the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs. The evidence is unambiguous and multiple. (1) The site runs on the state-tenant subdomain pattern `<state>.neva.gov.in` - `https://tripura.neva.gov.in/` - and the NeVA national homepage at `https://neva.gov.in/` hyperlinks **38 legislature tenants** on that pattern (apa, apc, arla, asla, bla, blc, chg, dla, goa, gujarat, hpvs, hrla, kerala, kla, klc, loksabha, manipur, mgla, mhla, mhlc, mizo, mpla, nagaland, odisha, puddu, pvs, raj, rajyasabha, sikkim, tnla, tripura, tsla, tslc, uplc, upvs, utkh, wbla). (2) The markup and routes are identical across tenants - Sikkim's `https://sikkim.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec` returns the same page template with the title "Reach out to us - Members Contact Directory | **Sikkim** Vidhan Sabha" against Tripura's "... | **Tripura** Vidhan Sabha", and both expose the identical AJAX endpoint `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=&MSLT=&Assemblyid=`. (3) The footer credits "National eVidhan Application ... Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India / Content managed by: Tripura Legislative Assembly", and the page reports "Version: 2.0.2, Last Updated: 25 Mar 2026, Zone(3)". (4) The Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs confirms it officially in PIB release 2294830 (05 Aug 2026), listing "Tripura Legislative Assembly" among the 21 legislative houses transitioned to digital houses under NeVA. The practical consequence for the Election Atlas is significant: **one NeVA scraper serves all 38 tenants** - the member roster, per-member questions with starred/unstarred typing, the roster PDF/Excel exports, and the JSON lookup endpoints all live at the same paths and the same ASP.NET session+culture mechanics on every one of them, with only the state subdomain and the `UnitCode`/`Assemblyid` values changing. Tripura therefore costs almost nothing once one NeVA adapter exists. Tripura's separate **election-results** sources are *not* on NeVA: the Chief Electoral Officer at `ceotripura.nic.in` is an ordinary Drupal site, and the ECI is a React SPA - those are two different stacks that need their own handling.

---

## Uttar Pradesh (IN-UP)

**Verdict:** partial - UP's MLA identity layer is fully official and even machine-readable (a 397-record ASMX JSON roster plus a photographed Liferay member directory), and election results and per-MLA starred/unstarred questions are officially published, but per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi fund figures have no public portal at all and MLA attendance is published nowhere on any official assembly property.

### Profile

Uttar Pradesh has a fully functioning **bicameral** legislature; MLAs sit in the **Uttar Pradesh Legislative Assembly (Vidhan Sabha)**, currently the **18th Assembly** (403 seats, constituted 2022). The upper house (Vidhan Parishad, whose members are MLCs and not MLAs) is out of scope here and its linked host `http://www.upvidhanparishad.nic.in/` returned no HTTP response during this pass.

Three separate **official** web properties were fetched and verified with curl, and the member data is deliberately split across two of them:

1. **`https://uplegisassembly.gov.in/`** - the primary Vidhan Sabha site (Hindi-first; an English entry page exists at `/index_en.html`, 90,862 bytes). It is AngularJS over ASP.NET and exposes a genuine **ASP.NET ASMX JSON web service** at `https://uplegisassembly.gov.in/angular.asmx/<method>`, called by HTTP POST with `Content-Type: application/json`. Verified methods:
   - `show_MemberList` with body `{"Id":"MLA_HI","parm":"18"}` → **397 records**, the sitting 18th-Assembly MLAs, each carrying `mla_name` (Hindi) and `mla_name_eng`, party in both scripts plus `partyShortNameEng` (SP, BJP, BSP, AD(S)…) and `party_id`, constituency as a number-prefixed name in both scripts (`1-बेहट` / `1-Behat`), `mla_code` (e.g. 18001), `mla_sno`, `assembly_id`, `district_name`/`district_name_eng`, `gender`, `flg_resign`.
   - `showAllMemberList` with `{"parm":"AssemblyWiseMember","parm1":"18"}` → **423 records** for the 18th Assembly term, including resigned, deceased and disqualified members.
   - `showAllMemberList` with `{"parm":"searchMember","parm1":""}` → **7,793 records** - every MLA across Assemblies 1-18. A complete historical roster in a single call.
   - `AllAssemblyMembersList` with `{"locale":"AAML"}` → the 18 Assemblies; `CurrentAssemblyPartyList` with `{"Id":"CurrentAssemblyPartyList","parm":"18"}` → party seat counts (e.g. `BHARTIYA JANATA PARTY (256)`).

   The ASMX responses carry **no photo field** and no attendance, question or bill counts. The endpoint map is discoverable because the AngularJS controller is served at `https://uplegisassembly.gov.in/Members/Members.js` (68,990 bytes), which enumerates every method and route.

2. **`https://upvidhansabhaproceedings.gov.in/`** - a Liferay 7 portal titled *Uttar Pradesh Legislative Assembly*, and the property that holds the **photographs**. `https://upvidhansabhaproceedings.gov.in/en/web/guest/member-s-information` returns a **3,512,767-byte** server-rendered page, *"Eighteenth UTTAR PRADESH LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY — Member's Information"*, containing **418 distinct `/web/guest/member?memberId=NNNNN` links**, 836 table rows, 3,344 cells and **57 embedded portrait JPEGs**. It supports search on Name, Designation, Portfolio, Party, Constituency or Address, List/Grid views, and grouping by District or Party. Verified assets: a portrait at `https://upvidhansabhaproceedings.gov.in/documents/20181/24536/upload_00000716.jpeg` (curl-200, `image/jpeg`, 23,109 bytes) and a per-member page at `https://upvidhansabhaproceedings.gov.in/web/guest/member?memberId=24722` (curl-200, 91,303 bytes). This portal also provides *Know your MLA* by **name**, **constituency** and **assembly**.

3. **`https://upvs.neva.gov.in/`** - UP's instance of the national NeVA platform (see Platform below), which additionally publishes portraits on its CMS host, e.g. `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/UP/FileStructures//Member/06_24_2022_11_29_39.jpg` (curl-200, `image/jpeg`, 28,609 bytes).

So **name, party, constituency, term/assembly number and photo are all official**, with the roster available as clean JSON and the photo as a plain JPEG. A fourth, lower-quality photo route exists and is worth naming because it is the scanned-image case: `https://uplegisassembly.gov.in/flipbooks/17whoswho/index.html` is a Flash/image flipbook "Who's Who" for the 17th Assembly whose pages are PNG scans (`files/assets/flash/pages/page0001_s.png`); it requires OCR and is superseded by the Liferay directory for the current house.

Verification level: the roster JSON, the member directory, the per-member page and the portrait JPEGs were all **fetched and confirmed by curl** (curl-200).

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund in Uttar Pradesh is called **विधायक निधि (Vidhayak Nidhi)**, also written विधायक क्षेत्र विकास निधि, and corresponds to the national MLA Local Area Development Fund (MLALAD) whose MP analogue is MPLADS.

**No official per-MLA data portal was found.** Unlike MPLADS, Uttar Pradesh does not appear to publish an equivalent portal carrying per-MLA allocated limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure. Every plausible dedicated hostname was probed and resolved to nothing (connection/DNS failure, 2026-09-14): `vidhayaknidhi.up.nic.in`, `mlalad.up.nic.in`, `upvidhayaknidhi.up.nic.in`, `nidhi.up.nic.in`, `vidhayaknidhi.gov.in`, `nidhi.up.gov.in`, `mlaup.gov.in`, `mlafund.up.gov.in`, `vidhayak.up.gov.in`, `eoffice.up.gov.in`, `nagarvikas.up.gov.in`, `samajkalyan.up.gov.in`, `upvidhansabha.gov.in`, `vidhansabha.up.nic.in`. These are recorded here **only as tested-and-absent, never as sources**. (The `up.nic.in` subdomain family is otherwise live for UP - `https://jansunwai.up.nic.in/` returns curl-200 - so this is a genuine absence, not a broken network path.)

What **does** exist officially is the **scheme-rules route, not a data portal**: UP Government Orders (शासनादेश) are published on the official state GO portal **`http://shasanadesh.up.gov.in/`** (curl-200, 146,959 bytes, *"UP Government Shasanadesh(GO) - उत्तर प्रदेश सरकार के शासनादेशों की अधिकारिक वेबसाइट"*). Its search filters by department (a 100-option list including समाज कल्याण विभाग, नगर विकास विभाग, वित्त विभाग and विधायी विभाग), section, category, date (`txtGOdate`) and subject (`txtSubj`). Individual GOs are served as PDFs from `http://shasanadesh.up.gov.in/frmPDF.aspx?qry=<base64>`, where the base64 decodes to a `deptcode#section#cat#year` tuple - decoded examples: `MSMxNjIjMSMyMDE3` = `1#162#1#2017`, `MTE0IzI1IzMyMDI2` = `114#25#3#2026`, `MTgjMTYyIzI4IzIwMTU=` = `18#162#28#2015`. A direct fetch returned a **1,088,605-byte PDF beginning `%PDF-1.4`**, confirming GO PDFs are retrievable when the `qry` code is known. **This is where the Vidhayak Nidhi Government Order lives** - but it is a scheme-rule document, not a per-MLA financial table.

The only other official figure is **aggregate**: the state budget portal `http://budget.up.nic.in/` (curl-200) carries the fund's annual state-level provision, never a per-MLA breakdown. Press coverage from 2024-2026 (Hindustan Times, Zee News Hindi, TV9 Hindi, Live Hindustan) repeatedly reports that UP is moving Vidhayak Nidhi sanction and expenditure onto a portal or app and has constituted a multi-member committee on the MLA Local Area Development Fund; **no live official per-MLA portal URL could be located or verified**, so this is recorded as not-found rather than as a working source.

To state it precisely: **the scheme exists and is funded; the per-MLA publication does not exist.** Per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi figures would require an RTI to the implementing authority or district-wise sanction lists, neither of which is a portal.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred and unstarred questions: YES, and per member.** The Uttar Pradesh Legislative Assembly Secretariat runs a public questions portal at **`https://vsopp.up.gov.in/uplaquest/Question_Online/`** (*"उत्तर प्रदेश विधान सभा के प्रश्न जन-सामान्य पोर्टल"*). Six query axes were all verified curl-200: `SessionWise.aspx` (30,376 bytes), `QuestionWise.aspx` (32,745 bytes), `DateWise.aspx`, `MemberWise.aspx`, `DepartmentWise.aspx` and `AdvanceSearch.aspx` (133,105 bytes - the largest, and the best single bulk-extraction target). The house selector covers the 18th, 17th and 16th Assemblies with sessions back to प्रथम सत्र, 2022.

- **Per MLA:** `MemberWise.aspx` carries a member dropdown with **413 options** (`ctl00$ContentPlaceHolder1$DdlMemberName`), so questions *are* tabulated per member.
- **Starred vs unstarred:** `QuestionWise.aspx`, titled *"प्रश्नों के प्रकार के अनुसार सूचीबद्ध प्रश्न"*, has a question-**type** dropdown whose options are **अल्पसूचित** (short notice), **तारांकित 29(4)** (starred, rule 29(4)), **तारांकित 38(2)** (starred, rule 38(2)), **तारांकित** (starred) and **अतारांकित** (unstarred). Both categories are therefore separately enumerable, and combined with the member filter they are per-MLA.
- One important caveat, stated by the portal's own disclaimer: the content is compiled from questions transmitted online by the Secretariat to UP government departments and the answers returned by those departments, and **certified text, supplementary questions, short-notice questions and oral replies to starred questions must be checked against the separately printed official proceedings**. The portal is a working record, not the certified verbatim record. NeVA also exposes a `/Questions` route on the UP instance.

**Attendance: NOT PUBLISHED.** No attendance data was found on any official property. `https://upvs.neva.gov.in/Attendance`, `/attendance` and `/MemberAttendance` all return **HTTP 404**; neither `uplegisassembly.gov.in` nor `upvidhansabhaproceedings.gov.in` has an attendance item in its navigation; and the NeVA member-detail template (e.g. `/Member/Details/104`) exposes only Home, Member, Hon'ble, All Members, General Questions, Notice, Assurances, Committee, Debates and BILLS tabs - **there is no attendance tab**. This is a documented absence, not an unsearched area.

**Private members' bills: PARTIAL.** Bill data exists but is **not segregated** by whether a bill is a government bill or a private member's bill. `https://upvidhansabhaproceedings.gov.in/en/web/guest/bills-search` (curl-200, 115,563 bytes) is a bills search, alongside Ordinance Search, Acts Search, Gazette Search, Gazette By Decade, Assembly Debates and Index To Proceedings. `https://uplegisassembly.gov.in/Legislation/Legislation_hi.aspx` is the Assembly site's legislation page whose controller is served at `https://uplegisassembly.gov.in/Legislation/js/Legislation.js` (curl-200, 8,036 bytes). **No per-member bill tally and no explicit government-vs-private-member classification was found.**

### Format note

A fetcher needs **four different techniques for four different sources**, so this jurisdiction is `mixed`. (1) **curl JSON is the cheapest win and should be the backbone**: `POST https://uplegisassembly.gov.in/angular.asmx/show_MemberList` with `Content-Type: application/json` and body `{"Id":"MLA_HI","parm":"18"}` returns the whole sitting roster as an ASMX `.d`-wrapped JSON array; the sibling methods cover history (7,793 records), the assembly index and party strength. No cookies, no tokens, no auth. (2) **curl HTML** works for the Liferay proceedings portal - `member-s-information` is fully server-rendered at 3.5 MB and its portraits are plain JPEGs - but the per-member page and any *search* on that portal go through Liferay's stateful portlet machinery (`p_p_id`, `p_p_lifecycle`, a `p_auth` token), so **queries, not reads, need a browser or a maintained session**. (3) **A headless browser is required** for `https://vsopp.up.gov.in/uplaquest/Question_Online/*`, which is classic ASP.NET WebForms: a single page carries a **34,524-character `__VIEWSTATE`** and a **9,212-character `__EVENTVALIDATION`** that must be round-tripped on every POST, plus a `__ncforminfo` field indicating a WAF; the questions data itself cannot be reached without replaying that state. The same applies to `shasanadesh.up.gov.in`, whose search additionally requires solving a **CAPTCHA** served from `JpegImage.aspx?guid=<base64>` - though its GO PDFs can be fetched directly by `frmPDF.aspx?qry=<base64>` once the code tuple is known. (4) **PDF text extraction** for the Government Orders (plain `%PDF-1.4`, not scans), and **OCR** for the older 17th-Assembly "Who's Who" flipbook, whose pages are PNG images. Finally, `https://www.eci.gov.in/` is a **JavaScript SPA** that serves only a 1,279-byte shell to curl, so ECI data must come from the legacy `old.eci.gov.in` Drupal file module (curl-able) or from a browser. Watch for one trap: the retired 2022 results microsite paths under `results.eci.gov.in/AcResultGenMarch2022/` all return 404 now, so any hard-coded 2022 microsite URL will silently break.

### Gap note

Two things are missing and cannot be sourced officially. First, and most seriously for this project, **there is no official per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi dataset anywhere** - no allocated limit, no works recommended, no works sanctioned, no works completed, no expenditure, per MLA. The fund is real and funded and its rules are officially published as Government Orders, but the money trail is not public at MLA granularity; the state budget gives only an aggregate provision. The 2024-2026 press says an app/portal is coming, but as of 2026-09-14 no such official portal exists and every candidate hostname failed to resolve, so nothing can be cited. Second, **MLA attendance is unpublished in every form** - not as a percentage, not as sittings attended, not per session - across all three official assembly properties, and the NeVA member template has no attendance tab at all. Third, a smaller gap: **private members' bills cannot be isolated**. Bill, ordinance, act and gazette records exist and are searchable, but no field, filter or page distinguishes a private member's bill from a government bill, and no per-member bill count is published - so an assembly-record column combining attendance, question counts and private members' bills can be built only two-thirds of the way, from the official questions portal, with attendance and the PMB tally left empty by design rather than by oversight.

### Platform

Uttar Pradesh runs on a **shared national platform (NIC template) plus a common vendor product**, with one bespoke site alongside - so a single scraper genuinely serves many states for the primary template. The decisive evidence is that **`upvs.neva.gov.in` and `neva.gov.in` resolve to the same IP address (45.127.75.72)** and serve the same ASP.NET/IIS application, differing only by host header: `neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` renders the national *"One Nation One Application"* dashboard while `upvs.neva.gov.in/` renders the UP tenant (18th Vidhan Sabha, Governor Anandiben Patel, CM Yogi Adityanath, Speaker Satish Mahana). The reusable contract is the **state-code path pattern `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/<STATE_CODE>/FileStructures/...`**: UP portraits live at `/NeVA/UP/FileStructures//Member/06_24_2022_11_29_39.jpg`, the live central NeVA HTML references `/NeVA/GJ/FileStructures//Notices/<uuid>.pdf` for **Gujarat**, `/NeVA/HP/` returned 200 while `/NeVA/MH/` and `/NeVA/RJ/` returned 403, and the identical member template returned the title *"Member Details | NeVA | eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"* for member id 104. Because every NeVA state shares the routes `/Member/Details/<id>`, `/Bill`, `/Debates`, `/Questions`, `/Committee` and `/Home/*`, one extraction pattern parameterised by two-letter state code covers UP and every other onboarded state. Government confirmation of scope comes from PIB release 2197047 (Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs, 01 DEC 2025): *"28 State / UT Legislatures have signed Memoranda of Understanding (MoUs) for implementation of the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA), and 20 of them have already transformed into fully digital Houses… aimed at digitalising all the 37 State/UT Legislatures."*

The **second** platform is a common vendor product: `upvidhansabhaproceedings.gov.in` is **Liferay 7** (235 `liferay`/`Liferay` occurrences, `Liferay.Theme` in the head, `/o/frontend-js-aui-web/` assets, `/documents/20181/<folderId>/<file>` asset URLs) branded with the theme **`upla-ind-theme` / `uplaindtheme`** (upla = UP Legislative Assembly) and carrying portlet namespaces **`com_semanticbits_upla_*`** - SemanticBits being a vendor acquired by **Persistent Systems** in 2021, whose own press release ("Uttar Pradesh Inaugurates Digital Platform for State Legislature Proceedings…") confirms the relationship for UP. However, I probed sibling state assembly hosts to test reuse - `mplegisassembly.gov.in`, `mahavidhan.in`, `rajassembly.nic.in`, `gujaratvidhansabha.gov.in` - and **all failed to resolve**, so while the Liferay portlet structure is reusable, **no evidence was found that other Indian states deploy this same build**, and that claim is deliberately not made. The **third** property, `uplegisassembly.gov.in`, is **bespoke** UP code: AngularJS over ASP.NET returning the hand-written .NET type `UPVidhanSabha.angular+Populate`, with jQuery 1.9.1/3.5.1, canvasjs, jspdf and tableExport bundled locally and a Hindi-first navigation (Karyasuchi, Karyawahi, Niyamavali, PapersLaid) matching no shared template.

---

## Uttarakhand (IN-UT)

**Verdict:** partial - Uttarakhand has a unicameral legislature with a strong official presence: the NeVA public portal (utkh.neva.gov.in) publishes a full 70-member roster with photos, party, constituency and per-member question/bill/committee tabs, and the CEO publishes AC-wise Form 20/21C/21E election results - but there is NO per-MLA Vidhayak Nidhi / MLA-LAD fund data anywhere official, and no attendance is published.

### Profile

The assembly's own site is **https://ukvidhansabha.uk.gov.in/** (title: "Legislative Assembly Uttarakhand | India"), built on NIC's S3WaaS WordPress platform (`wp-content/themes/sdo-theme`, footer "Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre, Ministry of Electronics & Information Technology, Government of India"). From this network the host answers **HTTP 401 Basic Auth** on every path (nginx `WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm=""`) - it is reachable in India but blocked to this fetcher; all content below was confirmed through Wayback Machine snapshots. The authoritative roster path is **/dpages/present-members**, linked directly from the official state portal https://uk.gov.in/ (which references `ukvidhansabha.uk.gov.in/dpages/present-members`, `/dpages/leader-of-opposition`, `/dpages/lok-sabha`, `/dpages/rajya-sabha`, `/dpages/secretariat`). The mirrored path /present-members/ renders a plain HTML table (`class="data-table-1"`, caption "Present Members") of **70 rows** with columns Sl No. / Constituency Name / Candidate's Name / Party / Votes / Margins / Voter Rate% - i.e. the roster is essentially the 2022 election result table. That table contains **zero `<img>` tags and zero `<a>` links**: no photographs, no per-member profile pages, and no per-member query on this site. Other roster pages exist by term: /members-of-first-legislative-assembly/, /members-of-second-/, /members-of-third-/, /members-of-fourth-legislative-assembly/, /members-of-interim-legislative-assembly/, /nominated-members/ and /women-members/.

**The real member data lives on NeVA, not on the assembly site.** **https://utkh.neva.gov.in/** (title: "Welcome to Uttarakhand Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application", header "उत्तराखंड विधान सभा - पंचम विधान सभा") is curl-200 and is the NIC National e-Vidhan Application instance for Uttarakhand. Its member directory is at **/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec** (nav item सदस्य), backed by the AJAX endpoint **/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList**, which returns **70 member cards**, each carrying: a **photograph** (served from `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/UK/FileStructures//Member/thumb/<file>.png|.jpg`), the member's name in Hindi, party in Hindi, निर्वाचन क्षेत्र का नाम (constituency, with district), जन्म की तारीख (date of birth), आवास (residential address), phone number, and a link to `/Member/Details/<id>?AssemblyID=5`. Party-wise representation is published on the homepage: **BJP 47, INC 20, Independent 2, BSP 1, total 70**. The directory can be filtered by assembly: पंचम (5th), चतुर्थ (4th), तृतीय (3rd), द्वितीय (2nd), प्रथम (1st), अनन्तिम (interim), plus Panel of Chairpersons, मंत्रिपरिषद (Council of Ministers), सचेतक (Whips) and जन्मदिन (birthdays). Two bulk exports are published: **/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember** (served as `application/excel`, actually an HTML-flavoured spreadsheet) and **/ContactDirectory/PDFforHonbleMember** (3,884,880-byte `application/pdf`). The per-member detail shell (`/Member/Details/28?AssemblyID=5`) loads its tabs by AJAX from `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail`, `/Member/Member_Question`, `/Member/Member_Notice` and `/Member/Member_Committee`; note that these partials return a bare "BLANK" template unless a session context is present, and the detail shell carries a stale breadcrumb reading "eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh" - a template artifact confirming the shared-platform lineage. Term/assembly number is explicit via the `AssemblyID` parameter (5 = पंचम विधान सभा / 5th Assembly), and each `/Member/Details/<id>?AssemblyID=N` URL is term-scoped. A secondary, lower-fidelity roster also exists at **https://cm.uk.gov.in/hi/विधान-सभा/** ("Member of Legislative Assembly | मुख्यमंत्री, उत्तराखंड सरकार", curl-200), an HTML table of 70 rows with columns क्र.सं. / विधायक का नाम / विधानसभा क्षेत्र का नाम / राजनीतिक दल का नाम - again with **no photos and no per-member links**.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

Uttarakhand's fund is called the **MLA Fund** (विधायक निधि / "MLA Nidhi Yojana"); it is also referred to as MLA-LAD in the press. The scheme page is **https://ukrd.uk.gov.in/scheme/gramin-bhandaran-yojana/** (Rural Development Department, Uttarakhand; the page title is literally "MLA Fund" - note the URL slug `gramin-bhandaran-yojana` is a reused CMS slug and does not describe the scheme). It states: "In order to fulfil the objectives of balanced development of local needs in the constituencies of Hon'ble Members of Legislative Assembly, 'MLA Nidhi Yojana' was started in the year 2002 and by constituting the MLA Fund by Government Order dated **07.06.2002**, guidelines were also framed for the implementation of the scheme... At present... the earlier MLA Fund related guidelines were downgraded and the guidelines were reorganized in the form of an integrated circular." Beneficiary: "Residents of constituencies represented by MLAs." How to apply: "Projects are proposed by MLAs and approved as per government guidelines."

**There is no per-MLA data portal.** The scheme's own document library, **https://ukrd.uk.gov.in/document-category/mla-fund/**, contains exactly **one** file - "MLA-Guideline", a 9,231,257-byte, **29-page PDF** at `https://cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in/s3e9412ee564384b987d086df32d6ce6b7/uploads/2025/02/202605061097580764.pdf` (`/Font` 15, `/Image` 31 - a digital, text-extractable PDF, not a scan). That is the scheme-rules document, not fund data. I swept the whole Rural Development Department site (`https://ukrd.uk.gov.in/`, curl-200): the only fund-related categories are `document-category/mla-fund/` (guidelines only) and a separate `document-category/mplads/` for the MP scheme. There is **no allocated-limit, works-recommended, works-sanctioned, works-completed or expenditure figure published per MLA, per constituency, per district or even in aggregate** - no Uttarakhand equivalent of https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/ was found. The state portal https://uk.gov.in/ exposes no fund module. Every per-MLA expenditure figure that exists in the public domain comes from **RTI petitions reported by the press** (e.g. NDTV's "34 फीसदी फंड खर्च नहीं कर पाए", Jagran's minister-wise RTI comparison, Bhaskar's Udham Singh Nagar report card, Hindustan Times on MLA-LAD being cut back to ₹5 crore/year) - the existence of a journalism-based data pipeline here is itself evidence that no official portal publishes it. An honest statement of the position: **the scheme exists, the guidelines are officially published, and the per-MLA figures are not public.**

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Starred and unstarred questions ARE published, per sitting and filterable per member - but only on NeVA, not on the assembly site.** The assembly site's only question-related page is https://ukvidhansabha.uk.gov.in/types-of-questions/, which is an explanatory page about question procedure, not data. On **https://utkh.neva.gov.in/Questions** the sidebar offers तारांकित प्रश्न (Starred), अतारांकित प्रश्न (Unstarred), अल्प सूचना प्रश्न (Short-notice) and अंतर-सत्र प्रश्न (Inter-session), plus answer lists (उत्तर तारांकित / उत्तर अतारांकित / संक्षिप्त सूचना प्रश्न). The underlying AJAX endpoints are `/Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem=&Type=` (LstItem 0=starred, 1=unstarred, 3=short-notice, 4=inter-session), `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionBySearch?AssId=&SessId=&SessDateId=` (sitting-wise) and `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionAdvBySearch?FromAssemblyID=&ToAssemblyID=&fromSessionCode=&toSessionCode=&DepartmentID=&Subject=&MemberID=&MinsitryMinistersID=` (advanced, **including a MemberID filter**). A live pull from `/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionBySearch?AssId=5&SessId=11&SessDateId=430` (13 March 2026) returned a table with columns क्रमांक / प्रश्न सं / विषय / प्रश्न / विभाग / मंत्रालय - the **full question text**, the answering department and ministry, and a link to the signed question PDF of the form `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/UK/FileStructures//PaperLaid/{assembly}/{session}/Signed/{a}_{s}_SN_{n}_V1_signed.pdf` (`_SN_` = starred notice). The homepage counter showed **641 questions** for the current house; the search form exposes members by name and code (e.g. श्री आदेश चौहान (26), श्रीमती अनुपमा रावत (128)), and `/Business/MemberListForQuestion1` returns that list as JSON. In practice the per-member (MemberID) filter returned zero rows for every member code I tested while the unfiltered assembly-5 query returned 8 rows, and the advanced search's result set was much smaller than the 641 counted on the dashboard - so the question text is officially published and sitting-wise queryable, while **per-member question aggregation is offered by the UI but did not yield data in testing**. The signed question PDFs themselves are **scanned images** (`/Font` 0, `/Image` 8) - the machine-readable question text is the HTML partial, not the PDF.

**Private members' bills: the filter exists, the data does not.** On **https://utkh.neva.gov.in/Bill** the search form exposes विधानसभा (all six terms), सत्र, बिल श्रेणी (Money / Financial / Ordinary / Constitution Amendment / Ordinance Replacing) and critically **BillTypeId with the option "Private Members Bills"** (id 2, against "This is a Government Bill (Other)" = 1), plus status filters (Assented / Passed / Pending / Withdrawn / Negatived / Lapsed / Introduced). Calling `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch?BillTypeId=2&...` returns **"कोई रिकॉर्ड नहीं मिला" (no records found)**, and the same empty result came back for BillTypeId=0 (all types) on assembly 5, 4 and 3; the portal dashboard itself reports "सरकारी विधेयक 0" (government bills 0). The assembly site's separate page **https://ukvidhansabha.uk.gov.in/list-of-bills-passed/** carries a single document, "List of Bills Signed by Hon'ble Governor 2001-2020" (393 KB PDF at `cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in/s353c3348f1fa3f4342107da7afbb8e3e0/uploads/2025/07/202507312038340874.pdf`, `/Font` 82 - a text PDF), alongside per-year categories `/document-category/bill-2001/` … `/bill-2023/` and `/document-category/proceedings-YYYY/`, `/minutes-of-session-YYYY/`, `/agenda-YYYY/`. So: bills are filed in bulk/legacy form, and there is **no per-member private-members'-bill record**.

**Attendance is NOT published anywhere.** I inspected the NeVA member detail page and its tabs - General / Questions (प्रश्न) / Notice (सूचना) / Assurances (आश्वासन) / Committee (समिति) / Debates (वाद-विवाद) / Bills (विधेयक) - and searched every public module (`/Today`, `/Business`, `/Committee`, `/Debates`, `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, `/Home/DigitalLibrary`) for attendance strings (उपस्थिति / उपस्थित / attendance). **No attendance field, page, tab or counter exists on any public page.** The NeVA CMS at **https://cmsutkh.neva.gov.in** presents a "NeVA CMS - Sign in" login form with a password field, which is where any internal attendance module would sit; it is not public. Roster-with-photo, questions and the fund are the three pillars; **attendance and per-member private-members'-bills are genuine, documented gaps.**

### Format note

A fetcher must handle **three different regimes, and the split runs right through the middle of the project**. (1) The assembly's own site `ukvidhansabha.uk.gov.in` is effectively **closed to a server-side fetcher** - every path answers **HTTP 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Basic realm=""`** from outside India (the host is real: nginx returns a full S3WaaS CSP including `*.s3waas.gov.in` and `feedback.digifootprint.gov.in`), so it needs either an Indian egress or a headless browser with a residential/Indian IP; the Wayback Machine is a workable fallback. (2) **NeVA `utkh.neva.gov.in` is the best target and needs no browser, but needs a two-step session**: it is ASP.NET MVC that sets `ASP.NET_SessionId`, `lang` and a state cookie on the homepage, and any module route (`/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Committee`, `/Debates`, `/Today`, `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`) fetched *without* that cookie silently 302-redirects to the national `/Home/NeVA` page and returns the wrong 192 KB document with an HTTP 200 - a trap that looks like success. With a cookie jar the module pages and the AJAX partials return ordinary HTML, and several endpoints (`/Home/AssemblyChange`, `/Home/SessionChangeNew`, `/Business/MemberListForQuestion1`) return `application/json; charset=utf-8` outright, so `curl-json` is genuinely available for lookups. The per-AC question text is in that HTML, **but every PDF the portal links is a scan**: the `PaperLaid/.../SN_..._signed.pdf` question files are `/Font` 0, and so are the assembly's agenda/notification PDFs on the S3WaaS CDN (Creator "HP Scan") - so anything derived from the PDFs needs **OCR**, while the HTML partials need only parsing. (3) **Election results are the OCR wall.** The CEO's own "Elected Candidate 2022" list and *all* Form 20/21C/21E per-constituency PDFs are `/Font` 0 with `Creator: HP Scan` - a fetcher gets an HTML index page of 70 links and 70 scanned images behind it. The ECI's own sites are the opposite problem: `www.eci.gov.in` is a **React SPA** (1,279-byte shell, `<div id="root">`), so it is `curl`-blind and needs a headless browser, and `results.eci.gov.in` now redirects to the current election with the 2022 paths returning 404. Net requirement: **cookie-jar curl for NeVA HTML/JSON, headless browser (or Indian egress) for the assembly site, PDF text extraction for the two text PDFs (MLA guidelines, bills list), and OCR for every election-result and question/agenda PDF.**

### Gap note

Three things cannot be sourced officially and should be marked "not sourced" rather than estimated. **(1) Per-MLA development-fund figures - the largest gap.** Uttarakhand has a real MLA Fund scheme with officially published guidelines (GO dated 07.06.2002, current integrated circular on `ukrd.uk.gov.in`), but **no portal publishes an allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure figure for any MLA** - not per-MLA, not per-constituency, not even in aggregate. The RD Department's entire MLA-fund document library is one guidelines PDF. The state has no equivalent of `mplads.mospi.gov.in`, and the fact that every per-MLA spending figure in circulation comes from RTI-based journalism confirms the absence rather than filling it. **(2) Attendance.** No attendance page, tab, field or counter exists on the NeVA public portal or the assembly site; the NeVA CMS at `cmsutkh.neva.gov.in` is login-gated and is the only place such a module could live. **(3) Per-member private members' bills.** The bill module offers a "Private Members Bills" filter and the status/category filters are populated, but the endpoint returns "no records found" for BillTypeId=2 and for all bill types across assemblies 5, 4 and 3, and the portal's own dashboard reads "सरकारी विधेयक 0". Bills survive only as a bulk legacy PDF ("List of Bills Signed by Hon'ble Governor 2001-2020") and per-year document categories - there is no per-member, per-bill record. Secondary gaps: the assembly site publishes **no member photographs** (its roster table has no `<img>` and no `<a>` at all) - photographs exist only on NeVA at the `cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/UK/FileStructures//Member/thumb/` pattern; and **per-member question aggregation** is offered by the advanced search's MemberID parameter but returned zero rows for every member code tested, so per-MLA question counts should be treated as unavailable until a real session-based run confirms otherwise.

### Platform

Uttarakhand is **doubly on shared NIC national platforms - not bespoke**, and both are reuse opportunities. First, its legislature runs on **NeVA (National e-Vidhan Application)**, developed by NIC under the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs: PIB Release 2294830 (05 Aug 2026) lists "Uttarakhand Legislative Assembly" among the 21 houses transformed into digital legislatures, and NIC's own news page records the CM and Speaker launching the NeVA portal on **21 February 2025**. The evidence is decisive and scrapeable: the portal lives at the pattern **`<housecode>.neva.gov.in`** (Uttarakhand = `utkh`), the national portal links **40** such house hosts (`upvs`, `raj`, `hpvs`, `gujarat`, `kerala`, `tsla`, `wbla`, `mhla`, `tnla`, `odisha`, …), and fetching eight of them returns the **identical template** with only the state name substituted - e.g. utkh / upvs / raj / hpvs / gujarat / kerala / tsla / wbla all render `Welcome to <State> Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application` with the same sidebar, the same AJAX route names (`/Questions/FetchDataListQuestionBySearch`, `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`) and the same footer "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India". **One NeVA scraper therefore serves many states**; only the house code, assembly/session IDs and the document-store path segment (`/NeVA/UK/FileStructures/`) change. Second, the assembly site, the CEO and the Rural Development Department are all built on **S3WaaS** ("Secure, Scalable and Sugamya Website as a Service", NIC), identifiable by `wp-content/themes/sdo-theme`, the S3WaaS/NIC/Digital-India footer SVGs, the `cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in/s3<hash>/uploads/…` document CDN and the literal footer "Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre" - `ukvidhansabha.uk.gov.in`, `ceo.uk.gov.in` and `ukrd.uk.gov.in` share all of these. S3WaaS themes vary by site class (the district site `dehradun.nic.in` uses `district-theme-6` on the same `s3waas.gov.in` CDN), but the document-table markup, the "Content Owned by … Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre" footer and the CDN URL shape are constant - so a single S3WaaS document-table parser also generalises across Uttarakhand's departments and across other states. The state portal `https://uk.gov.in/` is the one exception (no `sdo-theme`, no S3WaaS references) and is a separate build.

---

## West Bengal (IN-WB)

**Verdict:** partial - West Bengal has a fully constituted legislature (18th Vidhan Sabha) with an official NIC-built assembly site and a live National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) instance carrying profile / questions / bills modules, and ECI publishes complete assembly results; but the assembly's own bespoke site at https://assembly.wb.gov.in/ is currently unreachable/unusable from outside and no official portal publishes PER-MLA BEUP (MLALAD) fund figures at all.

### Profile
West Bengal **does** have a legislature: the West Bengal Legislative Assembly (পশ্চিমবঙ্গ বিধানসভা), a unicameral Vidhan Sabha currently in its **18th Assembly** (the Legislative Council was abolished in 1969). There is therefore no "no-legislature" position here.

Two official assembly sites exist, and they are different systems on different domains:

1. **https://assembly.wb.gov.in/** - the assembly's own (new) website. The long-standing domain **http://www.wbassembly.gov.in** now serves a redirect notice reading "You will be redirected to the new website of West Bengal Legislative Assembly. **https://assembly.wb.gov.in**" (verified from the Wayback Machine capture of the official old site, 2026-03-08). `assembly.wb.gov.in` resolves (103.60.135.52), TCP 80/443 are open, TLS completes with a valid DigiCert/RapidSSL certificate for `*.wb.gov.in`, and `http://` returns **301 → https://**. However every HTTPS `GET` from this network either hung with **0 bytes received for 45-150 s** or returned a **302 to `/Error_ISE.aspx?aspxerrorpath=/default.aspx`** (the ASP.NET "Internal Server Error" handler). I could not obtain a single rendered page from it. It is an **ASP.NET WebForms** application (`default.aspx`, `__doPostBack`, `ctl00$menu1$btn_*` controls). I could not retrieve it directly, so its roster was characterised from the Wayback capture of the same site (2026-06-07), which is *not* live evidence.

   From that archived capture the site's navigation (all of it JavaScript postbacks, not links) contains: **Members Who's Who**, **Member List**, Elected Members, Nominated Anglo-Indian Members, Casual Vacancies, Bye-Elections, Ministers, Parliamentary Secretaries, **Party-wise Position of the Members**, **Gender-wise Members**, Facilities to Members, Speakers/Deputy Speakers, Chief Ministers, Leaders of Opposition, **Question** and **Question Search**, Bulletin Part-I/II/III, **List of Business**, Bills introduced, Bills with Select Committee, Bills Withdrawn, Acts, Ordinances, Governor's Address, Budget Statement, Proceedings, Photo Gallery, Dignitaries, Secretariat. Its PDF archive under `/OtherPDF/` includes **`LMLA.pdf` (List of Members of the Legislative Assembly)**, `HandBookMembers.pdf`, `WBLA_Origin_Growth.pdf`, `Act_1947_2018.pdf`, `BCRBook.pdf` (Rules, Procedure and Conduct of Business) and `RPCB.pdf`. The footer credit reads: *"All Rights Reserved © 2026. Site Maintained and Contents provided by West Bengal Legislative Assembly. Site Designed, Developed & Hosted By National Informatics Center"* - i.e. an NIC-built **bespoke** state build, not the shared NeVA template.

2. **https://wbla.neva.gov.in/** - the West Bengal instance of the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, live and `curl-200` (85,030 bytes). Title: "Welcome to West Bengal Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application". It renders bilingual (Bengali/English) and shows the **18th Vidhan Sabha** with a stated **members count of 292**, party-wise representation (Bharatiya Janata Party 207, All India Trinamool Congress 80, Indian National Congress 2, AJSU Party 1, All India Secular Front 1, CPI(M) 1) and the current office-holders (Governor R. N. Ravi; Chief Minister Suvendu Adhikari; Speaker Rathindra Bose; Leader of Opposition Ritabrata Banerjee; Principal Secretary Soumendra Nath Das).

   The NeVA instance **does** publish a queryable member directory at **https://wbla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec** (`curl-200`, 91 KB, title "Reach out to us - Members Contact Directory | West Bengal Vidhan Sabha"). It exposes faceted filters for: assembly term (17th / 18th Vidhan Sabha), Cabinet, Whip, birthday month, gender (male/female members), nominated members, first-time elected members, re-elected members, **political party**, and **constituency name** (the full 294-constituency Bengali list is rendered into the filter panel). Individual member records are addressable at **`/Member/Details/{id}`** (e.g. `/Member/Details/306` returns `curl-200`, 22,426 bytes) - **but those pages currently render empty**: the record area shows "No Records Founds" for Questions, Assurances, Debates and BILLS, and the page template leaks a "eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh" label. The AJAX endpoint behind the directory, `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`, returns **HTTP 500** for every parameter combination tried (`MLT`/`MSLT`/`Assemblyid`), and the sibling `GetMembersDashbardData` returns **HTTP 404**. WB signed its NeVA MoU only on 4 June 2026, so the roster backend is not yet populated.

**PHOTO:** no member photograph could be obtained from any official source. The NeVA member-detail pages are empty and the member directory page contains no member `<img>` elements at all (only the G20 logo and the Bharat emblem). Photos are expected under the bespoke site's "Members Who's Who" / `LMLA.pdf` route, but that host is not retrievable and the archived `LMLA.pdf` (3,667,985 bytes, fetched from Wayback) contains only **1 font object and 5 embedded JPEG (DCTDecode) images with no extractable text operators** in its decompressed content streams - i.e. an image/scanned document. Photo coverage is therefore recorded as **partial/unknown**, not as "no".

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
In West Bengal the MLA local area development fund is **not** called MLALAD. Its statutory name is **Bidhayak Elaka Unnayan Prakalpa (BEUP)** - "বিধায়ক এলাকা উন্নয়ন প্রকল্প" (MLA Area Development Programme), commonly referred to in Bengali as **বিধায়ক তহবিল / Vidhayak Nidhi**. The national analogue is MPLADS, and WB's **Department of Planning & Statistics** is the nodal department for *both* MPLADS and BEUP (stated on https://wb.gov.in/departments-details.aspx?id=D171106123639518&page=Planning-and-Statistics, `curl-200`).

**Official scheme page:** **https://planning.wb.gov.in/departmentdetails/5** (`curl-200`, 18,327 bytes) - "Bidhayak Elaka Unnayan Prakalpa (BEUP) ... is a Flagship Programme of the State to enable the Members of Legislative Assembly (MLAs) for suggesting and executing development schemes for their respective constituencies." The same page publishes the entitlement history, which is the closest thing to a per-MLA *allocation* figure that exists publicly:

| Assembly / FY | Entitlement per MLA per FY |
| --- | --- |
| 12th Assembly, 2000-01 (introduction) | ₹15.00 lakh |
| 13th Assembly, from 2001-02 | ₹25.00 lakh |
| 14th Assembly, from 2006-07 | ₹40.00 lakh |
| FY 2008-09 | ₹50.00 lakh |
| FY 2010-11 | ₹60.00 lakh |
| From FY 2023-24 (present) | **₹70.00 lakh** |

The page states the fund "is released by the Department of Planning & Statistics in two equal instalments of Rs. 35.00 lakh each", that schemes span Irrigation & Flood Control, Drinking Water, Health, Electricity, Transport, Education, Water Supply & Sanitation, Housing, Social Welfare, Agriculture & Allied Activities, Animal Care, Non-Conventional Energy, Sports and Tourism, and that executing agencies are Panchayat bodies, Urban Local Bodies, Government Departments, Government Corporations and agencies under the Government.

**Official scheme rules / government guidelines:** **https://planning.wb.gov.in/uploads/photos/pdf/Bidhayak_Elaka_Unnayan_Prakalpa_2025.pdf** (`curl-200`, `application/pdf`, **36,143,910 bytes**, 82 pages) - "Bidhayak Elaka Unnayan Prakalpa (BEUP) Guidelines-2025". **This PDF has no text layer**: the file contains **0 `/Font` objects and 0 `/ToUnicode` entries**, and decompressed content streams yielded **no text operators (`BT`/`Tj`/`TJ`)**; the bulk of its 36 MB is JPEG image data. It is an **image-only PDF requiring OCR**.

**PER-MLA DATA: no official portal found.** No live government source publishes the per-MLA series (allocated limit / works recommended / works sanctioned / works completed / expenditure) for West Bengal. Specifically:

- The Department of Planning & Statistics' own BEUP page links only to the 2025 Guidelines; it publishes **no** per-MLA progress report, no district-wise or constituency-wise table, and no download of works/expenditure status.
- Its Circular & Memo page (https://planning.wb.gov.in/circularNotification, `curl-200`, 14,056 bytes) lists only 5 items - Jan Kalyan Shivir (06/06/2026), Jan Kalyan Shivir (06/06/2026), NITI Aayog (11/05/2026), Swanirbhar Bangla (14/02/2026), "Amader Para, Amader Samadhan" (26/07/2025) - **none of them BEUP progress data**.
- A **per-MLA BEUP reporting portal did once exist** at **`http://wbplan.gov.in/beupweb/`** (Department of Planning, Government of West Bengal). Search-engine indexes still hold example files from it: *"Status Report of the 15th Assembly - 2011-16 (as on 11.02.2013).pdf"* and *"15th Assembly - (2011-16) As on 15.05.2013.pdf"*, plus the department's annual report `http://www.wbplan.gov.in/docs/AnnualReport9-10/STATE  SECTOR SCHEME.pdf`. **The domain `wbplan.gov.in` is now NXDOMAIN (dead)** - DNS returns "server can't find wbplan.gov.in"; every URL on it failed with exit code 6. The Wayback Machine holds a 2018 capture of the bare root but **no snapshot of any `/beupweb/` URL** (`archived_snapshots: {}`). The successor department site is `planning.wb.gov.in`, which does not carry the old reports forward. So the historical per-MLA BEUP data is offline and not recoverable from the official web.
- District-level official sites restate the scheme but publish no per-MLA figures: `https://kalimpong.gov.in/district-planning/` (`curl-200`) carries the entry "1. Bidhayak Elaka Unnayan Prakalpa (BEUP) ... Each MLA is entitled to recommend schemes for Rs 50 lakhs for each financial year" - a **stale figure** (superseded by ₹70 lakh from FY 2023-24).

Net position: the BEUP **scheme** is documented officially (rules, nodal department, entitlement, sectors), but **per-MLA fund performance is not published by any official source**. It is obtainable only through RTI or from the MLA/district level.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
**Starred questions - partial.** NeVA WB exposes a Questions module at **https://wbla.neva.gov.in/Questions?IsStateDashBoard=True** (`curl-200`, 20,237 bytes), titled "West Bengal Vidhan Sabha Parliamentary Questions and Discussions", with the sub-sections **তারকাচিহ্নিত প্রশ্নাবলী** (Starred Questions), **তারকাবিহীন প্রশ্ন** (Unstarred Questions) and **সংক্ষিপ্ত বিজ্ঞপ্তি প্রশ্ন** (Short Notice Questions), plus answer-status views. The filter shells render and respond, but **no question records are present** in the result area for either the 18th or the 17th Assembly (`AssemblyId=18` and `AssemblyId=17` both return an empty table with a single `<tr>`). The legacy assembly site also advertised a "Question" and "Question Search" menu entry, but it is not retrievable.

**Private members' bills - partial.** NeVA WB's Bills module at **https://wbla.neva.gov.in/Bill?IsStateDashBoard=True** (`curl-200`, 36,223 bytes) filters by House (18th/17th Assembly), Session, bill category (Money Bill, Financial Bill, Ordinary Bill, Constitution Amendment Bill, Ordinance Replacing Bill), year (2010-2026), status (Assented / Passed / Pending / Withdrawn / Negatived / Lapsed / Introduced) and bill type - and the bill-type control explicitly includes **"Private Members Bills"** alongside "This is a Government Bill (Other)". The result grid is **empty** ("কোন রেকর্ড এখনও ফিল্টার করা.." - no records yet).

**Attendance - no.** Neither the NeVA instance nor any other official West Bengal source publishes MLA attendance. The string "উপস্থিতি" (attendance) appears **0 times** on the WB NeVA homepage, the NeVA platform has no attendance module for state members, and no attendance file was found on the assembly or CEO sites. Reported party-level pressure on MLA absenteeism exists only in news reporting (ANI, NDTV, Times of India, 2023), which is **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification**.

**Not published anywhere official:** per-member attendance, per-member question counts, per-member debate participation, and division/voting records. What the official sources *do* provide is the sitting-level legislative record - List of Business (`/Business?LstItem=4&IsStateDashBoard=True`, `curl-200`), Bulletins Part I/II/III, Synopsis of daily proceedings, Debates (`/Debates`, `curl-200`, 18,151 bytes), Committee reports, Demands for Grants, Budget, Governor's Address, Economic Review - but these are session documents, **not indexed per member**.

### Format note
A fetcher for West Bengal needs **three different pipelines**. (1) The NeVA instance `wbla.neva.gov.in` is plain server-rendered HTML and works with `curl` + a **session cookie jar** - several modules (`/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Debates`) return the *national* NeVA homepage instead of the state page unless a cookie is established by first hitting `/`, and the state dashboard variants need `?IsStateDashBoard=True`. The directory filters POST/GET through AJAX endpoints (`/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`) that currently answer **HTTP 500**, so the roster cannot be pulled today; there is no captcha and no geo-block on this host. (2) The assembly's own site `assembly.wb.gov.in` is an **ASP.NET WebForms** app whose entire navigation is `javascript:__doPostBack('ctl00$menu1$btn_*','')` against `__VIEWSTATE`; a link-following crawler can enumerate nothing and a headless browser or explicit postback+ViewState replay is required - and it is moot right now because every HTTPS GET either hangs (0 bytes for up to 150 s) or 302s to `/Error_ISE.aspx`, with substantially slower TLS negotiation than normal (the same slow-TLS pattern appears on `ceowestbengal.nic.in`, resolving in ~30-90 s). (3) The BEUP documents are **image PDFs**: `Bidhayak_Elaka_Unnayan_Prakalpa_2025.pdf` is 36 MB / 82 pages with **zero `/Font` and zero `/ToUnicode` objects**, so it demands **OCR** (no PDF text extraction is possible); the archived `LMLA.pdf` is likewise image-heavy (5 embedded JPEGs, no extractable text operators). `results.eci.gov.in` is the easy case - fully server-rendered HTML tables, `curl-200`, no JavaScript and no captcha, though the whole page is a single flat result set with no per-AC permalink. No captcha, geo-IP block or POST-only wall was encountered on `wbla.neva.gov.in`, `planning.wb.gov.in`, `ceowestbengal.wb.gov.in` or `results.eci.gov.in`; the only genuine failures were `assembly.wb.gov.in` (server-side ASP.NET error / hang) and `wbplan.gov.in` (domain no longer exists).

### Gap note
The largest documented gap is **per-MLA development-fund data**. West Bengal has a real, officially documented scheme (BEUP, nodal department: Planning & Statistics, presently ₹70 lakh per MLA per financial year, released in two ₹35 lakh instalments, rules published as BEUP Guidelines-2025) but **no public source publishes allocated limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure per MLA** - not the department site, not the district sites, not NeVA, and not the assembly site. The one portal that ever carried per-MLA BEUP progress reports (`wbplan.gov.in/beupweb/`, e.g. the 15th Assembly 2011-16 status reports as on 11.02.2013 and 15.05.2013) is **gone: the domain is NXDOMAIN, and the Wayback Machine has no capture of those URLs**, so even the historical series is not machine-recoverable. Second gap: **assembly-record data does not exist in populated form** - the NeVA Questions and Bills modules are live shells with correct starred/unstarred/short-notice and Private Members Bills taxonomies but zero records, and the member directory backend returns HTTP 500. Third gap: **attendance is not published anywhere official** (no attendance module exists in NeVA, and 0 occurrences of the term on the WB instance) - any attendance figure would have to come from an unofficial aggregator. Fourth gap: **member photographs cannot currently be sourced officially** - the only candidate routes are the bespoke assembly site's "Members Who's Who" (host not retrievable) and the scanned `LMLA.pdf` (no text layer, photos only as page images). Fifth gap: the assembly's **own website is currently unusable from outside**, so the 18th Assembly roster, its PDFs and its Question Search are all unreachable even though the domain and TLS certificate are live. Finally, the sitting-level legacy record (Bulletins, Debates, Synopsis, Acts, Ordinances) is published, but it is **not indexed per member**, so no per-MLA assembly record can be assembled from it without manual document parsing.

### Platform
West Bengal is a **hybrid**, and both halves are independently useful. It runs the **shared national NIC platform**: it joined the National e-Vidhan Application under a tripartite MoU signed **4 June 2026** (PIB release 2269037 - WB became the 33rd legislature to join, per PIB release 2289882 dated 27 JUL 2026 stating 33 of 37 State/UT Legislatures have signed NeVA MoUs and 21 Houses are fully digital). Its instance is **https://wbla.neva.gov.in/**, following the `<statecode>.neva.gov.in` pattern shared with the 39 instances listed on https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA (`bla`, `kerala`, `tripura`, `karnataka`, `raj`, `tsla`, `puddu`, `utkh`, `Jkla`, `loksabha`, `rajyasabha`, ...). Concrete identity evidence across states: `bla.neva.gov.in` (Bihar, 88,008 B), `kerala.neva.gov.in` (72,978 B), `tripura.neva.gov.in` (90,856 B) and `wbla.neva.gov.in` (85,030 B) all carry the byte-identical footer *"© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India / Content managed by: <State> Legislative Assembly"* and the identical build string **"Version: 2.0.2 / Last Updated: 25 Mar 2026"**, the same `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business`, `/Debates`, `/Member/Details/{id}` routes and the same `/2024/css/bootstrap.min.css` assets - **one scraper serves all of them, with only the hostname changing**. Alongside that, WB still maintains its **own bespoke NIC-built ASP.NET assembly site** at `assembly.wb.gov.in` (footer: "Site Designed, Developed & Hosted By National Informatics Center"; `ctl00$menu1$btn_*` postback menu; `/OtherPDF/*.pdf` archive), and its state election office runs on a bespoke state template at `ceowestbengal.wb.gov.in` (a `.wb.gov.in` domain, migrated from `ceowestbengal.nic.in` in Nov 2025) that nonetheless **delegates national results to the shared ECI portal** `results.eci.gov.in`. So: **shared NeVA + shared ECI results + bespoke NIC assembly/CEO sites**.

## Andaman and Nicobar Islands (IN-AN)

**Verdict:** no-legislature - The Andaman and Nicobar Islands is a union territory with **no Legislative Assembly**; it is administered by the President acting through the Lieutenant Governor/Administrator (Ministry of Home Affairs), so there is **no MLA, no MLA roster, and no MLA local area development fund**; the UT's last quasi-representative body, the Pradesh Council, lapsed in April 1994 and was replaced by a three-tier Panchayat system.

### Profile

**There is no legislature, therefore there is no assembly website and no member roster.** This is established by both constitutional text and the UT administration's own published statements.

**Constitutional position.** Article 240(1)(a) of the Constitution of India places "the Andaman and Nicobar Islands" among the union territories for which *"The President may make regulations for the peace, progress and good Government"* — i.e. the UT is legislated for by presidential regulation, not by a local assembly. Article 240's proviso refers to a body created under **Article 239A** to function as a Legislature **only for Puducherry**; no such body exists or is contemplated for the Andaman and Nicobar Islands. The UT is administered under Article 239 by an Administrator appointed by the President; for A&N the Administrator is the **Lieutenant Governor**, reporting to the **Ministry of Home Affairs** (the A&N Islands fall in the MHA's AGMUT cadre). There are no assembly constituencies in the UT at all.

**Official confirmation from the UT's own government.** The Directorate of Panchayat & Municipal Election (A&N Administration) publishes this on its official site: *"The Andaman & Nicobar Islands being a UT is directly governed by The central government through the Lieutenant Governor, who is also the Administrator of these Islands. The Andaman & Nicobar Islands (Panchayats) Regulation 1994 came into effect from 23rd April 1994 repealing A&N Islands (Administration) Regulation 1979 which provided for the Pradesh Council (vide A&N Islands (Municipal Board) Regulation 1994). The tenure of Pradesh Council expired on April 1994."* The same page describes only a three-tier Panchayat system (Gram Panchayat, Panchayat Samiti, Zilla Parishad) plus a municipal body at Port Blair — no assembly tier. Note that the Pradesh Council was an **advisory** body created by regulation, not a legislature; its members were not MLAs, and its 1994 lapse left the UT with no local legislative body whatsoever.

**Official confirmation from the Election Commission's own directory.** The CEO, Andaman and Nicobar Islands states: *"The Andaman & Nicobar Islands is a Union Territory with a Single Parliamentary Constituency."* The UT therefore elects **one Member of Parliament** and nothing else at the state level.

**The UT administration's own official URL** (which is what the project should use in place of an assembly site):
- **https://andamannicobar.gov.in/** — page title *"The official website of Andaman & Nicobar Administration"*, self-described as *"The State Portal of U.T Administration, A & N Islands"*. Verified: HTTP 200, `text/html`, 140,684 bytes, fetched with curl. Footer credit: *"© 2026 All rights reserved, Contents Owned by Andaman & Nicobar Administration. Designed & Developed By : SOVTECH, A & N Administration."*

**IMPORTANT — domain migration, verified 2026-09-14.** The longstanding UT domain **`andaman.gov.in` no longer resolves at all**. Cloudflare DoH returns `Status 2 (SERVFAIL)` with `EDE(22): No Reachable Authority at delegation andaman.gov.in` for the apex, for `www.`, and for subdomains such as `police.andaman.gov.in` and `lg.andaman.gov.in` — the authoritative nameservers for the zone are unreachable. The administration migrated its websites to `andamannicobar.gov.in` in phases (final round 5 September 2025, per a local-news lead from The Wave Andaman, **UNOFFICIAL**), and `andamannicobar.gov.in` now serves the live official portal. Any bookmark, citation or scraper still pointing at `andaman.gov.in` is dead.

**What the UT portal actually publishes about the UT's lone representative** (the closest available substitute for a "profile" record, and it is genuinely rich): **https://andamannicobar.gov.in/and_mp** (verified HTTP 200, HTML, curl-fetchable) publishes, for the single Member of Parliament — name (**Shri Bishnu Pada Ray**), party (**Bharatiya Janata Party**), constituency (the lone A&N Islands Parliamentary Constituency), **an official PHOTO** (`https://andamannicobar.gov.in/admin-pannel/profile/mp.png`, verified HTTP 200 `image/png`, 143,263 bytes, 307×376 px), vote count and share (102,436 votes, 50.58%), **term/assembly number equivalent** (described as his 4th term, elected to the 13th, 15th, 16th and 18th Lok Sabha), portfolios, committee memberships, and office/contact addresses. A separate officers directory is at **https://andamannicobar.gov.in/contact**.

**A trap to avoid.** The UT portal carries a nav tab literally labelled **"Legislative"**, which a careless scraper could mistake for an assembly section. It is not: the tab filters to **"Legislative Documents (Acts/ Regulation/ Rules/ Notification)"** — i.e. delegated legislation and gazette documents uploaded by departments — on `https://andamannicobar.gov.in/latestupdate_all.php`. Keyword counts on that page show **0 occurrences each of "Legislative Assembly", "Vidhan Sabha", "MLA", "Vidhayak" and "MPLADS"**. There is no member roster, no question list and no attendance data anywhere on the UT portal.

**Verification level:** the absence of a legislature is established at **curl-200** level from three independent official government sources (Article 240 text via India Code/Indian Kanoon; the A&N Directorate of Panchayat & Municipal Election's own "Overview" page; the Election Commission of India's CEO office directory **and** its public JSON API). Negative proof is also affirmative: no assembly domain exists (`assembly.andamannicobar.gov.in`, `vidhansabha.andamannicobar.gov.in`, `andamanassembly.gov.in`, `assembly.andaman.gov.in`, `vidhansabha.andaman.gov.in`, `vidhan.and.nic.in`, `assembly.and.nic.in` and the NeVA-pattern `anla.neva.gov.in` / `andla.neva.gov.in` all return NXDOMAIN or SERVFAIL — none resolves).

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**There is no MLA development fund in Andaman and Nicobar Islands, because there are no MLAs.** No scheme by the name *Vidhayak Nidhi*, *Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi*, *MLALAD* or *MLA LADF* operates in this UT, and **no official portal for any such fund exists** — the terms do not appear anywhere on the UT administration portal or on the CEO's site (keyword scan of `latestupdate_all.php`: zero hits for `vidhayak`, `vidhan`, `MLA`, `mlalad`).

Consequently there is **no state-level equivalent of the MPLADS portal** for this jurisdiction. There is no government resolution, guideline document, or scheme-rules page to cite, because the scheme itself does not exist here — this is a genuine structural absence, not an unfound document.

**The nearest real, official, per-member fund for this jurisdiction is MPLADS — for the lone Member of Parliament, not for MLAs.** The Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation (MoSPI) runs the **MPLADS–eSAKSHI portal at https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/** (verified HTTP 200, `text/html`, 62,712 bytes). Since 1 April 2023 this portal manages the revised fund-flow procedure, and the portal states that *"the annual entitlement of Hon'ble Members of Parliament (MPs) is made available through online authorisations"*. It publishes, per MP, entitled/released/sanctioned/expended/unspent amounts, work recommendation, sanction and completion status, and it has a revamped public dashboard (announced by PIB). **The Andaman and Nicobar Islands' single MP is one row in that national, per-MP dataset.** This is genuinely per-member fund data traceable to an official government portal — but it is *MP*LADS for *one MP*, and it must never be presented on the site as an MLA fund. For this UT the "development fund per MLA" section should be rendered as **not applicable — no MLA exists**, with MPLADS-for-the-single-MP offered only as clearly-labelled context.

**Verification level:** the *absence* of an MLA fund is verified at curl-200 level against the UT administration portal (0 keyword hits) and is a logical and constitutional consequence of the absence of MLAs. The MPLADS portal URL is **curl-200**, but its *data* is not curl-fetchable (see Format note).

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Not applicable — there is no Vidhan Sabha and no assembly record of any kind.** There is no attendance data, no starred questions, no unstarred questions, and no private members' bills for the Andaman and Nicobar Islands, because the UT has no Legislative Assembly in which such records could be created. Nothing is "not published" in the sense of a missing dataset: **the institution that would generate the data does not exist.**

The UT is represented in **Parliament only** — one seat in the Lok Sabha (the Andaman & Nicobar Islands Parliamentary Constituency, ECI state code **U01**) and no seat of its own in the Rajya Sabha (UTs without a legislature are represented in the Rajya Sabha by Members elected by Parliament, so there is no direct UT-level return). Parliamentary questions, attendance and private members' bills for the UT's lone MP are matters for the **Parliament of India** (Lok Sabha / Rajya Sabha secretariats, e.g. the Parliamentary Library's eparlib and the Lok Sabha questions portal) — **not** for any A&N assembly, and they are out of scope for an "MLA record" dataset.

The UT's subordinate elected bodies — Gram Panchayats, Panchayat Samitis, Zilla Parishad and the Port Blair Municipal Council — do hold elections and have elected members, and the Directorate of Panchayat & Municipal Election publishes those results. Those members are **not MLAs**, and panchayat business (e.g. no-confidence motions) is not an assembly record. If the Atlas needs any elected-representative record for this UT, that is the only other official body of elected members available.
