### Format note

A fetcher needs **three different techniques** for this jurisdiction, and no single scraper covers it. (1) **Plain curl + HTML** works for the UT administration portal (`andamannicobar.gov.in` — ordinary PHP-rendered HTML, including the MP profile page and the `/admin-pannel/` PDF downloads) and for the Directorate of Panchayat & Municipal Election site (`db.and.nic.in`). (2) **curl + JSON** works beautifully for the Election Commission of India's backend at `https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/…` — `get-ac-election-state`, `get-officer-directory`, `get-officer-list`, `get-nomination`, `get-list-of-theme` and `get-election-data?page_seo_name=…` all return JSON (or JSON-wrapped HTML) to an unauthenticated curl with a browser User-Agent, **but the ECI website pages themselves are a React single-page app** (`/static/js/main.e468b53e.js`, `<div id="root">`, `<noscript>You need to enable JavaScript to run this app</noscript>`) that serves a 1,279-byte shell to curl — so `www.eci.gov.in`, `results.eci.gov.in` indexes, `eci.gov.in/statistical-reports/` and `eci.gov.in/assembly-election/` are **browser-needed or API-only**. Note also that a few API routes are **POST-only** (`assebly-election-innerpage` returns HTTP 405 to GET). (3) **A real browser** is unavoidable for **MPLADS eSAKSHI** (`mplads.mospi.gov.in`), which runs on the ZK AJAX framework: curl receives only a scaffolding page (`/zkau/web/...`, `<title>FMS</title>`, `body{display:none !important;}` anti-clickjacking rule) and the per-MP figures are fetched over ZK's own AJAX channel.

Concrete blockers to expect: **the UT's legacy domain `andaman.gov.in` is dead at the DNS level** (SERVFAIL, unreachable authoritative nameservers) — every old link must be rewritten to `andamannicobar.gov.in`; **several linked PDFs on the CEO site are simply broken** (the Form 20 final result sheet and the DEO Summary Report both curl-404 even though they are linked from the live announcements page); **filenames contain spaces and a typo** (`Candidate Scrunity Report (Updated).pdf`) and one announcement PDF **exceeded a 60-second timeout** at curl; and the working PDFs are **huge scans (16–17 MB, 70 pages)**. Those PDFs are, however, **not pure images** — the scrutiny report contains a genuine extractable text layer (`BT`/`Tj`/`TJ` operators present, `/ToUnicode`, TrueType fonts, alongside 135 embedded JPEG images), and a raw Flate stream decompression recovered readable affidavit text, so **PDF text extraction works and OCR should be the fallback, not the default**. No captcha, login, session cookie or geo-IP block was encountered on any official A&N or ECI endpoint tested, except the MPLADS *login* area which is separate from its public dashboard.

### Gap note

The gaps here are **structural, not documentary**, and that is the finding. There is **no MLA roster, no MLA photograph, no MLA term/assembly number, no MLA fund allocation, no works-recommended/sanctioned/completed/expenditure data per MLA, no attendance, no starred or unstarred questions, and no private members' bills** for the Andaman and Nicobar Islands — because the UT has no Legislative Assembly in which any of these could exist. No amount of further searching will produce them, and any site that displays them for IN-AN would be fabricating. The genuinely available official material for this jurisdiction is: **one MP** (profile, party, constituency and photograph on the UT portal; MPLADS per-MP fund data on the MoSPI portal; Lok Sabha election results via ECI/CEO A&N), and **local-body representatives** (Panchayat and Municipal election results via the Directorate of Panchayat & Municipal Election). Also missing and not obtainable officially: a complete historical record of the **Pradesh Council** (1956–1994) — the UT's only past quasi-representative body — for which no dedicated official archive or member roster was found; its existence and 1994 lapse are documented only in one paragraph on the Directorate's "Overview" page. Finally, the **Form 20 final result sheet for GE 2024 is currently a dead link** on the CEO's own site, so the most authoritative single document for the UT's Lok Sabha result is not presently retrievable from its official URL.

### Platform

**Bespoke — there is no common platform, and specifically no NIC/NeVA assembly template, because there is no assembly to template.** The UT administration portal at `andamannicobar.gov.in` is **not** an NIC template: its footer credits *"Designed & Developed By : SOVTECH, A & N Administration"* (SOVTECH = Society for Promotion of Vocational & Technical Education, an A&N Administration body, whose tender notices are hosted on the same official domain) — so it is a bespoke, UT-owned build. The CEO's site at `ceoandaman.nic.in` **is** NIC-hosted (*"Website Developed & Hosted by NIC, Andaman & Nicobar State Unit"*) but is built on a **generic Hugo 0.84.0 + Bootstrap static template** with un-renamed leftovers (`<title>Ceo ANI</title>`, a stray second `<title>Bootstrap</title>`, and `meta author = "Mark Otto, Jacob Thornton, and Bootstrap contributors"`); comparison against other CEO sites (Delhi, Puducherry, Kerala, Assam) shows **different templates on each**, so even the CEO layer is not one shared platform. The Directorate of Panchayat & Municipal Election site carries the tell-tale blank vendor slot (*"Website Designed, developed and Hosted by Port Blair"*). Decisively, **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) does not include A&N at all** — the NeVA site contains **zero occurrences of "Andaman" or "Nicobar"**, and NeVA's per-legislature instances follow the `<statecode>la.neva.gov.in` pattern (e.g. `jhla`, `jkla`, `arla`) with **no A&N instance existing**; NeVA is by design a legislature platform and this UT has no legislature. Net effect for the project: **no shared scraper can be reused for IN-AN**, and the effort per source here is one-off.

---

## Chandigarh (IN-CH)

**Verdict:** no-legislature - Chandigarh is a Union Territory administered by the President through an Administrator (the Governor of Punjab) under Article 239 of the Constitution; it has never had a Legislative Assembly, and therefore has no MLA, no member roster, no MLA development fund and no assembly record.

### Profile

Chandigarh has **no Legislative Assembly**, so there is no Vidhan Sabha website, no member roster, no photo gallery, no starred or unstarred questions and no attendance record. None of these artefacts exist to be published. The correct primary target for this jurisdiction is therefore the Union Territory administration's own site, **https://chandigarh.gov.in/** (curl-verified HTTP 200), which publishes only executive-administration content (Administrator, Chief Secretary, departments, schemes, tenders, budget) and no legislative content whatsoever.

The constitutional and statutory position, from primary sources:

- **Article 239(1)** of the Constitution: *"Save as otherwise provided by Parliament by law, every Union territory shall be administered by the President acting, to such extent as he thinks fit, through an administrator to be appointed by him with such designation as he may specify."* **Article 239(2)** permits the President to appoint the Governor of a State as administrator of an adjoining Union territory, exercising that function independently of his Council of Ministers. (Constitution of India, Part VIII, text extracted from the Ministry of External Affairs–hosted PDF at `https://www.mea.gov.in/images/pdf1/Part8.pdf` — HTTP 200, `application/pdf`, 50,871 bytes, text-extractable, `/Font 0 /Image 0`, ~22,000 characters recovered.)
- **Article 239A(1)** empowers Parliament to create a legislature only *"for the Union territory of Puducherry"*. **Article 239AA** creates the special provisions for Delhi. **Chandigarh is named in neither article** — it is constitutionally excluded from the local-legislature provision. Article 240 (President's power to make regulations for "certain Union territories") lists Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Lakshadweep, Dadra and Nagar Haveli, Daman and Diu and Puducherry — **Chandigarh is not in that list either**.
- **The Government of Union Territories Act, 1963** (Act 20 of 1963), *"An Act to provide for Legislative Assemblies and Councils of Ministers for certain Union territories"*, does **not** extend to Chandigarh: extracting the MHA-hosted PDF (`https://www.mha.gov.in/sites/default/files/GovernmentUnionterritoriesAct1963.pdf` — HTTP 200, 161,981 bytes, 42 text pages, `/Font 66 /Image 0`, ~85,000 characters) yields **0 occurrences of "Chandigarh"** against **14 occurrences of "Pondicherry"**. The Act's commencement notes cover Goa/Daman and Diu, Pondicherry, Himachal Pradesh, Manipur, Tripura, Delhi, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Mizoram — Chandigarh is absent.
- **Administering authority:** the **Ministry of Home Affairs, Union Territories Division**, the nodal Ministry for all UT matters relating to legislation, finance and budget, services, and appointment of Administrators under the Government of India (Allocation of Business) Rules, 1961. MHA's own page states plainly: *"The two UTs with Legislatures and the UT of Andaman and Nicobar Islands have Lt. Governors as Administrators. The Governor of Punjab functions as the Administrator of the UT of Chandigarh and a senior IAS officer functions as Advisor to the Administrator."* and *"Every UT is administered by an Administrator appointed by the President under Article 239 of the Constitution of India."* (`https://www.mha.gov.in/en/division_of_mha/AGMUT_Cadre_Management`, curl-verified 200.)
- **The Chandigarh Administration's own page** confirms the origin and the absence of a legislature: *"From 1952 to 1966 (the year Haryana was carved out of Punjab) Chandigarh was the capital of Punjab. Citizens of the city were represented in the state's Legislative Assembly and a Chief Commissioner headed the local administration... the Central Government made Chandigarh a Union Territory (under Section 4 of the Punjab Re-Organisation Act, 1966, with effect from November 1, 1966) with its administration functioning directly under the Central Government... Ever since June 1984, the Governor of Punjab has been functioning as the Administrator of Union Territory of Chandigarh."* (`https://chandigarh.gov.in/administrator`, curl-verified 200.) Representation in a legislature ended in 1966; from that date the UT has had none of its own.

**Independent negative confirmation:** a full crawl of the Chandigarh Administration's sitemap (`https://chandigarh.gov.in/sitemap`, 155 links, curl-verified 200) contains **zero** links whose text *or* href matches `assembly | vidhan | legislature | MLA | sabha | council | advis`. The administration publishes no legislative page at all.

**No assembly portal exists on the national platform either:** the National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA) official portal (`https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA`) enumerates 39 legislature instances. The Union Territories present are **Delhi** (`dla.neva.gov.in`), **Jammu & Kashmir** (`Jkla.neva.gov.in`) and **Puducherry** (`puddu.neva.gov.in`). The raw string **"chandigarh" occurs 0 times** anywhere in the NeVA page (as do "ladakh", "andaman", "lakshadweep" and "dadra"). Chandigarh has no NeVA tenancy because it has no House.

**Parliamentary representation only:** Chandigarh is a single Lok Sabha constituency returning one MP (2024: Manish Tewari, Indian National Congress). The UT has no Rajya Sabha seat of its own.

**Note on the 2025 Article 240 proposal (not a legislature):** the Constitution (131st Amendment) Bill, 2025 proposed including Chandigarh within Article 240. That provision concerns the President's power to make *regulations* for certain UTs and would not create a legislature; the Bill was not enacted and the Centre stated no such Bill would be introduced. It does not change the no-legislature finding. (Reported by media; no official gazette text of an enacted amendment was found.)

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**There is no MLA development fund in Chandigarh, because there are no MLAs.** No "Vidhayak Nidhi", "Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi", "MLALAD", "MLA LADF" or equivalent scheme exists in this Union Territory, and consequently **no per-MLA portal exists or could exist**. This is not a data gap — it is the correct structural answer.

Two real but structurally *different* development-fund streams operate in Chandigarh, and neither is an MLA fund:

1. **MPLADS (Members of Parliament Local Area Development Scheme)** for the single Chandigarh MP — national portal `https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/` (curl-verified 200). This is a **per-MP** scheme (the national analogue named in the brief), not a per-MLA scheme. The portal is a JavaScript application on the **ZK framework** (assets served from `/zkau/web/...`), with a dashboard at `/digigov/dashboard.html`; its client JS uses an obfuscated, reversed-string, POST-only endpoint — the literal `"lrUtcerideRteg/ataDdraobhsaDnigoLerP/tser/"` decodes to `/rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getRedirectUrl`, which for a bare POST returns `{"redirectUrl":""}`. The string "Chandigarh" appears **0 times** in the MPLADS homepage, dashboard HTML and served JS, so per-MP Chandigarh figures are not retrievable by plain curl.

2. **Ward Development Fund** of the **Municipal Corporation Chandigarh** — a ward-level fund for elected *councillors*, reported in the press at ₹25 lakh per ward. This is **not** an MLA fund and the MC is a municipal body, not a legislature. **No official per-ward or per-councillor fund data portal was found:** searching the MC's own homepage (`https://mcchandigarh.gov.in/`, curl-verified 200) for "ward development fund", "Ward Development" and "development fund" returned **zero matches**, and the MC Schemes page (`https://mcchandigarh.gov.in/Page.aspx?id=NA8MTsWaE2Z8pSLiHpO3Tg==`) covers animal-birth-control and sterilisation schemes only. "Ward Development Fund" appears in MC tender listings and on third-party tender aggregators, which are UNOFFICIAL.

**Official scheme-rules page for an MLA fund: not found — because no such scheme exists in this jurisdiction.**

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Not applicable: Chandigarh has no Vidhan Sabha.** There is no attendance record, no starred question, no unstarred question and no private member's Bill for any Chandigarh representative, because the UT has no House in which such a record could be created. **Nothing is published because nothing exists** — this is not a case of an assembly withholding data.

Chandigarh is represented **in Parliament only**, by one Lok Sabha MP. Any parliamentary attendance, question or Bill record for that MP belongs to the Lok Sabha/Sansad record (https://sansad.in/), not to a Chandigarh assembly record, and is out of scope for this jurisdiction's own sources.

| Article 240 / Constitution 131st Amendment Bill proposal (news report) | https://www.ndtv.com/india-news/centre-bill-to-allow-president-to-frame-norms-for-chandigarh-sparks-row-punjab-aap-congress-9684514 | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | no-legislature | news article | search-result-only |
| Chandigarh ward development fund ₹25 lakh per ward (news report) | https://www.hindustantimes.com/cities/chandigarh-news/chandigarh-each-ward-to-get-25l-to-resume-devp-works-amid-cash-crunch-101747765384317.html | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news article | search-result-only |
| Chandigarh Administrator's Advisory Council (news report; not a legislature) | https://indianexpress.com/article/cities/chandigarh/administrators-advisory-council-only-7-meetings-in-nine-years-mp-kirron-kher-to-take-up-issue-2880513/lite/ | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | no-legislature | news article | search-result-only |
| "Chandigarh Vidhan Sabha" - domain does not resolve | https://chandigarhvidhansabha.gov.in/ | — | no-legislature | no host | unverified (DNS failure) |
| "Chandigarh Assembly" - domain does not resolve | https://chandigarhassembly.gov.in/ | — | no-legislature | no host | unverified (DNS failure) |

### Format note

A fetcher needs a **mixed** toolchain, and the hardest part is not the assembly (there is none) but the election results. The **CEO Chandigarh statistics page is the single most useful target**: one plain HTML `<table>` giving every Chandigarh MP from 1967 to 2024 with year, name, party, polling booths, votes polled, total and percentage — reachable by `curl` with a browser User-Agent, no JS, no cookies. For official Form-20 detail the 2004, 2009 and 2014 PDFs carry a real text layer and parse with ordinary PDF text extraction, but the **2024 Form-20 is a 17.2 MB pure scan** (`/Font 0`, `/Image 51`) requiring **OCR** — the main practical blocker. The 2019 Form-20 link is dead (HTTP 404). The ECI main site (`eci.gov.in`) is a **JavaScript React SPA** (`You need to enable JavaScript to run this app`, `/static/js/main.e468b53e.js`) and needs a headless browser; `results.eci.gov.in` is plain HTML but its folders are per-election and the 2024 Lok Sabha paths now return 404, so it cannot be relied on as a stable deep link. The **MPLADS portal needs a real browser**: it is a ZK-framework application whose data endpoints are POST-only and deliberately obfuscated (reversed string literals), and a bare POST returns an empty JSON payload. `chandigarh.gov.in` is server-rendered HTML and curls cleanly; `mcchandigarh.gov.in` is ASP.NET with `__VIEWSTATE` postback semantics; `ceochandigarh.gov.in` sets a `ci_session` cookie. No captcha was encountered on any source, and no geo-IP block was observed from this network.

### Gap note

Everything a "MLA profile", "MLA fund" and "MLA assembly record" section would normally contain is **absent and unobtainable, by design, not by omission**. There is no Chandigarh MLA roster, no MLA photograph, no MLA term or assembly number, no per-MLA development-fund allocation, no works-recommended/sanctioned/completed/expenditure figures, and no attendance, starred-question, unstarred-question or private-member's-Bill record — because the Union Territory of Chandigarh has no Legislative Assembly and therefore no MLAs. **The honest, correct deliverable for this jurisdiction is the negative finding plus its constitutional proof**, which has been established above from the Constitution itself, the Government of Union Territories Act 1963, the Ministry of Home Affairs and the Chandigarh Administration's own pages. Two genuine but non-equivalent gaps remain *within* the sources that do exist: (i) **no official per-ward/per-councillor Ward Development Fund dataset** was found on `mcchandigarh.gov.in` — the ₹25-lakh-per-ward figure is press-sourced only; and (ii) the **2024 Form-20 result is a scanned image PDF**, so a machine-readable official result table for the most recent Lok Sabha election requires OCR rather than parsing. A site that shows "MLA of Chandigarh" is showing a person who does not exist.

### Platform

**Not a shared assembly platform — there is no assembly platform at all.** Chandigarh has no NeVA tenancy: the National e-Vidhan Application's official list of 39 legislature instances (`https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA`, curl-200) contains Delhi (`dla`), Jammu & Kashmir (`Jkla`) and Puducherry (`puddu`) among Union Territories, but the string "chandigarh" appears **0 times** in the entire page, so the NIC-hosted NeVA template cannot serve this jurisdiction. The Union Territory administration's own site, `chandigarh.gov.in`, is a **bespoke** Drupal-style build (Drupal-flavoured `/node/5532` paths, `/sites/default/files/`, `/cadmin//uploads/*.pdf` document paths, `theme/css/site.css`, megamenu navigation) hosted in the NIC ecosystem rather than any state-template family. The CEO site `ceochandigarh.gov.in` is likewise **not** markup-identical to sibling CEO sites — its asset set (`/assets/css/colors/color-11.css`, `animate.css`, `chatbot.css`, `/cadmin/` paths, `ci_session` cookie) differs from CEO Delhi (`css/darkblue.css`, bilingual Hindi footers) and CEO Puducherry (`vendors/`, `icomoon`) — so **one scraper would serve one site here, not many**; only the shared *content* conventions (voters.eci.gov.in deep links, ECI link, SIR notices) recur across CEO sites, not the markup.

---

## Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu (IN-DH)

**Verdict:** no-legislature - The Union territory of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu has no Legislative Assembly; Article 239A of the Constitution now empowers Parliament to create a legislature only "for the Union territory of Puducherry", while Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu are governed under Article 240 by Presidential Regulations through an Administrator. There is therefore no MLA, no MLA roster, no MLA local area development fund, and no assembly record to source.

### Profile

**There is no Legislative Assembly and therefore no official assembly website, no member roster, and no MLA profile data of any kind.** The constitutional position is unambiguous and comes from the Government of India's own text of the Constitution (Part VIII, "The Union territories"), fetched from the Ministry of External Affairs at `https://www.mea.gov.in/images/pdf1/Part8.pdf` (curl-200, `application/pdf`, text-extractable):

- **Article 239A(1)** — "Parliament may by law create [for the Union territory of Puducherry] (a) ... and partly elected, to function as a Legislature for the ... (b) a Council of Ministers". The bracketed words "for the Union territory of Puducherry" carry footnote 4 in the official text: *"Subs. by the Goa, Daman and Diu Reorganisation Act, 1987 (18 of 1987), s. 63"*. Until 1987 Article 239A applied to Goa, Daman and Diu as well; the 1987 reorganisation removed Daman and Diu from it. **Dadra and Nagar Haveli was never covered by Article 239A at all.** So no law of Parliament under Article 239A can create a legislature for this UT.
- **Article 240(1)** — "Power of President to make regulations for certain Union territories" lists the UTs for which the President may make regulations for peace, progress and good government: "(a) the Andaman and Nicobar Islands; (b) Lakshadweep; (c) **Dadra and Nagar Haveli**; (d) **Daman and Diu**; (e) Puducherry". This is the governing provision for IN-DH.
- **Article 239(1)** — the UT is administered by the President acting through an **Administrator** appointed by him.

**Administering authority:** the Ministry of Home Affairs (Government of India), through the **U.T. Administration of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu**, headed by the Administrator. The Ministry of Home Affairs' own published "List of Regulations" (`https://www.mha.gov.in/sites/default/files/2026-03/List_Regulation_17032026.pdf`, curl-200, `application/pdf`, 3 pages, 29 `/Font` entries so it is a text PDF, not a scan) confirms that this UT is legislated for by **Presidential Regulation under article 240 of the Constitution** — e.g. item 11: *"Notification regarding the Union territory of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu (Adaptation of Central Law, State Laws and Presidential Regulations) in Gazette of India (Extraordinary) vide No. 230 dated 18.01.2022"*, and item 33: *"Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu (Amendment) ... Notification No. 48 dated 19.11.2025"*. The list contains many Daman and Diu / Dadra and Nagar Haveli Regulations and no assembly legislation.

**Official UT administration URL (given in place of an assembly site):** `https://ddd.gov.in/` — curl-200, 179,782 bytes, `text/html`. Site title "UT of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu | India". Footer credit verbatim: *"Content Owned by Copyrights 2023 © U.T. Administration of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu. Government of India. Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre, Ministry of Electronics & Information Technology, Government of India"*.

Four independent negative checks on that official site confirm the absence of a legislature:

1. **Site navigation contains no Legislative Assembly entry.** The full menu is Home / About UT / Administrative Setup (Hon'ble Administrator, Advisor, IAS, IPS, IFS, DANICS, DANIPS officers, Link Officer List, Directory) / Secretariat (Dadra & Nagar Haveli, Daman, Diu) / Helpline / Documents / Departments (Information Technology, Legal Metrology, Electricity, **Election**, Labour, Tourism, Municipal Corporation, Police, VAT, Collectorate, Revenue) / Notices / Schemes / RTI / Citizens Corner. There is a "UT Election Commission" page — that is the **State Election Commission for panchayats and municipalities**, not a legislature. There is no Speaker, no Assembly Secretariat, no MLA.
2. **The site's own search returns no results for legislature vocabulary.** `https://ddd.gov.in/?s=legislature` → `no-results` markup; `?s=vidhayak` → `no-results`; `?s=vidhan%20sabha` → `no-results`. The query `?s=assembly` returns 6 results and **every one of them is about the Gujarat Legislative Assembly** (e.g. *"Dry Day Notification In View of General Election To Legislative Assembly of Gujarat, 2022"*) — i.e. the only legislature the UT administration ever mentions is its neighbour's.
3. **The MHA body that runs UT elections covers only panchayats and municipalities here.** `https://secforuts.mha.gov.in/ut-of-dadra-nagar-haveli/` (curl-200, 136,863 bytes) is titled "UT of Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu | Election Commission for UTs" and its entire content is Panchayat Election (delimitation, wards and polling stations, results) and Municipal Election (polling stations, determination of seats, wards, results). Verbatim: *"The Director of Panchayat and Municipal Elections, Dadra & Nagar Haveli supervises the preparation of electoral rolls and conduct the elections to the Panchayati Raj Institutions and Dadra & Nagar Haveli Municipal Council ... under the guidance, superintendence, direction and control of the Election Commission for UTs"*. There is no assembly section because there is no assembly.
4. **NeVA — the national legislature platform — does not list this UT.** `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` (curl-200, 192,345 bytes) contains **0 occurrences of "Dadra", "Daman" or "Diu"**, while "Puducherry" appears 10 times and "Nagaland" 5 times. A UT with no legislature has nothing to onboard to NeVA.

**Historical note for completeness:** Daman and Diu was from 1961 to 1987 part of the Union territory of "Goa, Daman and Diu", which *did* have a legislature between 1963 and 1987; the Goa, Daman and Diu Reorganisation Act, 1987 (18 of 1987) made Goa a state and left Daman and Diu as a separate UT without a legislature. Dadra and Nagar Haveli became a UT by the Constitution (Tenth Amendment) Act, 1961 and was never given a legislature. The two UTs were merged into the present single UT by the **Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu (Merger of Union Territories) Act, 2019**, which did not create a legislature either — consistent with Article 239A covering only Puducherry.

**Verification level:** the "no legislature" finding rests on curl-200 primary sources from three separate Government of India domains (mea.gov.in, mha.gov.in, ddd.gov.in) plus neva.gov.in, and is corroborated by three independent absences. It is not inferred, not from a news article, and not from Wikipedia.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**There is no MLA local area development fund in Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, because there are no MLAs.** No MLA fund scheme — under the name *Vidhayak Nidhi*, *Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi*, *MLALAD*, *MLA LADF* or any other name — exists or can exist for this jurisdiction, and **no official government resolution, guideline page, or scheme portal for a UT MLA fund was found on any `.gov.in` / `.nic.in` domain** (`mlalad_url` = not found). The UT administration's own site search returns `no-results` for "vidhayak", and its Departments list contains no such scheme. This is a structural absence, not a publication gap: an MLA fund is a legislature-linked scheme and this UT has no legislature.

**The national analogue that does cover this jurisdiction's elected representatives is MPLADS**, the Members of Parliament Local Area Development Scheme, administered by the Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation:

- Portal: `https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/` (curl-200, 62,712 bytes) — this is the national portal and covers **MPs, not MLAs**.
- Pre-login dashboard: `https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/digigov/dashboard.html` (curl-200, 23,611 bytes). Its own published explanation states: *"The MPLADS–eSAKSHI web portal (www.mplads.mospi.gov.in) was introduced with effect from 1 April 2023 to manage the revised fund-flow procedure under the MPLADS Scheme ... The information regarding number of works recommended and sanctioned and their respective amounts in respect of the 17th Lok Sabha is available on the eSAKSHI portal for the year 2023-24 and not available for the first four years viz. 2019-20, 2020-21, 2021-22 and 2022-23."*
- The published dashboard tiles are exactly the five metrics the project wants for MLAs, but per MP: Allocated Limits, Works Recommended, Works Sanctioned, Works Completed, Expenditure (confirmed by the asset names `/zkImages/AllocatedLimits.png`, `/zkImages/WorksRecommended.png`, `/zkImages/WorksSanctioned.png`, `/zkImages/WorksCompleted.png`, `/zkImages/Expenditure.png` on the landing page), plus tables keyed on `HOUSE_NAME`, `STATE`, `CONSTITUENCY_NAME`, `MP_NAME`, `FRESH_LIMIT`, `AVAILABLE_LIMIT_( ₹ )`, `RECOMMENDED_AMOUNT`, `ALLOCATED_AMOUNT_( ₹ )`, `EXPENDITURE_AMOUNT_( ₹ )` (column names read out of `/libs/simplegrid/poptable.js`).
- **It is queryable per MP** and it is backed by a real JSON REST API, which I located by de-obfuscating the portal's own JavaScript (the paths are stored reversed, e.g. `"lrUtcerideRteg/ataDdraobhsaDnigoLerP/tser/"`). Decoded endpoints, all under `https://mplads.mospi.gov.in`:
  - `POST /rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getTilesReportData`
  - `POST /rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getTenureData`
  - `POST /rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getRedirectUrl`
  - `/rest/PreLoginCitizenWorkRcmdRest/getAttachmentById`
  I verified two of them live: `POST /rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getTenureData` with body `{"type":"LOKSABHA"}` returned HTTP 200, `text/plain;charset=ISO-8859-1`, body `[]`; `POST /rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getTilesReportData` with `{"type":"LOKSABHA","tenure":"17"}` returned HTTP 200 with body `{}`. So the API is live and JSON-returning, but the parameter schema needed to reach a specific MP's rows (state / tenure / house combinations) was **not** fully derived — treat the exact request contract as unverified.
- Jurisdiction coverage: IN-DH sends **two** MPs to the Lok Sabha (the Dadra and Nagar Haveli seat and the Daman and Diu seat), so MPLADS carries two per-MP records for this territory.

**If the site intends to show a "development fund" number for IN-DH at all, the only official per-representative figure available is MPLADS per MP — and it must be labelled as an MP fund, not an MLA fund.** No per-MLA figure exists and none can be sourced.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**There is no Vidhan Sabha, so there is no assembly record: no attendance, no starred questions, no unstarred questions, and no private members' bills — for anyone.** All five fields are `no` by structural absence rather than by publication failure. There is no Assembly Secretariat, no Speaker, no Question Hour, no Zero Hour and no Committee on Private Members' Bills in this Union territory.

Corroborating negative check on the Election Commission of India's own archive: `https://old.eci.gov.in/assembly-election` ("Assembly Election - Election Commission of India", curl-200, 95,432 bytes) contains **0 occurrences of "Dadra", "Daman" or "Diu"** — the ECI's historic "Past Assembly Elections" listing has no entry for this territory because no assembly election has ever been held there. Likewise ECI's per-state full statistical reports exist only for states with assemblies (`https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/all_files/full-statistical-reports/mizoram/2023/Detailed_Results.pdf` → curl-200, 9-page PDF; the same path pattern for Dadra/Daman → 404).

**Representation is parliamentary only.** IN-DH is represented in the Lok Sabha by two members and has no representation in the Rajya Sabha (it is not a state). Its governance is by the Administrator acting for the President under Articles 239 and 240, with laws made for it by Parliament and by Presidential Regulation. The only elected bodies below that are the **panchayats and the municipal councils**, whose elections are run by the Director of Panchayat and Municipal Elections under the Election Commission for UTs (`https://secforuts.mha.gov.in/ut-of-dadra-nagar-haveli/`) — these bodies are not a legislature and publish no attendance, questions or bills.

### Format note

A fetcher for this jurisdiction needs **no scraper for member, fund or assembly-record data, because none of it exists** — the correct output for IN-DH is a static "no legislature" page with the Article 239A / Article 240 citation. For the supporting evidence: `ddd.gov.in` is plain server-rendered **curl-html** (WordPress; also exposes a WordPress REST API at `https://ddd.gov.in/wp-json/`), as is `secforuts.mha.gov.in` and `neva.gov.in`. The two constitutional sources (MEA Part VIII, MHA List of Regulations) are **PDF text extraction** — both carry real `/Font` objects and no OCR is required (though the Constitution PDF's subset fonts and footnote layout mean extraction is untidy and needs regex repair). The blocker is the **Election Commission side and the CEO sites**: `https://www.eci.gov.in/` is a **JS-rendered React SPA** (`<noscript>You need to enable JavaScript to run this app.</noscript>`, `<div id="root">`, `main.e468b53e.js` at 2.93 MB) with a JSON backend on `/api/...` (`REACT_APP_API_HOST:"https://www.eci.gov.in/"`, endpoints including `/api/get-ac-election-state`, `/api/general-election-2019`, `/api/assebly-election-inpage`) that **did not respond to curl** — every `/api/` GET returned the 1,279-byte SPA shell rather than JSON, both with and without `X-Requested-With`/`Accept: application/json`/`Referer` headers, so a headless browser is required there. `https://results.eci.gov.in/` is the exception — it is static HTML and curl-fetchable, currently auto-redirecting to a bye-election results index. **The hardest blocker is that both of this UT's Chief Electoral Officer sites are unreachable from a normal client**: `ceodaman.nic.in` (164.100.178.160, Microsoft-IIS/8.0) answers HTTP with a 301 to HTTPS but then fails the TLS handshake outright — `openssl s_client` and curl both report `rsa routines:ossl_rsa_verify:wrong signature length` / `tls_process_key_exchange:bad signature`, ending in `Cipher is (NONE)`, under LibreSSL 3.3.6, OpenSSL 1.1.1n, TLS 1.2 with default, ECDHE-RSA and DHE-RSA cipher sets, and TLS 1.3 alike; `--cacert`, `-k`, `--ipv4` and `--tlsv1.2` all fail. `ceodnh.nic.in` (164.100.186.155) does not complete a connection at all. The MPLADS API is the one genuinely machine-readable fund source — `POST` JSON, no captcha on the pre-login endpoints tested — but it serves MPs, not MLAs, and its exact request contract for a named MP was not fully derived.

### Gap note

**Everything the project wants for IN-DH is missing, and permanently so — this is a structural gap, not a sourcing failure.** There is **no MLA** (so no name, party, constituency or photo), **no Legislative Assembly** (so no assembly website, no roster, no attendance, no starred or unstarred questions, no private members' bills), and **no MLA development fund** (so no allocation limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure at MLA level — no *Vidhayak Nidhi* scheme exists here at all, and no government resolution or portal for one was found). No amount of searching will close this: Article 239A of the Constitution permits a legislature only for Puducherry (Daman and Diu was removed from its scope by the Goa, Daman and Diu Reorganisation Act, 1987, and Dadra and Nagar Haveli was never in it), and Article 240 places both UTs under governance by Presidential Regulation through an Administrator. What *can* be built for IN-DH is a **parliamentary** profile: two Lok Sabha MPs (Dadra and Nagar Haveli; Daman and Diu), each with per-MP figures available from the national MPLADS portal at `https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/` — but those are **MP fund** numbers and must never be presented as an MLA fund. Two practical gaps remain even there: the MPLADS API's request contract for a specific MP is not fully reverse-engineered, and both CEO sites (`ceodaman.nic.in`, `ceodnh.nic.in`) that would carry this UT's electoral-roll and election-result material are unreachable due to a broken TLS configuration, so electoral data for IN-DH has to come from the ECI's own JS-rendered portal instead.

### Platform

**No legislative platform exists for this UT, and that absence is itself the platform finding: IN-DH is not a NeVA participant.** `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` (curl-200) contains zero occurrences of "Dadra", "Daman" or "Diu" while listing Puducherry and Nagaland, so there is no e-Vidhan instance to scrape and no shared assembly template to reuse — consistent with Article 239A covering only Puducherry. What *is* shared is the **administration** layer: `https://ddd.gov.in/` is a NIC-hosted WordPress site using a common NIC theme (asset path `https://ddd.gov.in/wp-content/themes/sdo-theme/css/print.css`, generator meta `Powered by WPBakery Page Builder`, footer *"Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre, Ministry of Electronics & Information Technology"*), and the MHA's UT election body at `secforuts.mha.gov.in` renders the **same NIC WordPress chrome** — identical accessibility toolbar, identical menu markup and the identical NIC footer — so one extraction pattern covers both. Separately, the national **MPLADS** portal at `mplads.mospi.gov.in` is a single ZK/eSAKSHI application with a shared JSON API (`/rest/PreLoginDashboardData/...`) serving every state and UT, so one MPLADS integration would serve this UT's two MPs alongside the rest of the country.

---

## Delhi (IN-DL)

**Verdict:** partial - Delhi has a real 70-seat legislature (8th Assembly, from Feb 2025) with two live official sites that publish a full member roster with photographs, and official election results and MLALAD fund documents exist; but there is no per-MLA fund dashboard (only scattered per-constituency PDFs and one scanned work-level report) and per-MLA attendance is published nowhere officially.

### Profile

Delhi is a union territory **with** a legislature: the unicameral **Legislative Assembly of the National Capital Territory of Delhi**, 70 seats, constituted under Article 239AA of the Constitution and the Government of National Capital Territory of Delhi Act, 1991. The current house is the **8th Assembly** (2025 onwards). There is no upper house. Two separate official websites carry member data.

**(a) `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/`** - the Assembly's own site (curl-200, 44,233 bytes). It is a **Drupal** site (`theme=dit_dlas`, `/sites/default/files/` asset paths) whose footer on every page reads "Designed, Developed and Maintained by **Amity Software Limited**". It publishes the member roster as a **born-digital, CID-encoded text PDF** at `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/sites/default/files/2026-08/list_of_member.pdf` (curl-200, 416,219 bytes; 25 fonts, **0 images**, ToUnicode CMaps - a standard PDF text extractor reads it, no OCR). Decoded, it is a 4-page table with one row per MLA across all 70 constituencies giving **AC number, constituency name, member name, postal address, mobile numbers, email ID, party, and category** - verified rows include `1 NARELA Raj Karan Khatri ... BJP`, `2 BURARI Sanjeev Jha ... AAP`, `3 TIMARPUR Surya Prakash Khatri ... BJP`. It carries **NO photograph and no term/assembly number column**. A second variant, `.../2025-03/list_of_membersnn.pdf` (curl-200, 218,128 bytes), is the copy linked from `https://delhi.gov.in/` under its "MLAs" menu item. The same site also has `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/dlas/members-inception-delhi-assembly`, an **HTML table** of every member since 1993 with the Assembly numbers (I-VIII) they sat in - useful for term reconstruction, but it too has **no photo**.

**(b) `https://dla.neva.gov.in/`** - the Delhi house on the national NeVA platform (curl-200, 69,971 bytes). This is the **only official source that publishes MLA PHOTOGRAPHS**. `https://dla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList` (curl-200, 651,479 bytes) returns card markup for exactly **70 unique members** (`/Member/Details/<id>?AssemblyID=8`) with **70 member photographs**, each an image at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/DL/FileStructures//Member/<file>.jpg`. Each card carries name, party, constituency and region ("बादली -उत्तरी दिल्ली"), **date of birth**, address, mobile and email; the per-member page `https://dla.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/5?AssemblyID=8` (curl-200) adds permanent/local address, a term label "आठवीं विधान सभा" (Eighth Assembly) and a portrait image. Party split as rendered: **48 BJP + 22 AAP = 70**, matching the house dashboard counts. The site is queryable per member (each member has a detail route) and the roster is additionally exportable as a spreadsheet via `https://dla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember` (curl-200, `application/excel`, 66,163 bytes) and a PDF via `.../PDFforHonbleMember`.

The **old domain `delhiassembly.nic.in` is dead**: HTTPS/HTTP both fail DNS resolution (`curl` exit 6, no A record) while `delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in` resolves to 182.74.205.45. Several search-result URLs still point at `delhiassembly.nic.in` - they are stale and must not be used.

Verification level: both assembly sites and the roster PDFs are **curl-200**; the NeVA photo host is a URL observed in the official page markup (**linked-from-official-page**).

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

The fund in Delhi is **NOT called "Vidhayak Nidhi"** (that is Uttar Pradesh's name). Delhi's scheme is the **MLALAD Scheme - MLA Local Area Development Scheme** (also written MLALADS in file numbers). It is administered by the **Department of Urban Development (UD), Government of NCT of Delhi**, 10th Level, Delhi Secretariat, I.P. Estate, New Delhi-110002, with a designated **Deputy Secretary (MLALAD)** as the signing officer. Its file-number format is `F.No.18A(15)/UD/MLALADS/<year>/...`.

The scheme landing page is **`https://udd.delhi.gov.in/ud/mlalad-scheme`** (curl-200), with sub-pages `/ud/mlalad-fund-guideline` (the scheme guidelines/GR, curl-200), `/ud/mla-fund-orders` (scheme orders, curl-200), `/ud/fund-utilization-report` (curl-200), `/ud/mla-fund-duda` (fund routed to DUDA, curl-200), `/mlalad-schemes` (sanction orders, curl-200) and `/mlalad-schemes-administrative-approval` (curl-200). A separate **CMLAD** (Chief Minister Local Area Development) scheme exists at `/ud/cmlad`.

What is actually published:

- **Allocation limit - uniform, not per-MLA-varying.** `https://udd.delhi.gov.in/sites/default/files/UD/mlalad/yearly_allocation_budget_2026-27_0.pdf` (curl-200, 376,127 bytes, **born-digital text PDF**) is an order reading: "The yearly allocation of fund under MLALAD Scheme has been kept to **Rs. 05.00 Crore per Assembly Constituency** for the financial year 2026-27." It is addressed to "All the Hon'ble MLA (**AC-01 TO 70**) of 08th Delhi Legislative Assembly" and signed by the Deputy Secretary (MLALAD). Allocation is therefore a single uniform figure covering all 70 MLAs; there is no province of per-MLA allocation variation to scrape for 2026-27. (Press reporting says the limit was cut from Rs 15 crore to Rs 5 crore in 2025 - UNOFFICIAL corroboration only.)
- **Scheme guidelines / GR:** `https://udd.delhi.gov.in/sites/default/files/UD/mlalad/mlalad_guidelines.pdf` (curl-200, 155,449 bytes), plus an amending order `mlalad_guidelines_partial_modification_order_16.06.2023.pdf` on the orders page.
- **Per-constituency sanction / release-of-funds orders - the closest thing to per-MLA data, but scattered PDFs with partial coverage.** `/mlalad-schemes` lists **52** unique PDFs and `/mlalad-schemes-administrative-approval` lists **51**, named by assembly constituency: `ac_01_12828-840_17.02.2022.pdf`, `ac_15_18147-18158_05-01-2026.pdf`, `ac_39_391-401_08-05-2026.pdf`, `ac_67_4388-4398_16-09-2025.pdf`, `ac_50_1703-1715_04.07.2023.pdf`. The `ac_<NN>` prefix is the AC number, so these are genuinely **per-MLA (per-AC)** documents - but the visible AC numbers are only **01, 03, 05, 10, 15, 25, 26, 30, 34, 37, 38, 41, 42, 44, 46, 47, 49, 50, 51, 56, 57, 59, 60, 61, 62, 64, 66** - roughly 27 of 70 constituencies. There is no per-MLA index, no consolidated table, and no query interface; each order is an individual dated PDF.
- **Work-level execution report - one scanned document.** `https://udd.delhi.gov.in/sites/default/files/mlalad/fundureport251119_1.pdf` (curl-200, 3,615,118 bytes, dated 19-11-2025) is titled "Year-wise information of works executed/sanctioned under MLALAD Scheme since 2013". It is the richest fund dataset found: a work-level table with columns **Sr No, AC, MLA Name, Scheme No, Area/Scope of work, Funds received, PO No, Issue date, Work Estimate, Status**. Verified rows include `Vijender Garg | AC 39 | Sl/01302/0008 | Installation of street lights at Narlana Village | .62 Lacs | 20.07.2016 | 03.08.2016 | Work Completed` and `Akhilesh Tripathi | AC 18 | ... | Work Completed`. Crucially it is a **scanned document with an OCR text layer** (6 font objects vs **149 image objects**), so its text extracts but is unreliable - OCR corruption is visible in the data itself ("Vlje1.1der Garg", "lnderpuri", "Sl./01304/0"). This file was located via web index on the official domain; it is **not linked** from the `/ud/fund-utilization-report` page.
- **The intended dashboard is EMPTY.** `https://udd.delhi.gov.in/ud/fund-utilization-report` ("Fund Utilization Reports received from various Executive Agencies") renders a year filter covering 2026-27, 2025-26, 2024-25 ... Before 2012-13, but the result region reads **"No Data Available."** on every setting tested, including with year query parameters (`?year=2025-26`, `?year=2024-25`, `?year=All`). So the one page designed to expose per-MLA fund utilisation publishes nothing.

**Bottom line:** there is **NO national-MPLADS-style per-MLA MLALAD portal** for Delhi. There is an official scheme-rules page, an official uniform yearly allocation order, and official per-AC sanction orders for roughly a quarter of constituencies as isolated PDFs, plus one scanned work-level report. Per-MLA allocated / recommended / sanctioned / completed / expenditure figures are **not available as a queryable per-MLA dataset**.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

Of the four record types requested, the official position is:

- **Unstarred questions - published, but as SCANNED IMAGES.** `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/dlas/assembly-question` (curl-200) is an **HTML table** with columns Session No / Date / "Questions Answers Starred Un-Starred" and a "View" link per sitting, linking to **364 question PDFs** on the pattern `.../2025-03/s<N>.pdf` (starred, ~84 files) and `.../2025-03/us<N>.pdf` (unstarred, ~280 files). Sampled `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/sites/default/files/2025-03/us1.pdf` (curl-200, 2,134,735 bytes) analyses as **0 font objects, 28 image objects, 0 text-show operators** - a **pure scanned-image PDF requiring OCR**. Sampled `.../2025-03/s1.pdf` is the same (0 fonts, 8 JPEGs, 0 text operators).
- **Starred questions - published, same SCANNED-IMAGE format.** Same table and same `s<N>.pdf` family as above. `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/dlas/questions` is a definitional/FAQ page only.
- **Starred and unstarred questions on the NeVA platform - published as structured, searchable, per-member data.** `https://dla.neva.gov.in/Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem=0&Type=0` (curl-200, 63,992 bytes) returns a **search form with an Advanced Search** offering an Assembly selector ("आठवीं विधान सभा" - Eighth Assembly), Session selector (Sixth/Fifth/Fourth/Third sessions), a **sitting-date selector** (11/10/7 August 2026), and a **member selector listing members with their IDs** ("अहीर दीपक चौधरी (5)", "आले मोहम्मद इकबाल (21)", "अभय कुमार वर्मा (58)", ...). The same route takes `LstItem=1` for unstarred, `LstItem=3` for short-notice questions, `LstItem=4` for inter-session questions, and `LstItem=2` with `Type=4/5/24/18/19` to reach listed starred, listed unstarred, short-notice, and **answer papers (तारांकित/अतारांकित उत्तर)**. The house dashboard counts **323 questions**. So on NeVA the questions record **is** filterable per member - this is the best machine-readable assembly-record source for Delhi.
- **Private members' bills - NOT published.** Government bills **are** published: `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/dlas-govt-bills` (curl-200) is a Drupal view listing **38 bill/act PDFs** under `/sites/default/files/dlas/govt-bills/`; NeVA's `https://dla.neva.gov.in/Bill?IsStateDashBoard=True` (curl-200) exposes a bill search filterable by Assembly, Session and **bill category** (Money Bill / Financial Bill / Ordinary Bill / Constitution Amendment Bill / Ordinance Replacing Bill) and shows **7 government bills**. The NeVA page `https://delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in/dlas/bills` ("Current Session / Bills") returns **"No content available."** No **private members' bills** list was found on either official platform; the per-member "विधेयक" (Bills) tab on the NeVA member page renders "कोई रिकॉर्ड नहीं मिला" (no record found) for the member tested. There is no sponsor-based or private-member bill index.
- **Attendance - NOT PUBLISHED AT ALL.** This is the clearest gap. No attendance page exists in the assembly site's sitemap (76 paths enumerated), and a direct grep for "attendance"/"उपस्थिति" across `dlas/questions`, `dlas/current-session`, `/assembly-proceedings` and `dlas/session-reviews` returns **0 matches on every page**. The NeVA member detail page (`/Member/Details/5?AssemblyID=8`) offers tabs for General, Questions, Latest Notices, **Assurances**, Committee, Debates and Bills - but **no attendance tab**, and the page text contains neither "attendance" nor "उपस्थिति". No per-member or house-level attendance figure is published by any Delhi government source. Per-MLA attendance for Delhi exists only in **UNOFFICIAL** compilations by ADR (Association for Democratic Reforms, an NGO) and PRS India, which derive it from assembly records rather than publishing a government figure.

### Format note

A fetcher for Delhi needs **three different pipelines**, and no single one covers the jurisdiction.

1. **curl + HTML/JSON (best coverage).** The NeVA house at `dla.neva.gov.in` is the most tractable: a first `curl -c cookies` GET to `/` sets an ASP.NET session cookie, after which `GET /ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList` returns the whole 70-member roster with photo URLs as a plain HTML fragment, `GET /ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember` returns an Excel workbook, and `GET /Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem=<n>&Type=<n>` and `GET /Bill?IsStateDashBoard=True` back the searchable questions and bills modules. Two gotchas: several endpoints that the page calls with **POST return HTTP 200 with a zero-byte body** and must be called with **GET** instead (`GetMembersStateDashbardData`), and endpoints called without their required `LstItem`/`Type` parameters return **HTTP 500** with a generic error page (`Questions/FetchDataList`, `Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`). Some NeVA routes on the house subdomain silently fall back to the national portal (identical 192,345-byte response), so a scraper must assert the house name in the response rather than trusting the status code. The assembly site `delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in` and the department site `udd.delhi.gov.in` are both plain Drupal and curl cleanly.

2. **PDF text extraction.** The 8th-Assembly roster PDF, the MLALAD yearly allocation order and the CEO Delhi election-result gazette are all **born-digital** - no OCR - but the roster uses **CID-keyed fonts with ToUnicode CMaps**, so a naive `strings`/regex pass yields glyph garbage; a real PDF library (pdfplumber, pdfminer.six, PyMuPDF, `pdftotext`) is required.

3. **OCR (the hard blocker).** Delhi's **entire assembly question record is scanned images**. Every sampled starred and unstarred question PDF contains zero font objects and zero text operators - only JPEG page scans. The MLALAD work-level execution report is a **hybrid**: a scan with an OCR text layer that extracts but is demonstrably corrupt ("Vlje1.1der Garg" for "Vijender Garg", "lnderpuri" for "Inderpuri"), so its MLA-name join keys cannot be trusted without re-OCR or manual repair.

No captchas, no geo-IP blocks and no POST-only requirements were encountered. `eci.gov.in` itself is a **JS-rendered SPA** (1,279-byte shell) and needs a headless browser; `results.eci.gov.in` is static HTML but **rotates to the current election** and its Delhi-February-2025 path now 404s.

### Gap note

The following cannot be sourced officially and must be treated as gaps rather than as absent-by-oversight.

- **Per-MLA attendance does not exist in any government publication.** Not on the assembly site (0 occurrences of "attendance"/"उपस्थिति" across its record pages, and no attendance path among its 76 sitemap entries), not on NeVA (the member detail page has Questions/Notices/Assurances/Committee/Debates/Bills tabs and no attendance tab). ADR and PRS publish Delhi MLA attendance, but both are **UNOFFICIAL - needs verification** NGO compilations; using them breaks the project's provenance promise.
- **No per-MLA MLALAD dashboard.** The one page built to expose fund utilisation (`/ud/fund-utilization-report`) renders "No Data Available" for every year from Before-2012-13 through 2026-27. There is no Delhi equivalent of the MPLADS portal. Per-MLA figures must be pieced together from per-constituency sanction PDFs that cover only about **27 of 70 constituencies** plus one OCR-degraded work-level report - and because the yearly allocation is a **uniform Rs 5 crore per constituency**, "allocated limit" carries no per-MLA information for FY 2026-27 anyway.
- **Works recommended vs works sanctioned cannot be separated.** The published per-AC documents are sanction/administrative-approval orders; no "recommended by MLA" stage is published, so the recommended/sanctioned distinction the portal spec asks for is not derivable.
- **Private members' bills are not published** by either official platform, and there is no sponsor-indexed bill list.
- **Photos exist only on NeVA**, not on the Assembly's own site, so a photo pipeline is coupled to the national platform remaining live.
- The legacy `delhiassembly.nic.in` URLs that still surface in search results and in older citation lists are **DNS-dead** and resolve to nothing.

### Platform

Delhi is a **hybrid: a bespoke state site plus the national NeVA template, running in parallel.** The Assembly's own portal `delhiassembly.delhi.gov.in` is **bespoke** - a Drupal site with the theme machine-name `dit_dlas` whose footer on every page credits "Designed, Developed and Maintained by **Amity Software Limited**"; the same vendor footer appears on `delhi.gov.in` and `udd.delhi.gov.in`, so Amity is a Delhi-GNCTD-wide web vendor (no evidence was found of a multi-state assembly product), meaning that site yields **no reuse across other states**. Separately, Delhi is live on **NeVA, the NIC-built national legislature template run by the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs**, at **`https://dla.neva.gov.in/`** - the national portal's own house directory lists Delhi as "Digital House Live on NeVA Platform" and states Delhi "became the **28th House** to implement the NeVA", with an MoU signed under the Delhi Government's 100-day agenda and the foundation stone laid by the Union Minister of Parliamentary Affairs on 14 June 2025. Concrete reuse evidence: the house subdomain pattern is uniform (`dla` = Delhi, `bla` = Bihar, `gujarat`, `hpvs` = Himachal Pradesh, `asla` = Assam, `mpla` = Madhya Pradesh); the `<title>` on each is the identical string "Welcome to **<State> Vidhan Sabha** Legislative session and proceedings"; every house serves the same `/Member/Details/<id>` route; and all file assets — member photographs, house logos, notices, assembly files — live under one shared host on the identical path shape `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/<HOUSE_CODE>/FileStructures/...` (Delhi's member photos are `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/DL/FileStructures//Member/<file>.jpg`). The footer on Delhi's NeVA member pages reads "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre". **Practical consequence: one NeVA scraper keyed on the house code covers Delhi, Bihar, Gujarat, Himachal Pradesh and the rest of the onboarded houses at once, and it is also the only place Delhi MLA photographs are published** - but the NeVA template does **not** carry the MLALAD fund data, which stays on the bespoke `udd.delhi.gov.in` Drupal site.

---

## Jammu and Kashmir (IN-JK)

**Verdict:** partial - J&K has a live legislature on the National e-Vidhan Application with a full 90-MLA roster including photos, per-MLA starred questions and per-MLA profile detail all fetchable by curl, but there is NO official per-MLA Constituency Development Fund data portal and NO attendance data published anywhere.

### Profile

Jammu and Kashmir **does have a legislature**. The official website of the Jammu and Kashmir Legislative Assembly is **https://jkla.neva.gov.in/** (curl-200; page title "Welcome to Jammu & Kashmir Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application"). It is not a standalone assembly site: it is J&K's tenancy on the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs / National Informatics Centre platform. The national NeVA dashboard at https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA lists J&K as "Onboarded" and links directly to https://Jkla.neva.gov.in.

The house is styled **"Ist J&K Legislative Assembly UT"** (AssemblyID=1) - i.e. the first Assembly of the Union Territory of J&K, constituted after the September-October 2024 election. Presiding officers shown on the homepage: Hon'ble Speaker Adv. Abdul Rahim Rather, Hon'ble Leader of Opposition Mr. Sunil Kumar Sharma, Secretary Manoj Kumar Pandit.

**What the site publishes for members (all verified by curl against a session cookie):**

- **Full roster, 90 MLAs, with photo.** `GET https://jkla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=16&MSLT=1&Assemblyid=1` returns 689,075 bytes of HTML containing exactly **90 member cards**. Each card carries the member's name, party and photo; the card body carries `Constituency Name`, `Date of Birth`, address and mobile. Parsed party tally from that call: JKNC 41, BJP 29, INDEPENDENT 7, INC 6, PDP 4, AAP 1, CPI(M) 1, J&K Peoples Conference 1 = 90.
- **Photos are present for all 90 members**, served from the shared NeVA CMS as `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/JK/FileStructures//Member/thumb/<file>.jpg` (full-size image on the member page).
- **Per-member profile page:** `https://jkla.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/<id>?AssemblyID=1` (the `AssemblyID=1` parameter is mandatory - without it the page renders an empty shell). Example: `/Member/Details/68?AssemblyID=1` = Mr. Abdul Majid Bhat (Larmi), JKNC, "Anantnag West -[43]- Anantnag".
- **Per-member biographical detail:** `GET https://jkla.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_ProfileDetail?MemberId=68&AssemblyID=1` returns a structured Personal Information block - Print Name, Father's Name, Date of Birth, Place of Birth, Email, Mobile, Alternate Mobile, Party Name, constituency, addresses.
- **Bulk downloads:** `https://jkla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember` returns a 91-row HTML `<table>` (header + 90 members), 65,295 bytes, served with `Content-Type: application/excel` - it is **HTML, not a real XLS binary**. `https://jkla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/PDFforHonbleMember` returns a genuine **PDF, 4,972,694 bytes, 4 pages**.
- The directory UI also exposes filters for Panel of Chairpersons, Council of Ministers, Whip, Birthday by month, Male/Female, Nominated, First Time Elected, Members Elected in Bye-Election, Political Party, Constituency, Resigned/Deceased/Disqualified, Caste Category (SC/ST/GEN/OBC), Ex-Members, plus a "Who's Who" and "Members Booklet" section.

**Format / queryability:** plain server-rendered HTML with `?MLT=<menu>&MSLT=<submenu>&Assemblyid=1` query parameters; DataTables sorting/paging is client-side over already-rendered rows, so no headless browser is needed. A session cookie obtained from `GET /` plus a `Referer` header is required - without them the bare paths `302`-redirect to `/Home/NeVA`. No JSON member API was found.

**Verification level:** curl-200 (fetched, parsed, counts reproduced).

The historical standalone assembly site **www.jklegislativeassembly.nic.in** - launched by Speaker Mubarak Gul in August 2013 and reported in the press - is **dead** (no response from curl). Anything that still cites it is stale.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**The fund in Jammu and Kashmir is NOT called Vidhayak Nidhi or MLALAD. It is the "Constituency Development Fund (CDF)", administered by the J&K Finance Department** (not by the Assembly Secretariat). Both governing instruments were located on the official Government of Jammu and Kashmir order repository and both were fetched:

- **Guidelines:** "Guidelines on Constituency Development Fund (CDF) Scheme", Finance Department, 10/03/2025 = **Government Order No. 76-FD of 2025** - `https://jkgad.nic.in/common/showOrder.aspx?actCode=O44256` (curl-200). **This is a SCANNED PDF**: 3,028,365 bytes, 19 pages, containing **18 full-page JPEG images** (`/Subtype /Image`, `/Filter [/FlateDecode /DCTDecode]`, widths 1185-1240 px, `/Quality 60`) and **no usable text layer** - naive text extraction produced only character noise. It requires OCR.
- **Amendment:** "Amendment in Existing Guidelines on Constituency Development Fund (CDF) Scheme", Finance Department, 04/07/2025 = **Government Order No. 185-FD of 2025** - `https://jkgad.nic.in/common/showOrder.aspx?actCode=O44447` (curl-200). 466,656 bytes, 1 page, **text-extractable** (`/Font 2`, `/Image 1`). Its text reads: *"In continuation to Government order No. 76-FD of 2025 Dated: 10/03/2025, sanction is hereby accorded to the following amendments in the existing guidelines on Constituency Development Fund (CDF) Scheme with immediate effect... Para 2.12 of the guidelines on Constituency Development Fund (CDF) scheme is omitted... 1. Enhancement of CDF from the existing Rs. 300.00 lakhs to Rs. 400.00 lakhs to each Hon'ble MLA per year under the scheme from the Financial Year 2025-26; 'The MLA shall recommend the works up to the annual ceiling within 90 days of commencement of Financial Year'."*

**So the official per-MLA annual ceiling is Rs 400.00 lakh (Rs 4 crore) from FY 2025-26, raised from Rs 300.00 lakh (Rs 3 crore).**

**Does a per-MLA data portal exist? NO OFFICIAL PORTAL FOUND.** This is the central finding for this jurisdiction. Searching the official GAD/Finance order repository (`https://jkgad.nic.in/En/SearchOrder.aspx`, POST-based ASP.NET search, radio "Order" + subject "Constituency Development Fund") returns **exactly 2 records** - the two scheme orders above, and nothing else. There is **no portal publishing per-MLA allocation consumed, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure**, and no J&K analogue of MPLADS at https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/. Attempts to resolve plausible hosts (`cdf.jk.gov.in`, `jkcdf.nic.in`, `jkfinance.nic.in`, `jkfad.nic.in`, `finance.jk.gov.in`) all failed to resolve or connect. The J&K apex portal `jk.gov.in` resolves in DNS to 164.100.223.4 (NIC) but **did not respond to any request within 45 seconds** from this vantage point; only subdomains such as https://ceo.jk.gov.in/ and https://singlewindow.jk.gov.in/ answered.

District-level monitoring apps do exist under the name **"e-Nigrani" (Constituency Development Management Information System, CDMIS)**, launched by the Deputy Commissioners of Srinagar and Budgam in February 2026 and built by the private vendor Econet Org (https://econet.org.in/portfolio/16). These are **district-scoped, app-based, and no official government URL for them could be verified** - they are reported only in news and a vendor portfolio page, both UNOFFICIAL. Even if a district portal exists, it would be district-scoped, not a per-MLA state-level publication.

**Verification level of the fund sources:** scheme instruments curl-200 on official .nic.in; per-MLA data not found (correctly reported as absent, not guessed).

### In the Vidhan Sabha

The NeVA J&K instance publishes a real assembly-record layer, but it is **incomplete at the per-member level and completely silent on attendance**.

**Published and verified by curl:**

- **Starred and unstarred questions: YES, per member.** The assembly-level Questions module (`https://jkla.neva.gov.in/Questions?IsStateDashBoard=True`) exposes the categories **Starred Question, UnStarred Question, Short Notice Question, Inter Session Questions List, Starred Reply, UnStarred Reply, Short Notice Questions**. Crucially, an undocumented per-member endpoint works: `GET https://jkla.neva.gov.in/Member/Member_Question?MemberId=68&AssemblyID=1` returned a 2,461-byte DataTable with columns **Sr No | Question Number | Question Type | Date | Minister | Subject | Document**, e.g. *"1 | 21 | Starred | 03/02/26 | Satish Sharma | AQ No. 21 Regarding: Play fields"*. This is a genuine per-MLA question record, in HTML table form.
- **Private members' bills: filter exists, DATA DOES NOT.** The Legislation module (`https://jkla.neva.gov.in/Bill?IsStateDashBoard=True`) offers Select House / Select Session / Bill Category (Money Bill, Financial Bill, Ordinary Bill, Constitution Amendment Bill, Ordinance Replacing Bill) / Year (2010-2026) / status (Assented, Passed, Pending, Withdrawn, Negatived, Lapsed, Introduced) / **Government Bill vs Private Member Bill**. Pulling the underlying endpoint `GET /Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch?BillStatusId=0&BillTypeId=0&BillCategoryId=0&BillTitle=&AssemblyID=1&SessionID=0&MinistryId=0&FromYear=2024&ToYear=2026&IsBicameral=False&HouseCode=JK` returns **"No Records Founds"** (verified across HouseCode values JK, JKA, JKLA, empty and 1). `/Bill/GetAllAssembliesList` returns empty and `/Bill/SessionList` returns an application Error Page. The per-member BILLS tab on every member page likewise renders "No Records Founds". So although J&K MLAs have in fact introduced private members' bills (press reports count 17 bills for the Autumn 2026 session alone), **none of it is published on the official site in machine-usable form**.
- **Notice / Assurances / Committee / Debates per member: the tabs exist, the data does not.** `/Member/Member_Notice?MemberId=68&AssemblyID=1` returns "No Records Founds"; `/Member/Member_Committee?MemberId=68&AssemblyID=1` returns "No Records Founds"; the Assurances and Debates tabs render "No Records Founds", as does the member page's BILLS tab.
- **Session documents DO exist as PDFs.** The homepage Notice Board links to real PDFs on the shared CMS under `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/JK/FileStructures//Notices/<guid>.pdf`, including bulletins "Regarding: AQs Starred and Unstarred 4 each for Autumn Session 2026", the provisional calendar for the Autumn Session 2026, the summon order, and a suspension-of-committees notice. The **Today** page (`/Today`) itemises Birthday, List of Business, Starred Question, UnStarred Question, Government Bills Introduced/Passed, Committee Meetings, Papers to be Laid, Bulletin I, Bulletin II and Daily Synopsis. The **Digital Library** (`/Home/DigitalLibrary`) categories are e-Library, Questions, Notice, BILLS, Committee Reports, List of Business, Other Paper, House Proceedings and News Clips.

**NOT published anywhere on the official site: MLA ATTENDANCE.** No attendance figure, percentage, sitting register or member-wise presence record exists. Searched across the homepage, the member directory, per-member profile, member tabs, Today, Business, Questions, Legislation and the Digital Library - the string "attend" does not appear in any of those responses. This is an absolute gap: **attendance cannot be sourced officially for J&K MLAs, and no unofficial aggregator (PRS India's MLA Track included) publishes it either.**
