### Format note

A fetcher needs an **HTTP client with a cookie jar, not a headless browser** for the assembly data, plus **OCR for the fund rules** and an **Excel/PDF parser for the exports**. Concretely: (1) NeVA J&K is server-rendered HTML, but the bare paths `302`-redirect to `/Home/NeVA` - you must first `GET /` to establish a session cookie and send a `Referer: https://jkla.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`, and you must supply either `?IsStateDashBoard=True` or an explicit `?MLT=<n>&MSLT=<n>&Assemblyid=1`; the member grid is then at `GET /ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList` and per-member questions at `GET /Member/Member_Question?MemberId=<id>&AssemblyID=1`. DataTables sorting is client-side, so no JS execution is required. (2) The J&K GAD order search `https://jkgad.nic.in/En/SearchOrder.aspx` is an **ASP.NET WebForms POST-only endpoint** - it requires replaying `__VIEWSTATE`, `__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR` and `__EVENTTARGET` from a prior GET, and a correct radio value (`O` for Order); GET with query strings returns the blank form. (3) The CDF guidelines PDF (`actCode=O44256`) is a **scan** - 18 full-page JPEGs at 1185-1240 px, no text layer - and therefore needs **OCR**; only the one-page amendment (`actCode=O44447`) is text-extractable. (4) `/ContactDirectory/ExcelforHonbleMember` advertises `application/excel` but delivers **HTML**, so parse it as HTML rather than opening it with an XLS library. (5) `https://www.eci.gov.in/` is a React SPA that returns a 1,279-byte shell to curl, but its **backend JSON API is open and needs no key** - call `https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/...` directly; `results.eci.gov.in` is plain static HTML. (6) No captcha, no login and no geo-block was encountered on NeVA, ceojk.nic.in, jkgad.nic.in or the ECI API. The one hard reachability failure is the J&K apex portal `jk.gov.in`, which resolves but never responds.

### Gap note

Four things **cannot be sourced officially and should be recorded as gaps, not filled from aggregators**. First, and most important for this project: **there is no per-MLA Constituency Development Fund data at all** - no allocation consumed, no works recommended, no works sanctioned, no works completed, no expenditure, for any of the 90 MLAs. Only the scheme rules and the per-MLA ceiling (Rs 400 lakh/year from FY 2025-26, GO No. 185-FD of 2025) are official; the financial *performance* data simply is not published, on any `.gov.in`/`.nic.in` host that could be reached. The only monitoring layer is district-scoped ("e-Nigrani" CDMIS in Srinagar and Budgam) with no verifiable government URL, and it is not per-MLA. Second, **MLA attendance does not exist officially in any form** - not on the assembly site, not on any member page, not in the Digital Library - and PRS India's MLA Track does not cover J&K MLA attendance either. Third, **private members' bills are not published**: the Legislation module offers a "Private Member Bill" filter, but every query returns "No Records Founds", so the bills J&K MLAs have actually introduced cannot be listed from an official source. Fourth, **per-MLA Assurances, Committee membership, Debates and Notices all render "No Records Founds"**, so the assembly-record story for J&K is effectively limited to starred/unstarred questions. A related archival gap: the **2024 J&K assembly Form-20 / AC-wise result tables are not stably hosted** - `results.eci.gov.in` now serves the August-2026 bye-elections and every 2024 assembly path returns HTTP 404, CEO J&K's Form-20 archive stops at the 2014 LA and 2019 LS elections, and the ECI JSON API publishes only the election *schedule* (phases, AC counts, poll and counting dates), not winners. Candidate-level 2024 J&K results must therefore be reconstructed from affidavit/candidate data or captured live during the counting window rather than re-fetched later.

### Platform

Jammu and Kashmir runs on a **shared national template, not a bespoke site**: the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**, owned by the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs with the National Informatics Centre, which also runs the Lok Sabha and Rajya Sabha instances. The evidence is explicit and multiple. Every page footer reads *"© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India — Content managed by: Jammu & Kashmir Legislative Assembly"*; the URL pattern is uniform `<housecode>.neva.gov.in` across roughly forty houses listed on https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA (jkla, mhla, upvs, hpvs, kerala, raj, tnla, puddu, bla, chg, goa, gujarat, manipur, mizoram, nagaland, odisha, punjab, sikkim, tripura, tsla, utkh, wbla, loksabha, rajyasabha); the same path on **eight different house subdomains** (jkla, mhla, upvs, hpvs, kerala, raj, tnla, puddu) `302`-redirects to the byte-identical 192,345-byte `/Home/NeVA` page; J&K member photos are served from the shared multi-tenant CMS at `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/JK/...`; and the shared markup even leaks its origin - the breadcrumb on J&K member-detail pages still reads **"eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"**, the Himachal Pradesh template text that was never cleaned. Practically, this means **one scraper for the NeVA endpoints (`/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`, `/Member/Member_ProfileDetail`, `/Member/Member_Question`) serves J&K and every other onboarded house** by swapping the host, the `UnitCode` (`JK`) and the `Assemblyid`, which is a far larger payoff than J&K alone.

---

## Ladakh (IN-LA)

**Verdict:** no-legislature - Ladakh is a union territory **without a Legislative Assembly**, a position stated verbatim by the Ministry of Home Affairs ("Legislature: It is a Union Territory without Legislature"), administered under Article 239 through a Lieutenant Governor with regulations made by the President under Article 240; there is therefore **no MLA, no member roster, no Vidhayak Nidhi/MLALAD fund and no assembly record**, and the closest elected body — the Ladakh Autonomous Hill Development Council (LAHDC) — elects **councillors**, not MLAs.

### Profile

**There is no legislature, so there is no assembly website and no MLA roster.** This is established by a direct, unambiguous statement from the administering ministry, not by inference.

**The governing citation (primary, official).** The Ministry of Home Affairs' own division page for Jammu & Kashmir and Ladakh Affairs states, in its "Overview of UT of Ladakh" block, verbatim: *"Formation: The Union Territory of Ladakh came into existence on 31.10.2019. **Legislature: It is a Union Territory without Legislature.** Districts: It comprises of 2 districts i.e. Leh and Kargil."* The same page describes the MHA's own statutory remit for Ladakh as *"the delegation of powers to the Administrator/Lieutenant Governor under **Article 239** of the Constitution of India"* and *"Formulation of regulations under **Article 240** of the Constitution for the peace, progress, and good governance of the Union Territory of Ladakh."* Article 240 — the President's power to legislate for a UT by regulation — is the constitutional marker of a UT with **no** legislature; it was invoked for Ladakh as recently as August 2026 (presidential regulation establishing a Jammu & Kashmir High Court bench at Ladakh). The UT was carved out of the former State of Jammu and Kashmir by the **Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation Act, 2019**, which created **two** UTs: Jammu & Kashmir, for which the Act provides a Legislative Assembly, and Ladakh, for which **the Act provides no legislature at all**. The contrast is drawn explicitly on the MHA page itself, which says of J&K: *"Legislature - It is a UT with Legislature."*

**Administering authority:** Ministry of Home Affairs, Government of India (Jammu & Kashmir and Ladakh Affairs Division); at UT level, the Lieutenant Governor of Ladakh as Administrator under Article 239, aided by the Chief Secretary and the UT Secretariat.

**The UT administration's own official URL** (what the project should use in place of an assembly site):
- **https://ladakh.gov.in/** — "The Administration of Union Territory of Ladakh". Verified HTTP 200, `text/html`, 235,661 bytes, fetched with curl. Footer credit: *"Content Owned by Administration of Union Territory of Ladakh, Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre, Ministry of Electronics & Information Technology, Government of India."*

**Machine-readable negative proof that Ladakh holds no assembly elections.** The Election Commission of India's own public JSON API `https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/get-ac-election-state` returns the list of States/UTs that held **Assembly** elections, by year. **Ladakh is absent from every year.** The 2024 entry lists `"U08":"Jammu & Kashmir"` — the *other* UT created by the same 2019 Act, which does have a legislature — while Ladakh (which would carry `U09`) appears in no year's list whatsoever. This is a verified, curl-reachable, official confirmation rather than an argument from silence.

**The closest thing to a member roster — and why it is NOT an MLA roster.** Ladakh's only elected legislative-tier bodies are the two **Ladakh Autonomous Hill Development Councils** (LAHDC Leh, constituted 1995; LAHDC Kargil, constituted 2003), statutory bodies created under the **Ladakh Autonomous Hill Development Councils Act, 1997** (earlier 1995 for Leh). They are *district-level autonomous councils with development and planning powers*, not legislatures: they cannot pass laws, and their members are **councillors**, not MLAs. The Atlas must not present them as MLAs. What they do publish is genuinely useful and is curl-fetchable HTML:

- **LAHDC Leh councillor roster — https://leh.nic.in/lahdc-leh-2/** (curl-200, 141,930 bytes). Publishes a plain HTML table with **26 rows** and columns **S. No. | Name | Constituency | Contact No.** E.g. `1. | Shri Ghulam Mehdi | Turtuk |` (contact number blank for many rows). Also publishes the five Executive Councillors with their portfolios (CEC Adv. Tashi Gyalson; ECs Tsering Angchuk, Tashi Namgyal Yakzee, Ghulam Mehdi, Stanzin Chosphel). **No party column. No photograph. No term/assembly number.** Names carry the honorific "Shri".
- **LAHDC Kargil councillor roster — https://kargil.nic.in/councilors/** (curl-200, 119,555 bytes). Publishes a plain HTML table with **26 rows** and columns **No. and Name of Constituency | Name of Councilor**. E.g. `1-Ranbirpora | ABDUL SAMAD` … `26-Stakchey Khangral | PADMA DORJEY`. **No party column. No photograph. No contact.** (LAHDC Kargil has 30 councillors in total — 26 elected plus 4 nominated from minority and women — but only the 26 elected constituency councillors are listed.)
- **LAHDC Leh party affiliation IS published — but on a different page.** **https://leh.nic.in/election-trends-results/** (curl-200, 144,278 bytes) publishes the *General Election-2020 to the 6th LAHDC Leh* result as a plain HTML table with **party affiliation and winning margin per constituency** for all 26 constituencies, plus a party-wise summary (BJP 15, INC 9, Independent 2). This is the only official page found that attaches a **party** to Ladakh's elected representatives. It covers the 2020 election only.

**Verification level:** the absence of a legislature is established at **curl-200** level from primary official sources (the Ministry of Home Affairs' own division page stating "without Legislature"; the ECI's own JSON API showing Ladakh in no assembly-election year; the absence of any NeVA instance). The councillor rosters and the 2020 result table are **curl-200** HTML. Positive confirmation of the *non-existence* of an assembly site: no `assembly.ladakh.gov.in`, `vidhansabha.ladakh.gov.in`, `ladakhassembly.gov.in` or NeVA instance exists (see Platform).

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**There is no MLA development fund in Ladakh, because there are no MLAs.** No scheme named *Vidhayak Nidhi*, *Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi*, *MLALAD* or *MLA LADF* operates in this union territory, and **no official portal for any such fund exists**. This is a structural absence, not an unfound document: a legislature that does not exist cannot vote a constituency development fund for members it does not have.

**The real LAHDC analogue — and it is for COUNCILLORS, not MLAs.** The fund that does exist in Ladakh is the **CCDA — Councillor Constituency Development Allocation** (also reported in local media as "Councillor Constituency Development Fund"), administered by each Hill Council for its own elected councillors. The figure is documented on an **official government source**, the UT administration's own portal: *"the CEC proposed the enhancement of **Councilor Constituency Development Allocation (CCDA)** in LAHDC Kargil from Rs 01 cr to **Rs 1.50 cr for each Councilor** from the next financial year 2023-2024, which was approved by the Executive Council. Further, it was decided that 85 percent from the CCDA shall be proposed for major works while as 15 percent for machinery and equipment during the financial year 2023-2024. Moreover, it was decided that the Councilors shall identify the 80 percent of works having estimated cost above Rs 5 lakhs while as the rest of 20 percent shall be within the estimated cost of Rs 1 lakh to 5 lakhs."* (https://ladakh.gov.in/cec-feroz-khan-chairs-executive-council-meeting-at-kargil/) This is an official per-member allocation figure with works-splitting rules — but the member is a **councillor**, and this must never be rendered on the site as an "MLA fund".

**There is no per-councillor public data portal.** No official portal was found publishing, per councillor, the allocation limit, works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure — i.e. **Ladakh has no state-level equivalent of the MPLADS per-member dashboard for its councillors.** CCDA figures appear only as aggregate district-plan totals (e.g. LAHDC Leh district plan figures announced in UT press releases) and as statements in individual UT press releases, not as a queryable dataset. The fund exists as an allocation within the district plan; **its per-member utilisation is not published.**

**The one genuinely per-member fund that DOES publish for Ladakh is MPLADS — for the single Member of Parliament.** The MoSPI MPLADS–eSAKSHI portal at **https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/** carries Ladakh as `STATE_ID: 130` and publishes, for the 18th Lok Sabha: **Allocated Limit ₹14,70,00,000 (₹14.70 Crore); Works Recommended 450 (₹12,94,20,851 / ₹12.94 Cr); Works Sanctioned 341 (₹10,22,25,851 / ₹10.22 Cr); Works Completed 101 (₹3,42,35,763 / ₹3.42 Cr); Expenditure on Completed and On-going Works ₹4,14,12,412 (₹4.14 Cr); Amount consented for Calamity ₹0.** Because Ladakh has exactly **one** Lok Sabha constituency (1-Ladakh PC), this state-level row **is** the per-MP figure for the lone Ladakh MP — a genuinely per-member, official, curl-fetchable dataset. It is *MP*LADS for one MP and must be labelled as such, never as an MLA fund. District-level splits (Leh/Kargil) return zeroes from the same API, so the state-level row is the only granularity available.

**Verification level:** the CCDA per-councillor figure is **curl-200** from an official UT government page. The *absence* of any MLA fund portal is a constitutional consequence plus a negative keyword search across the UT portal. The MPLADS figures are **curl-200 JSON** from an official government API, but MPLADS itself is a JS/ZK-rendered site for humans (see Format note).

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**Not applicable — there is no Vidhan Sabha and no assembly record of any kind.** There is **no attendance, no starred questions, no unstarred questions and no private members' bills** for Ladakh, because the UT has no Legislative Assembly in which such records could be created. Nothing is "unpublished" in the sense of a missing dataset: **the institution that would generate the data does not exist.** The MHA states plainly that Ladakh is "a Union Territory without Legislature", and the ECI's assembly-election API places Ladakh in no year's assembly-election list.

**The UT is represented in Parliament only.** Ladakh elects **one Member of Parliament** to the Lok Sabha (1-Ladakh Parliamentary Constituency); it has no assembly constituencies at all. Being a UT without a legislature, it has no seat of its own in the Rajya Sabha (such UTs are represented by Members elected by Parliament). Parliamentary attendance, starred/unstarred questions and private members' bills for the Ladakh MP exist — but as **Parliament of India** records (Lok Sabha/Rajya Sabha secretariats), not as an assembly record, and the ECI officer directory's `ceo-ladakh@eci.gov.in` entry is the only UT-level election office found.

**The subordinate elected bodies are the LAHDCs, and they are not legislatures.** LAHDC Leh (1995) and LAHDC Kargil (2003) elect councillors who hold executive-council portfolios and control district plans, and their 2020/2023 election results are published officially — but a Hill Council passes no laws and keeps no question register, no attendance register and no private members' bills. Councillor business is not an assembly record.

### Format note

A fetcher needs **four different techniques** for this jurisdiction, and no single scraper covers it. (1) **Plain curl + HTML parsing** works for everything on the UT administration and district sites — `ladakh.gov.in`, `leh.nic.in` and `kargil.nic.in` are ordinary WordPress-rendered S3WaaS pages whose councillor rosters and the LAHDC Leh 2020 result table are **literal HTML `<table>` markup**, no JS required; the only care needed is that these pages are large (118–236 KB) and much of it is an identical navigation mega-menu. (2) **curl + JSON with a POST body** is required for MPLADS: the per-MP figures live behind `POST /rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getTilesData` with `Content-Type: application/json` and body `{"uname":"<house>,<state>,<district>,<tenure>"}` — for Ladakh `{"uname":"0,130,0,2"}` (state 130, tenure 2 = 18th Lok Sabha). A GET returns the ZK framework's HTML scaffolding instead of data, and an unauthenticated curl **does** receive the JSON, so no browser is needed for the numbers even though the human-facing page at `mplads.mospi.gov.in` is a ZK/AJAX SPA (`/zkau/web/...`, `body{display:none !important}` anti-clickjacking rule) that renders nothing to curl. (3) **A real browser is needed for `www.eci.gov.in`**, which is a React single-page app: every route — including `/general-election-to-lok-sabha-2024`, `/statistical-reports` and any `/ceo-ladakh` path — returns the identical 1,279-byte shell with `<div id="root">` and `<noscript>You need to enable JavaScript to run this app</noscript>`, so **URL-existence cannot be probed by status code on eci.gov.in at all** (a fabricated ECI path returns 200, same as a real one). Its `/eci-backend/public/api/…` JSON endpoints, by contrast, answer plain curl. (4) **PDF text extraction** for the UT and district documents; a sample Leh 2024 result PDF on the S3WaaS CDN carries a genuine text layer (28 `/Font` objects against 14 `/Image`), so text extraction works and **OCR should be a fallback, not the default**.

Concrete blockers: **the CEO Ladakh website listed by the ECI's own officer directory is dead at the TCP level** — `ceo.ladakh.gov.in` resolves in DNS to 164.100.85.52 (a NIC address, NS `ns2.nic.in`) but connections to **both port 80 and port 443 time out** after 25 s, while `ladakh.gov.in` on the same infrastructure connects fine; every CEO-level election document for Ladakh is therefore unreachable at its advertised URL. **The India Code bitstream link for the LAHDC Act, 1997 is a 404** despite appearing in search results and in older archives, so the statute's own text is not presently retrievable from that official URL. `results.eci.gov.in` uses **ephemeral, rotating paths** — it currently HTTP-refreshes to `/ResultAcByeAugust2026/index.htm`, and the historical `PcResultGenJune2024/…` constituency paths now 404 — so any hard-coded results URL will rot. No captcha, login, session cookie or geo-IP block was encountered on any official endpoint tested except the MPLADS **login** area, which is separate from the public dashboard data used here.

### Gap note

The gaps here are **structural, not documentary**, and that is the finding. There is **no MLA roster, no MLA photograph, no party-wise MLA list tied to a term/assembly number, no MLA fund allocation, no per-MLA works-recommended/sanctioned/completed/expenditure data, no attendance, no starred questions, no unstarred questions and no private members' bills** for Ladakh — because the UT has no Legislative Assembly in which any of these could exist. No further searching will produce them, and any site displaying them for IN-LA would be fabricating. Three narrower gaps are worth stating precisely. **First**, the elected representatives who *do* exist — 26 LAHDC Leh councillors and 26 (of 30) LAHDC Kargil councillors — are published officially with **name and constituency only**; **no photograph exists on either official roster**, and party affiliation exists for Leh (2020 election page) but **not for Kargil**. **Second**, the CCDA per-councillor fund is documented only as a **per-member allocation headline** (Rs 1.50 Cr in Kargil from FY 2023-24) plus works-splitting rules; there is **no official portal publishing, per councillor, the works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed or expenditure**, so the four of the five fund metrics the project wants are unobtainable for councillors, and none are obtainable for MLAs because none exist. **Third**, the exact per-councillor CCDA rate for **LAHDC Leh** was not found on any official page (the Rs 1.50 Cr figure is documented for Kargil specifically), and no CCDA figure at all was found for the five newly announced Hill Councils for the Sham, Nubra, Zanskar, Drass and Changthang districts — a governance change reported in September 2026 but which had not yet produced an elected council or a published fund at the time of research. The single genuinely per-member official fund dataset available for Ladakh is **MPLADS for its one MP**, which is a Parliament-level scheme and must be labelled as such.

### Platform

**Common NIC template at the UT/district layer, but no assembly platform — because there is no assembly.** All three Ladakh government sites are built on **S3WaaS** (*Secure, Scalable and Sugamya Website as a Service*), the National Informatics Centre's shared WordPress-based website service, evidenced by three concrete markers: each page loads assets from an NIC S3WaaS CDN bucket (`cdn.s3waas.gov.in/s3291597a100aadd814d197af4f4bab3a7` for `leh.nic.in`, `cdn.s3waas.gov.in/s341ae36ecb9b3eee609d05b90c14222fb` for `kargil.nic.in`); each uses a numbered stock NIC theme (`wp-content/themes/district-theme-6` on Leh, `district-theme-7` on Kargil, `sdo-theme` on `ladakh.gov.in`); and each footer carries the same credit, *"Content Owned by … , Developed and hosted by National Informatics Centre, Ministry of Electronics & Information Technology, Government of India."* The practical consequence for this project is real and useful: **one S3WaaS-aware scraper serves the UT portal and both district sites**, and because sibling districts across India share `district-theme-N` markup, the same parser very likely generalises to other states' district sites — though the councillor tables themselves are hand-built page content, not a templated component. Decisively, however, **there is no assembly platform**: NeVA (the NIC's National e-Vidhan Application, the template a real Vidhan Sabha would use) contains **zero occurrences of "Ladakh"**, and while the NeVA instance pattern `<statecode>la.neva.gov.in` demonstrably works — `jkla.neva.gov.in` returns HTTP 200 for the Jammu & Kashmir legislature created by the same 2019 Act — **`ladla.neva.gov.in`, `lahdcla.neva.gov.in` and `lkla.neva.gov.in` do not resolve at all**. The absence of a NeVA instance is itself confirmation that Ladakh has no legislature.

---

## Lakshadweep (IN-LD)

**Verdict:** no-legislature - Lakshadweep is a Union Territory for which no legislature has ever been constituted; it is administered by an Administrator appointed by the President under Article 239, with the Ministry of Home Affairs responsible for general administration and budget, so there is no MLA, no member roster, no Vidhan Sabha record and no MLA development fund.

### Profile

**There is no Legislative Assembly and no MLA for Lakshadweep.** The official website of the UT administration is **https://lakshadweep.gov.in/** (fetched, HTTP 200, "Official Website of Administration of Lakshadweep"). It publishes no member roster, no MLAs and no assembly record, because none exists; a site search on the administration's own portal for the phrase "legislative assembly" returns literally **"No results found!"** (https://lakshadweep.gov.in/?s=legislative+assembly, fetched, HTTP 200).

**The constitutional and statutory position.** Lakshadweep is one of the Union Territories specified in Part II of the First Schedule to the Constitution. No legislature has been created for it: Parliament's power to create legislatures for certain Union Territories (Article 239A, "Creation of local Legislatures or Council of Ministers or both for certain Union territories") has been exercised only for Puducherry, and the National Capital Territory of Delhi's assembly rests on the separate Article 239AA. Lakshadweep instead falls under **Article 239 (Administrator appointed by the President) and Article 240 (power of the President to make regulations for certain Union Territories)**. This is stated in the jurisdiction's own governing statute: the **Lakshadweep Panchayat Regulation, 2022 (No. 5 of 2022)**, published in the Gazette of India Extraordinary, opens "In exercise of the powers conferred by **article 240** of the Constitution, the President is pleased to promulgate the following Regulation", and defines "**Administrator**" as the Administrator of the Union territory of Lakshadweep "**appointed by the President of India under article 239 of the Constitution**" (https://cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in/s32b8eba3cb0d0f1d761cb74d94a5ace36/uploads/2024/08/202408271408443684.pdf, fetched, HTTP 200, text extracted).

The classification is made explicitly by the Comptroller and Auditor General of India — an official, primary source — in **Report No. 3 of 2018, "Union Territories without Legislatures"**: "There are seven Union Territories (UTs) specified under Part II of the First Schedule to the Constitution of India, viz. Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Chandigarh, Dadra and Nagar Haveli, Daman and Diu, **Lakshadweep**, National Capital Territory of Delhi and Puducherry. **Except for the National Capital Territory of Delhi and Puducherry, UTs do not have Legislatures.** This report includes audit observations arising from the audit of the five UTs without Legislatures." The same report states it "has been prepared for submission to the **President under Article 151** of the Constitution of India" — the route used precisely because there is no State Legislature to lay it before — and it carries a full audit chapter on the "**Union Territory of Lakshadweep Administration (UTLA)**" (https://cag.gov.in/webroot/uploads/download_audit_report/2018/Report_No_3_of_2018_-_Union_Territories_without_Legislatures_Compliance_Audit_Observations_Union_Government.pdf, fetched, HTTP 200, text extracted).

**Administering authority.** The Ministry of Home Affairs. The CAG report records that "The budget provisions in respect of UTs are under the administrative control of the **Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA)**. The MHA prepares the Demands for Grants and Detailed Demand for Grants (DDGs) relating to these UTs for approval of Parliament. While the general administration of the UTs is the responsibility of the MHA...", and that "**Administrators are also separately appointed** for Dadra and Nagar Haveli, Daman and Diu and **Lakshadweep**. The **Administrator's Advisory Councils** in these UTs advise the administrators." The Administration's own profile page names the incumbent: "**Shri Praful Patel**, Administrator of UT of Lakshadweep... He has taken charge as Lakshadweep Administrator from 05th Dec 2020" (https://lakshadweep.gov.in/about-lakshadweep/profile-administrator/, fetched, HTTP 200).

**What exists instead — an elected local tier, not a legislature.** Lakshadweep has **Village (Dweep) Panchayats and one District Panchayat**, constituted under the Lakshadweep Panchayats Regulation, 1994 and now the **Lakshadweep Panchayat Regulation, 2022**, both promulgated by the President under Article 240. The administration states the District Panchayat has 26 directly elected members plus the ten Village Panchayat Chairpersons and the Member of Parliament, with 88 elected members across the ten Village Panchayats (https://lakshadweep.gov.in/district-panchayath/, fetched, HTTP 200). Note that this page also carries internally inconsistent figures (it elsewhere says 22, then 26, directly elected District Panchayat members and 79 vs 85 vs 88 Village Panchayat members, reflecting successive delimitations) — the panchayat roster on it is undated and lists the 4th District Panchayat, elected in December 2012/January 2013. **Critical for this project:** the 2022 Regulation itself disposes of any assembly analogy — where other law says "the House of the People or the Legislative Assembly of a State", the Regulation substitutes the word "**Panchayat**".

**Verification level.** All statements above rest on URLs fetched successfully (curl-200). No profile, photo, party or term data for an MLA exists to be published, and none was found.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD

**There is no MLA local area development fund in Lakshadweep, under any name.** "Vidhayak Nidhi", "Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi", "MLALAD" and "MLA LADF" are all state-legislature schemes tied to the existence of MLAs; with no legislature there are no MLAs, no constituencies and no per-MLA fund. **No official portal, scheme-rules page, government resolution or guidelines document for any MLA fund in Lakshadweep was found** — this is a genuine absence, not an unsearched one. The UT administration's own site (https://lakshadweep.gov.in/) publishes gazette notifications, annual reports and policies but no MLA-fund scheme or per-MLA expenditure data.

The closest official analogues, stated precisely so they are not mistaken for the thing requested:

1. **MPLADS — the national per-representative fund, and it does cover Lakshadweep's MP.** The portal **https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/** (fetched, HTTP 200) is the national analogue named in the brief. Its backing REST service confirms Lakshadweep is a participating State/UT: `POST https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/rest/PreLoginDashboardData/getStateData` returns `{"STATE_NAME":"Lakshadweep","STATE_ID":19}`; `getConstituencyData` with `{"id":"19"}` returns `[{"ID":215,"CAPTION":"LAKSHADWEEP(ST)"}]`; and `getMpAndConstCombo` with `{"const_combo":"215,1,18"}` returns `[{"ID":3042791,"CAPTION":"MUHAMMED HAMDULLAH SAYEED"}]`. So per-MP allocated-limit / works-recommended / works-sanctioned / works-completed / expenditure figures for Lakshadweep's Member of Parliament are published on this national portal. **This is per-MP, never per-MLA** — `mlalad_per_mla` is definitively `none`. The fund is called **MPLADS** (Members of Parliament Local Area Development Scheme); there is no Lakshadweep-specific local name. The per-MP figures themselves are loaded by the dashboard's `getTilesData` endpoint, which requires a live ZK session (see Format note).

2. **Administrator's delegated financial powers — the UT-level substitute.** The CAG report records that "**Administrators of the UTs have been delegated financial powers up to a certain limit by MHA for sanction of plan schemes**", with MHA preparing the Demands for Grants for these UTs for approval of Parliament. This means development spending in Lakshadweep is sanctioned through the **Administrator within MHA-delegated limits and through the UT budget**, i.e. an executive/administrative route with no MLA intermediary — not a per-representative constituency fund.

3. **Panchayat funds.** The Lakshadweep Panchayat Regulation, 2022 constitutes a **Gram Fund** and a **District Panchayat Fund** with grants and own revenue, and the 2022 Act PDF carries a heading "Constitution of District Panchayat Fund... Grants. Properties vested in District Panchayat." These are local-body funds for the panchayats, published (if at all) only in UT budget documents and the CAG's UT audit reports — **no per-panchayat-member development fund portal exists**.

### In the Vidhan Sabha

**There is no Vidhan Sabha, so there is no attendance record, no starred questions, no unstarred questions, no private members' bills, no committee record and no per-member assembly activity of any kind for Lakshadweep.** Nothing is published because nothing exists to publish; this is an absence of the institution, not a gap in a publisher's disclosure.

Attendance, starred questions, unstarred questions and private members' bills: **not published — no legislature**. No assembly secretariat, no Speaker, no Question Hour, no Departmentally Related Standing Committees, no Assembly committee reports. The National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA), which is the platform that would carry these records for a legislature, does not include Lakshadweep (see Platform).

**Parliamentary representation instead.** Lakshadweep is represented in **Parliament only**, by a single Lok Sabha member. The official UT administration page states "The Lakshadweep Lok Sabha constituency is the only parliamentary constituency representing the entire Union Territory. This seat is reserved for Scheduled Tribes and, as of 2014, it is the smallest Lok Sabha constituency in India by number of voters" (https://lakshadweep.gov.in/about-lakshadweep/administrative-setup/constituencies/, fetched, HTTP 200). The Election Commission of India's own backend service corroborates the absence of assembly elections: `GET https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/get-ac-election-state` (fetched, HTTP 200, `application/json`) enumerates every State/UT with a Legislative Assembly election, year by year, and lists exactly **three Union Territories — U05 NCT of Delhi (2025), U07 Puducherry (2026), U08 Jammu & Kashmir (2024)** — with **Lakshadweep absent from every year**. Parliamentary business for the Lakshadweep member is recorded in Lok Sabha parliamentary material rather than in any UT assembly record; no parliamentary-library URL was verified in this pass, so none is asserted here.

### Format note

A fetcher needs **mixed** handling. Plain `curl` + HTML parsing suffices for the UT administration's WordPress/S3WaaS pages (https://lakshadweep.gov.in/) and for the CEO Lakshadweep site's HTML tables (`/elected-members`, `/election-history`, `/state-profile`) — these are server-rendered and returned complete markup with a browser User-Agent. The **eci.gov.in** front end is a client-routed React SPA whose every route returns the same 1,279-byte shell with a "You need to enable JavaScript" notice, so its HTML needs a headless browser — **but its backend is plain JSON and needs none**: `https://www.eci.gov.in/eci-backend/public/api/<endpoint>` answered `curl` directly (`get-ac-election-state`, `get-election-data?page_seo_name=...`, `general-election-2019`); a naive crawler that only reads eci.gov.in HTML will conclude the site is empty, which is wrong. The **MPLADS** portal is a ZKoss/ZK application: `GET` on its `/rest/PreLoginDashboardData/*` endpoints returns **405 Method Not Allowed**, and the JSON requires **POST with `Content-Type: application/json`** and a JSON body (`{}` for `getStateData`; `{"id":"19"}` for `getConstituencyData`; `{"const_combo":"215,1,18"}` for `getMpAndConstCombo`); the actual **per-MP fund figures** (`getTilesData`) additionally require a **live ZK session cookie** and returned the ZK login shell to anonymous callers — so per-MP MPLADS numbers need a browser/session, not plain curl. The SEC-for-UTs and CEO Lakshadweep documents are **PDFs**, and they are of three different kinds: the 2022 Regulation and the Gazette election-rules PDFs are **text PDFs** that extract cleanly (with letter-spacing artefacts from the gazette typesetting); the 11.12.2012 elected-members notification is a **scan with an OCR text layer** — the literal string "Scanned by CamScanner" appears in its content stream — and is **OCR-quality at best**, its names only partially recoverable; and the 23.08.2019 notification is a text PDF whose **digit glyphs map wrongly** (2012 extracts as "2072"), so any number taken from it must be cross-checked against the printed image. The CEO's Form 7A candidate list is a **pure scanned image PDF** (`/Font` = 0, `/Image` = 4) and **requires OCR**. Note also that CEO Lakshadweep serves documents through **base64-encoded path segments** (`/Users/download_pdf/<base64 of filename>`), and that server responses carry a `text/html` content type even for PDF bodies — a fetcher that filters on content type will silently drop them. No captcha, login, geo-IP block or POST-only obstacle was encountered on any DCHosted source except the MPLADS ZK session.

### Gap note

The gaps here are institutional, and they are the finding. **There is no MLA profile to source** — no name, party, constituency or photograph of an MLA for Lakshadweep exists in any official record, because no Legislative Assembly has been constituted; the site's "profile" dimension is unsatisfiable for this jurisdiction and must be rendered as "no legislature" rather than left blank. **There is no MLA development fund and no per-MLA fund data of any kind** — no Vidhayak Nidhi portal, no scheme guidelines, no allocation or works figures, official or otherwise; the only per-representative fund that touches Lakshadweep is MPLADS, which is **per-MP and belongs to the MP, not to an MLA**, so it cannot be presented as an MLA figure without misrepresenting it. **There is no assembly record** — attendance, starred questions, unstarred questions and private members' bills are all absent as a category, not merely unpublished. If the project wishes to show Lakshadweep's elected representatives at all, the only officially sourced alternatives are (a) the **Lok Sabha member**, whose 2024 result is published by CEO Lakshadweep both as an HTML table and as candidate/Form 20 PDFs, and (b) the **panchayat members** (District Panchayat and ten Village Panchayats), whose names appear only inside a **2012 scanned notification** hosted on secforuts.mha.gov.in and on an undated, internally inconsistent administration web page — there is **no current, complete, machine-readable panchayat member roster** anywhere official. Finally, no official consolidated Constitution text could be fetched to quote Article 239/239A/240 directly: indiacode.nic.in deep links for the Constitution returned 404, and the Legislative Department's page returned no content to curl — so the constitutional position above is evidenced through the **CAG's own "Union Territories without Legislatures" report** and through the **Lakshadweep Panchayat Regulation, 2022**, both official and both fetched, rather than through a Constitution PDF that could not be retrieved.

### Platform

**No assembly platform applies, because there is no assembly: NeVA does not cover Lakshadweep.** The National e-Vidhan Application site (https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA, fetched, HTTP 200) is the Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs' national legislature platform, and its own content mentions **Puducherry (12 times) and Delhi (25 times)** — the UTs that do have legislatures — while **Lakshadweep, Ladakh, Chandigarh and the Andaman & Nicobar Islands appear zero times**. This is the sharper form of the finding: the common assembly template exists nationally but deliberately excludes this jurisdiction, so **no scraper written for NeVA can ever serve Lakshadweep**.

What *is* shared is the layer beneath: the UT administration site runs on **NIC's S3WaaS** template (the page exposes `S3WaaSAccessibilityParams`, `blogInfoName:"Lakshadweep"`, and assets on `cdn.s3waas.gov.in` — 74 S3WaaS markers), and the **State Election Commission for UTs at secforuts.mha.gov.in uses the same S3WaaS platform** (32 markers, assets on `cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in`) with a strictly repeated per-UT URL scheme — `/ut-of-lakshadweep/`, `/ut-of-andaman-nicobar-islands/`, `/ut-of-dadra-nagar-haveli/`, `/ut-of-ladakh/` — each with identically named children (`act-and-rules/`, `panchayat-election-result/`, `delimitation-of-panchayats/`). That URL pattern plus identical markup across four UTs is concrete common-platform evidence: **one scraper for secforuts.mha.gov.in serves the local-body election documents of every legislature-less UT**. The CEO Lakshadweep site is the exception — a **bespoke** ASP.NET MVC application with its own `/Users/<topic>` routing and base64 document paths, footer-credited "Software Design & Development Credits : National Informatics Centre [Lakshadweep]", so it is NIC-built but not a shared template and must be written per CEO.

---

## Puducherry (IN-PY)

**Verdict:** partial - Puducherry has a real legislature and an official assembly site (NeVA at `puddu.neva.gov.in`) that publishes a full per-member roster with name, party, constituency and photo, and the ECI publishes complete per-constituency results as plain HTML; but the MLA-LAD fund is published only as scanned Government-Order PDFs with per-constituency annexures (no per-MLA portal, no per-MLA works-completed or expenditure), and MLA attendance is published nowhere.

### Profile
Puducherry **does** have a legislature. It is a Union Territory with a Legislative Assembly provided for by **Article 239A of the Constitution of India** (inserted by the Constitution (Fourteenth Amendment) Act, 1962) and constituted under the **Government of Union Territories Act, 1963** (the Act provides for 30 elected members and up to 3 members nominated by the Central Government - the MLA-LAD Government Orders consistently read "30 constituencies and 3 nominated MLAs"). The current house is the **SIXTEENTH LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY** (constituted 2026; the Fifteenth ran 06/04/2021 - 05/05/2026) and was sworn in on 20.05.2026.

There is **no standalone Puducherry assembly website**. `pyassembly.gov.in`, `pyassembly.nic.in`, `puducherryassembly.gov.in`, `assembly.py.gov.in` and `pyassembly.py.gov.in` all fail to resolve. The assembly's official site is the state instance of the national NeVA platform: **https://puddu.neva.gov.in/** (curl-200, ~128 KB, title "Welcome to Puducherry Vidhan Sabha Legislative session and proceedings | National eVidhan Application"). It carries the state masthead "Puducherry Legislative Assembly / புதுச்சேரி சட்டப்பேரவை / SIXTEENTH LEGISLATIVE ASSEMBLY" and the office-holders LG K. Kailashnathan, CM N. Rangasamy, Speaker A. Anbalagan, Leader of Opposition A.M.H. Nazeem and Secretary J. Dayalane.

**Member roster (name / party / constituency / photo / term) - all five fields are published, officially.** The roster is served by an AJAX endpoint on the assembly's own subdomain:
- `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec` - the "Members Contact Directory" page, exposing a "Member by Assembly" selector covering **all sixteen assemblies with exact date ranges**, plus filters for Political Party, Constituency Name (with numbers), Panel of Chairpersons, Council of Ministers, Whip, Birthday, Male/Female, Nominated, First Time Elected Members and Members Elected in Bye-Election.
- `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/ContactDirectory/GetMembersDashbardData` - returns a ~265 KB HTML fragment containing **29 member cards**, each with portrait thumbnail, name, **party**, constituency number and name, date of birth, address and mobile. A per-assembly variant is `.../ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList?MLT=16&MSLT=16&Assemblyid=16`.
- Per-member page: `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/<MemberID>?AssemblyID=16` (e.g. `/Member/Details/63?AssemblyID=16` = Speaker A. Anbalagan) - full profile: name, party, "Constituency Name 15 - OUPALAM -[15]- Puducherry", permanent address, local address, mobile, email, portrait. All 29 `Member/Details` URLs are listed in the dashboard fragment.

**Critical access caveat:** every one of these routes **silently redirects to the national NeVA landing page unless a session cookie is presented first**. A plain `curl` GET of `/Questions`, `/Bill`, `/Business` or `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec` returns the 192 KB national NeVA home; issuing one GET to `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/` with a cookie jar and then reusing that jar makes the state routes return their real content. There is no login, captcha or credential - only a session cookie.

Two independent government sources cover the roster, and they disagree in count, which is itself informative:
- `https://py.gov.in/mlas` (Government of Puducherry portal, Drupal 7, server-rendered HTML) lists **30 constituency blocks** with constituency, name, **photo** (`https://py.gov.in/sites/default/files/<name>mla.jpg`, 29 distinct images), residential address, mobile and a per-constituency `mla<constituency>[at]py[dot]gov[dot]in` email. It publishes **no party field at all** (grep for AINRC/DMK/BJP/INC/ADMK/TVK returns zero matches).
- NeVA returns **29 sitting members**, and the missing one is constituency **09 - THATTANCHAVADY**. This is consistent: N. Rangasamy won both 04 - MANGALAM and 09 - THATTANCHAVADY in 2026 (`py.gov.in/mlas` lists "Shri. N. RANGASAMY" twice), vacated one, and the CEO Puducherry site is currently running a bye-election notice for "09-Thattanchavady AC". So NeVA is the more current roster and `py.gov.in/mlas` carries the stale duplicate.

For **party**, only NeVA and the ECI publish it. `py.gov.in/mlas` cannot supply party; the ECI constituency-wise results table (see below) is the cleanest official authority for name + party + constituency together.

Verification level: the NeVA routes above were **actually fetched and parsed** (HTTP 200 with substantive content); `py.gov.in/mlas` was actually fetched and parsed. Nothing is inferred.

### Development fund - Vidhayak Nidhi / MLALAD
The fund is **not** called "Vidhayak Nidhi" or "MLALAD" in Puducherry. Its exact official name, taken from the scheme document and the sanctioning orders, is the **"MLA-LAD Scheme"** - *Member of Legislative Assembly - Local Area Development Scheme*. It has been implemented by the **Local Administration Department (LAD)** since **1997**. The scheme rules page is **https://lad.py.gov.in/mla-scheme** (curl-200), which links the scheme document **https://lad.py.gov.in/sites/default/files/mlaschemelad1.pdf** (6 pages, 405 KB, a text PDF - 35 `/Font` references - not a scan).

The fund is **Rs 2 crore per MLA / per constituency per year**, enhanced to that level by **G.O.Ms.No.44/LAD(SW)/2019-20 dated 24.12.2019** ("Enhancement of MLA LAD Fund to Rs 2 Crores"), page `https://lad.py.gov.in/enhancement-mla-lad-fund-rs-2-crores-goms-no44ladsw2019-20`. Money is routed as Grant-in-Aid to local bodies and via a PUDA corpus fund (created 2014, Rs 3 crore limit).

**There is no per-MLA fund portal.** No `mlalad.py.gov.in`, `mla.py.gov.in`, `ladfund.py.gov.in` or `vidhayaknidhi.py.gov.in` exists (all fail to resolve). Instead the per-MLA figures exist as **per-constituency annexures inside scanned sanctioning Government Orders** hosted on the LAD site:

- **G.O.Ms.No.24/LAS/A1/2024 dated 22.11.2024** (`https://lad.py.gov.in/sites/default/files/g.o.ms.no.24-dt-22.11.2024.pdf`, 1.24 MB) - page `https://lad.py.gov.in/local-administration-release-gia-lbs-tune-rs6600-crores-implementing-mla-lad-scheme-2nd-phase-mla`. Releases **Rs 66.00 crore** for the 2nd Phase of 2023-24 and 1st Phase of 2024-25. Its **ANNEXURE "Elected MLA - Statement A"** is per-constituency and gives, in lakhs, for each of the 30 constituencies: `Eligible Fund per year = 200.00`, `2023-24 II & Final Phase = 123.00`, `2024-25 I Phase = 77.00`, `GIA released to the LBs = 200.00`. **"Statement B"** does the same for the **3 nominated MLAs**, named there as **K. Venkatesan, V.P. Ramalingam and R.B. Ashok Babu** (600.00 lakhs total). **GRAND TOTAL (A+B) = 6600.00 lakhs** (4059.00 + 2541.00).
- **G.O.Ms.No.03/LAS/A1/2024 dated 13.03.2024** (`https://lad.py.gov.in/sites/default/files/g.o.ms-no-03-las-a1-2024.pdf`) - same shape, Rs 25.41 crore, 1st Phase 2023-24, per-constituency annexure with the same four columns.
- **GIA to MLA LAD Scheme 2021-22** (`https://lad.py.gov.in/sites/default/files/gia-mla-lad-scheme2021-22.pdf`, 3.32 MB) - page `https://lad.py.gov.in/las-release-gia-local-bodies-implementation-mla-lad-scheme-2021-22`.

So the fund is **per-MLA (per-constituency) for allocation and release**, and that is a genuine official per-MLA figure. What is **not** published per MLA anywhere: **works recommended, works sanctioned, works completed and expenditure**. The scheme document carries only *aggregate* year-wise allocation/expenditure (2010-11: 3220.21 / 3214.35; 2011-12: 3750.00 / 3749.60; 2012-13: no provision; 2013-14: 1624.19 / 1624.19; 2014-15: 1899.82 / 1899.82 - lakhs), a per-constituency **count of works** for one past year (341 works sanctioned / 33.00 crore; 47 work orders issued; 298 work orders pending, broken down across the 30 constituencies and the 3 nominated MLAs in a table), and PUDA corpus releases. There is no per-MLA works-completed or per-MLA expenditure series, and no queryable interface of any kind.

Format and access: the annexures are **scanned Government Orders**, and their text layers differ in quality. `g.o.ms.no.24-dt-22.11.2024.pdf` and `g.o.ms-no-03-las-a1-2024.pdf` are scans that carry an **imperfect OCR text layer** (extractable, but with characteristic corruption - "Administratlon", "imPlementing", "PuducherrY", "1lst Phase" - and column values that need positional repair). `gia-mla-lad-scheme2021-22.pdf` and `go44-mp-mla.pdf` are **pure image scans with zero fonts and no text layer at all** - `pypdf` extracts 0 characters, so they require **OCR**. Verification level: scheme page, enhancement order, both per-constituency G.O.s and the 2021-22 GIA page were all **actually fetched**; the two per-constituency annexures were extracted and read.

### In the Vidhan Sabha
The assembly's record lives on NeVA at `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/`, and the platform's modules are genuinely present - but they are **search-driven, session-gated, and largely unpopulated for Puducherry**.

**Questions - starred and unstarred: partial.** `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/Questions` (needs the session cookie) offers **Starred Question, UnStarred Question, Short Notice Question and Inter Session Questions**, plus a "List" group with **Starred, Unstarred, Short Notice, Reply Starred, Reply UnStarred and Reply Short Notice**. The data behind those tabs is loaded by `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/Questions/FetchDataList?LstItem=<n>&Type=<n>` - `LstItem=0` = Starred, `LstItem=1` = UnStarred, `LstItem=3` = Short Notice, `LstItem=4` = Inter Session. That endpoint (70 KB) returns a **search form**, not rows: it presents a "Listed Questions" heading, an **Advanced Search**, a **Select House** dropdown over all sixteen assemblies with date ranges, and a **Select Session** dropdown. Rows therefore require a further House + Session selection, and the five successive responses for the starred/unstarred variants returned byte-identical payloads (70,596 bytes each), i.e. the form rather than the data. Session cookies are mandatory. On the **per-member** page (`/Member/Details/<id>?AssemblyID=16`) the **Questions tab is also a live AJAX tab** (`/Member/Member_Question`) - but it returned **"No Records Founds"**, and the same for Notice, Assurances, Committee, Debates and BILLS. So questions are published at the *house* level as downloadable/listed documents, and **not** as a per-member record.

**Unstarred questions** are exposed exactly as starred ones (`LstItem=1`), same form and same limitation.

**Attendance: no - not published, in any format.** The string "attendance" does not occur on the assembly home page, the Questions page, the Business page or the Members directory. NeVA has no attendance module for Puducherry, and no other Puducherry government site publishes MLA attendance. This is a hard gap.

**Private members' bills: partial, and effectively absent for Puducherry.** `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/Bill` (needs the cookie) presents a "Select House" dropdown with every assembly from the 1st to the current 16th, and links to **Government Bills (Introduced / Passed)**, List of Business, LOB PDF, Notice, Paper-Laid, Committee Reports, Bulletin I, Bulletin II, Daily Synopsis, Verbatim Debates, Provisional Calendar and Rotation of Ministries. Its data endpoints are `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, `/Bill/GetBillDashBoardData`, `/Bill/SessionList` and `/Bill/GetAllAssembliesList`. Note the module is titled **Government Bills** - there is **no separate private members' bills listing**, and the per-member BILLS tab returns "No Records Founds". `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/Business` and `/Today` expose List of Business, Government Bills, Notice, Committee Meetings, Papers to be Laid and Bulletins, but the `/Today` body rendered empty for the session date.

What the assembly site **does** publish usefully, per-member and without login beyond the cookie, is the **profile** (see above) and the **full assembly-term table** (16th: current; 15th: 06/04/2021 - 05/05/2026; 14th: 09/06/2016 - 18/01/2021; 13th: 28/03/2012 - 10/03/2016; 12th: 29/08/2006 - 15/02/2011; 11th: 09/06/2001 - 17/10/2005; 10th: 10/06/1996 - 13/03/2001; 9th: 04/07/1991 - 14/05/1996; 8th: 05/03/1990 - 04/03/1991; 7th: 16/03/1985 - 05/03/1990; 6th: 16/01/1980 - 24/06/1983; 5th: 02/07/1977 - 12/11/1978; 4th: 06/03/1974 - 28/03/1974; 3rd: 17/03/1969 - 03/01/1974; 2nd: 29/08/1964 - 18/09/1968; 1st: 01/07/1963 - 24/08/1964).

The government portal `py.gov.in` additionally hosts Legislative Assembly Secretariat material - RTI manuals, the list of enactments 1964-1973, and recruitment notices - reachable through `https://py.gov.in/search/node/Assembly`, but no assembly record data.
