| CEO Puducherry - Bye Election 09-Thattanchavady AC 2026 | https://ceopuducherry.py.gov.in/bye2026/ | yes | election-results | HTML + PDFs | curl-200 |
| Planning & Research Department, Puducherry | https://pandr.py.gov.in/ | yes | (secondary) | HTML | curl-200 |
| Puducherry portal search - "Assembly" (Legislative Assembly Secretariat RTI, enactments, tenders) | https://py.gov.in/search/node/Assembly | yes | assembly-record / profile | HTML (Drupal 7 search) | curl-200 |
| Wikipedia - Puducherry Legislative Assembly | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pondicherry_Legislative_Assembly | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | profile | HTML | search-result-only |
| The Week - Pondy CM announces proposal to hike funds under MLA Local Area Development scheme | https://www.theweek.in/wire-updates/national/2025/03/12/mes16-pd-funds-cm.html | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| ABP Live - Puducherry Budget 2025-26 boosts MLA development funds | https://news.abplive.com/news/india/puducherry-budget-2025-26-rangasamy-boosts-mla-funds-raises-aid-for-women-heads-of-families-details-1757163 | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| Asianet Newsable - MLA fund hiked to Rs 3 crore | https://newsable.asianetnews.com/india/puducherry-s-vision-industrial-growth-mla-fund-hiked-to-rs-3-crore-articleshow-spz6k01 | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news | search-result-only |
| Indian Express - In numbers: What is the MLALAD scheme | https://indianexpress.com/article/political-pulse/mlalad-scheme-delhi-assembly-9074154/lite/ | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | fund | news explainer | search-result-only |
| sarkaritel.com - Members of Legislative Assembly 2026: Puducherry | https://www.sarkaritel.com/members-of-legislative-assembly-puducherry/ | UNOFFICIAL - needs verification | profile | aggregator | search-result-only |

### Format note
A fetcher needs **a session cookie jar plus HTML fragment parsing** for the assembly, and **OCR** for the fund. Specifically: (1) `puddu.neva.gov.in` - issue one `curl -c jar https://puddu.neva.gov.in/` and then reuse `-b jar`; without the cookie every state route silently 302s to the national NeVA home and you get a plausible-looking 192 KB page containing none of the state's data - a particularly nasty failure mode because it looks like a successful fetch. The member roster then arrives as a **265 KB HTML fragment** from `GetMembersDashbardData` (not JSON - there is no JSON API anywhere on NeVA Puducherry), with party logos inlined as base64 data URIs and portraits served from `https://neva.gov.in/NeVA/PY/FileStructures//Member/...`. (2) Questions and Bills are **not** direct-download: their endpoints (`/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`) return *search forms* parameterised by house and session, so a real crawl must enumerate 16 assemblies x N sessions, and the responses for different `LstItem` values were byte-identical in testing. (3) `results.eci.gov.in` is the easy win - plain HTML tables, no JS, no cookie; but the **main** `www.eci.gov.in` is a React SPA (`<div id="root">` + `main.e468b53e.js`) and needs a headless browser. (4) `ceopuducherry.py.gov.in` serves its shells as plain HTML, but its results tables (Previous Elections at a Glance, Past Elections & Affidavit) are populated by AJAX and will be empty to a plain `curl`. (5) The LAD fund orders need **PDF handling**: `g.o.ms.no.24-dt-22.11.2024.pdf` and `g.o.ms-no-03-las-a1-2024.pdf` are scans carrying a rough OCR text layer whose numbers land in the wrong columns and whose words are mangled, so positional repair or fresh OCR is needed; `gia-mla-lad-scheme2021-22.pdf` and `go44-mp-mla.pdf` have **no text layer at all** and require OCR. No captcha, no login, no geo-block was encountered anywhere. Note `www.eci.gov.in` returns an HTTP 200 for almost any path (SPA catch-all), so status-code checks are meaningless there.

### Gap note
Four things cannot be sourced officially. First and most important, **MLA attendance is published nowhere** - not on NeVA, not on `py.gov.in`, not on the CEO site; the word does not appear on any assembly page, and NeVA has no attendance module at all. Second, **per-MLA expenditure and per-MLA works-completed are absent**: the LAD orders give a per-constituency *allocation and release* figure and one historical per-constituency *count of works sanctioned*, but never money spent or works finished per MLA, and the aggregate expenditure table stops at 2014-15. Third, **per-member questions, notices, assurances, debates and bills are empty** on NeVA - the member detail tabs all return "No Records Founds", so the assembly record is not resolvable to an individual MLA. Fourth, **the Rs 3 crore figure** reported by several news outlets for the 2025-26 budget could not be confirmed from any official LAD or budget document; the highest officially evidenced figure is Rs 2 crore per MLA per year (G.O.Ms.No.44/LAD(SW)/2019-20), so unless an official order is produced the site should show Rs 2 crore and cite that G.O. also, **nominated MLAs are named only inside fund annexures** - the three 2024 nominees (K. Venkatesan, V.P. Ramalingam, R.B. Ashok Babu) appear in G.O.Ms.No.24 but are absent from both `py.gov.in/mlas` (which shows only the 30 elected constituencies) and the NeVA 29-member roster; and the currency of `py.gov.in/mlas` is imperfect (it lists N. Rangasamy twice - once for Thattanchavady, once for Mangalam - and still shows Thattanchavady, which NeVA treats as vacant pending the 2026 bye-election).

### Platform
Puducherry's assembly site is **not bespoke**: it is a state instance of **NeVA (National e-Vidhan Application)**, the shared NIC/MoPA legislative platform, hosted at its own subdomain `puddu.neva.gov.in` alongside 40 sibling house subdomains listed on `https://neva.gov.in/` (`kerala.neva.gov.in`, `gujarat.neva.gov.in`, `tnla.neva.gov.in`, `mpla.neva.gov.in`, `wbla.neva.gov.in`, `loksabha.neva.gov.in`, `rajyasabha.neva.gov.in`, ...). The evidence is explicit: the footer reads "© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India / Content managed by: Puducherry Legislative Assembly"; the subdomain is a single letter-swap from the pattern; route names (`/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Bill/FetchDataListBillsBySearch`, `/Member/Details/<id>?AssemblyID=<n>`, `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec`) are platform-wide; and the strongest tell is a leaked template string - the `<title>` of `https://puddu.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/63?AssemblyID=16` reads "Puducherry Vidhan Sabha Member Details | NeVA | **eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh**", i.e. the same codebase serves Himachal Pradesh. PIB confirms the rollout: MoS Dr. L. Murugan inaugurated NeVA for the Puducherry Legislative Assembly on **9 June 2025** after a trial run during the 6th session of the 15th Assembly, and the Puducherry Assembly Secretariat tendered for a NeVA "System Integrator". **One NeVA scraper therefore serves Puducherry and every other NeVA house.** The fund side is the opposite - **bespoke**: `lad.py.gov.in` and `py.gov.in` are separate Drupal 7 sites on the Puducherry government domain with their own themes and no shared vendor, and the MLA-LAD data is not on any national platform (there is no state-level MPLADS equivalent portal).

---

# Cross-cutting findings

## 1. The single most important finding: NeVA is a common platform — but "onboarded" is not "populated"

**Yes, most states share a common platform.** It is the **National e-Vidhan Application (NeVA)**,
built by the **National Informatics Centre** under the **Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs**,
Government of India. It is the closest thing this project has to a single national scraper.

The evidence that NeVA is one codebase rather than many similar sites is unusually strong, and it
is worth recording because it is what makes one fetcher viable:

* **One hostname pattern.** Instances follow `<statecode>.neva.gov.in` — `asla` (Assam), `bla`
  (Bihar), `gujarat`, `hpvs` (Himachal), `jkla` (J&K), `kerala`, `mizo`, `nagaland`, `odisha`,
  `puddu` (Puducherry), `sikkim`, `tnla`, `tripura`, `tsla` (Telangana), `upvs` (UP), `utkh`
  (Uttarakhand), `wbla` (West Bengal), `pvs` (Punjab), `raj` (Rajasthan), `mgla` (Meghalaya),
  `hrla` (Haryana), `jhla` (Jharkhand), `arla` (Arunachal), `mpla` (MP), `goa`, `manipur`, and
  more. The national portal at `https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` enumerates them.
* **One footer, byte-identical.** `© 2026 National eVidhan Application : All Rights Reserved
  Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs & National Informatics Centre : Government of India`, plus the
  build badge `Version: 2.0.2  Last Updated: 25 Mar 2026`, on every instance.
* **One route vocabulary.** `/Member/Details/{id}`, `/ContactDirectory/MenuContactDirec?MLT=..&MSLT=..`,
  `/ContactDirectory/FetchMembersList`, `/Member/Member_Question?MemberId=&AssemblyID=`,
  `/Questions/FetchDataList`, `/Bill/GetBillDashBoardData`, `/Debates`, `/Committee?ItemId=`.
* **One asset store, namespaced by state.** `https://cms.neva.gov.in/NeVA/<STATE>/FileStructures/...`
  — so `/NeVA/PB/`, `/NeVA/GJ/`, `/NeVA/HPVS/`, `/NeVA/OR/` and so on.
* **One shared template string, leaking between tenants.** This is the decisive evidence, because
  it can only happen inside a single application instance. The string **`eVidhan- Himachal
  Pradesh`** appears in the page title of member-detail routes belonging to *other* states:
  Assam's `/Member/Details/135`, Punjab's `pvs.neva.gov.in/Member/Details/146`, and every
  `/Member/Details/*` probe on the Telangana instance. Gujarat's instance returns Himachal
  Pradesh's assembly shell to an unseeded session.

### NeVA publishes its own onboarding status — and it is NOT a proxy for data availability

`https://neva.gov.in/Home/NeVA` renders a status badge against each of its **35 listed instances**
(verified 2026-09-14, HTTP 200, 192,345 bytes). This is machine-readable and is the single most
useful artefact in this whole file, because it tells a fetcher which instances are meant to be live:

| Status | Instances | Which |
|---|---|---|
| **Digital House** (21) | `asla` `bla` `blc` `dla` `gujarat` `hpvs` `hrla` `manipur` `mgla` `mizo` `nagaland` `odisha` `puddu` `pvs` `raj` `sikkim` `tnla` `tripura` `uplc` `upvs` `utkh` | Assam, Bihar (both houses), Delhi, Gujarat, Himachal, Haryana, Manipur, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Odisha, Puducherry, Punjab, Rajasthan, Sikkim, Tamil Nadu, Tripura, UP (both), Uttarakhand |
| **Onboarded** (10) | `apa` `apc` `arla` `chg` `kla` `klc` `mpla` `tsla` `tslc` `wbla` | Andhra Pradesh (both houses), Arunachal, Chhattisgarh, Karnataka (both), Madhya Pradesh, Telangana (both), West Bengal |
| **Prospective** (4) | `goa` `kerala` `mhla` `mhlc` | Goa, Kerala, Maharashtra (both houses) |

**Two caveats on that table.** First, the listing is **not exhaustive**: `jhla.neva.gov.in`
(Jharkhand, HTTP 200, 64,480 bytes) and `jkla.neva.gov.in` (Jammu and Kashmir, HTTP 200, 105,222
bytes) both serve live instances but appear in neither the 35-entry listing nor its badges, so the
status page must be treated as a starting point rather than the register. Second — and this is the
one that matters — *the badge does not predict whether any data is actually there.* Several
instances marked **Digital House** return nothing at all — **Bihar** (`/Member/Details/504` and
`/132` return an identical 22,426-byte shell reading *"Assurances No Records Founds / Debates No
Records Founds / BILLS No Records Founds"*), **Himachal Pradesh** (roster AJAX empty for every id;
Questions/Bills/Debates redirect home), **Haryana** (`/Member/Details/32` returns a shell whose
async endpoint answers *"Questions … No Records Founds"*), **Odisha** (`/Member` 404s,
`/Member/AllMembers` returns HTTP 500), **Tamil Nadu** (Questions/Bill/Debates/Business all 302 to
the national landing page), and **Manipur** (`/Members` 404s). Conversely **Telangana**, marked
only *Onboarded*, is an empty shell whose member-detail route renders a blank template still
carrying the label *"eVidhan- Himachal Pradesh"*; and **West Bengal**, also *Onboarded*, signed its
MoU only on 4 June 2026 and returns HTTP 500 from `FetchMembersList`.

So there are two tiers, and conflating them is the real risk:

**Tier A — NeVA instances that actually serve per-member data.** Arunachal Pradesh, Delhi, Gujarat,
Jammu and Kashmir, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Punjab, Tripura, Uttarakhand, Uttar Pradesh, plus
(roster only) Assam, Rajasthan and Sikkim — **fourteen or so legislatures today.**

**Tier B — NeVA instances that render the template and hold no records.** At minimum Bihar,
Himachal Pradesh, Haryana, Jharkhand, Manipur, Madhya Pradesh, Odisha, Tamil Nadu, Telangana and
West Bengal — **ten.**

**All 36 jurisdictions in this reconnaissance are `curl_fetchable: mixed`**, and Tier B is exactly
why: the template is uniform, the population is not. A NeVA fetcher is worth building, but it must
probe for records per instance rather than trusting the badge.

**Tier C — legislatures not on NeVA at all.** Andhra Pradesh (Liferay DXP, customised by the
**Centre for Good Governance, Hyderabad**), Chhattisgarh, Goa, Karnataka, Kerala (listed
*"Prospective — yet to sign MoU"*), Maharashtra, and Assam's and West Bengal's primary sites.

### Other shared platforms worth knowing about

* **Centre for Good Governance (CGG), Hyderabad** — a genuine multi-state vendor. The footer
  `Designed & Developed by : <cgg logo>` and the vendor asset path
  `o/com.cgg.constants/...` appear on both **Andhra Pradesh** and **Telangana**, and Telangana's
  member module uses the `ce-mip-mp-*` template prefix (`ce-mip-mp-tile-container`,
  `ce-mip-mp-picture`, `ce-mip-mp-name`). One CGG-pattern adapter should reach both states.
* **NIC S3WaaS** — a shared WordPress-based national template (`sdo-theme`, `district-theme-6/7`,
  tenant buckets on `cdn.s3waas.gov.in`) used by the **union territory administrations and district
  sites**, including the legislature-less UTs. `secforuts.mha.gov.in` repeats an identical per-UT
  URL scheme (`/ut-of-lakshadweep/`, `/ut-of-andaman-nicobar-islands/`, `/ut-of-ladakh/`), so one
  scraper serves all five legislature-less UTs.
* **`results.eci.gov.in`** — a genuinely shared national results template keyed by an `S<nn>`/`U<nn>`
  state code, serving every state from one directory with identical markup. See §6 for its
  rotating-path trap.
* **DSpace 6.3** — Assam's Assembly Digital Library and Odisha's document repository run on the
  same open-source product (`/handle/123456789/...`), credited in-page to a third-party integrator.

## 2. The development fund is the biggest and most uniform gap in the country

**There is no national equivalent of MPLADS for MLAs.** MPLADS (`https://mplads.mospi.gov.in/`)
covers Members of Parliament only — Lok Sabha and Rajya Sabha. Every state runs its own scheme
under its own name, its own rules, and its own (usually non-existent) publishing regime.

Of the 31 jurisdictions that have a legislature:

| Fund data availability | Count | Jurisdictions |
|---|---|---|
| **Per-MLA data published** | **4** | Mizoram (`planning.mizoram.gov.in`), Odisha (`mlalads.odisha.gov.in` — a real JSON API), Rajasthan (`ework.rajasthan.gov.in` — a per-MLA JSON API), Puducherry (`lad.py.gov.in`). All four URLs were re-fetched at the end of this reconnaissance and all four returned HTTP 200. |
| Aggregate only | 1 | Assam (statewide Rs 12,600.00 lakh against 126 LACs; one district's works list) |
| Fund exists, but no per-MLA data published | 24 | Andhra Pradesh, Arunachal Pradesh, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Goa, Gujarat, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Karnataka, Kerala, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Manipur, Meghalaya, Nagaland, Punjab, Tamil Nadu, Telangana, Tripura, Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand, West Bengal, Jammu and Kashmir |
| No MLA fund exists at all | 1 | Sikkim — the state operates no MLA local area development fund |
| Not established either way | 1 | Delhi — the MLALAD scheme page exists at `udd.delhi.gov.in`, but the fund-utilisation page renders *"No Data Available"* and only ~27 of 70 constituencies have sanction orders |

**The fund has no single name, which is itself a finding.** Recorded local names include
*Vidhayak Nidhi* (Bihar, Jharkhand, UP, West Bengal colloquially), *Vidhayak Sthaniya Kshetra Vikas
Nidhi* (Madhya Pradesh, Rajasthan), *Vidhayak Kshetra Vikas Nidhi / VKVN* (Himachal Pradesh),
*Vidhayak Adarsh Nagar Avam Gram Yojana / VANGY* (Haryana — **not** called MLALAD at all),
*MLAAD / MLA Area Development* (Assam), *MLA Area Development Fund* (Tripura), *MLALADS*
(Arunachal Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Mizoram), *MLA LAD* (Odisha), *MLA LAD Scheme* (Delhi,
Puducherry), *MLACDS* (Tamil Nadu), *MLA-LAD Scheme* (Puducherry), *Bidhayak Elaka Unnayan
Prakalpa / BEUP* (West Bengal), *Constituency Development Fund / CDF* (Telangana, Jammu and
Kashmir), *MLA Local Development Programme / आमदार निधी* (Maharashtra), *Special Development Fund
for MLAs* (Kerala), *Local Area Development Programme / LADP* (Nagaland), *Special Rural Works
Programme + CMSRDF* (Meghalaya), *Rangla Punjab Vikas Scheme* (Punjab, press-reported only), and
*Councillor Constituency Development Allocation / CCDA* (Ladakh — for councillors, not MLAs).
**Any fetcher must be aware that a keyword search for "MLALAD" will miss most of the country.**

**Where a portal does exist, it is usually closed.** Chhattisgarh's MLALADS Online
(`descg.gov.in/mlaladsonline/`) displays *"Software closed for updation"* behind a Login control and
every public report path 404s. Himachal Pradesh's SMIS 2.0 and MLA Priority Scheme Monitoring are
login-gated. Maharashtra's e-Samarth tracker is internal with no published URL, and `esamarth.in` is
a parked non-government domain. Madhya Pradesh's MeitY NEGD directory entry for its "MLALAD
Application" has an **empty Website Link field**.

**Two negative results are worth recording as positive findings:** Andhra Pradesh and Punjab were
each probed across 15 and 6 candidate hosts respectively, all of which failed DNS resolution
(`HTTP 000`), while the state portals themselves contain zero occurrences of "MLalad", "Vidhayak"
or "Nidhi". That is evidence of *absence*, not merely of a failed search.

## 3. Attendance is the second-biggest gap — and NeVA has no module for it

Of 36 jurisdictions:

* **34 publish no attendance data in any form.**
* **Haryana** is `partial`: attendance exists as born-digital per-sitting PDFs, never aggregated
  per member — any percentage shown would be the Atlas's own arithmetic over dozens of PDFs.
* **Maharashtra** is `partial`: attendance exists only for 2014–2017, as a 27 MB scanned PDF.

**NeVA has no attendance module at all.** The string "attendance" appears zero times across NeVA
Meghalaya's questions page, bills page, member directory and member detail pages; the same
zero-hit result was obtained on NeVA Odisha. The NeVA member detail page's tab set is
`General | Questions | Notice | Assurances | Committee | Debates | BILLS` — no attendance.
Several states add a *statistics* page whose attendance-adjacent columns are simply empty
(Andhra Pradesh's session calendar has blank Statistics and WorkingAnalysis columns for every row).

This is the sharpest contrast with the already-built MP page: **Sansad publishes per-MP attendance
cleanly**, through three JSON endpoints (`getMemberAttendanceByMpsno`,
`getMemberAttendanceMemberWise`, `getMemberAttendanceDateWise`) with a documented six-value legend.
No state assembly comes close.

## 4. Private members' bills are named everywhere and recorded nowhere

**Not one jurisdiction publishes a clean, per-member private members' bills dataset.**
Eleven are `partial`, and in every case the reason is the same: the category exists in the
interface but the data does not, or the data exists but carries no sponsor attribution.

* NeVA defines `Private Members Bills` as a filter (Sikkim, Puducherry, J&K and others) and returns
  **"No Records Founds"**.
* Where bills *are* published, they are session-level PDFs with **no member column and no
  government-vs-private classification** — Assam (seven list pages, introducer absent), Punjab
  (343 rows, 373 PDF links, no member column), Bihar (a "Non Official Bill" list that is a scanned
  image), Tamil Nadu.
* Chhattisgarh's `prastav.htm` is only the text of procedural rules 130–132.
* Maharashtra, Karnataka, Rajasthan, Meghalaya, Madhya Pradesh, Odisha and West Bengal publish zero
  PMB rows.

The **Lok Sabha** route already in use for the MP page — `api_rs/legislation/getBills` with
`billType=Private%20Member`, filtered client-side on `billIntroducedBy` — has no state equivalent
anywhere in the country.

## 5. The richest and most machine-readable jurisdictions

Ranked by how much of the three sections a `curl`-only fetcher can genuinely obtain today:

1. **Rajasthan** — the strongest overall. `ework.rajasthan.gov.in` is a **per-MLA fund JSON API**
   (the only state besides Odisha with one), plus a 200-member roster with per-MLA photographs and
   starred/unstarred questions. Gaps: attendance endpoints all 302 to `/404?aspxerrorpath=…`, and
   no per-MLA *sanctioned allocation limit* is published (the allocated amount appears only as one
   unnamed component of the *उपलब्ध राशि* available-amount formula).
2. **Odisha** — `mlalads.odisha.gov.in` publishes per-MLA MLALAD works as a **real JSON API**, plus
   a 147-member roster with photographs. Gaps: attendance and PMBs published nowhere; the per-MLA
   allocation limit is login-gated; Form 20 and gazette documents are scans.
3. **Mizoram** — the only jurisdiction scoring on all six axes (roster, photos, starred *and*
   unstarred questions, a per-MLA fund, official results). Gaps: no attendance at all, no statewide
   per-MLA fund portal (works data is scattered across district sites, with no allocated limit or
   expenditure total), and the 2023 result link is broken.
4. **Kerala** — a complete 140-member roster with **100% photo coverage** and genuinely
   **curl-fetchable JSON for starred and unstarred questions**. Gaps: no per-MLA fund data in any
   format, no attendance, and a PMB search UI with an empty backend.
5. **Andhra Pradesh** — 175-member roster with 175 per-MLA JPEGs (480×720) plus a **384-page
   born-digital, text-extractable "Who is Who"** containing 175 full biographies with a photograph
   on the facing page. One of the best single profile artefacts found. Gaps: no fund portal of any
   kind; the Questions module is behind login + CAPTCHA; debates are 100–200 MB scans.
6. **Telangana** — a 119-member roster with **119 curl-able high-resolution JPEGs** (2784×3551) and
   per-MLA profile pages, plus an ECI statistical report that is a 120-page **text-layer** PDF.
   Gaps: no fund portal; ECI's own CEO Form-20 PDFs for all 119 ACs are **scans requiring OCR**;
   the state's NeVA instance is an empty shell.
7. **Uttar Pradesh** — a 397-record **ASMX JSON roster** plus a photographed Liferay member
   directory, and per-MLA starred/unstarred questions. Gaps: no public per-MLA fund portal,
   attendance nowhere.

Also notable: **Assam** publishes a bespoke Laravel site (126 members, photographs, per-member term
dates) alongside NeVA and a DSpace 6.3 digital library — the widest source surface of any state,
though questions are not indexed by member and attendance is absent.

## 6. Election results: two authorities, and a rotating-path trap

**34 of 36 jurisdictions have both an ECI route and a state CEO route.** The practical division is:

* **`https://www.eci.gov.in`** is authoritative for national aggregation, but the new site is a
  **React SPA** that serves only a ~1,279-byte shell to `curl`, exposing a JSON backend under
  `/eci-backend/public/api/`. Some endpoints require a static header secret (`ECI@MAIN825`) taken
  from ECI's own public JS bundle. **A headless browser or the JSON backend is required.**
* **`https://old.eci.gov.in`** is the curl-friendly legacy archive, server-rendered HTML, with state
  statistical reports going back decades. This is the better target for historical work.
* **`https://results.eci.gov.in`** is a genuinely shared template across all states (keyed by
  `S<nn>`/`U<nn>`), but its **path rotates per election cycle**. On 2026-09-14 the root
  meta-refreshed to `/ResultAcByeAugust2026/index.htm`, and every 2024 assembly folder tested
  returned 404 — **including all 2022 Punjab folders**, which have already been purged. Any URL
  hardcoded against this host will rot; it must be re-resolved at fetch time.
* **State CEOs publish the statutory final documents** — Form 20 (AC-wise result sheet) and
  Form 21E/21C. These are the legally authoritative record, and ECI's own "Link to Form 20" page
  points at the CEO site. **But they are frequently scanned images needing OCR**: verified scans
  include Andhra Pradesh (1.5 MB, 15 pages, zero extractable characters), Bihar (11.9 MB, produced
  by "HP Scan Extended Application"), and all 119 of Telangana's Form-20 PDFs (`/Font` = 0).
  Chhattisgarh is a welcome exception — its 90 Form-20 PDFs carry a real text layer.

A recurring pattern worth noting: several states' CEO sites are **dead or broken** —
`ceoarunachal.nic.in` returns HTTP 500.30, `ceoladakh.nic.in` times out on both 80 and 443 despite
resolving in DNS, `ceodaman.nic.in` fails TLS with a signature-length error, and
`ceoandaman`/`ceoassam` hosts return 404 for their linked Form-20 documents.

## 7. Five union territories have no legislature — and must render as "not applicable"

**Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Chandigarh, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, Ladakh, and
Lakshadweep** have no Legislative Assembly. **Delhi, Jammu and Kashmir and Puducherry do.**

For the five, the correct rendering on the MLA page is *not applicable*, never a blank or an
invented source. The constitutional basis is consistent and citable:

* **Article 239A** empowers Parliament to create a legislature only *"for the Union territory of
  Puducherry"*. Its footnote records that the **Goa, Daman and Diu Reorganisation Act, 1987**
  removed Daman and Diu in 1987; Dadra and Nagar Haveli was never covered.
* **Article 240(1)** lists these territories as governed by Presidential Regulation, and
  **Article 239(1)** has them administered by an Administrator. The Ministry of Home Affairs'
  own J&K and Ladakh Affairs page states verbatim: *"Legislature: It is a Union Territory without
  Legislature."*
* Administering authority in every case: the **Ministry of Home Affairs**.

Two independent machine-readable confirmations were obtained, and both are stronger evidence than
prose: **NeVA contains zero occurrences of "Andaman"/"Nicobar", "Ladakh", "Chandigarh" and
"Dadra"/"Daman"/"Diu"** while listing Puducherry and Delhi; and **ECI's own JSON API
`get-ac-election-state` lists no entry for any of the five in any year** — including 2024, which
does list `U08: Jammu & Kashmir`, the other UT created by the same 2019 Reorganisation Act.

**What exists instead, and must not be mislabelled as MLA data:**

* Each UT has an administration portal (NIC S3WaaS) and a CEO — and for those with only Lok Sabha
  representation, per-MP MPLADS data *does* exist and *is* fetchable.
* **Ladakh** has two **Lahdakh Autonomous Hill Development Councils** (LAHDC Leh, LAHDC Kargil)
  with 26 councillors each. They pass no laws and elect **councillors, not MLAs**. Their rosters are
  plain HTML tables (name + constituency; **no party for Kargil, no photograph for either**) on
  `leh.nic.in` and `kargil.nic.in`.
* **Chandigarh** has a Municipal Corporation Ward Development Fund for councillors.
* **Puducherry, Delhi and J&K** are ordinary legislatures in every respect relevant here and are
  documented above as such.

## 8. Scanned PDFs are the default format for assembly record documents

Across the 31 legislatures, the pattern for questions, debates and bills is a per-sitting-day PDF,
and the majority are **image scans with no text layer**. Verified scans found during this
reconnaissance include:

* Chhattisgarh question lists — `q13072026.pdf`, **56,105,807 bytes, 409 pages, 0 font resources,
  4,374 images**;
* Andhra Pradesh daily debates — a sample is **140,373,821 bytes** with zero fonts and 220 images;
* West Bengal's BEUP 2025 scheme rules — 36 MB, 82 pages, 0 `/Font`, 0 `/ToUnicode`;
* Arunachal Pradesh's MLALADS Revised Guidelines 2014 — pure image scan;
* Bihar's question and "Non Official Bill" lists — all scans;
* the 2023 Form-20 sheets of Andhra Pradesh, Bihar and all 119 of Telangana's.

Practical consequence: for those jurisdictions the choice is **OCR**, or accepting a documented
gap. Several sites publish *some* born-digital documents alongside scans — Chhattisgarh's
proceedings (`proc13072026.pdf`, 172 font resources), Andhra Pradesh's "Who is Who", Telangana's
ECI statistical report, Jammu and Kashmir's district result PDFs — so a fetch pipeline should test
each document for a text layer rather than assuming either way.

One further blocker is worth flagging because it is invisible until it bites: **several NeVA
instances 302-redirect to the national portal unless an `ASP.NET_SessionId` cookie is seeded with
one GET to the instance root first.** This affects Assam, Puducherry, Telangana and J&K. A `Referer`
header alone does not work. A cookie jar with a single warm-up request is the fix.

## 9. Honest summary of coverage

Across the 36 jurisdictions this reconnaissance recorded **1,826 individual URLs**, of which the
large majority are on government domains; each carries its own verification level in the
per-jurisdiction `### Sources` tables below.

Counts are **out of the 31 jurisdictions that have a legislature**, since a legislature-less UT
cannot fail to publish an MLA record.

| Section | Covered officially | Partial | Not covered |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Profile** — member roster | 29 | 1 | 1 (Himachal Pradesh) |
| **Profile** — photograph | 27 | 0 | 4 |
| **Development fund** — per-MLA figures | **4** | 1 | 26 |
| **Assembly record** — starred questions | 16 | 11 | 4 |
| **Assembly record** — unstarred questions | 13 | 12 | 6 |
| **Assembly record** — attendance | **0** | 2 | 29 |
| **Assembly record** — private members' bills | **0** | 11 | 20 |
| **Election results** | 31 | 0 | 0 |

Counting all 36 instead: 34 publish no attendance in any form, 25 publish no private members'
bills, and 5 have no legislature and therefore no MLA record of any kind.

The pattern is unmistakable: **identity is published, accountability is not.** Photographs, names,
parties and constituencies are widely and often beautifully published — Andhra Pradesh's 384-page
"Who is Who", Telangana's 119 high-resolution JPEGs, Kerala's 140-member roster at 100% photo
coverage. But the three things that would let a citizen judge an MLA's *work* — what they spent,
whether they turned up, and what they proposed — are, in almost every state, either unpublished,
locked behind a login, or buried in a scanned PDF that no one has ever parsed.

**The one place India does this well is Parliament, not the states.** The MP page of this Atlas
already runs on two sources that have no state equivalent: MPLADS, which publishes per-MP
allocation, works recommended, sanctioned, completed and expenditure for every MP in the country
from one portal; and Sansad, which publishes per-MP attendance through three JSON endpoints,
per-MP questions with the official reply document attached, and private members' bills. For the
state layer, **nothing comparable exists in any of the 36 jurisdictions.** That is not a gap in
this reconnaissance — it is what the reconnaissance found, and it is the honest answer the MLA
page's empty states should keep saying until per-state fetchers are written.
